# 11 — Change Log (Class A)

Every substantive change made in Phase 2A between the manuscript (`work/docx/manuscript.md`) and the Class B chapters, one block per chapter, entries `CH-NN-KKK` (NN = chapter). Types: clarification, correction, completion, cut, restructure, style, mechanical change. Mechanical changes cite `Source: Gate 1 — OQ-NN` (see `05_gate1_decisions.md`).

## Chapter 02 — Game Components

### CH-02-001 · §2 Game Components
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "Two set codes are used throughout: PWA is the Painted World of Ariamis expansion, and ToG is the Tomb of Giants expansion."
- Rationale: The set codes PWA and ToG were used without definition; a one-line key is stated once in the chapter intro.
- Source: F-054; clean-up list

### CH-02-002 · §2.1 Characters
- Type: style
- Before: "Each character has a unique miniature, character dashboard, three to four starting equipment cards, and ten class-specific treasure cards."
- After: "Each character has a model, a character dashboard (see §3.1), three or four starting equipment cards, and ten class-specific treasure cards, of which five are transposed treasure (see §2.5)."
- Rationale: "model" is the single term for the physical piece; copied official prose rewritten in the book's voice with cross-references; "(Characters & PWA)" tags rewritten without the ampersand.
- Source: C-049, F-093, C-112

### CH-02-003 · §2.2 Enemies
- Type: correction
- Before: "1x HungryVoracious Mimic (Explorers)"
- After: "2x Mimic (Explorers), with the Hungry Mimic and Voracious Mimic data cards"
- Rationale: Two names run together and the count was wrong; the expansion supplies two models and two data cards.
- Source: C-006, D-019, CMP-0007

### CH-02-004 · §2.2 Enemies
- Type: correction
- Before: "1x Mushroom Child & Parent (Darkroot Basin)"
- After: "1x Mushroom Child (Darkroot)" and "1x Mushroom Parent (Darkroot)"
- Rationale: Two enemies with separate data cards were written as one line; the Darkroot set supplies 14 models and seven data cards, which the split list now matches.
- Source: D-026, C-112

### CH-02-005 · §2.2 Enemies
- Type: correction
- Before: "1x Skeletal Beast (ToG)"
- After: "1x Skeleton Beast (ToG)"
- Rationale: The printed name is Skeleton Beast.
- Source: C-007

### CH-02-006 · §2.2 Enemies
- Type: style
- Before: "3x Ironclad Soldiers (Iron Keep)" / "2X Large Hollow Soldier (Core)" / "3x Plough Scarecrow (Darkroot Basin)"
- After: "3x Ironclad Soldier (Iron Keep)" / "2x Large Hollow Soldier (Core)" / "3x Plow Scarecrow (Darkroot)"
- Rationale: One quantity format "Nx Singular Name"; printed card names keep their printed spelling; the expansion is named Darkroot.
- Source: C-014, C-108, C-010, D-027, Gate 1 — OQ-17

### CH-02-007 · §2.2 Enemies
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Each enemy has a number of miniatures and a data card."
- After: "Each enemy has a data card (see §8.2) and the number of models listed. Crystal lizards (see §14) and mimics (see §13) are optional enemies with their own rules."
- Rationale: Cross-reference to the owning sections; the two optional enemies in the list are flagged as such. Counts verified for the core, Darkroot, Iron Keep, Explorers, Executioner's Chariot, and Tomb of Giants sets.
- Source: C-049, C-077

### CH-02-008 · §2.3 Bosses
- Type: correction
- Before: "Dragonslayer Ornstein (Core)" / "Executioner Smough (Core)"
- After: "Ornstein & Smough (Core)" and "Ornstein & Smough is one main-boss fought with two models, Dragonslayer Ornstein and Executioner Smough. The pair shares one health dial and one behaviour deck, and each behaviour card names the model that acts (see §17.1)."
- Rationale: The pair is one main-boss with a shared deck and dial; the component list now matches the campaign lists.
- Source: D-022, A-039, C-013, Gate 1 — OQ-25

### CH-02-009 · §2.3 Bosses
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The Last Giant" added to the mega-boss list
- Rationale: A boss used by the Big Game Hunting campaign was absent from the component list.
- Source: A-108, C-075, D-023, CMP-0008

### CH-02-010 · §2.3 Bosses
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "Each boss has a model, a health dial, a data card (see §15.4), a behaviour deck (see §15.5), and between two and four boss treasure cards (see §2.5)."
- Rationale: The boss component set was never stated; mega-boss set tags replaced by one sentence ("supplied by the expansion of the same name, except the Stray Demon"); headings normalised to Title Case (Mega-Bosses).
- Source: D-024, C-041, C-101

### CH-02-011 · §2.4 Phantoms
- Type: correction
- Before: "Xanthous King Jeramiah" / "Armourer Dennis" / "Marvellous Chester" / "Sword Master Sabre" / "Sirris of the Sunless Realm"
- After: "Xanthous King Jeremiah" / "Armorer Dennis" / "Marvelous Chester" / "Sword Master" / "Sirris of the Sunless Realms"
- Rationale: Printed card names.
- Source: C-005, C-008, C-010, C-011, D-027, Gate 1 — OQ-17

### CH-02-012 · §2.4 Phantoms
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Invaders (Standard)" … "Summons (Advanced)" (headings only)
- After: "Phantoms are the summons and invaders of the Phantoms expansion (see §12). Each phantom has a model, a data card, and behaviour cards; Kirk, Knight of Thorns and Longfinger Kirk share one model."
- Rationale: phantom = umbrella, summon = friendly, invader = hostile; the use of standard and advanced phantoms is stated in one sentence each with cross-references to §12.1 and §12.2; headings renamed Standard Summons, Advanced Summons, Standard Invaders, Advanced Invaders.
- Source: C-064, C-101, C-103, D-024

### CH-02-013 · §2.5 Treasure Cards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "- Legendary treasure cards - 10x (Core) - 20 (Expansions)"
- After: "**Treasure card.** Any card from a treasure deck: starting equipment, class-specific treasure, common treasure, legendary treasure, or boss treasure. A treasure card that may be equipped to a dashboard slot is an equipment card (see §4.1)." Counts set in a table.
- Rationale: The treasure card and equipment card relationship is defined once; the missing "x" is moot in a table; the ten class-specific cards are explained as five class and five transposed.
- Source: C-054, C-078, C-108

### CH-02-014 · §2.6 Encounter Cards and Ambush Cards
- Type: cut
- Before: "10x Level 1 event cards / 10x Level 2 event cards / 10x Level 3 event cards / 5x Special Trap cards"
- After: deleted
- Rationale: Event cards do not exist in this book; the Special Trap cards go with them.
- Source: A-090, D-001, A-091, Gate 1 — OQ-06

### CH-02-015 · §2.6 Encounter Cards and Ambush Cards
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Each ambush card shows Treasure or Mimic on its face. When mimics are in play, one ambush card is dealt face down beside each encounter tile and is revealed only when the characters choose to open that tile's chest (see §13)."
- Rationale: Ambush cards are used by the rules but were absent from the component list.
- Source: A-092, CMP-0012(b)

### CH-02-016 · §2.6 Encounter Cards and Ambush Cards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Total 169 encounter cards in all available content."
- After: "An encounter card lists the level, enemies, terrain, and traps of one encounter. One encounter card is dealt face down to each encounter tile at setup (see §6.5) …" with the counts in a table and "Level 4 encounter cards are supplied only by the mega-boss expansions, four with each".
- Rationale: What the card is and where Level 4 cards come from were unstated; heading renamed without the deleted event cards.
- Source: C-103, CMP-0012(a)

### CH-02-017 · §2.7 Dice
- Type: completion
- Before: "2X Orange"
- After: "2x Orange dice" and the face table "Black 0, 1, 1, 1, 2, 2; Blue 1, 1, 2, 2, 2, 3; Orange 1, 2, 2, 3, 3, 4; Green blank, blank, blank, dodge, dodge, dodge" with one sentence each on attack, block and resist, and dodge dice.
- Rationale: A standalone rulebook must state the faces of its dice; quantity format corrected.
- Source: B-001, C-108, Gate 1 — OQ-23

### CH-02-018 · §2.8 Tokens and Terrain Elements
- Type: correction
- Before: "35x Condition tokens - 7x each of poison, *Bleed*, *Stagger*, and frostbite tokens - 4x each of *Calamity* and *4x Corrosion* tokens"
- After: "36x Condition tokens: 7x each of bleed, poison, frostbite, and stagger (Core and expansions); 4x calamity (Black Dragon Kalameet); 4x corrosion (Gaping Dragon)"
- Rationale: 7×4 + 4×2 = 36; the stray italicised "4x" is removed; the two expansion conditions carry their set tags. D-020's five-per-condition count is not adopted (see issues).
- Source: A-117, B-027, C-073, C-029, D-020 (partial)

### CH-02-019 · §2.8 Tokens and Terrain Elements
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "8x Hollow cubes" and "Hollow cubes are of a colour distinct from the damage, stamina, and stat cubes; two are placed on the endurance bar of a character who is hollow (see §7.2)."
- Rationale: The hollow marker was used under four names and never listed; the 16 white cubes are the stat cubes and none are spare.
- Source: C-050, B-029

### CH-02-020 · §2.8 Tokens and Terrain Elements
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "4x Pillar tokens (Asylum Demon)" and "Pillar tokens are placed only on the pillar nodes of the Asylum Demon mega-boss game board (see §5.5, Pillars)."
- Rationale: Pillar tokens are used by the Asylum Demon and Stray Demon rules but were never listed.
- Source: A-114, CMP-0012(a), CMP-0012(f)

### CH-02-021 · §2.8 Tokens and Terrain Elements
- Type: clarification
- Before: "10x1, 5x3, 3x5, and 1x8 Soul tokens"
- After: "10x Soul tokens of value 1, 5x Soul tokens of value 3, 3x Soul tokens of value 5, and 1x Soul token of value 8 (Core)" and "the supply of soul tokens is not a limit, and when it is exhausted the players track souls with any other counters."
- Rationale: Individual soul tracking exceeds the printed supply in ordinary play; wound tokens set in the same format.
- Source: B-028, C-108

### CH-02-022 · §2.8 Tokens and Terrain Elements
- Type: style
- Before: "4x Estus *flask* tokens" / "1x Fog Gate token" / "2x Boss encounter tiles" / "6x Double-sided encounter tiles" / "4x Treasure token or models"
- After: "4x Estus flask tokens" / "1x Fog gate token" / "2x Boss tiles (Core), the mini-boss tile and the main-boss tile" / "6x Encounter tiles, double-sided" / "4x Chest tokens" with "The Explorers expansion supplies 18x Terrain models, which stand in place of the corresponding tokens."
- Rationale: One name per component (wound token, fog gate token, boss tiles, encounter tiles, chest); italics only on glossary keywords; the Explorers terrain models listed once; each component group gets one or two sentences and a cross-reference to its owning section.
- Source: A-113, C-029, C-058, C-063, C-077, D-024

### CH-02-023 · §2.8 Tokens and Terrain Elements
- Type: cut
- Before: new text (a "first player token" was proposed for the list)
- After: not listed
- Rationale: The token is removed from the game; threat order fixes the first character turn. The findings proposing to add it are superseded.
- Source: CMP-0011, A-017; D-018 and B-030 superseded

- After: new section listing the Phantoms set (summon and invader models, data cards, behaviour cards, treasure cards, health dials, invader tokens), the Explorers mimic set and terrain models, the Iron Keep Titanite Scale cards, the Characters armour set models, and the mega-boss set (game board, Level 4 cards, data card, health dial, behaviour cards, treasure cards, strafe cards, track behaviour cards, setup card, pillar tokens), each with a cross-reference.
- Rationale: Every component referenced by a rule is now listed; the Level 4 tile is identified as the mega-boss game board's encounter side.
- Source: CMP-0012(a), CMP-0012(e), D-024, C-077, A-092

### CH-02-025 · §2 Game Components
- Type: style

- After: "Tokens and Terrain Elements" / "Mega-Bosses" / straight apostrophes throughout; glossary keywords italicised at first use per H2 section; icon tags appended to the ToG enemies, encounter level and trap icons, dice, condition and terrain tokens

- After: n/a

### CH-02-027 · §2.9 Expansion Components (invader tokens)
- Type: correction
- Before: "When invaders are in play, one invader token is placed face down on each face-down encounter card and is revealed when the characters enter that tile (see §12.2)."
- After: "When an *invasion* is provoked, one invader token is placed face down on each face-down encounter card, and each is revealed when the characters fight that encounter (see §12.2)."
- Rationale: §12.2 places tokens only when an invasion is provoked and reveals one only when its encounter is fought.
- Source: QA-006 (example and consistency verification)

### CH-02-028 · §2.3 Bosses
- Type: correction
- Before: "and between two and four *boss treasure* cards (see §2.5)"
- After: "and two or more *boss treasure* cards (see §2.5)"
- Rationale: The upper limit of four was an editorial addition that the expansion total of 63 boss treasure cards cannot satisfy; the limit is withdrawn and the count kept (D-025 remains open).
- Source: QA-007 (example and consistency verification)

### CH-02-029 · §2.5 Treasure Cards
- Type: correction
- Before: "Each boss has between two and four boss treasure cards, one of which"
- After: "Each boss has two or more boss treasure cards, one of which"
- Rationale: As CH entry above: the unsupported upper limit is withdrawn.
- Source: QA-007 (example and consistency verification)

### CH-02-030 · §2.8
- Type: ruling
- Before: "- 16x Stat cubes (white) (Core)
- 8x Hollow cubes
- 1x Spark dial (Core)"
- After: "- 16x Stat cubes (white) (Core)
- 1x Spark dial (Core)"
- Rationale: Hollow cubes are not a separate component; they are the white cubes of the core set that are not marking a stat, so the unsourced line is removed.
- Source: final rulings, core-E01 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Core 2017 component list: 16x Level Up Tokens (white cubes), DS-Core-Rules-lores.txt l.244; author's source 03_characters.md l.58 and 07_gameplay.md l.20 ('white cubes').

### CH-02-031 · §2.8
- Type: ruling
- Before: "*Hollow cubes* are of a colour distinct from the damage, stamina, and stat cubes; two are placed on the endurance bar of a character who is *hollow* (see §7.2)."
- After: "*Hollow cubes* are white cubes that are not in use as stat cubes; two are placed on the endurance bar of a character who is *hollow* (see §7.2). A white cube is placed on an endurance bar for no other purpose, so a hollow cube cannot be taken for a damage cube or a stamina cube. The supply of white cubes is not a limit: when every white cube is in use, the players mark the blocked spaces with any other markers that are neither red nor black."
- Rationale: Names the physical component (white cube, never otherwise on the endurance bar) and gives the fallback for a party of four, whose 16 white cubes are all stat cubes.
- Source: final rulings, core-E02 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Author's source ('a marker such as white cubes'); Extended Rules l.57-59 (a marker of any type when the box lacks a colour); §2.8 already treats the soul token supply the same way.

### CH-02-032 · §2.8
- Type: correction
- Before: "- 36x Condition tokens: 7x each of bleed, poison, frostbite, and stagger (Core and expansions); 4x calamity (Black Dragon Kalameet); 4x corrosion (Gaping Dragon)"
- After: "- 28x Condition tokens: 5x each of bleed, poison, frostbite, and stagger (Core); 4x calamity (Black Dragon Kalameet); 4x corrosion (Gaping Dragon)"
- Rationale: The core set supplies five of each condition token and no first-edition expansion adds more; seven each cannot be supported.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E03 (`06_final_rulings.md`); DS-Core-Rules-lores.txt 252-261 (5x each); BDK booklet line 82 (4x calamity); Gaping Dragon booklet contents (4x corrosion); no other supplied or downloaded booklet lists bleed, poison, frostbite or stagger tokens.

### CH-02-033 · §2.8
- Type: clarification
- Before: "*calamity*, and *corrosion* (see §8.5)."
- After: "*calamity*, and *corrosion* (see §8.5). The supply of condition tokens is not a limit; when it is exhausted the players mark the condition with any other counters."
- Rationale: Poison stacks without limit (§8.5) and five tokens can run out; the same sentence already covers soul tokens.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E04 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §8.5 sets no supply limit (chapter 08 issue list, A-081 not applied); §2.8 soul-token wording.

### CH-02-034 · §2.2
- Type: correction
- Before: "- 5x Phalanx (PWA)
- 1x Phalanx Hollow (PWA)"
- After: "- 5x Phalanx Hollow (PWA); the Phalanx (PWA) has a data card of its own and no model, and is shown by three Phalanx Hollow models placed together"
- Rationale: The set holds 13 enemy models, five of them Phalanx Hollows; six Phalanx models would make 14.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E05 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Publisher and retailer listings give 18 miniatures (3 characters, 13 enemies, 2 bosses); community Unofficial Guide and Rules FAQ, set overview table: '5x Phalanx Hollows ... 1x Phalanx (no distinct miniature, represented with 3 Phalanx Hollow miniatures pushed together)'.

### CH-02-035 · §2.9
- Type: correction
- Before: "- 1x Mega-boss model (the Executioner's Chariot has two), 1x Mega-boss data card, 1x Health dial, the behaviour cards listed on the data card, and 2x Mega-boss treasure cards (see §6.7)"
- After: "- 1x Mega-boss model (the Executioner's Chariot has two and The Four Kings four), 1x Mega-boss data card, 1x Health dial (The Four Kings has four), the behaviour cards listed on the data card, and 2x Mega-boss treasure cards (see §6.7)"
- Rationale: The Four Kings expansion supplies four models and four health dials.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E06 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official Four Kings booklet, Contents: '4x Four Kings Miniatures, 4x Four Kings Health Dials, 1x Four Kings Data Card, 4x Four Kings Identifier Tokens, 20x Behaviour Cards, 2x Treasure Cards, 4x Level 4 Encounter Cards'.

### CH-02-036 · §2.9
- Type: completion
- Before: "- Asylum Demon only: 4x Pillar tokens (see §17.3)"
- After: "- Asylum Demon only: 4x Pillar tokens (see §17.3)
- Gaping Dragon only: 4x Corrosion tokens (see §8.5)
- The Four Kings only: 4x Identifier tokens (see §17.10)
- Guardian Dragon only: 4x Fiery Breath cards (see §17.10)
- Old Iron King only: 6x Fire Beam cards (see §17.10)"
- Rationale: Lists the components particular to four mega-boss expansions, now verified from their booklets.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E07 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official booklets, Contents pages: Gaping Dragon (4x Corrosion Condition Tokens), Four Kings (4x Identifier Tokens), Guardian Dragon (4x Fiery Breath Cards), Old Iron King (6x Fire Beam Cards). Vordt, The Last Giant and Manus list no component beyond the common set.

### CH-02-037 · §2.9
- Type: completion
- Before: "The Asylum Demon expansion also supplies the Asylum Demon mini-boss (see §2.3)."
- After: "The Asylum Demon expansion also supplies the Asylum Demon mini-boss, with its own data card, nine behaviour cards, and two boss treasure cards; the one model serves for both the Asylum Demon and the Stray Demon (see §2.3)."
- Rationale: The expansion holds one model and two complete sets of boss cards.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E08 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official Asylum Demon booklet, Contents: 1x Asylum Demon Miniature, 1x Asylum Demon Data Card, 9x Mini Boss Behaviour Cards, 2x Asylum Demon Treasure Cards, 1x Stray Demon Data Card, 13x Mega Boss Behaviour Cards, 2x Stray Demon Treasure Cards, 4x Pillar Tokens, 4x Level 4 Encounter Cards.

### CH-02-038 · §2.5
- Type: correction
- Before: "| *Boss treasure* | 18 | 63 |"
- After: "| *Boss treasure* | 18 | 48 |"
- Rationale: The expansions supply 48 boss treasure cards; 63 also counted the 12 invader treasure cards and the 3 Titanite Scale cards, which the book lists separately.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E09 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Booklet contents: Darkroot 3+3, Explorers 3+2, Iron Keep 2+3, Asylum Demon 2+2, nine other mega-boss expansions 2 each, ToG 5 (DS-TG contents), PWA 5 (by subtraction, agreeing with the community count of 95 item cards).

### CH-02-039 · §2.5
- Type: correction
- Before: "In all, 534 treasure cards are available. Each character's ten class-specific treasure cards comprise five class treasure cards and five *transposed treasure* cards. Each boss has two or more boss treasure cards, one of which is awarded when the boss is defeated (see §16.5). The 12 invader treasure cards of the Phantoms expansion are listed with the expansion components (see §2.9)."
- After: "In all, 519 treasure cards are available. Each character's ten class-specific treasure cards comprise five class treasure cards and five *transposed treasure* cards. Each boss has two or more boss treasure cards, one of which is awarded when the boss is defeated (see §16.5). The 12 invader treasure cards of the Phantoms expansion and the three Titanite Scale cards of the Iron Keep expansion are not counted in the table and are listed with the expansion components (see §2.9)."
- Rationale: The total follows the corrected boss treasure figure, and the two card types counted outside the table are named.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E10 (`06_final_rulings.md`); 13+45+40+120+60+145+10+20+18+48 = 519; 519+12+3 = 534, the manuscript's total.

### CH-02-040 · §2.6
- Type: correction
- Before: "| Level 1 | 12 | 31 |
| Level 2 | 12 | 31 |
| Level 3 | 12 | 31 |
| Level 4 | 0 | 40 |

In all, 169 encounter cards are available."
- After: "| Level 1 | 12 | 37 |
| Level 2 | 12 | 37 |
| Level 3 | 12 | 37 |
| Level 4 | 0 | 40 |

In all, 187 encounter cards are available. Of each of Levels 1 to 3, the Darkroot and Iron Keep expansions supply six cards each, the Explorers expansion five, the Executioner's Chariot expansion two, and ToG and PWA nine each."
- Rationale: Thirty-one per level matches no combination of sets; the per-set figures are stated so that the total can be checked.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E11 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Darkroot booklet 131-132 and Iron Keep booklet 100-101 (six of each level), Explorers booklet 277-278 (five of each), Executioner's Chariot booklet (10 cards: 4 Level 4 and six named Level 1 to 3 cards), ToG rulebook (29 cards, two of them boss encounter cards), community guide (ToG and PWA 27 each, 9/9/9).

### CH-02-041 · §2.3
- Type: correction
- Before: "fought with two models, Dragonslayer Ornstein and Executioner Smough. The pair"
- After: "fought with two models, Dragon Slayer Ornstein and Executioner Smough. The pair"
- Rationale: The printed name is two words (Gate 1 OQ-17: printed spellings for proper nouns).
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E12 (`06_final_rulings.md`); DS-Core-Rules-lores.txt 170, 177, 1662-1663 and the 2023 Sunless City rulebook print 'Dragon Slayer Ornstein'.

### CH-02-042 · §2.9, Phantoms

- After: "When an *invasion* is provoked, one invader token is placed face down on each face-down encounter card that carries no black cube, and each is revealed when the characters fight that encounter (see §12.2)."

### CH-02-043 · §2.5
- Type: correction
- Before: "**Treasure card.** Any card from a treasure deck: *starting equipment*, *class-specific treasure*, *common treasure*, *legendary treasure*, or *boss treasure*."
- After: "**Treasure card.** Any card from a treasure deck or from Blacksmith Andre's shop: *starting equipment*, *class-specific treasure*, *common treasure*, *legendary treasure*, *boss treasure*, or an *ember card* (see §4.4)."

### CH-02-044 · §2.3, Main-Bosses
- Type: ruling
- Before: "Ornstein & Smough is one main-boss fought with two models, Dragon Slayer Ornstein and Executioner Smough. The pair shares one health dial and one behaviour deck, and each behaviour card names the model that acts (see §17.1)."
- After: "Ornstein & Smough is one main-boss fought with two boss models, Dragon Slayer Ornstein and Executioner Smough. Each model has its own data card and health dial, the pair shares one behaviour deck, and both models act on each boss turn (see §17.2)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Keeps §2.3 in step with boss-E10.

### CH-02-045 · §2.9, Mega-Boss Expansions
- Type: clarification
- Before: "- Old Iron King only: 6x Fire Beam cards (see §17.10)"
- After: "- Old Iron King only: 6x Beam cards (see §17.10, Old Iron King)"
- Rationale: The six node-targeting cards are named Beam cards in the rules, which keeps them apart from the three Fire Beam behaviour cards.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E44 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Old Iron King booklet lines 349-375 ('the six Beam cards') and 435-460

### CH-02-046 · §2.3, Mega-Bosses
- Type: completion
- Before: "Each mega-boss is supplied by the expansion of the same name, except the Stray Demon, which is supplied by the Asylum Demon expansion together with the Asylum Demon mini-boss."
- After: "Each mega-boss is supplied by the expansion of the same name, except the Stray Demon, which is supplied by the Asylum Demon expansion together with the Asylum Demon mini-boss. Both demons are fought on that expansion's game board (see §17.3)."
- Rationale: Keeps §2.3 in step with boss-E11.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E47 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Asylum Demon booklet lines 183-190

### CH-02-047 · §2.3
- Type: correction
- Before: "Each *boss* has a model, a *health dial*, a *data card* (see §15.4), a *behaviour deck* (see §15.5), and two or more *boss treasure* cards (see §2.5)."
- After: "Each *boss* has a model, a *health dial*, a *data card* (see §15.4), a *behaviour deck* (see §15.5), and two or more *boss treasure* cards (see §2.5). A boss fought with several models has a health dial for each of them (see §17.1)."
- Rationale: Ornstein & Smough and the Four Kings have a health dial for each model (boss-E08, boss-E10, boss-E20).
- Source: final rulings, lead-E14 (`06_final_rulings.md`); boss memo, cross-cluster effects

### CH-02-048 · §2.3
- Type: correction
- Before: "A boss fought with several models has a health dial for each of them (see §17.1)."
- After: "A boss fought with several models that stand on separate nodes has a health dial for each of them (see §17.1); the two models of the Executioner's Chariot share one health dial (see §17.9)."
- Rationale: §2.9 and §17.9 give the Executioner's Chariot two models and one health dial, and §17.1 limits the rule to models on separate nodes.
- Source: final rulings, VA-002 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-02-049 · §2.8
- Type: correction
- Before: "- 2x Boss tiles (Core), the mini-boss tile and the main-boss tile"
- After: "- 2x Boss tiles (Core): the mini-boss tile, and a second boss tile that is joined to it to form the main-boss tile"
- Rationale: §15.3, §6.6 step 6 and the glossary entry Boss tile make the main-boss tile the two boss tiles joined as one, not the second tile.
- Source: final rulings, VA-003 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

## Chapter 00 — Front Matter

### CH-00-001 · Foreword
- Type: cut
- Before: "SECOND EDITION NOTES" chapter (manuscript lines 371–381: publisher history, first-person process notes, requests for comment)
- After: unnumbered Foreword (165 words) stating scope, the supersession of card text, and the book's conventions
- Rationale: Class B admits no meta-commentary; the one fact the Notes contained that a reader needs (this book governs over card text) survives in the Foreword.
- Source: F-002, F-003, F-004, F-005, F-006, F-055, F-082, C-001, C-025, C-026, C-118, CMP-0013; Gate 1 — OQ-01

### CH-00-002 · Colophon
- Type: restructure
- Before: "By PittedPanda / Based on related works by Steam Forged Games (SFG), Konami, Santuric, …"
- After: Colophon crediting the compiler and contributors, naming the rights holders, and stating that the book is an unofficial, non-commercial fan work
- Rationale: Credits belong in a colophon, not a byline; Konami is not a rights holder of Dark Souls; the fan-work statement was missing.
- Source: F-001, CMP-0013

### CH-00-003 · Foreword
- Type: style
- Before: "Each defensive choice — *block*, *resist*, *dodge* and *parry* — is viable."
- After: "Each defensive choice, whether to *block*, *resist*, *dodge* or *parry*, is viable."
- Rationale: House style avoids dashes in running text.
- Source: C §14

## Chapter 01 — Introduction

### CH-01-001 · §1 Introduction
- Type: restructure
- Before: four short paragraphs (manuscript lines 361–369) including the glossary promise and "Dark Souls: The Board Game is a cooperative dungeon crawl…"
- After: three paragraphs: the game in one sentence, the objective with cross-references to §6.5, §6.7, §7.2 and §7.4, and the closing line retained
- Rationale: The glossary convention moved to the Foreword; the Introduction states the objective and the hollowing and spark loops once each with references instead of restating rules.
- Source: C-030, C-101, F-007, F-008, CMP-0013 (Option B material folded in); Gate 1 — OQ-01

### CH-01-002 · §1
- Type: correction
- Before: "The bonfire's *sparks* are few, and every rest spends one (see §7.4)."
- After: "The bonfire's *sparks* are few, and every rest spends one, except the rest that follows the defeat of a boss (see §7.4)."
- Rationale: §7.4 and §11.2 make the rest that follows a boss's defeat free of any spark.
- Source: final rulings, VA-001 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

## Chapter 03 — Characters

### CH-03-001 · §3 Characters
- Type: style
- Before: new text
- After: "Each character is one of the undead who answer the bonfire's call. The character is a model on the tiles and a dashboard beside the player, and the dashboard records what the character carries, learns, and suffers."
- Rationale: A chapter intro of under 60 words with no rule, as the brief requires; the manuscript chapter opened directly on the first section.
- Source: brief §2 (chapter intro); F §8 voice guide

### CH-03-002 · §3.1 Character Dashboards
- Type: restructure
- Before: "1. Name 2. Illustration 3. Heroic Action 4. Equipment Slots 5. Endurance Bar 6. Stat Progression 7. Threat Level 8. Heroic action token slot 9. Luck token slot 10. Estus flask token slot 11. Ember token slot"
- After: "The four equipment slots hold the character's armour and weapons: the armour slot, the left weapon slot, the right weapon slot, and the back-up slot (see §3.2)."
- Rationale: The numbered anatomy list was a procedure in form; the parts are now bulleted full sentences, each with a cross-reference to its owning section, and the four token slots are one item.
- Source: C §14 style sheet; F §8 voice guide §6; C-027

### CH-03-003 · §3.1 Character Dashboards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Threat Level"
- After: "The value that fixes a model's place in the turn order. Characters act in descending order of threat, and the character with the highest threat takes the aggro token at the start of each encounter (see §7.5, Encounter Turn Order). The dashboard prints the character's threat as its Taunt Level."
- Rationale: The dashboard value was listed but never defined; threat is kept as the single term for characters, enemies, and phantoms, with one bridge to the label printed on the physical dashboard so that readers can find the value.
- Source: A-125, D-039 (threat kept per the preserve list), C-064

### CH-03-004 · §3.1 Character Dashboards
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "The character's souls are kept on the dashboard as soul tokens (see §7.6). The character's inventory is kept beside the dashboard (see §3.2)."
- Rationale: The soul tokens and the inventory live on or beside the dashboard but appeared nowhere in the dashboard description.
- Source: glossary (Souls, Inventory); C-048

### CH-03-005 · §3.2 Equipment Slots
- Type: completion
- Before: "Weapon cards, as noted by either the one or two-handed weapon symbol, may only be equipped to the weapon slots."
- After: "The left weapon slot and the right weapon slot. Each holds one weapon card, marked with the one-handed weapon icon or the two-handed weapon icon. A one-handed weapon may be equipped in either weapon slot, and the other weapon slot may hold a second one-handed weapon."
- Rationale: The two weapon slots are now named left and right, as the parry and riposte rules (§9.6) require; the shield's slot is flagged with a cross-reference and the decision on which slot is left to §9.6.
- Source: A-068, D-030, B-022 (pointer to §4.1 for the shield definition)

### CH-03-006 · §3.2 Equipment Slots
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Only equipment cards assigned to the armour or weapon slots are considered equipped."
- After: "A card is equipped while it is in the armour slot or in a weapon slot. Cards in the back-up slot and in the inventory are not equipped. A character attacks only with the attack options of equipped cards (see §10.3) and rolls only the block, resist, and dodge dice shown on equipped cards (see §9.4, §9.5)."
- Rationale: "Equipped" is defined as a headword with its consequences cross-referenced; spells, catalysts, and upgrade cards are pointed to their owning sections.
- Source: C §14 style sheet (definitions); glossary (Equip, Catalyst, Upgrade card)

### CH-03-007 · §3.2 Equipment Slots
- Type: style
- Before: "Each equipment slot may contain card(s) of the appropriate equipment type as described."
- After: "Each slot holds only cards of the kind stated below."
- Rationale: Dangling prose cross-reference removed; the slot rules follow in the same section.
- Source: F-057

### CH-03-008 · §3.2 Equipment Slots
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Once the encounter has started players should not interact with their character's inventory cards."
- After: "During an encounter, the inventory cannot be accessed."
- Rationale: "should" made a mandatory restriction advisory; the inventory is now defined as a headword with its limit (none), its location, and its access rules in lawbook modals.
- Source: C-019, F-089, C-048

### CH-03-009 · §3.2 Equipment Slots
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "The Knight has a one-handed sword in the right weapon slot, a shield in the left weapon slot, armour in the armour slot, and a two-handed axe in the back-up slot."
- Rationale: A worked example shows the two-handed rule (the other weapon slot must be empty) and the back-up slot limit of two cards.
- Source: brief §2 (worked examples); A-068

### CH-03-010 · §3.3 Stat Progression Track
- Type: style
- Before: "(i.e., to upgrade strength from base tier to tier two, one must pay the cost in souls for the tier one upgrade, and then the tier two upgrade)."
- After: "The Warrior pays the Firekeeper two souls for stat tier one and then four souls for stat tier two, and moves the strength stat cube two spaces to the right."
- Rationale: The indefinite "one" and the Latin abbreviation are replaced by a worked example with a named character; "tier" is qualified as "stat tier" throughout; "The Bonfire" becomes the bonfire; "white cube" becomes stat cube.
- Source: F-087, C-096, C-045, C-042, C-050

### CH-03-011 · §3.3 Stat Progression Track
- Type: restructure
- Before: "The characters may upgrade their stats while resting at The Bonfire by paying the appropriate amount of souls and moving the white cube one space to the right."
- After: "Characters raise stats only while resting at the bonfire, by paying souls to the Firekeeper (see §11.3). Each tier must be bought in sequence, and each tier has its own cost (see §11.3; for campaign play, see §18.6, Stat Tier Costs)."
- Rationale: The costs are owned by §11.3 and §18.6; this section states the track, the cube movement, and the sequence rule, and cites the costs.
- Source: brief §5 (one owning section); C-092 (campaign example belongs to §18.6)

### CH-03-012 · §3.3 Stat Progression Track
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "If a character reaches stat tier four they mark this on their character dashboard by replacing the tier three white cube with a red cube. Tier four stats are all considered 5 greater than the tier three stat value."
- After: "The track has no space for stat tier four. When a stat rises to stat tier four, the character replaces the stat cube in the stat tier three space with a red cube and returns the stat cube to the supply. A stat at stat tier four has a value of 40, or the stat tier three value plus five, whichever is higher."
- Rationale: The stat tier four representation belongs to the track; the value follows the Gate 1 ruling.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-09

### CH-03-013 · §3.3 Stat Progression Track
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "A character must meet an equipment card's stat requirements to equip it (see §4.1). A weapon whose highest stat requirement the character's stat exceeds by five or more is scaled (see §10.5)."
- Rationale: The two uses of a stat value are pointed to their owning sections so that the reader knows why stats matter.
- Source: glossary (Scaled); Gate 1 — OQ-19 (value cited, owned by §10.5)

### CH-03-014 · §3.4 Endurance Bar
- Type: correction
- Before: "The effect of these hollow markers is to reduce the functional capacity of the endurance bar from a total of eight stamina/damage cubes to only six."
- After: "The endurance bar is a row of 10 spaces along the bottom of the dashboard." and "Those spaces hold no stamina or damage cubes while the hollow cubes remain, so a hollow character's endurance bar has eight spaces."
- Rationale: The dashboards have 10 spaces; hollow blocks two, leaving eight.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-08; B-015, D-021

### CH-03-015 · §3.4 Endurance Bar
- Type: correction
- Before: "they block off the two furthest right spaces of the endurance bar with a marker such as white cubes. The effect of these hollow markers"
- After: "When a character is revived hollow (see §7.2), two hollow cubes are placed in the two rightmost spaces of the endurance bar."
- Rationale: One component with one name; white cubes are the stat cubes and are all in use at four players. The hollow cubes are listed in §2.8.
- Source: C-050, B-029, Gate 1 — OQ-08

### CH-03-016 · §3.4 Endurance Bar
- Type: style
- Before: "A character's stamina bar tracks their stamina and damage."
- After: "It records both the stamina the character has spent and the damage the character has suffered."
- Rationale: The bar is the endurance bar everywhere; "stamina bar" misdescribes it. The copied official phrasing of the spend and suffer rules is rewritten in the house pattern.
- Source: C-035, F-094

### CH-03-017 · §3.4 Endurance Bar
- Type: restructure
- Before: "If a character is ever forced to place a stamina cube on their endurance bar, but there is no space to do so, the character is instead marked staggered with the appropriate condition marker. A character may never elect to spend excess stamina"
- After: "If a character must place a stamina cube and no empty space remains, the cube is not placed and the character is staggered instead; a character cannot choose to spend more stamina than the bar holds (see §10.1)."
- Rationale: Stamina overflow and its stagger consequence are owned by §10.1; this section keeps one sentence and the cross-reference. "stagger*ed*" and "condition marker" are corrected.
- Source: brief §5 (one owning section); C-028, C-063; A-009 (owned by §10.1 and §8.5)

### CH-03-018 · §3.4 Endurance Bar
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "A recovery that finds no cube of the right kind to remove has no effect. A character recovers one stamina at the start of each of their turns (see §10.1)."
- Rationale: The manuscript did not say what happens when a character recovers stamina or damage with no cube to remove; the start-of-turn recovery is cited so that the bar's cycle is visible here.
- Source: fact source (official text); glossary (Turn)

### CH-03-019 · §3.4 Endurance Bar
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "The Warrior places three damage cubes in the three empty spaces and cannot place the fourth, so the Warrior dies."
- Rationale: Two worked examples show the bar filling from both ends to death, and the eight-space bar of a hollow character.
- Source: brief §2 (worked examples); Gate 1 — OQ-08

### CH-03-020 · §3.4 Endurance Bar
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Characters may skip previously cleared encounters at the cost of two stamina cubes placed in the endurance bar of each character."
- After: deleted
- Rationale: The encounter skip cost (one stamina per skipped encounter, maximum three) is owned by §7.5, Encounter Start; this chapter restates only the capacity (eight spaces for a hollow character) on which that arithmetic rests.
- Source: B-014, A-008, Gate 1 — OQ-10 and OQ-08

### CH-03-021 · §3.5 Character Tokens
- Type: completion
- Before: "There are three different types of character tokens: heroic actions, luck, and estus flask. Each character token has a read and a used side."
- After: "There are four types of character token: heroic action, luck, estus flask, and ember. Each character has one token of each type, and the dashboard has a slot for each."
- Rationale: The three-or-four contradiction is resolved as four types; "read" becomes "ready"; the double full stop and the punctuation inside italics are corrected.
- Source: CMP-0001, A-012, C-015, C-106, C-027

### CH-03-022 · §3.5 Character Tokens
- Type: clarification
- Before: "The token must be flipped face-down when the character uses the related effect."
- After: "When a character uses a token's effect, the character flips the token to its used side."
- Rationale: Tokens have a ready side and a used side; "face down" is for cards and tiles.
- Source: C-051

### CH-03-023 · §3.5 Character Tokens
- Type: correction
- Before: "The ember tokens are treated differently, and should be placed in the condition token supply at the start of the game.."
- After: "It begins the game beside the dashboard, not in its slot, and is placed in its slot only when the character becomes embered (see §7.3)."
- Rationale: The approved completion places the ember token beside the dashboard, which matches Character Setup; the token supply wording of A-052 is superseded.
- Source: CMP-0001; A-052 (superseded); C-019

### CH-03-024 · §3.5 Character Tokens
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "They are flipped back to the ready side only when the characters rest at the bonfire (see §11.2). Clearing an encounter does not ready them (see §7.5, Encounter Recovery)."
- Rationale: The refresh rule for the three ready-side tokens was stated nowhere in the chapter; it is now stated once for all three and repeated for the estus flask as the completion requires.
- Source: CMP-0001; preserve list (partial recovery after a clear)

### CH-03-025 · §3.5 Heroic Action
- Type: style
- Before: "A character may flip their heroic action token to the used side as a minor action to resolve the heroic action as listed on their dashboard. The specific wording of the heroic action overrides any other rules."
- After: "A character may flip the heroic action token to the used side as a minor action (see §10.1) to resolve the heroic action as printed. The printed wording of a heroic action overrides any other rule with which it conflicts."
- Rationale: Copied official prose rewritten in the house pattern with a cross-reference to the minor action.
- Source: F-095

### CH-03-026 · §3.5 Luck
- Type: clarification
- Before: "A character may flip their luck token to the used side to reroll any attack or defence roll. Any or all of the dice may be rerolled all at once, but the results of the reroll must be kept."
- After: "After rolling the dice for one of their own attack rolls or defence rolls (a block, resist, or dodge roll), a character may flip the luck token to the used side to reroll any or all of those dice."
- Rationale: The timing (after the roll), the scope (the character's own roll), and the kinds of defence roll are made explicit; the any-or-all reroll is preserved.
- Source: F-096; preserve list (luck rerolls any or all dice)

### CH-03-027 · §3.5 Estus Flask
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A character may flip the estus flask token to the used side as the Drink Estus major action (see §10.1) to remove all damage cubes from their endurance bar. Stamina cubes are not removed. Hollow cubes are not removed. Condition tokens are not removed."
- Rationale: The sub-section existed only in the table of contents; the rule is restored from the approved completion.
- Source: CMP-0001, A-012

### CH-03-028 · §3.5 Ember
- Type: restructure
- Before: "Ember represents a character's return to humanity and allows the character to discard any hollow tokens from the character dashboard. If a character with an ember token suffers three or more damage from a single attack, they may reduce the damage by one."
- After: "A character becomes embered by using an ember card (see §4.4). The effects of being embered, and when it ends, are stated with the embering rules (see §7.3). An embered character may summon a phantom before a boss encounter and attracts invaders (see §12)."
- Rationale: Embering is defined in three places in the manuscript; §7.3 owns it, and this sub-section keeps only the token's placement and removal with cross-references. "that player discards" becomes the character's act; the advisory "players should be aware" is cut; summons and invaders are named under the phantom umbrella.
- Source: A-006, C-048, C-021, C-064, F-097

### CH-03-029 · §3 Characters (style sweep)
- Type: style
- Before: "stagger*ed*" / "*heroic actions, luck, and estus flask.*" / "re-roll" / "The Bonfire" / "i.e.," / curly quotes
- After: "*staggered*" / "*heroic action*, *luck*, *estus flask*, and *ember*" / "reroll" / "the *bonfire*" / "for example" / straight quotes
- Rationale: Italics policy (first use per section, whole words, no punctuation), one spelling per compound, straight quotes, serial commas, and lawbook modals applied throughout.
- Source: C-027, C-028, C-030, C-042, C-060, C-096, C-111, C-049 (no "miniature" remained), C-103 and C-104 (headings follow the section map)

### CH-03-030 · §3 Characters (skipped OPTIONAL findings)
- Type: cut
- Before: n/a
- After: n/a
- Rationale: A-009 (double stamina overflow while staggered) is owned by §10.1 and §8.5, Stagger, not by this chapter. A-072 (Equip once per turn) is owned by §10.1; the glossary already states it. A-105 (adding and dropping players) is owned by §18.4. C-092 (campaign cost example) is owned by §18.6. D-039 (restore "taunt" for characters) is rejected; the preserve list keeps threat as the single term, with the printed label noted once (A-125).
- Source: A-009, A-072, A-105, C-092, D-039

### CH-03-031 · §3.2 Equipment Slots
- Type: clarification
- Before: "**Weapon slots.** The left weapon slot and the right weapon slot. Each holds one weapon card"
- After: "**Weapon slots.** The left weapon slot and the right weapon slot: the weapon slot on the left of the dashboard as its player reads it, and the weapon slot on its right."
- Rationale: The parry (§9.6) depends on which slot is the left one; the F.A.Q. ruling needed a home in the owning section so that the F.A.Q. carries no rule of its own.
- Source: A-068, CMP-0003

### CH-03-032 · §3.5
- Type: clarification
- Before: "They are flipped back to the ready side only when the characters rest at the *bonfire* (see §11.2). Clearing an encounter does not ready them (see §7.5, Encounter Recovery)."
- After: "They are flipped back to the ready side only when the characters rest at the *bonfire* (see §11.2), so each is used at most once between rests. Clearing an encounter does not ready them (see §7.5, Encounter Recovery)."
- Rationale: The one refresh rule of the three tokens is stated once, with its consequence, in the paragraph that covers all three.
- Source: final rulings, core-E12 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official 2017 and 2022 ready all three tokens at the bonfire rest (DS-Core-Rules-lores.txt l.702-706; DS-TG-Rulebook l.1929-1933).

### CH-03-033 · §3.5, Heroic Action
- Type: clarification
- Before: "The printed wording of a heroic action overrides any other rule with which it conflicts. The token is readied only at the bonfire, so a character resolves the heroic action at most once between rests."
- After: "The printed wording of a heroic action overrides any other rule with which it conflicts."
- Rationale: Removes a restatement of the shared refresh rule so that the three token sub-sections are parallel.
- Source: final rulings, core-E13 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §3.5 general paragraph.

### CH-03-034 · §3.5, Estus Flask
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Condition tokens are not removed.

The estus flask token is not readied when an encounter is *cleared*. It is flipped to the ready side only when the characters rest at the bonfire (see §11.2)."
- After: "Condition tokens are not removed."
- Rationale: Removes a restatement of the shared refresh rule so that the three token sub-sections are parallel.
- Source: final rulings, core-E14 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §3.5 general paragraph.

### CH-03-035 · §3.5, Luck
- Type: clarification
- Before: "After rolling the dice for one of their own attack rolls or defence rolls (a *block*, *resist*, or *dodge* roll), a character may flip the luck token to the used side to reroll any or all of those dice."
- After: "After rolling the dice for one of their own attack rolls or defence rolls (a *block*, *resist*, *dodge*, or *parry* roll), a character may flip the luck token to the used side to reroll any or all of those dice."
- Rationale: §9.6 already allows the luck token on a parry roll; the list in §3.5 omitted it.
- Source: final rulings, core-E18 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §9.6 parry procedure, step 4.

### CH-03-036 · §3.2
- Type: ruling
- Before: "Which weapon slot holds a shield matters for the *parry* and the *riposte* (see §9.6)."
- After: "A shield in either weapon slot permits a *parry*, and the *riposte* is made with the weapon in the other weapon slot (see §9.6)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Follows §9.6 as changed by combat-E22.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E30 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As combat-E22.

### CH-03-037 · §3.1
- Type: correction
- Before: "takes the *aggro* token at the start of each encounter (see §7.5, Encounter Turn Order)."
- After: "takes the *aggro* token at the start of each encounter (see §7.5, Encounter Start; §7.5, Encounter Turn Order)."
- Rationale: The placing of the aggro token at the start of an encounter is stated in §7.5, Encounter Start, step 10, not under Encounter Turn Order.
- Source: final rulings, VA-004 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-03-038 · §3.3
- Type: correction
- Before: "A weapon whose highest stat requirement the character's stat exceeds by five or more is *scaled* (see §10.5)."
- After: "A weapon whose highest stat requirement the character's value in that stat exceeds by five or more is *scaled*; a weapon whose stat requirements are all zero is never scaled (see §10.5)."
- Rationale: §10.5 never scales a weapon whose four stat requirements are all zero, and compares only the relevant stat.
- Source: final rulings, VA-005 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

## Chapter 04 — Equipment

### CH-04-001 · §4 Equipment
- Type: cut
- Before: "#" (empty heading, manuscript line 893)
- After: deleted
- Rationale: Empty heading used as a page break; page breaks belong to layout.
- Source: C-098

### CH-04-002 · §4.1 Equipment Cards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Equipment cards include weapons, spells, shields, armour, and items which the character equips to improve their combat capabilities."
- After: "**Treasure card.** Any card drawn from a treasure deck or bought from Blacksmith Andre's *shop* (see §2.5). **Equipment card.** A treasure card that a character equips in a weapon or armour slot, stores in a *catalyst*, or attaches to an equipped card"
- Rationale: States the relationship treasure card ⊃ equipment card once and removes bare "items".
- Source: C-054, brief §3

### CH-04-003 · §4.1 Equipment Cards
- Type: style
- Before: "Players should include all equipment cards from expansions available in their games to increase variety."
- After: "Treasure cards from every expansion the party owns are included in the treasure decks (see §6.2)."
- Rationale: Advice with rationale rewritten as an inclusion rule in voice.
- Source: F-009, C-018

### CH-04-004 · §4.1 Equipment Cards
- Type: restructure
- Before: "1. Equipment name. 2. Equipment type (common, class-specific, transposed, legendary, boss). 3. Equipment slot (one-handed weapon, two-handed weapon, or armour). 4. Icon to indicate what core or expansion set the card is from."
- After: "**Slot icon.** The one-handed weapon icon {{icon:ast-0043-one-handed-weapon}}, the two-handed weapon icon {{icon:ast-0045-two-handed-weapon}}, or the armour icon {{icon:ast-0047-armour}} shows which dashboard slot the card occupies (see §3.2)."
- Rationale: Numbered fragments rewritten as a full-sentence anatomy list with icon references and cross-references to the owning sections.
- Source: C-119, brief §2 (Icons)

### CH-04-005 · §4.1 Equipment Cards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Equipment cards with either a one or two-handed weapon icon are considered weapons unless the card depicts a spell as indicated by a picture of a scroll with an overlying spell icon in the centre of the card."
- After: "A *shield* is a one-handed weapon that shows block or resist dice and has no attack option. A shield is a weapon for the purpose of equipment slots (see §3.2) and counts as equipment in the other hand for two-handed attacks (see §10.4)."
- Rationale: Defines the five card kinds the shop is stocked by, including the previously undefined shield; catalysts and throwable weapons classed as weapons.
- Source: A-069, B-022

### CH-04-006 · §4.1 Equipment Cards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Each equipment card has four listed stat requirements which indicate what stat values on a character's dashboard are required to equip the item."
- After: "A character may equip an equipment card only if each of the character's four stats equals or exceeds the card's matching *stat requirement*."
- Rationale: Description turned into a rule with trigger, actor, and outcome.
- Source: C-119, C-054

### CH-04-007 · §4.1 Equipment Cards
- Type: restructure
- Before: new text
- After: "A card's stat requirements also decide its treasure tier, split at a requirement of 25 (see §6.2)." / "A character whose stat exceeds a weapon's requirements by a wide margin wields it as a *scaled* weapon and gains a bonus to its attacks (see §10.5)."
- Rationale: Treasure tiers are owned by §6.2 and stat scaling (threshold five, OQ-19) by §10.5; this chapter only cites them.
- Source: brief §4 and §5, Gate 1 — OQ-19

### CH-04-008 · §4.1 Equipment Cards
- Type: style
- Before: "Each line on the card represents a separate attack option that can be used as a character's main action. The specifics of using equipment cards offensively is explained in detail later on in the section title "Character Attacks"."
- After: "A character attacks by using one attack option of a weapon or spell equipped in a weapon slot as the *Attack* *major action* (see §10.3)."
- Rationale: "main action" replaced by "major action"; prose cross-references converted to "(see §N.M)".
- Source: C-038, F-058, F-059, F-060, C-084

### CH-04-009 · §4.1 Equipment Cards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Some equipment cards have dice modifiers that add a fixed value to the die roll or subtract a fixed value from the die roll."
- After: "Some dice carry a fixed modifier, +1 or −1 for example, which is added to or subtracted from the total rolled on those dice (see §9.4, §10.3)."
- Rationale: Copied prose rewritten in voice; modifiers on defence dice are now cited to the owning section (detail for §9.4 raised in issues).
- Source: F-099, D-033, A-120

### CH-04-010 · §4.1 Equipment Cards
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "*Example.* The Knight has 18 STR, 14 DEX, 9 INT, and 11 FTH. A Morning Star requires 16 STR only, so the Knight equips it. A Halberd requires 31 STR and 18 DEX; the Knight's strength is below 31, so the Knight cannot equip the Halberd."
- Rationale: Worked example of the stat requirement rule using Appendix A values.
- Source: brief §2 (Worked examples)

### CH-04-011 · §4.2 Upgrades
- Type: style
- Before: "those marked with a sword which may only be used to upgrade weapons, and those marked with play mail, which may only be used up grade armour."
- After: "An upgrade card bearing the weapon upgrade icon {{icon:ast-0044-weapon-upgrade}} may be attached only to a weapon card. An upgrade card bearing the armour upgrade icon {{icon:ast-0046-armour-upgrade}} may be attached only to an *armour* card."
- Rationale: Typos corrected and copied prose rewritten in voice with icon references.
- Source: C-015, F-100

### CH-04-012 · §4.2 Upgrades
- Type: style
- Before: "For example, a character could attach up to two weapon upgrade cards to a weapon card with an upgrade limit of two."
- After: "*Example.* The Knight's Sunless Armour has two upgrade slots. While the party rests at the bonfire, Andre attaches a Chloranthy Ring to it. The Chloranthy Ring fills one upgrade slot, and one upgrade slot remains free."
- Rationale: Inline example set apart as an *Example.* paragraph in the indicative; "could" removed.
- Source: C-022, F-083, C-092

### CH-04-013 · §4.2 Upgrades
- Type: restructure
- Before: "characters cannot equip weapons or armour if they do not meet the minimum stat requirements of the equipment and all attached upgrades." (manuscript line 1929, Blacksmith Andre)
- After: "An upgrade card has its own *stat requirements*: a character may equip a weapon or armour card only if they meet the requirements of the card and of every upgrade card attached to it (see §4.1, Stat Requirements)."
- Rationale: The equipping condition belongs with the upgrade rules; §11.4 cites it.
- Source: brief §5 (owning section)

### CH-04-014 · §4.2 Upgrades
- Type: correction
- Before: "any upgrade card, including the word Titanate (i.e., Titanite Shard) must be returned to the bottom of the tier one treasure deck along with the weapon it is attached to when sold."
- After: "When a weapon with Titanite attached is sold, the Titanite is returned with the weapon to the bottom of the treasure deck of the weapon's tier (see §11.4)."
- Rationale: Titanite Chunk is a tier two card, so sold Titanite follows the weapon's deck; "Titanate" and the Latin abbreviation removed.
- Source: A-074, C-002, C-096

### CH-04-015 · §4.2 Upgrades
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Instead, upgrade spells are attached to a character's equipment as a main action similar to casting an attack spell."
- After: "The character pays the stamina cost printed on the upgrade spell and attaches it to one weapon or armour card equipped to the same character." / "An attached upgrade spell occupies no upgrade slot."
- Rationale: Attachment is the *Attack* major action with a stamina cost, counts as the turn's one spell attack option, and does not use an upgrade slot.
- Source: D-034, A-071, C-038

### CH-04-016 · §4.2 Upgrades
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A Titanite Scale is awarded for killing a crystal lizard (see §14)." / "*Example.* The Warrior's Morning Star has one upgrade slot. Andre attaches a Titanite Shard to it."
- Rationale: Names all four Titanite cards, cites the crystal lizard reward, and adds a worked example of permanence.
- Source: brief §2, glossary Titanite

### CH-04-017 · §4.3 Spells
- Type: restructure
- Before: "## Spells / ### Spells" with a "<!-- -->" list separator
- After: "## 4.3 Spells / ### Spell Cards / ### Catalysts"
- Rationale: Duplicate heading merged into one section with two sub-sections; conversion artefact deleted.
- Source: CMP-0016, C-099, C-117

### CH-04-018 · §4.3 Spells
- Type: style
- Before: "Spells are a type of equipment or upgrade card defined by the presence of a scroll as part of the equipment card's art, usually with a coloured magic effect in the foreground."
- After: "A *spell* is an *equipment card* or *upgrade card* whose art shows a scroll. Spells belong to one of three schools, shown by the colour of the magic in the art and by the card's *stat requirements*."
- Rationale: Hedge removed; bullet fragments rewritten as rules in prose.
- Source: F-090, C-119

### CH-04-019 · §4.3 Spells
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Spells stored within their respective *catalyst* may use their *attack options* as if equipped."
- After: "A stored spell provides only its attack options; its block, resist, and dodge dice are not rolled. Using a stored spell's attack option counts as the catalyst's one attack for that turn."
- Rationale: Prevents a catalyst with stored spells becoming a multi-attack slot or adding defence dice; stored spells occupy no slot; the stat requirements of a stored spell still apply.
- Source: A-070, CMP-0016

### CH-04-020 · §4.3 Spells
- Type: style
- Before: "Spells may be freely transferred between a characters' catalyst and their inventory between encounters."
- After: "A character may move spells between a catalyst and their *inventory* only outside an encounter."
- Rationale: Typo and filler removed; rule stated with actor and condition.
- Source: C-015, CMP-0016

### CH-04-021 · §4.3 Spells
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A spell attack option that bears the *magical damage* icon {{icon:ast-0042-deal-magical-damage}} deals magical damage, which the target reduces with its *resist* rather than its *block* (see §9.4)." / "*Example.* The Herald equips a talisman in the left weapon slot and stores three miracles beneath it."
- Rationale: Magical damage cited to its owning section; worked example of the one-spell-per-turn and catalyst rules.
- Source: brief §2, task instruction (spells)

### CH-04-022 · §4.4 Embers
- Type: style
- Before: "Ember cards may be stored in a player's inventory, but must be equipped to the backup slot to be available for use during an encounter. Discarding an *ember* card permits the character to place an *ember* token on their character dashboard with the aforementioned effects."
- After: "During an *encounter*, a character may consume an ember card only from the back-up slot (see §3.2). An ember card in the inventory cannot be consumed until the encounter ends."
- Rationale: "player's" corrected to the character; "backup slot" normalised; "aforementioned" replaced by cross-references.
- Source: C-048, C-060, F-061, C-084

### CH-04-023 · §4.4 Embers
- Type: clarification
- Before: "*Ember* cards may only be purchased from the tier one shop and are in limited supply."
- After: "Ember cards are stocked in the tier one *shop* at setup only and are not restocked (see §6.3)." / "A character may carry at most one ember card at a time, in the *back-up slot* or in the *inventory*. An embered character cannot carry an ember card."
- Rationale: "Limited supply" made definite by citing the setup-only stocking rule; the carry limit and the embered exclusion are stated here as the owning section.
- Source: A-046, A-006, glossary Ember card

### CH-04-024 · §4.4 Embers
- Type: restructure
- Before: new text
- After: "A character consumes an ember card by discarding it, as the *Ember* *major action* during an encounter or at any time outside an encounter. The character then becomes embered and places an *ember token* on their dashboard (see §7.3)."
- Rationale: Consumption named once with its two timings; the effects of embering (full heal and hollow removal) are owned by §7.3 and cited, not restated.
- Source: A-006, Gate 1 — OQ-30

### CH-04-025 · §4 Equipment
- Type: style
- Before: "The Bonfire" / "Andre" / "*Dodge*" / curly quotes / inconsistent italics
- After: "the *bonfire*" / "Blacksmith Andre" at first mention per section / "*dodge*" / straight quotes / glossary keywords italicised at first use in each H2 (style sweep)
- Rationale: Style sheet applied mechanically throughout the chapter.
- Source: C-030, C-042, C-043, C-047, C-111

### CH-04-026 · §4 Equipment
- Type: style
- Before: (OPTIONAL findings not applied)
- After: C-092 skipped: its campaign cost example belongs to §18.6, not this chapter. D-033 and A-120 applied only as an anatomy statement; the defence-total detail is owned by §9.4 (see issues). F-098 and F-100 applied through the voice rewrite.
- Rationale: Grouped record of OPTIONAL findings outside this chapter's ownership.
- Source: C-092, D-033, A-120, F-098, F-100

### CH-04-027 · §4.2 Upgrade Cards (Titanite)
- Type: correction
- Before: "Once attached, a Titanite card cannot be removed."
- After: "Once attached, a Titanite card cannot be removed, except when a Titanite Chunk or a Titanite Slab takes its place (see Appendix B, Titanite Replacement)."
- Rationale: Appendix B lets a Chunk replace a Shard and a Slab replace a Chunk; §4.2 stated no exception.
- Source: QA-002 (example and consistency verification)

### CH-04-028 · §4.2 Upgrade Cards (Titanite example)
- Type: correction
- Before: "The Morning Star holds no further upgrade card, and the Titanite Shard stays attached for the rest of the game."
- After: "The Morning Star holds no further upgrade card, and the Titanite Shard stays attached unless a Titanite Chunk later takes its place (see Appendix B)."
- Rationale: The example contradicted Titanite Replacement in Appendix B.
- Source: QA-002 (example and consistency verification)

### CH-04-029 · §4.4 Ember Cards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "An ember card in the inventory cannot be consumed until the encounter ends."
- After: "During an encounter, the inventory cannot be accessed, so an ember card there cannot be consumed until the encounter ends."
- Rationale: §3.2 and §7.3 require the ember card to be in the back-up slot whenever it is consumed; the sentence implied otherwise outside an encounter.
- Source: QA-011 (example and consistency verification)

### CH-04-030 · §4.4 Ember Cards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "A character consumes an ember card by discarding it, as the *Ember* major action"
- After: "A character consumes an ember card by discarding it from the back-up slot, as the *Ember* major action"
- Rationale: Brings §4.4 into line with §3.2 and §7.3.
- Source: QA-011 (example and consistency verification)

### CH-04-031 · §4.4
- Type: completion
- Before: "A character consumes an ember card by discarding it from the back-up slot, as the *Ember* major action during an encounter or at any time outside an encounter."
- After: "A character consumes an ember card by discarding it from the back-up slot, as the *Ember* major action during an encounter or at any time outside an encounter. The consumed card is returned to the game box and takes no further part in the game."
- Rationale: Same destination stated where the ember card is described.
- Source: final rulings, core-E24 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As core-E23.

### CH-04-032 · §4.3
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Spells are never *two-handed*: a spell gains no two-handed reroll, and a spell equipped with the other weapon slot empty is not two-handed (see §10.4)."
- After: "Spells are never *two-handed*: a spell gains no two-handed reroll, and a spell equipped with the other weapon slot empty is not two-handed (see §10.4). A spell card that bears the two-handed weapon icon requires the other weapon slot to be empty while it is equipped in a weapon slot (see §3.2), and it gains no reroll by this."
- Rationale: Separates the slot a spell occupies (its printed icon) from the two-handed reroll (never for spells).
- Source: final rulings, core-E26 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §3.2 weapon slots; chapter 04 issues list, item 7.

### CH-04-033 · §4.1, Stat Requirements
- Type: clarification
- Before: "A card's stat requirements also decide its treasure tier, split at a requirement of 25 (see §6.2)."
- After: "A card's stat requirements also decide its treasure tier, split at a requirement of 25 (see §6.2). The stat requirements of the weapons listed in Appendix A are those given there, in place of the printed values (see Appendix A)."
- Rationale: The main text never told the reader that Appendix A replaces printed requirements.
- Source: final rulings, core-E27 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Chapter 23 issues list, cross-chapter pointers.

### CH-04-034 · §4.2, Titanite
- Type: ruling
- Before: "When a weapon with Titanite attached is sold, the Titanite is returned with the weapon to the bottom of the treasure deck of the weapon's tier (see §11.4)."
- After: "When a weapon with Titanite attached is sold, the Titanite is sold with it and adds nothing to the sell value. The weapon and the Titanite are separated, and each goes where a sold card of its kind goes (see §11.4, Sales)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] One destination rule for every sold card: each card returns to the place of its own kind, as a replaced Titanite card already does (Appendix B).
- Source: final rulings, core-E31 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Author's source 11_returning_to_the_bonfire.md (sold Titanite goes to the tier one deck); Appendix B, Titanite Replacement.

### CH-04-035 · §4.2, Titanite
- Type: ruling
- Before: "*Titanite* upgrade cards (Titanite Shard, Titanite Chunk, Titanite Slab, and Titanite Scale) are weapon upgrades that fuse permanently to the weapon they are attached to."
- After: "A *Titanite* card is a weapon upgrade card whose name includes the word Titanite: Titanite Shard, Titanite Chunk, Titanite Slab, Titanite Scale, Demon Titanite, and Blue Titanite. A Titanite card fuses permanently to the weapon it is attached to."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] A test by name settles Demon Titanite and Blue Titanite without a second list: every card called Titanite behaves as Titanite.
- Source: final rulings, core-E34 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official 2017: no weapon upgrade is ever removed (DS-Core-Rules-lores.txt l.672-679); CMP-0004 labels both cards 'Weapon upgrade (Titanite)'.

## Chapter 05 — Tiles and Nodes

### CH-05-001 · §5 Tiles and Nodes
- Type: cut
- Before: "#" (empty heading used as a page break, manuscript line 983)
- After: deleted
- Rationale: Page breaks belong to layout, not structure.
- Source: C-098; clean-up list

### CH-05-002 · §5 Tiles and Nodes
- Type: style
- Before: "# TILES & NODES" / "## The Basics"
- After: "# 5. Tiles and Nodes" / "## 5.1 Tiles and Nodes"; a chapter intro of 60 words with no rule in it
- Rationale: Chapter and section titles follow the section map; no ampersand in headings.
- Source: C-104, C-112; brief §4

### CH-05-003 · §5.1 Tiles and Nodes
- Type: style
- Before: "All combat takes place during encounters which take place on encounter tiles and Boss tiles. All tiles have one or more doorways. Doorways allow characters to travel between tiles."
- After: "Every encounter is fought on one or more tiles: an encounter tile, the boss tiles (see §15.3), or the mega-boss game board (see §6.7). The bonfire tile has no nodes, and no encounter is fought on it (see §11.1)."
- Rationale: Copied official prose rewritten in the book's voice; the mega-boss game board added as the third kind of tile; cross-references replace prose pointers.
- Source: F-101, F-102; CMP-0012(e)

### CH-05-004 · §5.1 Tiles and Nodes
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The edge of every tile is a wall. ... A tile's orientation is fixed when the tile is placed, and the tile is neither rotated nor moved until the tiles are next laid out for setup."
- Rationale: Walls, doorways and tile orientation were used by the push and boss-arc rules but never defined; the chapter now owns them.
- Source: A-085, A-082; glossary Adjacent node; brief chapter instructions

### CH-05-005 · §5.1 Tiles and Nodes
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Basic nodes are the most common type of node."
- After: "**Basic node.** The most common node type, printed in yellow."
- Rationale: The colour was used as an identifier in the trap rule without being introduced where nodes are defined.
- Source: A-084

### CH-05-006 · §5.1 Tiles and Nodes
- Type: restructure
- Before: "All basic nodes that are adjacent to doorways are entry nodes and basic nodes. Entry nodes are where characters are placed when they enter the tile."
- After: "**Entry node.** A basic node adjacent to a doorway. When the characters enter a tile, they are placed on the entry nodes beside the doorway through which they entered (see §7.5, Encounter Start)."
- Rationale: Node types restated as definitions in the glossary's form; the entry nodes are tied to the doorway used, as the glossary states; "entrance node" and "entryway" are not used.
- Source: C-062, C-119, F-102

### CH-05-007 · §5.1 Tiles and Nodes
- Type: completion
- Before: "The mini-boss spawn node is where the mini-boss is placed ... The main-boss spawn node is where the main-boss is placed ..."
- After: "The mini-boss tile has a mini-boss spawn node, the main-boss tile a main-boss spawn node, and a mega-boss game board a mega-boss spawn node; the boss is placed there at the start of its encounter (see §15.3)."
- Rationale: The mega-boss spawn node on the game board's mega-boss side is added; the placement step itself is owned by §15.3.
- Source: CMP-0012(e), A-122

### CH-05-008 · §5.1 Tiles and Nodes
- Type: completion
- Before: "An adjacent node is any node located directly next to another node: horizontally, vertically, or diagonally."
- After: "Two nodes separated by a wall ... are not adjacent. Nodes on different tiles are not adjacent; the two boss tiles joined for a main-boss encounter form one tile (see §15.3)."
- Rationale: Adjacency across walls and tile joins was unstated; the glossary's definition is stated in full in its owning section. Spawn and terrain nodes are stated to count as basic nodes for movement and range.
- Source: glossary Adjacent node; A-082; F-103

### CH-05-009 · §5.2 Movement
- Type: completion
- Before: "When a character moves, they place their model on an adjacent node."
- After: "**Move.** Any change of node that a model makes by the Walk or Run minor action (see §10.2), by a shift icon (see §10.3, Shift), or by an enemy or boss movement icon (see §9.2; §16.2)."
- Rationale: "Move" is defined once so that every terrain rule can use one verb; the four different verb lists of the manuscript are retired. Character and enemy movement costs are cross-referenced, not restated.
- Source: C-086, A-082; cut/simplify list ("Terrain owns it")

### CH-05-010 · §5.2 Movement
- Type: completion
- Before: "A valid node is any node that does not contain terrain that would block movement such as a gravestone or chest." (manuscript Character Movement, line 1840)
- After: "**Valid node.** A node that a model may enter by a given form of movement ... If no valid node exists for a move, a dodge, or a push, the model stays on its node."
- Rationale: The term the push fallback and the dodge rely on is defined in the chapter that owns movement; the no-valid-node outcome is stated once for all forms of movement.
- Source: A-085, A-086, C-086

### CH-05-011 · §5.2 Movement
- Type: restructure
- Before: "Characters may not leave the encounter tile(s) noted on the current encounter card during their movement." (manuscript Character Movement, line 1840)
- After: "During an encounter, no model may leave the tile or tiles on which the encounter is fought, and no model may pass through a doorway."
- Rationale: The rule applies to every model, so it is owned by the general movement section; §10.2 cites it.
- Source: C-088; cut/simplify list; A-082

### CH-05-012 · §5.3 Node Model Limits
- Type: style
- Before: "the players must push one of the three models already on the node. characters must choose a friendly model to push from the overcrowded node if possible. Enemy models must push other enemy models for the over-crowded node if possible."
- After: "When a fourth non-boss model enters a node, the players must push one of the three models already on the node to a valid node chosen by the players (see §8.4)."
- Rationale: Actor policy (players choose, models are pushed); lower-case sentence start removed; the direction of the overcrowding push is defined (any valid node, players' choice); summons and invaders are assigned to their sides.
- Source: C-048, F-086, C-114, A-086 (OPTIONAL, applied), F-104

### CH-05-013 · §5.3 Node Model Limits
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If a boss model moves onto a node that already contains a boss, the players must push the boss that was on the node first."
- After: "When a boss model moves onto a node that holds another boss model, the boss already on the node is pushed to a valid node chosen by the players (see §17.1)."
- Rationale: Ligature removed; the only encounters with two boss models are owned by §17.1 (Twin Gargoyles, Ornstein & Smough as one boss with two models).
- Source: C-110, A-039, Gate 1 — OQ-25, F-105

### CH-05-014 · §5.3 Node Model Limits
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Therefore, a node may contain one boss and three characters, for example."
- After: a worked example ("*Example.* The Knight, the Herald, and a Hollow Soldier share a node. ... the boss does not count towards the limit, so no model is pushed.")
- Rationale: The verified in-line example becomes a worked example in the house form; the rhetorical "Therefore" is removed.
- Source: C-094, F §8 voice guide

### CH-05-015 · §5.4 Range
- Type: correction
- Before: "Attacks and eﬀects have a maximum range ... Range 2 means the models can be up to two nodes apart, etc. Range ∞ (inﬁnity) indicates unlimited range and can aﬀect any model"
- After: "A range of any number N reaches a model up to N nodes away, including every closer node. Range ∞ (infinity) is unlimited and reaches every model in the encounter."
- Rationale: Ligature glyphs replaced with plain letters; "etc." replaced by a closed rule; copied official prose rewritten.
- Source: C-110, C-113, C-109, C-116, F-106

### CH-05-016 · §5.4 Range
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "It is counted along the shortest chain of adjacent nodes ... Range is never blocked. Terrain, trap tokens, and other models on the nodes between two models do not block range and do not lengthen it."
- Rationale: How range is counted past walls and terrain was unstated; the chapter instruction requires each terrain type's effect on the line of range, which is stated once here and cited from §5.5. Sources of each range value are cross-referenced; a worked example is added.
- Source: A-082; brief chapter instructions

### CH-05-017 · §5.5 Terrain
- Type: restructure
- Before: terrain rules as bullet fragments in §Terrain and a second, different set in §Character Movement
- After: §5.5 owns every terrain rule for every model ("The rules in this section apply to every model alike: characters, enemies, phantoms, and the boss."), in prose; the Character Movement statements are reduced to a citation of §5.5
- Rationale: One statement of each terrain rule; enemies and bosses were never stated to be blocked by terrain.
- Source: C-119, C-086, A-082; cut/simplify list ("Terrain movement rules in two places → Terrain owns it")

### CH-05-018 · §5.5 Terrain
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If an encounter is reset, face-up trap tokens are flipped face-down but remain on their original nodes." / "When the characters rest at the bonfire, ... tokens ... are not moved."
