- After: "When the characters rest at the bonfire and the encounters are reset (see §11.5), terrain stays on its nodes."
- Rationale: "Reset" is tied to its definition in §11.5; the persistence of terrain through resets is stated once for all kinds.
- Source: A-056

### CH-05-019 · §5.5 Gravestones
- Type: cut
- Before: "Event scrying allows the players to reveal a single face-down event card. They then decide to return the card face-down to its encounter tile or replace it with the top card of the same level event deck."
- After: deleted
- Rationale: Event cards are removed from the game.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-06; A-090, D-001

### CH-05-020 · §5.5 Gravestones
- Type: cut
- Before: "When the characters rest at the bonfire, gravestone tokens are not flipped from their current side or moved."
- After: "When the characters rest at the bonfire, a gravestone stays on its node."
- Rationale: Gravestones are single-sided; the "not flipped" clause is a vestige.
- Source: D-003; clean-up list

### CH-05-021 · §5.5 Gravestones
- Type: correction
- Before: "Boss Behaviour scrying allows the players to reveal the top card of the upcoming boss's Behaviour Deck."
- After: "Boss behaviour: reveal the top card of the behaviour deck of the boss the party must face next; the deck is built at setup and kept face down on that boss's tile (see §6.5; §15.5)."
- Rationale: The deck must exist before the boss encounter for the scry to be usable; the chapter states where it is and cites the sections that build it. "Behaviour Deck" is lower-case.
- Source: A-043, C-057

### CH-05-022 · §5.5 Gravestones
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Scrying is defined as revealing a hidden card and then either returning to its place or sending it away. ... replace it with the top card of the same level encounter deck."
- After: "**Scry.** To reveal one hidden card to every player and then either return it to its place or send it away." / "... place the revealed card face down at the bottom of that deck."
- Rationale: Scry is defined in the glossary's form; the destination of a replaced encounter card was unstated and is now definite; the scry is stated to be one card per cleared encounter however many gravestones the tile holds, and its timing is cross-referenced to §7.5, Encounter Resolution.
- Source: brief chapter instructions; C-119; preserve list (scry on clearing a gravestone tile)

### CH-05-023 · §5.5 Gravestones
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Models cannot walk, run, dodge, or be pushed into nodes containing a gravestone."
- After: "No model may move, dodge, or be pushed into a node containing a gravestone."
- Rationale: The defined verb "move" covers walk, run, shift and enemy movement, so enemies and bosses are blocked as well as characters.
- Source: A-082, C-086

### CH-05-024 · §5.5 Barrels
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Barrels are destroyed, and the token removed, when a model dodges into or is pushed into that node. ... barrel tokens are restored to their unbroken state but are not moved."
- After: "the barrel is destroyed: turn the token to its destroyed side, or remove a barrel model from the tile. ... return each removed barrel model to its terrain node."
- Rationale: A token that is removed cannot be "not moved"; the token's two sides are used and the model's return is stated, so restoration is definite. "Models cannot walk or run" becomes "No model may move", which blocks enemies and shift too.
- Source: A-082, C-086, C-051

### CH-05-025 · §5.5 Chests
- Type: correction
- Before: "If they cannot decide, the treasure should be awarded to a randomly selected character."
- After: "A chest is opened, and the card beneath it awarded, only when the encounter that contains it is cleared (see §7.7). If mimics are in play, a chest is opened only as §13 describes."
- Rationale: The contested-treasure tie-break (aggro holder) and the opening and mimic procedures are owned by §7.7 and §13; the chest section keeps the component rules and cites them.
- Source: C-087, D-037, A-058, A-059, C-020, CMP-0014, Gate 1 — OQ-29, OQ-11

### CH-05-026 · §5.5 Chests
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Chests always begin encounters with the closed side face-up and with one face-down treasure Card of the appropriate Tier beneath it."
- After: "A chest is placed closed side up, and one face-down treasure card from the treasure deck of the current tier is placed beneath it ... an opened chest is never closed again and holds no further treasure."
- Rationale: The pre-dealt card is the reward; the token's sides are named; capitalisation of common nouns corrected; copied prose rewritten.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-11; F-107; C-051

### CH-05-027 · §5.5 Traps
- Type: completion
- Before: "When a character is pushed onto or moves into a node containing a face-down trap token, flip the token face-up and resolve its effect on all models on that node."
- After: "A trap token is blank or shows a dodge difficulty and a damage value. ... Trap damage cannot be blocked or resisted. ... Only characters trigger traps, and only characters are affected by them"
- Rationale: What a trap token shows and how it is resolved were never stated. Of the two approved fixes, D-009 (enemies unaffected, the restored rule) is followed and A-083's alternative reading is adopted; the conflict is logged in the issues file.
- Source: A-083, D-009

### CH-05-028 · §5.5 Traps
- Type: style
- Before: "All trap tokens must be shuffled face-down and dealt out to all basic (yellow) nodes ... simply stop adding trap tokens when none are available. Players should fill the basic nodes furthest from the tiles entry with trap tokens first."
- After: a three-step procedure ("Shuffle every trap token in the supply face down." ... "If the supply runs out before every such node has a token, leave the remaining nodes without a trap.")
- Rationale: Dealing is a procedure; "should", "simply" and the typo "tiles entry" are removed; "(yellow)" is now introduced in §5.1; "set-up" becomes "set up".
- Source: C-019, F-089, F-090, C-123, C-015, A-084, C-059

### CH-05-029 · §5.5 Traps
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If an encounter is reset, face-up trap tokens are flipped face-down but remain on their original nodes."
- After: "Trap tokens are dealt to its tile the first time the encounter is set up ... Trap tokens are not dealt to the same tile again when its encounter is reset."
- Rationale: Persistence through resets (preserved mechanic) is stated with its consequence that traps are dealt once; a worked example is added.
- Source: preserve list (traps persist through resets); A-056

### CH-05-030 · §5.5 Pillars
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Pillar tokens exist only in the Asylum Demon expansion. ... No model may move, dodge, or be pushed into a node containing a pillar token. A pillar is destroyed only as §17.3 describes."
- Rationale: Pillars are a terrain type used by the boss rules but absent from the terrain chapter; destruction and restoration stay with §17.3.
- Source: CMP-0012(f)

### CH-05-031 · §5 Tiles and Nodes (style sweep)
- Type: style
- Before: inconsistent italics; "face-down"/"face-up" for tokens; "Behaviour Deck", "treasure Card", "Tier"; "set-up"; numerals in prose
- After: glossary headwords italicised at first use per H2; "face down" / "face up" as predicates and "face-down" attributive; common nouns lower-case; "set up"; words for zero to nine in prose, digits for printed labels (Range 0, Level)
- Rationale: Style sheet applied mechanically.
- Source: C-030, C-051, C-057, C-059, C-116

### CH-05-032 · Findings mapped to this chapter but owned elsewhere
- Type: clarification
- Before: n/a
- After: not applied here
- Rationale: A-033 (PvP trap placement) is owned by §20.6; A-122 (boss facing at placement) by §15.3 and §16.2; A-085 (push fallback) by §8.4, which now has the term "valid node" to rely on; A-039 (two-boss encounters) by §17.1. OPTIONAL findings skipped: none (A-086 and F-101 to F-107 all applied).
- Source: brief §4 section map

### CH-05-033 · §5.5 Terrain (Gravestones)
- Type: restructure
- Before: "**Scry.** To reveal one hidden card to every player and then either return it to its place or send it away. The players choose one of the following: …" (full procedure under Gravestones)
- After: "The scry is resolved during Encounter Resolution, which states the cards that may be scried and what is done with them (see §7.5, Encounter Resolution)."
- Rationale: A rule is stated once. The scry is executed during Encounter Resolution, so §7.5 owns the procedure and §5.5 keeps the trigger (lead editor, at integration of chapter 07).
- Source: C-088 (duplicated rules), chapter 07 issues list

### CH-05-034 · §5.5 Terrain (Traps)
- Type: correction
- Before: "When a character moves into or is pushed onto a node holding a face-down trap token, the token is turned face up at once"
- After: "When a character enters a node holding a face-down trap token, whether by a move, by the movement that follows a successful dodge, or by a push, the token is turned face up at once"
- Rationale: §5.2 defines a move so as to exclude dodge movement, which left a dodge onto a trap node untriggered. The approved F.A.Q. ruling is that any character movement onto the node, including a dodge, triggers the trap.
- Source: CMP-0003 (F.A.Q., traps), review of PR #2

### CH-05-035 · §5.5 Terrain (Traps)
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Trap damage cannot be blocked or resisted."
- After: "Trap damage is not an attack, and it cannot be blocked or resisted."
- Rationale: The F.A.Q. cites §5.5 for the ruling that trap damage is not an attack; the section now states it.
- Source: QA observation (example and consistency verification)

### CH-05-036 · §5.5, Gravestones
- Type: ruling
- Before: "When an encounter that contains one or more gravestones is *cleared*, the players may *scry* one card, however many gravestones the tile holds. The scry is resolved during Encounter Resolution, which states the cards that may be scried and what is done with them (see §7.5, Encounter Resolution)."
- After: "When an encounter is *cleared*, the players may *scry* one card for each gravestone on its tile. Each scry is resolved during Encounter Resolution, which states the cards that may be scried and what is done with them (see §7.5, Encounter Resolution)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] One scry for each gravestone: the token count is the rule, and the "however many" clause is deleted.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E01 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official 2017 keys the benefit to each gravestone (DS-Core-Rules-lores.txt 755-759, 1385-1386); official 2022 lets each gravestone be used once (DS-TG 952-960).

### CH-05-037 · §5.1
- Type: clarification
- Before: "**Entry node.** A basic node adjacent to a doorway. When the characters enter a tile, they are placed on the entry nodes beside the doorway through which they entered (see §7.5, Encounter Start)."
- After: "**Entry node.** A basic node along the tile edge that holds the doorway through which the characters entered the tile. When the characters enter a tile, they are placed on its entry nodes (see §7.5, Encounter Start)."
- Rationale: A node cannot be adjacent to a doorway, which is not a node; the edge fixes which nodes are meant, and one definition serves §5.1, §7.5, §15.3 and the glossary.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E05 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official 2017 example places the characters on any node along the edge of the doorway they entered by (DS-Core-Rules-lores.txt 415-422, 853-855).

### CH-05-038 · §5.1
- Type: completion
- Before: "A spawn node or terrain node that holds no enemy or terrain is entered, left, and counted for range exactly as a basic node is."
- After: "Tiles and encounter cards tell a primary spawn node from a secondary spawn node {{icon:ast-0010-secondary-spawn-node}}, and a primary terrain node from a secondary terrain node {{icon:ast-0018-secondary-terrain-node}}, by their icons. The difference shows which node receives which models or terrain and has no other effect.

A spawn node or terrain node that holds no enemy or terrain is entered, left, and counted for range exactly as a basic node is."
- Rationale: The encounter card cannot be read without knowing that the two icons name two different nodes.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E09 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official 2017 symbol key lists primary and secondary spawn and terrain nodes (DS-Core-Rules-lores.txt 1830-1842).

### CH-05-039 · §5.5, Traps
- Type: correction
- Before: "The dodge follows every rule in §9.5, including the move that follows a successful dodge."
- After: "The dodge follows every rule in §9.5, including the movement that follows a successful dodge."
- Rationale: §5.2 states that the movement after a dodge is not a move; the noun is corrected.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E10 (`06_final_rulings.md`); This book, §5.2 (Move) and §9.5.

### CH-05-040 · §5.5, Traps
- Type: ruling
- Before: "except the basic nodes along a tile edge that adjoins another tile,"
- After: "except the basic nodes along a tile edge whose doorway is part of the route (see §6.5),"
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] The number of traps must not depend on which tiles happen to touch; the exclusion is keyed to the doorways the party uses.
- Source: final rulings, lead-E01 (`06_final_rulings.md`); core memo, cross-cluster effects; official core rules exclude nodes along a wall that has a doorway

### CH-05-041 · §5.5, Pillars
- Type: correction
- Before: "At the start of a mega-boss encounter fought on that expansion's mega-boss game board, one pillar token is placed on each of its four pillar nodes (see §17.3)."
- After: "At the start of a boss encounter fought on the mega-boss side of that expansion's mega-boss game board, one pillar token is placed on each of its four pillar nodes (see §17.3)."
- Rationale: The Asylum Demon mini-boss is fought on the same board and meets the same pillars.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E45 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Asylum Demon booklet lines 197-205

### CH-05-042 · §5.1
- Type: correction
- Before: "Tiles are joined so that a doorway of each tile faces a doorway of the tile beside it (see §6.5)."
- After: "Tiles are joined so that a doorway of each tile faces a doorway of the tile that follows it on the route; a tile that merely lies beside another is not joined to it (see §6.5)."
- Rationale: The route is a chain of any shape (§6.5 steps 3 and 4), so a tile may lie beside a tile that is not next on the route and shares no doorway with it.
- Source: final rulings, VA-006 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-05-043 · §5.3
- Type: correction
- Before: "Then the Gargoyle moves onto a node that holds the Knight, the Herald, and the Assassin; the boss does not count towards the limit, so no model is pushed."
- After: "In a later boss encounter the Gargoyle moves onto a node that holds the Knight, the Herald, and the Assassin; the boss does not count towards the limit, so no model is pushed."
- Rationale: A boss encounter holds no Hollow Soldier (§15.2), and the Herald has just been pushed off the node, so the two halves of the example cannot be one scene.
- Source: final rulings, VA-007 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-05-044 · §5.5
- Type: correction
- Before: "There are five kinds: *gravestones*, *barrels*, *chests*, and pillars, which are placed on *terrain nodes* as the *encounter card* directs, and traps, which are dealt to *basic nodes* (see §7.5, Encounter Start)."
- After: "There are five kinds: *gravestones*, *barrels*, and *chests*, which are placed on *terrain nodes* as the *encounter card* directs (see §7.5, Encounter Start); pillars, which are placed on the pillar nodes of one game board (see §5.5, Pillars); and traps, which are dealt to *basic nodes* (see §5.5, Traps)."
- Rationale: §5.5, Pillars and §17.3 place pillar tokens on the four pillar nodes at the start of a boss encounter, to which no encounter card is dealt (§15.2).
- Source: final rulings, VA-008 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-05-045 · §5.1
- Type: correction
- Before: "the *boss* is placed there at the start of its encounter (see §15.3)."
- After: "the *boss* is placed there at the start of its encounter, unless its special rules name another node (see §15.3; §17)."
- Rationale: §15.3 now excepts a boss whose special rules name another node (the Old Iron King is placed on an Iron King node, §17.10).
- Source: final rulings, VA-033 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

## Chapter 06 — Setup

### CH-06-001 · §6 Setup
- Type: restructure
- Before: "##  " (empty heading used as a page break) and twenty-four mixed-case H2/H3 headings
- After: deleted; the chapter follows the fixed section map (6.1 to 6.7) with Title Case H3s and a chapter intro
- Rationale: Empty headings and sentence-case headings removed; the chapter's structure now matches the book's section map.
- Source: C-098, C-101, C-103, brief §4

### CH-06-002 · §6.1 Character Setup
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Each player places their selected character dashboard(s) in front of them."
- After: "Each player chooses one character (see §2.1). The number of characters in the party sets the scaling of enemy health and attack values (see §8.3) and the starting number of sparks (see §6.4, §7.4)."
- Rationale: One character per player, as the Introduction states; the two rules keyed to character count are cross-referenced where the count is fixed.
- Source: C-048, F-086, chapter 01 draft

### CH-06-003 · §6.1 Character Setup
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Place an ember token beside the character's board."
- After: "Place the ember tokens, damage cubes, stamina cubes, hollow cubes, soul tokens, condition tokens, trap tokens, and wound tokens in a supply within reach of every player (see §2.8)."
- Rationale: One setup location for the ember tokens (the supply); the hollow cube is named by its canonical term.
- Source: A-052, C-050

### CH-06-004 · §6.1 Character Setup
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Assign the aggro token to the highest threat character."
- After: "Place the aggro token on the character with the highest threat value (see §7.5, Encounter Start)."
- Rationale: The threat tie-break is owned by §7.5 (CMP-0011); no first player token exists.
- Source: A-014, CMP-0011, C-033

### CH-06-005 · §6.1 Character Setup
- Type: style
- Before: "Place luck, heroic action, and estus flask tokens face-up on each character's dashboard."
- After: "Place the character's luck, heroic action, and estus flask tokens on the dashboard, ready side up (see §3.5)."
- Rationale: Tokens have a ready side and a used side; cards are face up or face down.
- Source: C-051

### CH-06-006 · §6.2 Treasure Deck Setup
- Type: style
- Before: "to suggest which class might benefit most from its use. Aside from this suggestion of ideal character class, there are no gameplay differences from common treasures."
- After: "The emblem has no rules effect."
- Rationale: Speculation removed; the only rule content is that the emblem has no effect.
- Source: C-023, F-044

### CH-06-007 · §6.2 Treasure Deck Setup
- Type: correction
- Before: "These represent exceedingly rare relics that do not appear in every game and cannot be purchased. All legendary treasure cards are shuffled during set-up, after Andre's Shop is stocked, and five unrevealed legendary treasures are then shuffled into the tier two treasure deck."
- After: "Legendary treasure cannot be purchased, and no legendary treasure card enters a treasure deck at the start of the game. The first time the tier two shop is stocked, five legendary treasure cards drawn at random are shuffled into the tier two treasure deck"
- Rationale: Three passages gave two moments; the tier-two-shop moment is kept and the flavour is cut.
- Source: A-075, F-045, A-118, CMP-0007

### CH-06-008 · §6.2 Treasure Deck Setup
- Type: correction
- Before: "Include all common treasures without a required statistic =\> 25."
- After: "every common treasure card and every class-specific treasure card whose stat requirements are all below 25"
- Rationale: The escaped operator is replaced by words; the two decks' contents are stated in prose.
- Source: A-065, C-109

### CH-06-009 · §6.2 Treasure Deck Setup
- Type: restructure
- Before: "Exclude starting equipment. Exclude ember cards."
- After: "Set aside every boss treasure card, every legendary treasure card, every ember card, and every starting equipment card that is not equipped."
- Rationale: The two numbered inclusion lists become content paragraphs and one building procedure; unequipped starting equipment is returned to the box.
- Source: brief §2 (procedures), C-116

### CH-06-010 · §6.2 Treasure Deck Setup
- Type: style
- Before: "DStBG has many expansion sets available of which many include new treasure cards. Players should include all treasure cards from expansion sets in their appropriate type of treasure deck as above."
- After: "Treasure cards from every expansion in the party's collection are included and are sorted into the tier one and tier two treasure decks by the rules of this section."
- Rationale: Product description and advisory "should" replaced by the inclusion rule.
- Source: F-010, C-018, F-062, clean-up list

### CH-06-011 · §6.2 Treasure Deck Setup
- Type: style
- Before: "Transposed treasure cards all have at least one stat requirement greater than or equal to 25 and are thus shuffled into the tier two treasure deck at set-up."
- After: "All transposed treasure is tier two."
- Rationale: The rule is stated without its reasoning; "set-up" becomes "setup".
- Source: C-059, glossary (Transposed treasure)

### CH-06-012 · §6.3 Shop Setup
- Type: completion
- Before: "3 weapons, 3 armour, 2 shields, 2 upgrades, and 2 spells."
- After: "For stocking, a treasure card belongs to the first of the following categories that describes it (see §4.1)." followed by the five categories
- Rationale: The five stocking categories were never defined; a card is now classed deterministically, and the quota is written in words.
- Source: A-069, B-022, D-006, C-116

### CH-06-013 · §6.3 Shop Setup
- Type: completion
- Before: "Draw tier one treasure cards one at a time to stock tier one shop ... Reshuffle extra treasures back into the tier one treasure deck."
- After: "Repeat steps 1 and 2 until every category is full or the deck is exhausted. A category still unfilled when the deck is exhausted stays short."
- Rationale: The procedure now terminates when a deck cannot supply a category. B-023's twelve-draw cap is not applied: it would alter the preserved category-quota mechanic and is not among the five signed-off mechanical changes.
- Source: B-023 (partial), preserve list

### CH-06-014 · §6.3 Shop Setup
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "Draw tier one treasure cards one at a time to restock tier one shop"
- After: "Place every card remaining in the tier one shop, other than ember cards, on the bottom of the tier one treasure deck."
- Rationale: Unsold cards are swept to the bottom of their deck before a shop is restocked.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-27, A-046

### CH-06-015 · §6.3 Shop Setup
- Type: completion
- Before: "Add ember cards equal to the number of characters."
- After: "Ember cards remaining in the tier one shop stay there. No ember card is added to a shop after the start of the game."
- Rationale: The ember supply is fixed at setup; "equal to the number of characters" is written as "one ember card per character".
- Source: A-046, C-048

### CH-06-016 · §6.3 Shop Setup
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "If a legendary treasure card is drawn, shuffle it back into the tier two treasure deck and draw again."
- Rationale: Once legendary cards are in the tier two deck, a later restock would otherwise put one in the shop.
- Source: A-076

### CH-06-017 · §6.3 Shop Setup
- Type: completion
- Before: "Shuffle five random legendary treasure cards."
- After: "draw five legendary treasure cards at random without revealing them, shuffle them into the tier two treasure deck, and return the remaining legendary treasure cards to the game box."
- Rationale: Truncated step completed with its destination.
- Source: A-118, CMP-0007, A-075

### CH-06-018 · §6.3 Shop Setup
- Type: style
- Before: "### After Boss is Defeated:"
- After: "### Restocking After a Boss"
- Rationale: Headings carry no trailing punctuation and are nouns.
- Source: C-100

### CH-06-019 · §6.3 Shop Setup
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "Purchase and sale prices are set out in Andre's rules (see §11.4)."
- Rationale: Prices are owned by §11.4; the shop setup cites them.
- Source: C-070 (owned by §11.4), C-043

### CH-06-020 · §6.4 Bonfire Setup
- Type: style
- Before: "Set the *sparks* dial equal to six minus the number of characters."
- After: "Set the spark dial to six minus the number of characters (see §7.4)."
- Rationale: Component name is "spark dial".
- Source: C-052

### CH-06-021 · §6.4 Bonfire Setup
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "*Example.* The Knight, the Herald, and the Assassin set out together. The party sets the spark dial to three, six minus three characters, and every enemy they meet is scaled for three characters."
- Rationale: A worked example of the two values fixed by character count.
- Source: brief §2 (worked examples), F-083

### CH-06-022 · §6.4 Bonfire Setup
- Type: style
- Before: "Place the tier one shop cards face up in a pile on the table next to Andre."
- After: "Place the tier one shop face up beside the bonfire tile, leaving room beside it for the tier two shop."
- Rationale: The shop is placed by the tile, not by a figure; room for the tier two shop is reserved.
- Source: C-043, C-051

### CH-06-023 · §6.5 Encounter Setup
- Type: style
- Before: "Players may now decide if they wish to pursue a longer adventure ending in an epic battle with a mega-boss if they like."
- After: "The party may add a mega-boss as the final boss (see §6.7)."
- Rationale: Doubled permission and flavour removed; the option is its own step.
- Source: F-012, C-121, C-124

### CH-06-024 · §6.5 Encounter Setup
- Type: style
- Before: "For added challenge the players may forgo choice and instead randomly determine the bosses they will oppose by blindly shuffling available mini-boss data cards then revealing one at random."
- After: "Random boss selection is an optional rule. It requires no expansion. The party decides during setup whether random boss selection is in play."
- Rationale: The advice becomes an option in the fixed optional-rule form.
- Source: F-013, C-124, C-121

### CH-06-025 · §6.5 Encounter Setup
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "Keep the main-boss's components, and the mega-boss's if one is in play, aside until they are needed (see §6.6, §6.7)."
- Rationale: Boss selection takes every selected boss's components; the later bosses' components need a place until their legs begin.
- Source: brief §2 (procedures)

### CH-06-026 · §6.5 Encounter Setup
- Type: correction
- Before: "arrange them so they are connected in a two by six tile grid with the bonfire tile and mini-boss tile on opposite corners."
- After: "in a grid of two rows of three tiles, with the bonfire tile and the mini-boss tile in opposite corners."
- Rationale: Six tiles form a two-by-three grid; the single route through all four encounter tiles is kept. D-031's straight line is used for the second leg (§6.6), as the manuscript itself does there.
- Source: A-088, C-090, D-031

### CH-06-027 · §6.5 Encounter Setup
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Place the fog gate token on the doorway between the fourth encounter tile and the mini-boss tile (see §15.1)."
- Rationale: The initial fog gate placement was missing; "Place the fog-gate as before" pointed to it.
- Source: C-080, D-015, A-089, C-058

### CH-06-028 · §6.5 Encounter Setup
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Place the previously selected mini-boss's model, data card, behaviour cards, and treasure cards on the boss tile."
- After: "Build the mini-boss's behaviour deck (see §15.5) and place it face down on the mini-boss tile."
- Rationale: The behaviour deck is built at encounter setup so that gravestone scrying has a deck to reveal.
- Source: A-043

### CH-06-029 · §6.5 Encounter Setup
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Draw four random encounter tiles"
- After: "Place each encounter tile with either side face up, as the party chooses."
- Rationale: The tiles are double-sided and are flipped after the mini-boss; the initial side is the party's choice.
- Source: brief §2 (options are their own step)

### CH-06-030 · §6.5 Encounter Setup
- Type: cut
- Before: "Deal one face-down event card of the encounter card's level to each tile."
- After: deleted
- Rationale: Event cards are removed from the book.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-06, A-090, D-001

### CH-06-031 · §6.5 Encounter Setup
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "Deal one tier one treasure per character face-down to each encounter tile."
- After: "These cards are the encounter's treasure reward and are awarded when the encounter is first cleared (see §7.7)."
- Rationale: The pre-dealt cards are the reward; nothing is drawn on clearing.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-11, A-057, D-041

### CH-06-032 · §6.5 Encounter Setup
- Type: style
- Before: "Encounter levels should increase from the tile adjacent to The Bonfire tile towards the mini-boss tile."
- After: "Encounter levels increase from the tile adjacent to the bonfire tile towards the boss tile."
- Rationale: Mandatory step stated as a rule; "level one, two, and three" becomes "Level 1, 2, and 3" with the level icons.
- Source: C-019, F-089, C-046, C-042

### CH-06-033 · §6.5 Encounter Setup
- Type: style
- Before: "DStBG has many expansion sets available which include new encounter cards. By default, players should include only the encounter cards from the same set as the boss they will face next."
- After: "Each encounter deck is built only from the encounter cards of the set that supplies the next boss."
- Rationale: Rule first, option second in the fixed form, example set apart as an *Example.* paragraph with the full name Great Grey Wolf Sif.
- Source: F-011, C-018, C-012, C-093, F-083, C-122, clean-up list

### CH-06-034 · §6.5 Encounter Setup
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If playing with optional mimics for increased challenge, deal one random face-down ambush card beside each encounter tile."
- After: "If mimics are in play, deal one ambush card face down beside each encounter tile (see §13)." and "If invaders are in play, place the invader tokens on the encounter cards (see §12.2)."
- Rationale: Optional-rule condition in the fixed form; the invader token step is added with its rule owned by §12.2.
- Source: C-124, A-045, Gate 1 — OQ-28, A-092

### CH-06-035 · §6.5 Encounter Setup
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The party decides during setup whether each of the following optional rules is in play. Each is stated in full in its own section."
- Rationale: Every optional rule decided during setup is listed once, with its owning section.
- Source: C-124

### CH-06-036 · §6.6 Setup After the Mini-Boss
- Type: correction
- Before: "Place the fog-gate as before."
- After: "Move the fog gate token to the doorway between the fourth encounter tile and the main-boss tile (see §15.1)."
- Rationale: Reference to a step that did not exist; the placement is now explicit.
- Source: C-080, A-089, C-058

### CH-06-037 · §6.6 Setup After the Mini-Boss
- Type: completion
- Before: "Place all character models on the bonfire tile where they may now without spending a spark. They may visit the Firekeeper and Andre's Shop as usual after resting at the bonfire."
- After: "The characters rest at the bonfire without spending a spark (see §11.2) and may visit the Firekeeper (see §11.3) and Blacksmith Andre (see §11.4)."
- Rationale: Truncated sentence completed; "as usual" replaced by cross-references.
- Source: A-118, CMP-0007, F-064, C-085, C-043

### CH-06-038 · §6.6 Setup After the Mini-Boss
- Type: style
- Before: "Ensure the tier two shop is stocked as previously described." / "Resume encounters in the same way as before starting with the encounter tile closest to The Bonfire tile."
- After: "Restock the tier one shop and stock the tier two shop (see §6.3, Restocking After a Boss)." / "Exploration resumes from the encounter tile adjacent to the bonfire tile (see §11.5)."
- Rationale: Prose cross-references converted to section references.
- Source: F-063, F-065, C-084, C-085

### CH-06-039 · §6.6 Setup After the Mini-Boss
- Type: clarification
- Before: "ending in the main-boss tile"
- After: "The main-boss tile is the two boss tiles joined as one (see §15.3)."
- Rationale: The main-boss encounter uses both boss tiles; the setup says so where the tile is laid.
- Source: glossary (Boss tile), §15.3

### CH-06-040 · §6.6 Setup After the Mini-Boss
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "If player versus player (PvP) encounters are in play, a PvP encounter follows the mini-boss's defeat (see §20.1)."
- Rationale: PvP follows every boss; the setup chapter cites the owning section (also stated for the main-boss in §6.7).
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-15, C-124

### CH-06-041 · §6.6 Setup After the Mini-Boss
- Type: cut
- Before: "Return all previously used encounter and event cards to the game box." / "Deal one face-down event card of the encounter card's level to each tile."
- After: "Return the four encounter cards to the game box." / deleted
- Rationale: Event cards removed; the tier two treasure dealt to each tile is stated to be the reward.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-06, OQ-11

### CH-06-042 · §6.7 Setup After the Main-Boss
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A mega-boss is an optional rule. It requires a mega-boss expansion. The party decides during setup whether a mega-boss is in play (see §6.5, Boss Selection). If no mega-boss is in play, the main-boss is the final boss"
- Rationale: The mega-boss is an optional final act; the fixed optional-rule form is used and the no-mega-boss case is stated.
- Source: C-124, preserve list, glossary (Final boss)

### CH-06-043 · §6.7 Setup After the Main-Boss
- Type: completion
- Before: "Place the Level 4 encounter tile associated with the chosen mega-boss adjacent to The Bonfire tile with the fog-gate placed at the entrance."
- After: "Place the mega-boss game board adjacent to the bonfire tile, with its Level 4 encounter side face up ... and with one of its doorways aligned with a doorway of the bonfire tile."
- Rationale: The "Level 4 encounter tile" is the double-sided mega-boss game board.
- Source: CMP-0012 (e), C-062

### CH-06-044 · §6.7 Setup After the Main-Boss
- Type: clarification
- Before: "with the fog-gate placed at the entrance"
- After: "Flip the game board to its mega-boss side and align one of its doorways with a doorway of the bonfire tile. Place the fog gate token on that doorway (see §15.1)."
- Rationale: The fog gate marks a boss entrance and its shortcut rule would otherwise trigger on the Level 4 encounter; the fog gate clause of CMP-0012 (e) is moved to the flip.
- Source: A-095, D-014, glossary (Fog gate)

### CH-06-045 · §6.7 Setup After the Main-Boss
- Type: completion
- Before: "Shuffle mega-boss Items and deal one face-down to the encounter tile."
- After: "Shuffle the mega-boss's boss treasure cards and deal one face down to the game board. This card is the Level 4 encounter's treasure reward (see §6.7, Level 4 Encounters). No tier two treasure cards are dealt to the game board."
- Rationale: "Items" is undefined; the mega-boss's own boss treasure cards are the only cards that fit, and bare "item" is not used.
- Source: CMP-0012 (d), A-093, C-054; D-032 superseded

### CH-06-046 · §6.7 Setup After the Main-Boss
- Type: cut
- Before: "No event card is used for this encounter."
- After: deleted
- Rationale: Event cards removed from the book.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-06

### CH-06-047 · §6.7 Setup After the Main-Boss
- Type: completion
- Before: "Characters then attempt to clear the Level 4 encounter as they have with previous encounters."
- After: "All four basic nodes along the wall beside the doorway are entry nodes." / "A Level 4 encounter that is cleared is not fought again and does not reset when the characters rest at the bonfire. A Level 4 encounter that is failed resets as any other encounter does (see §11.2)."
- Rationale: Level 4 encounter rules added: entry nodes, card density, unavailable sets, rewards, no repeat once cleared, reset of a failed attempt, campaign placement.
- Source: CMP-0023, D-014, A-123, A-094

### CH-06-048 · §6.7 Setup After the Main-Boss
- Type: cut
- Before: "employing any special rules described in the mega-boss rule booklet."
- After: "Apply the mega-boss's special rules (see §17)."
- Rationale: External booklet reference replaced by the in-book section.
- Source: F-049, F-066, C-081, A-115, clean-up list

### CH-06-049 · §6.7 Setup After the Main-Boss
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "If a character dies they are permanently removed from play. If all characters die, the players lose the game. If any character survives to defeat the mega-boss, the players win the game."
- After: "Death and hollowing are resolved as in any encounter (see §7.2), and a failed mega-boss encounter is resolved as any other failed boss encounter (see §15.2). Defeating the mega-boss wins the game (see §7.1)."
- Rationale: Mega-boss permadeath is not kept; the usual hollowing and loss rules apply.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-14, A-049, A-050, D-007, C-088

### CH-06-050 · §6.7 Setup After the Main-Boss
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If the characters successfully clear the Level 4 encounter they are presented one last chance to spend a spark to rest at the bonfire prior to challenging the mega-boss."
- After: "Clearing the Level 4 encounter grants no free rest. Before passing through the fog gate the party may rest at the bonfire at the cost of one spark (see §11.2)"
- Rationale: "One last chance" is flavour; the rule is that no free rest is granted and a paid rest remains available.
- Source: A-050, C-121

### CH-06-051 · §6.7 Setup After the Main-Boss
- Type: restructure
- Before: "Flip the Level 4 encounter tile to the mega-boss side and place the mega-boss model on the marked node. Set the heat-up and data card next to the mega-boss tile. Shuffle the mega-boss behaviour cards."
- After: "Set up the mega-boss encounter as a boss encounter (see §15.2), with the mega-boss's model on the mega-boss spawn node and its data card, health dial, and behaviour deck (see §15.5) beside the game board."
- Rationale: Boss encounter setup and deck building are owned by §15; the mega-boss setup cites them.
- Source: C-057, brief §5 (owning section)

### CH-06-052 · §6.7 Setup After the Main-Boss
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "In campaign play, a mega-boss that is not the campaign's final boss is fought under the same rules, and the campaign continues when it is defeated (see §18.1)."
- Rationale: Campaigns place mega-bosses mid-campaign; one sentence points to the owning section. The campaign Level 4 placement is cited the same way.
- Source: A-049, A-094 (rules owned by §18.1)

### CH-06-053 · §6 Setup (style sweep)
- Type: style
- Before: "set-up / fog-gate / The Bonfire tile / Andre's Shop / face-up / sparks dial / This will create / Heat-up / curly quotes"
- After: "setup / fog gate / the bonfire tile / Andre's shop / face up / spark dial / present tense / heat-up / straight quotes"
- Rationale: Terminology, hyphenation, capitalisation, tense, and italics policy applied throughout the chapter.
- Source: C-030, C-042, C-043, C-051, C-057, C-058, C-059, C-062, C-101, C-111, C-116, C-122, F-088, F-086, C-019

### CH-06-054 · §6 Setup (findings owned by other chapters)
- Type: clarification
- Before: n/a
- After: cross-references only
- Rationale: These findings are mapped to this chapter but their rules belong elsewhere and are cited here: A-003 (§7.6), A-045 (§12.2), A-048 (§18.3, §18.6), A-051 (§18.8), A-092 and C-077 (§2), C-070 (§11.4, §18.6), C-033 (§7.5; no first player token appears in §6), C-050 (term used), A-030, B-010, C-044, and C-093 (no change required).
- Source: as listed

### CH-06-055 · §6 Setup (OPTIONAL findings skipped)
- Type: clarification
- Before: n/a
- After: not applied
- Rationale: A-004 (solo character never hollows): owned by §7.2. A-047 (spark maximum in the standard game): owned by §7.4. A-014 (threat tie-break): applied by cross-reference to §7.5 only. B-010 (raise solo souls to ten): no change recommended. A-095, D-014, D-041, F-044, F-045, and C-121 were applied.
- Source: as listed

### CH-06-056 · §6.3 Shop Setup (Restocking After a Boss)
- Type: correction
- Before: "If a legendary treasure card is drawn, shuffle it back into the tier two treasure deck and draw again."
- After: "If a legendary treasure card is drawn, set it aside with the other set-aside cards. It is shuffled back into the tier two treasure deck with them when stocking ends."
- Rationale: Shuffling a legendary card straight back made a later restock unable to finish once only legendary cards remained in the deck; setting it aside lets the deck-exhaustion exit apply and keeps legendary cards out of the shop.
- Source: A-075 (legendary cards cannot be purchased), review of PR #2

### CH-06-057 · §6.5, Encounter Tiles
- Type: ruling
- Before: "3. Arrange the bonfire tile, the four encounter tiles, and the mini-boss tile, which is one of the two *boss tiles* (see §15.3), in a grid of two rows of three tiles, with the bonfire tile and the mini-boss tile in opposite corners."
- After: "3. Lay the bonfire tile, the four encounter tiles, and the mini-boss tile, which is one of the two *boss tiles* (see §15.3), in a single chain, each tile beside the next, with the bonfire tile at one end and the mini-boss tile at the other. The chain may run straight or turn at any tile, as the table allows; two rows of three tiles, with the bonfire tile and the mini-boss tile in opposite corners, form one such chain."
- Rationale: One layout rule for both legs: the route is a chain of tiles and its shape is free; the two-by-three grid stays as a permitted arrangement.
- Source: final rulings, core-E05 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official 2017 setup lays the tiles 'in any way you wish, aligning their doorways as you go' (DS-Core-Rules-lores.txt l.295-300); findings A-088, C-090, D-031 disagree only about shape.

### CH-06-058 · §6.5, Encounter Tiles
- Type: clarification
- Before: "4. Turn each encounter tile so that exactly two of its doorways open on to adjacent tiles, forming a single route from the bonfire tile through all four encounter tiles to the mini-boss tile."
- After: "4. Turn each encounter tile so that one of its doorways faces the tile before it in the chain and another faces the tile after it, forming a single route from the bonfire tile through all four encounter tiles to the mini-boss tile. A doorway that faces neither of those tiles is not used."
- Rationale: 'Exactly two doorways' cannot be met by a tile with three or four doorways placed in a grid; the route is fixed by the chain, and spare doorways are ignored.
- Source: final rulings, core-E06 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official tiles carry differing numbers of doorways (DS-Core-Rules-lores.txt l.409, l.835-850).

### CH-06-059 · §6.6
- Type: ruling
- Before: "6. Arrange the *bonfire* tile, the four encounter tiles, and the main-boss tile in a straight line, with the bonfire tile and the main-boss tile at opposite ends. The main-boss tile is the two *boss tiles* joined as one (see §15.3)."
- After: "6. Lay the *bonfire* tile, the four encounter tiles, and the main-boss tile in a single chain, as in the first setup (see §6.5, Encounter Tiles), with the bonfire tile at one end and the main-boss tile at the other. The main-boss tile is the two *boss tiles* joined as one (see §15.3)."
- Rationale: Second leg uses the same chain rule as the first; a straight line of seven tile widths is no longer required.
- Source: final rulings, core-E07 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As core-E05.

### CH-06-060 · §6.6
- Type: ruling
- Before: "13. Deal one *tier two treasure* card per character face down to each encounter tile. These cards are the encounter's treasure reward (see §7.7).
14. If mimics are in play, deal one ambush card face down beside each encounter tile (see §13).
15. If *invaders* are in play, place the *invader tokens* on the encounter cards (see §12.2).
16. Restock the tier one *shop* and stock the tier two shop (see §6.3, Restocking After a Boss)."
- After: "13. Restock the tier one *shop* and stock the tier two shop (see §6.3, Restocking After a Boss).
14. Deal one *tier two treasure* card per character face down to each encounter tile. These cards are the encounter's treasure reward (see §7.7).
15. If mimics are in play, deal one ambush card face down beside each encounter tile (see §13).
16. If *invaders* are in play, place the *invader tokens* on the encounter cards (see §12.2)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] The shop is stocked before the treasure rewards are dealt, as in the first setup (§6.3 before §6.5), so the five legendary cards are in the tier two deck when the second leg's rewards are dealt.
- Source: final rulings, core-E08 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official 2017 adds the legendary cards to the deck when the mini-boss falls so that they can be drawn in the second half (DS-Core-Rules-lores.txt l.371-381); author's source: legendary treasure 'do not appear in every game'.

### CH-06-061 · §6.1
- Type: correction
- Before: "4. Place the character's *luck*, *heroic action*, and *estus flask* tokens on the dashboard, ready side up (see §3.5)."
- After: "4. Place the character's *luck*, *heroic action*, and *estus flask* tokens on the dashboard, ready side up, and the character's *ember* token beside the dashboard (see §3.5)."
- Rationale: §3.5 and the glossary start the ember token beside the dashboard; §6.1 placed it in the common supply.
- Source: final rulings, core-E19 (`06_final_rulings.md`); CMP-0001 (approved at Gate 1): 'The ember token begins the game beside the dashboard'.

### CH-06-062 · §6.1
- Type: correction
- Before: "5. Place the *ember* tokens, *damage cubes*, *stamina cubes*, *hollow cubes*, soul tokens, *condition* tokens, *trap tokens*, and *wound tokens* in a supply within reach of every player (see §2.8)."
- After: "5. Place the *damage cubes*, *stamina cubes*, remaining white cubes, soul tokens, *condition* tokens, *trap tokens*, and *wound tokens* in a supply within reach of every player (see §2.8). The remaining white cubes serve as *hollow cubes* (see §7.2)."
- Rationale: Ember tokens leave the common supply (core-019) and the hollow cubes are named as the spare white cubes (core-001).
- Source: final rulings, core-E20 (`06_final_rulings.md`); CMP-0001; core-001.

### CH-06-063 · §6.2, Expansion Treasure
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Treasure cards from every expansion in the party's collection are included and are sorted into the tier one and tier two treasure decks by the rules of this section."
- After: "Treasure cards from every expansion in the party's collection are included and are sorted into the tier one and tier two treasure decks by the rules of this section. The cards listed in Appendix B are added or amended as that appendix states before the decks are built (see Appendix B)."
- Rationale: Setup now points to the card amendments it depends on.
- Source: final rulings, core-E28 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Chapter 23 issues list, cross-chapter pointers.

### CH-06-064 · §6.5, Encounter Cards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "2. Deal one encounter card face down to each encounter tile, of the level the mini-boss's data card gives for that tile's position on the route."
- After: "2. Deal one encounter card face down to each encounter tile, of the level the mini-boss's data card gives for that tile's position on the route. Keep the rest of each encounter deck face down beside the bonfire tile."
- Rationale: The encounter scry of §7.5 draws a replacement from the encounter deck, so the decks stay on the table.
- Source: final rulings, core-E29 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §7.5, Encounter Resolution (scry); chapter 07 issues list, item 1.

### CH-06-065 · §6.7
- Type: correction
- Before: "6. Place the mega-boss's model, *data card*, *health dial*, *behaviour cards*, and remaining boss treasure cards beside the game board."
- After: "6. Place the mega-boss's model, *data card*, *health dial*, and remaining boss treasure cards beside the game board, build its *behaviour deck* as its rules direct (see §15.5; §17), and place the deck face down beside them."
- Rationale: §15.5 says every boss's deck is built at the setup step that places its components; §6.7 placed the cards without building the deck, so a gravestone in the Level 4 encounter granted a scry of a deck that did not exist.
- Source: final rulings, core-E30 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §15.5 ('built during setup ... see §6.5, §6.6, §6.7'); §6.5 Encounter Tiles step 7; §6.6 step 10; §7.5, Encounter Resolution (scry).

### CH-06-066 · §6.5, Encounter Cards, step 5
- Type: clarification
- Before: "(see §13).
5. If *invaders* are in play, place the *invader tokens* on the encounter cards (see §12.2)."
- After: "(see §13).
5. If *invaders* are in play and any character is *embered*, place the *invader tokens* on the encounter cards (see §12.2)."
- Rationale: The step read as unconditional although §12.2 places tokens at setup only when a character is embered.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E14 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §12.2, Invasions of this book; official Phantoms booklet places tokens only 'when a character gains an Ember token' (lines 363-366).

### CH-06-067 · §6.6, step 15
- Type: clarification
- Before: "16. If *invaders* are in play, place the *invader tokens* on the encounter cards (see §12.2)."
- After: "16. If *invaders* are in play and any character is *embered*, place the *invader tokens* on the encounter cards (see §12.2)."
- Rationale: Same wording as §6.5 step 5, so that the two setups state the same condition. Lead editor: Step renumbered by core-E08.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E15 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §12.2, Invasions of this book.

### CH-06-068 · §6.7, Level 4 Encounters
- Type: clarification
- Before: "which is awarded when the encounter is *cleared* in the same way as a chest's treasure card (see §7.7)."
- After: "which is awarded when the encounter is *cleared* in the same way as a chest's treasure card (see §7.7). In campaign play a Level 4 encounter is rewarded as the other encounters of its section are (see §18.1)."
- Rationale: Points to the campaign rule made by campaign-E16.
- Source: final rulings, lead-E07 (`06_final_rulings.md`); campaign memo, cross-cluster effects

### CH-06-069 · §6.1
- Type: ruling
- Before: "7. If the party consists of one character, place six *souls* on that character's dashboard (see §7.6)."
- After: "7. If the party consists of one character, place six *souls* on that character's dashboard (see §7.6). Before leaving the bonfire tile for the first time, that character may visit the *Firekeeper* and Blacksmith Andre as during a rest (see §11.3; §11.4)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] The six souls exist to help a lone character through the first encounters, and could not be spent before a rest that costs a spark.
- Source: final rulings, lead-E10 (`06_final_rulings.md`); core memo, observation without an edit

### CH-06-070 · §6.5
- Type: completion
- Before: "6. Place the mini-boss's model, data card, health dial, and boss treasure cards on the mini-boss tile."
- After: "6. Place the mini-boss's model, data card, health dial, and boss treasure cards on the mini-boss tile. When the Asylum Demon is the mini-boss, its expansion's game board takes the place of the mini-boss tile throughout this setup (see §17.3)."
- Rationale: The Asylum Demon mini-boss is fought on its expansion's game board (boss-E11); the setup now points to it.
- Source: final rulings, lead-E15 (`06_final_rulings.md`); boss memo, cross-cluster effects

### CH-06-071 · §6.1
- Type: ruling
- Before: "7. If the party consists of one character, place six *souls* on that character's dashboard (see §7.6). Before leaving the bonfire tile for the first time, that character may visit the *Firekeeper* and Blacksmith Andre as during a rest (see §11.3; §11.4)."
- After: "7. If the party consists of one character, place six *souls* on that character's dashboard (see §7.6)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] lead-E10 is withdrawn. Letting a lone character spend before setting out made an exception to the one rule that souls are spent at a rest (§7.6, §11.3, §11.4, and three glossary entries); the author's rule stands as written, and the six souls are spent at the first rest, which leaves the lone character a real choice between resting early and pressing on.
- Source: final rulings, lead-E22 (`06_final_rulings.md`); review finding on the pull request; the author's manuscript says only that a character adventuring alone starts with six souls

### CH-06-072 · §6.1
- Type: correction
- Before: "sets the scaling of enemy health and attack values (see §8.3)"
- After: "sets the scaling of enemy health, movement, and attack values (see §8.3)"
- Rationale: The table of §8.3 scales enemy movement as well as health and attack value.
- Source: final rulings, VA-009 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-06-073 · §6.2
- Type: correction
- Before: "Every *treasure card* belongs to one of six types (see §2.5), and its type and its stat requirements (see §4.1) decide which deck, if any, it enters."
- After: "The *treasure cards* from which the decks are built are of the six types set out below, and a card's type and its stat requirements (see §4.1) decide which deck, if any, it enters; the number of cards of each type is given with the components (see §2.5). An *ember card*, an *invader*'s treasure card, and a Titanite Scale card are of none of the six types and enter neither deck (see §2.9; §4.4)."
- Rationale: §2.5, §4.4, §7.7 and §11.4 count ember cards, invader treasure cards and Titanite Scale cards as treasure cards, and none of them is one of the six types; §2.5 names no sixth type.
- Source: final rulings, VA-010 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-06-074 · §6.2
- Type: correction
- Before: "**Common treasure.** A treasure card that is not starting equipment, class-specific, transposed, legendary, or boss treasure."
- After: "**Common treasure.** A treasure card that is not starting equipment, class-specific, transposed, legendary, or boss treasure, and is not an *ember card*, an *invader*'s treasure card, or a Titanite Scale card."
- Rationale: As written the catch-all makes invader treasure cards and Titanite Scale cards common treasure and puts them in the decks, against §11.4, §12.2 and §14, which keep them outside every treasure deck.
- Source: final rulings, VA-011 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-06-075 · §6.2
- Type: correction
- Before: "1. Set aside every boss treasure card, every legendary treasure card, every ember card, and every starting equipment card that is not equipped."
- After: "1. Set aside every boss treasure card, every legendary treasure card, every ember card, every *invader*'s treasure card, every Titanite Scale card, and every starting equipment card that is not equipped."
- Rationale: Step 3 sorts every card not set aside into the decks; §11.4, §12.2 and §14 require invader treasure cards and Titanite Scale cards to stay out of them.
- Source: final rulings, VA-012 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-06-076 · §6.3
- Type: correction
- Before: "including rings and *titanite*."
- After: "including rings and *Titanite*."
- Rationale: The glossary and §4.1, §4.2 and §11.4 spell the term *Titanite*.
- Source: final rulings, VA-013 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-06-077 · §6.5
- Type: correction
- Before: "Encounter levels increase from the tile adjacent to the bonfire tile towards the boss tile."
- After: "Encounter levels increase along the route, from the first encounter tile after the bonfire tile to the last encounter tile before the boss tile."
- Rationale: In a folded chain, such as the two rows of three of §6.5 step 3, two encounter tiles lie beside the bonfire tile, so 'the tile adjacent to the bonfire tile' names no single tile.
- Source: final rulings, VA-014 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-06-078 · §6.6
- Type: correction
- Before: "19. Exploration resumes from the encounter tile adjacent to the bonfire tile (see §11.5)."
- After: "19. Exploration resumes from the first encounter tile of the route (see §11.5)."
- Rationale: As VA-014: with a route of any shape the first encounter tile is fixed by the chain, not by lying beside the bonfire tile.
- Source: final rulings, VA-016 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-06-079 · §6.5, Optional Rules
- Type: correction
- Before: "- Player versus player (PvP) encounters follow the defeat of each boss (see §20.1)."
- After: "- Player versus player (PvP) encounters follow the defeat of each boss or, in campaign play, of each boss whose defeat completes a *section* (see §20.1)."
- Rationale: §20.1 and §18.1 now limit the PvP encounter in campaign play to a boss whose defeat completes a section, and §18.1 sends campaign players to this list.
- Source: final rulings, VA-034 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-06-080 · §6.5, Expansion Encounters
- Type: ruling
- Before: "Each encounter deck is built only from the encounter cards of the set that supplies the next boss. Encounter cards of other sets are not used on that leg of the exploration."
- After: "Each encounter deck is built only from the encounter cards of the set that supplies the next boss. Encounter cards of other sets are not used on that leg of the exploration. If that set supplies no encounter cards of Levels 1 to 3, as when the Asylum Demon is the mini-boss, the encounter decks of that leg are built from the core set's encounter cards."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] The Asylum Demon's set supplies only Level 4 encounter cards, so a leg that ends at it had no decks to build; the core set, which every party owns, is the fallback.
- Source: final rulings, VA-015 (`06_final_rulings.md`); lead editor's decision on a judgement call of the second verification (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

## Chapter 07 — Gameplay

### CH-07-001 · §7 Gameplay
- Type: restructure
- Before: "- Characters die whenever they cannot add a damage cube to their endurance tracker because it is already full. Upon death, a character is temporarily removed from play."
- After: "A character dies when they must place a *damage cube* on their *endurance bar* and no space is free (see §3.4). A dead character is out of play for the rest of the *encounter* and takes no *turns*."
- Rationale: The chapter's bullet fragments are rewritten as standing rules and numbered procedures in lawbook voice; the empty page-break heading is deleted and "&" is written "and" in the two section titles.
- Source: C-119, F-085, C-098, C-104, C-112, C-035

### CH-07-002 · §7.1 Objective and Loss Conditions
- Type: completion
- Before: "The players lose collectively if at any point, all characters are dead and there are no remaining sparks to revive them."
- After: "The players lose the game when the characters are required to rest at the *bonfire* and the *spark* dial shows zero."
- Rationale: A hollow death fails the encounter while other characters live and forces a rest that a zero dial cannot pay; tying the loss to the required rest also covers a total wipe.
- Source: A-002, B-012, CMP-0020, CMP-0006

### CH-07-003 · §7.1 Objective and Loss Conditions
- Type: correction
- Before: "Players may also choose to add a player versus player (PvP) encounter after the final boss is defeated to determine a single intimate victor."
- After: "If the party chose player versus player (PvP) during setup, a PvP *encounter* is fought after each boss is defeated (see §20)."
- Rationale: PvP is fought after every boss, as §20 states; "intimate victor" and the advisory "Players should agree" are removed.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-15; A-031, F-014, C-016, C-019, F-089, C-124, CMP-0009

### CH-07-004 · §7.1 Objective and Loss Conditions
- Type: correction
- Before: "If a character dies they are permanently removed from play. If all characters die, the players lose the game."
- After: "These rules apply in every encounter, including boss and *mega-boss* encounters."
- Rationale: The mega-boss sudden-death clause is not kept; death, hollowing, and the loss condition apply unchanged in the mega-boss encounter (stated in §7.1 and §7.2).
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-14; A-050, D-007, C-088

### CH-07-005 · §7.2 Death and Hollowing
- Type: correction
- Before: "Place two white cubes on the right side of the stamina bar. Hollow status reduces the total endurance of the character by two."
- After: "A hollow character has two hollow cubes on the two rightmost spaces of their endurance bar, which reduce the bar from ten usable spaces to eight."
- Rationale: One component name (hollow cube, defined here) and the ten-space endurance bar replace "white cubes" and "stamina bar"; the arithmetic is restated on the ten-space basis.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-08; C-050, B-029, C-035, B-015

### CH-07-006 · §7.2 Death and Hollowing
- Type: correction
- Before: "Hollow status is only removed by using a spark or an ember token."
- After: "Hollow is removed only by resting at the bonfire (see §11.2) or by becoming *embered* (see §7.3)."
- Rationale: The free rest after a boss costs no spark yet removes hollow; the restriction names the rest, not the spark.
- Source: A-005

### CH-07-007 · §7.2 Death and Hollowing
- Type: completion
- Before: "If all characters die before the end of an encounter, or any character with hollow status dies, then the characters are forced to immediately spend one spark and rest at the bonfire."
- After: "A hollow character who dies is *lost*. A lost character is removed from play exactly as any dead character: their souls are dropped, their ember token is discarded, and their model is placed beside the bonfire tile."
- Rationale: The lost state is defined once here, replacing the three scattered versions; the failed encounter, the forced rest, and the zero-spark loss follow from it, and §11.2 and §6.7 cite it.
- Source: CMP-0006, A-001, C-033, C-088, D-038

### CH-07-008 · §7.2 Death and Hollowing
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "If every character dies, the encounter is *failed* instead (see §7.5, Encounter Resolution); a character who adventures alone is therefore never revived hollow."
- Rationale: States the consequence of the revival branch for a lone character without altering it.
- Source: A-004 (OPTIONAL, applied)

### CH-07-009 · §7.2 Death and Hollowing
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "If the character holds the *aggro token*, move it to the living character with the highest *threat* (see §7.5, Encounter Turn Order)."
- Rationale: The aggro holder's death left enemy targeting unresolvable for the rest of the round; the rule is owned by §7.5 and applied as a step of the death procedure.
- Source: A-013

### CH-07-010 · §7.3 Embering
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Ember cards may be discarded as an action to ember a character. Remove all damage cubes. Remove all hollow cubes. Place the ember token on their character dashboard."
- After: "To ember a character: 1. Discard the ember card. 2. Remove every *damage cube* from the character's *endurance bar*. 3. Remove the character's *hollow cubes*, if present. 4. Place an *ember token* in the ember slot of the character's dashboard"
- Rationale: The manuscript's full heal is kept and stated once as a procedure; §3.5, §4.4, and §10.1 cite it.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-30; A-006

### CH-07-011 · §7.3 Embering
- Type: completion
- Before: "Characters cannot carry an ember card while embered."
- After: "If a character becomes embered while holding an ember card, that character must at once give the card to a character who is neither embered nor holding an ember card. If no such character exists, the card is returned to the tier one shop (see §6.3)."
- Rationale: Boss death embers every character at once, which left a held ember card in an undefined state.
- Source: A-007

### CH-07-012 · §7.3 Embering
- Type: style
- Before: "If suffering greater than two damage from an attack, reduce damage by one. Phantoms can be summoned before a boss encounter. Invaders will attempt to kill the characters."
- After: "When an embered character suffers three or more damage from a single attack, the character reduces that damage by one. An embered character may call a *summon* before a boss encounter (see §12.1). Embered characters draw *invaders* into their encounters (see §12.2)."
- Rationale: Present tense, the summon and invader terminology, and a definite threshold.
- Source: C-122, F-088, C-064

### CH-07-013 · §7.3 Embering
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "The ember token stays on the dashboard until the character dies (see §7.2); it is not removed by resting at the *bonfire*. When a boss is defeated, every character becomes embered without discarding an ember card (see §16.5)."
- Rationale: The token's duration and the embering at boss death were stated only in §3.5 and §16.5; the owning section states the duration and cites the boss case.
- Source: C-088; preserve list (Sparks, Boss Death)

### CH-07-014 · §7.4 Sparks
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Players begin sparks equal to six minus the number of characters. Defeating a mini-boss or main-boss provides one additional spark. Sparks can not be gained by any other means."
- After: "Each time a *mini-boss*, *main-boss*, or *mega-boss* is defeated, the party gains one spark (see §16.5), and the characters then rest at the *bonfire* without spending a spark."
- Rationale: The party, not the players, holds sparks; the mega-boss and the free rest after a boss are stated with the gain; campaign variations are delegated to §18.3.
- Source: F-086, C-103; preserve list (Sparks)

### CH-07-015 · §7.4 Sparks
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The party cannot rest at the bonfire while the spark dial shows zero. If the characters are required to rest while the dial shows zero, the players lose the game (see §7.1)."
- Rationale: A voluntary rest at zero sparks was never excluded in words.
- Source: A-002, CMP-0020

### CH-07-016 · §7.5 Encounter Start
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "Characters may skip previously cleared encounters at the cost of two stamina cubes placed in the endurance bar of each character."
- After: "Each character places one *stamina cube* on their *endurance bar* for each encounter skipped since the party last fought an encounter or rested at the bonfire, to a maximum of three."
- Rationale: Two stamina per skip left a hollow character with no free space after three skips; one per skip, capped at three, keeps the toll survivable. The party may skip only if every character has the free spaces.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-10; B-014, A-008

### CH-07-017 · §7.5 Encounter Start
- Type: style
- Before: "For example, if the first three encounters are marked cleared then the characters could skip to the fourth encounter but would all start with 6 stamina expended."
- After: "*Example.* The first three encounters are marked cleared. The party skips all three and enters the fourth encounter. Each character begins the fourth encounter with three stamina cubes on their endurance bar."
- Rationale: The example is set apart, put in the indicative, and corrected to the new cost.
- Source: C-091, F-092, F-083, C-022, C-116

### CH-07-018 · §7.5 Encounter Start
- Type: cut
- Before: "Reveal the tile's event card, if not already revealed."
- After: deleted
- Rationale: Event cards are removed from the game.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-06; A-090, D-001, CMP-0012

### CH-07-019 · §7.5 Encounter Start
- Type: cut
- Before: "Place the first player token on the character with the highest threat value."
- After: "If two or more characters share the highest threat, the players choose which of them takes the aggro token and the order in which the tied characters take their *turns*; the order is kept for the whole encounter."
- Rationale: The token is not a component and has no function; the tie rule supplies what its position would have recorded.
- Source: CMP-0011, A-017, B-030, D-018, D-040, A-014

### CH-07-020 · §7.5 Encounter Start
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Characters start with the encounter closest to the bonfire tile and advance tile-by-tile towards their ultimate goal of the boss tile."
- After: "1. Turn the tile's encounter card face up, if it is face down. 2. If the encounter card carries a black cube, the party may skip the encounter: the characters move on to the next tile and begin again from step 1."
- Rationale: The reset state (card face down, black cube kept) is read at the tile so that a cleared encounter may be skipped or fought after a rest.
- Source: CMP-0019, A-054

### CH-07-021 · §7.5 Encounter Start
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Populate the tile with enemies and terrain per encounter card directions."
- After: "Place the enemy models, scaled for the number of characters (see §8.3), on the spawn nodes and the *terrain* tokens on the terrain nodes shown on the encounter card (see §5.1 and §5.5)."
- Rationale: Trap tokens, invader tokens, and crystal lizards are added as conditional steps with cross-references to their owning sections; the chest card is dealt from the deck of the current tier.
- Source: C-084, CMP-0012, CMP-0015

### CH-07-022 · §7.5 Encounter Turn Order
- Type: correction
- Before: "After all characters have taken their turn, place the aggro token on the character who placed the most damage tokens on enemy models."
- After: "After the last character's turn of each round, the aggro token moves to the character who inflicted the most damage on enemy models during that round, counting wound tokens placed and damage recorded on an invader's health dial."
- Rationale: "Damage tokens" are wound tokens; damage to an invader is recorded on a dial and now counts; the round is named so that cumulative damage is not counted.
- Source: C-036, A-113, A-015 (OPTIONAL, applied)

### CH-07-023 · §7.5 Encounter Turn Order
- Type: style
- Before: "Continue alternating between enemies and characters until either all enemy or character models are defeated."
- After: "An encounter is fought in *rounds*. In each round every enemy model takes a turn, then every character takes a turn. Rounds continue until every enemy model has been killed or the encounter is *failed*"
- Rationale: Enemies are killed and encounters failed; the round is named, and the invader and boss cadences are cited rather than restated.
- Source: C-061, C-119, A-124

### CH-07-024 · §7.5 Encounter Resolution
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Encounters are failed if either all characters die. Encounters are failed if any character with hollow status dies."
- After: "The encounter is failed when every character has died, or when a *hollow* character dies and is *lost* (see §7.2)."
- Rationale: One sentence with its trigger; the hollow death names the lost state defined in §7.2; crystal lizards are excluded from the clear condition.
- Source: C-119, CMP-0006, CMP-0015

### CH-07-025 · §7.5 Encounter Resolution
- Type: completion
- Before: "Failed encounters flip their encounter card face-down. Failed encounters are reset when characters rest at the bonfire. Failed encounters may not be skipped once reset."
- After: "Turn the encounter card face down and remove its black cube, if it has one."
- Rationale: A cleared encounter that is later failed loses its mark and so cannot be skipped; the preserved rule is kept by the cube's removal.
- Source: CMP-0019, A-055

### CH-07-026 · §7.5 Encounter Resolution
- Type: completion
- Before: "Cleared encounters are reset when characters rest at the bonfire."
- After: "**Reset.** The state of an encounter after the characters have rested at the bonfire: its encounter card is face down, its enemy models have been removed, and its terrain tokens remain in place (see §11.5)."
- Rationale: "Reset" was used without a definition and a literal reading flipped the tiles; the cleared mark survives a reset.
- Source: A-056, CMP-0019, A-054

### CH-07-027 · §7.5 Encounter Resolution
- Type: restructure
- Before: "Event scrying allows the players to reveal a single face-down event card. They then decide to return the card face-down to its encounter tile or replace it with the top card of the same level event deck."
- After: "When an encounter containing a gravestone is cleared, the players may scry one card of their collective choice (see §5.5, Gravestones). The card is one of the following."
- Rationale: The scry procedure is stated under Encounter Resolution, where it is triggered; event scrying and the "not flipped" gravestone clause are removed.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-06; D-003, clean-up list, chapter instructions

### CH-07-028 · §7.5 Encounter Resolution
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Characters must all drop their souls on that encounter tile."
- After: "Place all of each living character's souls on the tile, in a pile separate from any souls already there (see §7.6)."
- Rationale: Only living characters still hold souls; piles stay separate so that each character reclaims their own.
- Source: A-003

### CH-07-029 · §7.5 Encounter Resolution
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Characters gain soul rewards and one treasure card each."
- After: "Award each character, including each character who died, the full soul reward for the encounter's level (see §7.6)."
- Rationale: Treasure was awarded "even if they died" but the soul reward was silent; dead characters receive both.
- Source: A-121 (OPTIONAL, applied)

### CH-07-030 · §7.5 Encounter Resolution
- Type: restructure
- Before: "If playing with mimics, determine if a mimic is encountered and fight immediately before encounter recovery is resolved."
- After: "If *mimics* are in play and the tile holds an unopened chest, the characters decide whether to open it before step 1, and a mimic that appears is fought before any reward is collected (see §13)."
- Rationale: §13 owns the mimic procedure; this chapter keeps the ordering pointer.
- Source: CMP-0014, A-060, C-089

### CH-07-031 · §7.5 Encounter Recovery
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Remove one damage cube from each character. Remove all stamina cubes from each character. Remove all condition tokens from each character."
- After: "1. Remove all condition tokens from the character (see §8.5). 2. Remove all stamina cubes from the character's endurance bar. 3. Remove one *damage cube* from the character's endurance bar."
- Rationale: One damage cube is confirmed, and conditions are removed before stamina so that frostbite cannot block the recovery.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-12; A-061, D-035, A-010

### CH-07-032 · §7.5 Encounter Recovery
- Type: completion
- Before: "All characters must rest at the bonfire if an encounter is failed: Spend one spark. Place all character models upright on the bonfire tile."
- After: "If the *spark* dial shows zero, the characters cannot rest and the players lose the game (see §7.1). Otherwise the party spends one spark and rests (see §11.2)"
- Rationale: The forced rest at zero sparks is defined; the rest procedure itself is owned by §11.2 and cited rather than repeated.
- Source: CMP-0020, C-088

### CH-07-033 · §7.5 Encounter Recovery
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "After recovery the party chooses either to advance to the next encounter tile (see §7.5, Encounter Start) or to return to the bonfire tile and rest, spending one spark (see §7.4)."
- Rationale: The voluntary return between encounters was mentioned only in the Mimics chapter.
- Source: A-063

### CH-07-034 · §7.5 Encounter Recovery
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Mark the character hollow with two white cubes on the rightmost spaces of the endurance bar."
- After: "Place two *hollow cubes* on the two rightmost spaces of the character's endurance bar (see §7.2)."
- Rationale: Component name; the revived character's dropped souls are stated to stay on the tile.
- Source: C-050, B-029, A-003

### CH-07-035 · §7.6 Souls
- Type: completion
- Before: "Upon successfully clearing an encounter that has been previously cleared, reward the reduced soul reward to each character."
- After: "**Reduced reward.** Half the full soul reward, rounded down." (table column 1 / 2 / 2 / 3)
- Rationale: The reduced reward was referenced but never quantified; the campaign column is owned by §18.5.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-05; A-053, B-008, C-068, CMP-0010

### CH-07-036 · §7.6 Souls
- Type: style
- Before: "Soul Reward per characteri"
- After: "| Encounter | Full reward per character | Reduced reward per character |"
- Rationale: Stray character removed; the HTML table is set in Markdown with "and" for "&".
- Source: C-107, C-069, CMP-0007, C-112

### CH-07-037 · §7.6 Souls
- Type: correction
- Before: "Successfully defeated invaders provide additional souls per player."
- After: "When an *invader* is killed, every character gains the invader reward in addition to the encounter reward (see §12.2)."
- Rationale: Rewards are per character, not per player, and the figures (3 and 4) are stated in the table beside the rule.
- Source: C-048, B-009

### CH-07-038 · §7.6 Souls
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Dropped souls are lost forever if the character who dropped them dies before reclaiming the souls."
- After: "If the character who dropped the souls dies before reclaiming them, the dropped souls are returned to the supply; souls dropped at the moment of that death remain on the tile."
- Rationale: A hollow death both drops souls and fails the encounter; the souls dropped by that death are not forfeited by it, and dropped souls survive resets.
- Source: A-003

### CH-07-039 · §7.6 Souls
- Type: cut
- Before: "The character who dropped them by successfully clearing or mastering the encounter on which they were dropped can reclaim dropped souls."
- After: "Only the character who dropped the souls may reclaim them, and only by surviving an encounter on that tile that ends cleared"
- Rationale: "Mastering" is undefined; only cleared and failed outcomes exist.
- Source: A-112, D-029

### CH-07-040 · §7.6 Souls
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "*Example.* The Knight dies in the second encounter, a Level 2 encounter, holding seven souls, which are placed on that tile."
- Rationale: A worked example of dropping, revival, skipping, reclaiming, and the reduced reward.
- Source: A-003, F-083

### CH-07-041 · §7.7 Treasure Rewards
- Type: correction
- Before: "Draw cards from the Tier one treasure deck before defeating the mini-boss, and Tier two treasure deck thereafter."
