- After: "takes one of the treasure cards dealt face down to that tile at setup (see §6.5 and §6.6). The dealt cards are the whole reward; no cards are drawn from the treasure decks at that time."
- Rationale: Setup pre-deals one card per character to each tile; drawing again on clearing doubled the reward.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-11; A-057, D-041, C-045

### CH-07-042 · §7.7 Treasure Rewards
- Type: restructure
- Before: "If any surviving character wishes to open a chest after an encounter ends, they must reveal the ambush card beside that encounter. See the detailed rules on mimics later in these rules for details."
- After: "If mimics are in play, a chest is opened only as the mimic rules describe, and the encounter is not resolved until the characters have decided whether to open it (see §13). A killed mimic awards two treasure cards in place of the chest's one (see §13)."
- Rationale: The five mimic bullets duplicated §13; one pointer remains.
- Source: CMP-0014, C-089, A-058, A-059, A-060, F-056, F-067

### CH-07-043 · §7.7 Treasure Rewards
- Type: correction
- Before: "If playing with the option of crystal lizards, award a Titanite Chunk card to any character who killed a crystal lizard during the encounter."
- After: "If crystal lizards are in play, a character who kills a crystal lizard takes one Titanite Scale card at once (see §14)."
- Rationale: The Iron Keep expansion supplies Titanite Scale cards; §14 owns the rule and its timing.
- Source: CMP-0015, A-096, C-056, B-032, D-028

### CH-07-044 · §7.7 Treasure Rewards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Players decide which character should receive this treasure. If the players cannot decide, the treasure will be awarded to the character who ended the encounter with the aggro token."
- After: "the players decide which character receives it. If they cannot agree, the card goes to the character who holds the aggro token when the encounter ends."
- Rationale: The tie-break is stated once for every card awarded to the party (chest, mimic, boss, Level 4); §5.5 and §16.5 cite it. For the per-character encounter reward the aggro holder decides the assignment.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-29; C-087, D-037, C-020, C-122, F-088

### CH-07-045 · §7.7 Treasure Rewards
- Type: restructure
- Before: "If a boss was defeated the characters are awarded one of the treasure cards marked with the defeated boss's icon drawn at random."
- After: "When a boss is defeated, the party is awarded one of that boss's treasure cards, drawn at random (see §16.5). The treasure reward of a Level 4 encounter is the mega-boss treasure card dealt to its tile (see §6.7)."
- Rationale: Boss death is owned by §16.5 and the Level 4 reward by §6.7; one sentence and a cross-reference each.
- Source: CMP-0012, C-084

### CH-07-046 · §7 Gameplay (style sweep)
- Type: style
- Before: "*failed:*" / "*clear an*" / "*C*haracters" / "set-up" / "Tier one" / "condition marker" / "will be awarded"
- After: style sweep
- Rationale: Italics confined to glossary headwords at first use per section with punctuation outside; "setup"; lower-case "tier one"; "player versus player (PvP)" once; words for numbers below ten; serial commas; present tense; the actor policy (players decide, characters act, the party holds sparks); "(see §N.M)" for every prose cross-reference.
- Source: C-027, C-028, C-029, C-030, C-045, C-059, C-060, C-062, C-063, C-116, C-122, F-086, F-088, C-019, C-020, F-089, C-124, C-084

### CH-07-047 · §7 Gameplay (OPTIONAL findings skipped)
- Type: clarification
- Before: n/a
- After: n/a
- Rationale: A-047 (spark maximum in the standard game): would add a cap the manuscript does not have; the campaign cap is owned by §18.3. A-062 (one repeat per rest): alters the preserved repeat-for-souls mechanic. A-022 (boss character order), A-034 (PvP), A-105 (campaign players), D-039 (taunt), A-125 (glossary note on taunt), B-004 (scaling), B-035 (mimic stamina), A-104 (Demon Hunting): owned by §16, §20, §18.4, §3.1 and §21, §8.3, §13, and §19 respectively; not applied here.
- Source: A-047, A-062, A-022, A-034, A-105, D-039, A-125, B-004, B-035, A-104

### CH-07-048 · §7 Gameplay (findings owned by other chapters)
- Type: clarification
- Before: n/a
- After: n/a
- Rationale: Not applied in this chapter because another section owns the rule: A-019 and A-122 (§16.1, §15.2), B-015 (§3.4), B-028 and C-077 (§2.8), A-010 (frostbite wording, §8.5; the recovery order is applied here), A-123 (§6.7 and §11.5; no change), A-124 (no change), C-103 and C-104 (headings outside this chapter).
- Source: A-019, A-122, B-015, B-028, C-077, A-010, A-123, A-124, C-103, C-104

### CH-07-049 · §7.5 Encounters (Encounter Resolution)
- Type: correction
- Before: "When the characters rest at the bonfire, every encounter is reset (see §11.5)."
- After: "… every encounter is reset (see §11.5), except a cleared Level 4 encounter, which is not fought again (see §6.7)."
- Rationale: CMP-0023 (approved) and the F.A.Q. state that a cleared Level 4 encounter does not reset; A-123's alternative is not adopted. Aligns §7.5 with §6.7 (lead editor, at integration).
- Source: CMP-0023, A-123

### CH-07-050 · §7.6 Souls
- Type: correction
- Before: "Souls cannot be transferred between characters and are spent only while resting at the bonfire (see §11.3 and §11.4)."
- After: "Souls are spent while resting at the bonfire (see §11.3 and §11.4). The one exception is the cost of calling a summon at the fog gate, which is paid before a boss encounter (see §12.1)."
- Rationale: The unconditional restriction forbade the summon cost that §11.3 and §12.1 allow; A-100 resolves the manuscript's conflict by naming the fog-gate summon as the one exception.
- Source: A-100, review of PR #2

### CH-07-051 · §7.5 Encounters (Encounter Resolution); §7.6 Souls
- Type: correction
- Before: "When an encounter whose encounter card carries no black cube is cleared: …" (full reward and treasure keyed to the black cube)
- After: "An encounter is cleared for the first time while the treasure cards dealt to its tile at setup still lie on the tile … an encounter that was cleared, later failed, and then cleared again is a repeated clear, and step 6 marks it cleared once more."
- Rationale: A failed encounter loses its black cube (CMP-0019), so keying rewards to the cube let a cleared-then-failed encounter pay the full reward and treasure a second time, with no treasure cards left to award. The reward history is now kept by the treasure cards themselves; the cube records only the permission to skip.
- Source: CMP-0019, A-053, review of PR #2

### CH-07-052 · §7.6 Souls
- Type: correction
- Before: "The rewards of the standard game are as follows; campaign play uses its own table (see §18.5)."
- After: "The rewards are as follows, and campaign play uses the same rewards (see §18.5)."
- Rationale: Under Gate 1 OQ-21 campaign play keeps the standard soul rewards, so no separate campaign table exists (see CH-18-038).
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-21

### CH-07-053 · §7.6 Souls (Dropped Souls)
- Type: correction
- Before: "the character retrieves them during Encounter Resolution (see §7.5, Encounter Resolution)."
- After: "the character retrieves them during Encounter Resolution (see §7.5, Encounter Resolution). The one exception is the setup that follows the defeat of a boss, which returns all dropped souls still on the tiles to the characters who dropped them (see §6.6; §6.7)."
- Rationale: §6.6 step 1 and §6.7 step 1 return dropped souls after a boss; "only" excluded that case.
- Source: QA-003 (example and consistency verification)

### CH-07-054 · §7.5 Encounters (Aggro)
- Type: correction
- Before: "In boss encounters the aggro token moves after any damage to the boss (see §16.4)."
- After: "In boss encounters the aggro token moves at the end of the turn of each character who inflicted damage on the boss during that turn (see §16.4)."
- Rationale: §16.4 moves the token at the end of the character's turn, not at the moment of the damage.
- Source: QA-005 (example and consistency verification)

### CH-07-055 · §7.4 Sparks
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Each time a *mini-boss*, *main-boss*, or *mega-boss* is defeated, the party gains one spark (see §16.5)"
- After: "Each time a *mini-boss*, *main-boss*, or *mega-boss* is defeated and the game does not end, the party gains one spark (see §16.5)"
- Rationale: §16.5 grants no spark and no rest after the final boss.
- Source: QA observation (example and consistency verification)

### CH-07-056 · §7.2
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**Hollow cube.** One of eight cubes of a colour distinct from the damage, stamina, and stat cubes. Two *hollow cubes* block the two rightmost spaces of a hollow character's endurance bar."
- After: "**Hollow cube.** A white cube placed on an endurance bar. Two *hollow cubes* block the two rightmost spaces of a hollow character's endurance bar. Hollow cubes are taken from the white cubes that are not in use as *stat cubes* (see §2.8)."
- Rationale: Definition follows the component ruling.
- Source: final rulings, core-E03 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As core-E02.

### CH-07-057 · §7.5, Encounter Resolution
- Type: clarification
- Before: "3. Give each character, including each character who died, one of the treasure cards dealt to the tile (see §7.7)."
- After: "3. Award the treasure cards dealt to the tile, one to each character, including each character who died; where a single card was dealt to the tile, award it to the party (see §7.7)."
- Rationale: Step 3 now also fits the Level 4 encounter, to which one card is dealt for the whole party (§6.7).
- Source: final rulings, core-E15 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §6.7 step 5; §7.7 final paragraph.

### CH-07-058 · §7.7
- Type: clarification
- Before: "The players decide which character takes which card; if they cannot agree, the character holding the *aggro token* decides."
- After: "The cards are turned face up, and the players decide which character takes which card; if they cannot agree, the character holding the *aggro token* decides."
- Rationale: States that the dealt cards are revealed before they are shared out.
- Source: final rulings, core-E16 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Gate 1 OQ-11 and OQ-29.

### CH-07-059 · §7.7
- Type: correction
- Before: "A treasure card a character receives is placed in that character's inventory (see §4.1)."
- After: "A treasure card a character receives is placed in that character's inventory (see §3.2)."
- Rationale: The inventory is defined in §3.2, not §4.1.
- Source: final rulings, core-E17 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §3.2, Inventory.

### CH-07-060 · §7.2
- Type: clarification
- Before: "2. Discard the character's *ember token*, if present (see §7.3)."
- After: "2. Remove the character's *ember token* from the dashboard, if present, and place it beside the dashboard (see §7.3)."
- Rationale: Matches §3.5: the token is the character's own and returns to its place beside the dashboard.
- Source: final rulings, core-E21 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §3.5, Ember.

### CH-07-061 · §7.2
- Type: clarification
- Before: "their souls are dropped, their ember token is discarded, and their model is placed beside the bonfire tile"
- After: "their souls are dropped, their ember token is removed, and their model is placed beside the bonfire tile"
- Rationale: Same wording as the death procedure.
- Source: final rulings, core-E22 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §3.5, Ember.

### CH-07-062 · §7.3
- Type: completion
- Before: "1. Discard the ember card."
- After: "1. Return the ember card to the game box. It takes no further part in the game."
- Rationale: States where a consumed ember card goes; the supply of one per character at setup is the limit on embering by card.
- Source: final rulings, core-E23 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §6.3 (one ember card per character, never restocked; A-046); chapter 04 and chapter 07 issues lists.

### CH-07-063 · §7.5, Encounter Resolution
- Type: ruling
- Before: "5. If the tile holds a *gravestone* {{icon:ast-0017-gravestone}}, the players may *scry* one card."
- After: "5. The players may *scry* one card for each *gravestone* {{icon:ast-0017-gravestone}} on the tile."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Keeps the resolution step in line with §5.5.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E02 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As combat-E01.

### CH-07-064 · §7.5, Encounter Resolution
- Type: ruling
- Before: "When an encounter containing a gravestone is cleared, the players may scry one card of their collective choice (see §5.5, Gravestones). The card is one of the following."
- After: "When an encounter is cleared, the players may scry one card of their collective choice for each gravestone on its tile (see §5.5, Gravestones). The scries are resolved one at a time, and each card scried is one of the following."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Keeps the scry rule in line with §5.5 and fixes the order of several scries.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E03 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As combat-E01.

### CH-07-065 · §7.5, Encounter Turn Order
- Type: clarification
- Before: "the model with the most *wound tokens* takes its turn first."
- After: "the model whose *wound tokens* have the greatest total value takes its turn first."
- Rationale: Wound tokens have values of 1, 3 and 5, so their number is not the damage.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E31 (`06_final_rulings.md`); This book, §2.8 and §8.2.

### CH-07-066 · §7.5, Encounter Turn Order
- Type: clarification
- Before: "counting wound tokens placed and damage recorded on an invader's health dial."
- After: "counting the total value of the wound tokens placed and the damage recorded on an invader's health dial."
- Rationale: As combat-E31.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E32 (`06_final_rulings.md`); This book, §2.8 and §8.2.

### CH-07-067 · §7.7
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Whenever a treasure card is awarded to the party rather than to a named character (a chest's card, a mimic's cards, a boss treasure card, or the *mega-boss* treasure card of a Level 4 encounter), the players decide which character receives it. If they cannot agree, the card goes to the character who holds the aggro token when the encounter ends."
- After: "Whenever a treasure card is awarded to the party rather than to a named character (a chest's card, a mimic's cards, an invader's treasure card, a Titanite Scale card, a boss treasure card, or the *mega-boss* treasure card of a Level 4 encounter), the players decide which character receives it. If they cannot agree, the card goes to the character who holds the aggro token when the card is awarded."
- Rationale: The list now names every card awarded to the party, and 'when the card is awarded' covers a card gained during an encounter as well as one gained at its end.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E12 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Gate 1 OQ-29; §12.2, Invader Rewards and §14 of this book.

### CH-07-068 · §7.7
- Type: ruling
- Before: "If crystal lizards are in play, a character who kills a crystal lizard takes one Titanite Scale card at once (see §14). A killed invader awards its treasure (see §12.2)."
- After: "If crystal lizards are in play, a killed crystal lizard awards the party one Titanite Scale card at once (see §14). A killed invader awards its treasure card at once (see §12.2)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Keeps §7.7 in step with §14: the Titanite Scale card is awarded to the party, whatever killed the crystal lizard.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E13 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official Iron Keep booklet: 'add one Titanite Scale card to the inventory', the party's shared inventory (DS-IronKeep-Rules-Web.txt lines 125-127).

### CH-07-069 · §7.4
- Type: correction
- Before: "In the standard game sparks cannot be gained in any other way; campaign play varies these rules (see §18.3)."
- After: "In the standard game sparks are gained in no other way, except when a player leaves the game (see §18.4); campaign play varies these rules (see §18.3)."
- Rationale: §18.4 gives the party one spark when a player leaves, and applies to a standard game in progress as well as to a campaign.
- Source: final rulings, VA-017 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-07-070 · §7.5
- Type: correction
- Before: "The characters begin at the encounter tile adjacent to the *bonfire* tile and advance one tile at a time towards the boss tile."
- After: "The characters begin at the first encounter tile of the route, the tile that follows the *bonfire* tile in the chain (see §6.5), and advance one tile at a time along the route towards the boss tile."
- Rationale: As VA-014: with a route of any shape two encounter tiles may lie beside the bonfire tile.
- Source: final rulings, VA-018 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-07-071 · §7.5, Encounter Start
- Type: correction
- Before: "6. If the encounter card shows the trap icon, deal *trap tokens* (see §5.5, Traps)."
- After: "6. If the encounter card shows the trap icon and the encounter is set up for the first time, deal *trap tokens* (see §5.5, Traps)."
- Rationale: §5.5, Traps deals trap tokens only the first time the encounter is set up and never again after a reset; step 9 already carries the same limit for chests.
- Source: final rulings, VA-019 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-07-072 · §7.5, Encounter Start
- Type: correction
- Before: "on the character with the highest *threat* {{icon:ast-0026-threat-level}}."
- After: "on the character with the highest *threat* {{icon:ast-0059-taunt}}."
- Rationale: §3.1 prints a character's threat with the Taunt icon of the dashboard; ast-0026 is the threat icon of enemy, boss and phantom data cards (the asset manifest entry for §7.5 moves with it).
- Source: final rulings, VA-020 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-07-073 · §7.5, Encounter Recovery
- Type: correction
- Before: "Recovery does not ready the *estus flask*, *luck*, or *heroic action* tokens, and does not remove the hollow cubes of a character who was already hollow; only resting at the bonfire does so (see §11.2)."
- After: "Recovery does not ready the *estus flask*, *luck*, or *heroic action* tokens, which only resting at the bonfire readies (see §11.2). It does not remove the hollow cubes of a character who was already hollow, which are removed only by resting at the bonfire or by becoming *embered* (see §7.2)."
- Rationale: §7.2 and §7.3 step 3 remove hollow cubes when a character becomes embered as well as at a rest.
- Source: final rulings, VA-021 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-07-074 · §7.1
- Type: correction
- Before: "If the party chose player versus player (PvP) during setup, a PvP *encounter* is fought after each boss is defeated (see §20)."
- After: "If the party chose player versus player (PvP) during setup, a PvP *encounter* is fought after each boss is defeated or, in campaign play, after each boss whose defeat completes a *section* (see §20.1; §18.1, Player versus Player Encounters)."
- Rationale: As VA-034: §20.1 and §18.1 limit the PvP encounter in campaign play.
- Source: final rulings, VA-035 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

## Chapter 11 — Returning to the Bonfire

### CH-11-001 · §11 Returning to the Bonfire
- Type: restructure
- Before: "# RETURNING TO THE BONFIRE" / "## Resting at The Bonfire" / "### Stat Tier Costs" / "### Shop Rules" / "### Shop Price Table" and four empty headings used as page breaks
- After: "# 11. Returning to the Bonfire" with H2s 11.1 The Bonfire, 11.2 Resting, 11.3 The Firekeeper, 11.4 Blacksmith Andre, 11.5 Resuming Exploration; the empty headings are deleted
- Rationale: Headings follow the section map. Page breaks belong to layout. The H3s under §11.4 (Shop Tiers, Purchases, Sales, Prices, Upgrades) do not repeat any heading title of §18.6.
- Source: C-098, C-104, C-103, C-042; brief §4

### CH-11-002 · §11 Returning to the Bonfire
- Type: style
- Before: "The *bonfire* serves as a safe haven in an otherwise treacherous world. When the characters arrive, there are two strangers, who wish to help the characters on their journey. The first of which is the Firekeeper, a mysterious blindfolded woman"
- After: "The *bonfire* is a safe haven in a treacherous world. Two strangers wait beside it. The *Firekeeper*, blindfolded and silent, strengthens the characters in exchange for *souls*."
- Rationale: The 93-word flavour paragraph becomes a 38-word chapter intro; the rule it carried (Andre upgrades equipment "if they bring him suitable materials") moves to §11.4.
- Source: F-019, F-123, C-121

### CH-11-003 · §11.1 The Bonfire
- Type: cut
- Before: "Resting at the *bonfire* is paramount to restoring the characters' strength and keeping them from being *lost* to the *hollow.* ... *Sparks* are the most precious resource in this game"
- After: deleted
- Rationale: Strategy advice and flavour inside rules text; "lost to the hollow" used a keyword that was undefined.
- Source: F-020, C-121, C-033, C-027

### CH-11-004 · §11.1 The Bonfire
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The bonfire tile has no nodes, and no *encounter* is fought on it. A model on the bonfire tile stands anywhere on the tile."
- Rationale: §5.1 cites this section for the bonfire tile; the tile and the two figures who wait there are now stated as rules, with the visits limited to a rest.
- Source: F-020; dependency from §2.8 and §5.1; chapter instructions

### CH-11-005 · §11.1 The Bonfire
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "After an encounter is *cleared*, the party may return to the bonfire tile instead of advancing to the next tile (see §7.5, Encounter Recovery)."
- Rationale: The chapter never said when the party returns. The three cases (voluntary return after a cleared encounter, forced return after a failed encounter or a hollow character's death, return after a boss) are listed, each citing its owning section.
- Source: A-063, C-088, CMP-0006

### CH-11-006 · §11.1 The Bonfire
- Type: clarification
- Before: "The characters must collectively spend one *spark to* visit the *bonfire* to rest, improve stats, upgrade equipment, and shop."
- After: "Returning to the bonfire tile costs nothing. The party spends a *spark* only when it rests (see §11.2)."
- Rationale: The spark pays for the rest, not for standing on the tile; the sentence also italicised a fragment ("spark to"). The models are placed on the tile without passing back through the encounter tiles.
- Source: F-020, C-029; chapter instructions

### CH-11-007 · §11.2 Resting
- Type: restructure
- Before: "Resting at the *bonfire* costs a *spark* and allows characters the following. - Remove all damage and stamina from the character dashboards."
- After: "The party rests as follows. 1. Turn the spark dial down by one, unless the rest costs no spark."
- Rationale: The rest is a procedure followed at the table and is set as nine numbered steps, one action each, in the order in which they are carried out.
- Source: C-119, C-120

### CH-11-008 · §11.2 Resting
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "When an *encounter* is *failed*, the party must rest; if the spark dial shows zero, the party cannot rest and the players lose the game (see §7.1)."
- Rationale: The section that owns the rest now states when the party may rest, when it must, and what follows a forced rest with no sparks; the loss condition itself is owned by §7.1.
- Source: CMP-0006, C-088

### CH-11-009 · §11.2 Resting
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Characters rest at the *bonfire* without needing to spend a *spark* after defeating a boss or completing player-versus-player (PvP)."
- After: "A rest that follows the defeat of a boss costs no spark (see §7.4). If a player versus player (PvP) encounter follows the boss, that free rest is taken after the PvP encounter (see §20.9)."
- Rationale: One free rest follows each boss; with PvP in play it follows the PvP encounter. The campaign rule (free only after a section is completed) is cited from §18.3.
- Source: C-060, A-005; Gate 1 OQ-15

### CH-11-010 · §11.2 Resting
- Type: correction
- Before: "Resting at the *bonfire* also revives *lost* characters who have been removed from play due to dying while *hollow.*"
- After: "Revive each dead or *lost* character: stand the character's model upright on the bonfire tile (see §7.2)."
- Rationale: "Lost" is defined once, in §7.2; the rest revives every dead character, lost or not, and restores all characters alike.
- Source: A-001, D-038, C-033, C-088, C-027, CMP-0006

### CH-11-011 · §11.2 Resting
- Type: correction
- Before: "Remove all *hollow* cubes from the character dashboards. - Flip *luck*, *estus flask*, and *heroic action* tokens face-up."
- After: "Remove every *hollow cube* from each character's endurance bar (see §7.2)." / "Flip each character's *heroic action*, *luck*, and *estus flask* tokens to the ready side (see §3.5)."
- Rationale: Canonical component names; tokens have a ready side and a used side. Hollow is removed by the rest whether or not a spark was spent.
- Source: C-050, C-051, A-005

### CH-11-012 · §11.2 Resting
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Remove every *condition* token from each character (see §8.5)."
- Rationale: Conditions are removed by Encounter Recovery only after a cleared encounter; after a failed encounter nothing removed them.
- Source: chapter instructions; §7.5, Encounter Recovery

### CH-11-013 · §11.2 Resting
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Resting neither places nor removes an *ember token*. An *embered* character stays embered through the rest"
- Rationale: The manuscript removes the ember token only at death; the rest procedure now says so in the place a reader looks for it.
- Source: chapter instructions; §7.3

### CH-11-014 · §11.2 Resting
- Type: completion
- Before: "Shops are only restocked after a boss is defeated."
- After: "If the *shop* has not been restocked since a boss was last defeated, restock it (see §6.3, Restocking After a Boss)."
- Rationale: The restock is tied to the defeat of a boss and never to a rest; the step does nothing once the setup after a boss has restocked the shop. The restock procedure, with the sweep of unsold cards, is owned by §6.3.
- Source: A-046; Gate 1 OQ-27

### CH-11-015 · §11.2 Resting
- Type: completion
- Before: "Pay the Firekeeper *souls* to increase character stats. - Purchase and/or sell treasure cards at Andre's Shop."
- After: "The characters may visit the *Firekeeper* (see §11.3) and Blacksmith Andre (see §11.4), in either order and any number of times."
- Rationale: The order of the two visits was unstated. The freedom to rearrange and trade cards while resting is cited from §3.2.
- Source: C-043, C-119; chapter instructions

### CH-11-016 · §11.2 Resting
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "*Example.* The Knight, the Herald, and the Assassin clear the second encounter, a Level 2 encounter. ... The party returns to the bonfire tile and rests. The spark dial is turned down to two."
- Rationale: A worked example of a full rest with two purchases and one sale.
- Source: chapter instructions

### CH-11-017 · §11.3 The Firekeeper
- Type: restructure
- Before: "All character stats start at their base tier and must be increased to the next tier sequentially. Each stat tier requires payment for all lesser stat tiers."
- After: "Each purchase raises one of the character's four stats by one *stat tier* (see §3.3). Stat tiers are bought in sequence"
- Rationale: The rule is stated as one purchase for one tier, any number of purchases per rest; "tier" in the stat sense is always "stat tier".
- Source: C-045, C-119

### CH-11-018 · §11.3 The Firekeeper
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "| Tier Four  | **8**           |"
- After: "| Stat tier four | 10 |"
- Rationale: Stat tier four cost the same as stat tier three; the cost is raised to 10 souls, keeping the ratio to the campaign table.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-09; B-007, C-071, A-067, D-005

### CH-11-019 · §11.3 The Firekeeper
- Type: restructure
- Before: "If a character reaches stat tier four they mark this on their character dashboard by replacing the tier three white cube with a red cube. Tier four stats are all considered 5 greater than the tier three stat value."
- After: "the value of a stat at stat tier four and the way that tier is marked are stated there (see §3.3)"
- Rationale: §3.3 owns the stat tier four value and its marker; this section cites it and does not restate it.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-09; B-017, D-005, C-116; chapter 03 issues list

### CH-11-020 · §11.3 The Firekeeper
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Characters may only spend *souls* to improve character stats when resting at the *bonfire*."
- After: "Souls cannot be pooled or transferred between characters (see §7.6). Outside a rest, souls are spent only to call a *summon* at the *fog gate* (see §12.1)."
- Rationale: The general restriction contradicted the summoning cost; the one exception is named and each character is stated to pay with their own souls.
- Source: A-100

### CH-11-021 · §11.3 The Firekeeper
- Type: style
- Before: "For example, to increase a character's strength (STR) stat from base tier to tier two, they would first have to pay two souls to reach tier one before paying four additional souls to reach tier two."
- After: "*Example.* The Herald's strength stat cube is in the base space, and the Herald holds six souls. During a rest the Herald pays the Firekeeper two souls for stat tier one and then four souls for stat tier two."
- Rationale: The manuscript's example is kept and set as a separate worked example with a named character and concrete numbers.
- Source: F-083, C-092

### CH-11-022 · §11.4 Blacksmith Andre
- Type: completion
- Before: "Only when the characters spend a spark at the bonfire to rest, they may visit Blacksmith Andre to upgrade equipment or visit Andre's Shop."
- After: "The tier two shop opens when the *mini-boss* is defeated (see §6.6); in campaign play it opens when the *bonfire ascetic* is burnt (see §18.7)."
- Rationale: The section now states the two shops, when each opens, and that a visit requires a rest (free or paid); stocking and restocking are cited from §6.3.
- Source: C-043, A-005, A-046; Gate 1 OQ-27

### CH-11-023 · §11.4 Blacksmith Andre
- Type: restructure
- Before: "Boss treasures may be sold to Andre but never purchased."
- After: "A card in the tier one shop costs two souls, and a card in the tier two shop costs four souls." / "*Boss treasure* and *legendary treasure* are not placed in the shop and cannot be bought (see §6.2)."
- Rationale: The shop rules are rewritten from bullet fragments into standing rules under Purchases and Sales, each with an actor and an outcome; a bought card goes to the buyer's inventory.
- Source: C-119, A-076, C-116

### CH-11-024 · §11.4 Blacksmith Andre
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Each character may only carry one *ember* card at a time."
- After: "An *ember card* is a tier one shop card and costs two souls. A character who is not permitted to carry an ember card cannot buy one (see §4.4)."
- Rationale: §4.4 owns the limits on carrying ember cards; this section states the price, which §4.4 cites, and does not restate the limits.
- Source: A-007; chapter 04 issues list

### CH-11-025 · §11.4 Blacksmith Andre
- Type: correction
- Before: "Sold items are placed at the bottom of their respective treasure deck."
- After: "A sold tier one or tier two treasure card is placed face down at the bottom of the treasure deck of its tier (see §6.2)." / "A sold boss treasure card is returned to the game box."
- Rationale: "Item" becomes "treasure card". Boss treasure and ember cards belong to no treasure deck, so each is given a destination (the game box; the tier one shop).
- Source: C-054, A-077; chapter 06 issues list

### CH-11-026 · §11.4 Blacksmith Andre
- Type: correction
- Before: "Titanite upgrades (i.e.: Titanite Shards) attached to sold weapons are returned to the bottom of the tier one treasure deck."
- After: "When a *weapon* with *Titanite* attached is sold, the Titanite goes with the weapon to the bottom of the treasure deck of the weapon's tier"
- Rationale: Titanite follows its weapon, which may be a tier two card; the rule is owned by §4.2 and cited. The misused "i.e." is removed.
- Source: A-074, C-096

### CH-11-027 · §11.4 Blacksmith Andre
- Type: restructure
- Before: "Weapon upgrades aside from Titanite upgrades (i.e.: gems) that are attached to sold weapons are returned to the character. - Armour upgrades attached to sold armour are returned to the character."
- After: "every other *upgrade card* attached to a sold card returns to the character's inventory (see §4.2, Titanite)"
- Rationale: Two bullets state one rule, which §4.2 owns; the destination is named as the inventory.
- Source: C-096, C-119; chapter 04 issues list

### CH-11-028 · §11.4 Blacksmith Andre
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Starting equipment cannot be sold."
- After: "A character may sell to Andre any treasure card they hold, whether it is *equipped*, in the *back-up slot*, or in the inventory. *Starting equipment* cannot be sold."
- Rationale: The rule said what cannot be sold but not what may be; the sale price in prose (one soul; six for boss treasure) now agrees with the table.
- Source: C-070, C-119

### CH-11-029 · §11.4 Blacksmith Andre
- Type: correction
- Before: "| Boss Treasure | **N/A**       | **6**      |"
- After: "| *Boss treasure* card | - | 6 |" followed by "Boss treasure cannot be purchased. Campaign play uses its own prices (see §18.6, Shop Price Table)."
- Rationale: One null marker in both price tables; the campaign sell value is owned by §18.6 and cited. Row labels are lower case and name the card, not the shop, because reward cards are sold at the same value.
- Source: C-070, C-045, C-103

### CH-11-030 · §11.4 Blacksmith Andre
- Type: restructure
- Before: "Characters may attach any weapon upgrade cards from their inventory to equipped weapon cards and armour upgrade cards to equipped armour. However, this is limited by the upgrade slots of the equipment being upgraded."
- After: "The *upgrade slots* that limit the number of attached cards, the *stat requirements* of upgrade cards, the permanence of Titanite, and the attaching of *upgrade spells* are stated with the upgrade rules (see §4.2)."
- Rationale: §4.2 owns the upgrade limits; this section states only what Andre does and when. The copied official phrasing and the lower-case sentence start are gone.
- Source: F-124, C-114; chapter 04 issues list

### CH-11-031 · §11.4 Blacksmith Andre
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "Andre also removes an attached upgrade card other than Titanite and returns it to the character's inventory. Andre charges no souls for either service"
- Rationale: §4.2 states that Andre removes upgrade cards; the manuscript names no cost for either service, and the chapter says so.
- Source: §4.2 dependency; issues list

### CH-11-032 · §11.5 Resuming Exploration
- Type: restructure
- Before: "Once the characters are restored to strength and no longer wish to spend souls they are nearly ready to resume their exploration of encounters."
- After: "The encounters are reset as follows. 1. Turn every face-up *encounter card* face down."
- Rationale: A procedure written as narrative becomes five numbered steps; conversational filler ("nearly ready", "no longer wish") is cut.
- Source: F-021, C-120, C-123

### CH-11-033 · §11.5 Resuming Exploration
- Type: correction
- Before: "Prior to setting out, flip all cleared and failed encounter tiles face-down to reset them."
- After: "Turn every face-up *encounter card* face down." / "Leave the black cube on each encounter card that carries one (see §7.5, Encounter Resolution)."
- Rationale: Encounter cards are turned, not the double-sided tiles. A cleared encounter keeps its black cube through the reset and a failed one has none.
- Source: A-056, CMP-0019

### CH-11-034 · §11.5 Resuming Exploration
- Type: correction
- Before: "If the encounter card on the tile is face-down (*cleared* or *failed),* they must flip the card face-up and proceed with fighting the enemies of that encounter."
- After: "When the characters enter a tile whose encounter card carries a black cube, the party may skip the encounter or fight it again."
- Rationale: "Must fight" contradicted the skip rule. A cleared encounter may be skipped or fought again for the reduced reward; an encounter with no black cube must be fought. The skip cost is owned by §7.5 and cited, not restated.
- Source: A-054, CMP-0019, C-027; Gate 1 OQ-10 (logged with §7.5)

### CH-11-035 · §11.5 Resuming Exploration
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Do not move terrain (chests, gravestones, barrels, and traps) when resetting the encounter tiles."
- After: "Leave every *terrain* token and model on its node, and restore each kind of terrain as its rules direct (see §5.5)."
- Rationale: §5.5 owns what happens to each kind of terrain at a reset (barrels restored, trap tokens turned face down); this step cites it.
- Source: CMP-0019; cut/simplify list

### CH-11-036 · §11.5 Resuming Exploration
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A cleared *Level 4 encounter* is the exception to the reset: it is not reset and is not fought again (see §6.7, Level 4 Encounters)."
- Rationale: The reset now states what it leaves alone: dropped souls stay on their tiles, a cleared Level 4 encounter is not reset, a failed boss encounter follows §15.2, and an invader that has appeared does not return.
- Source: CMP-0019; chapter 07 issues list (integration note); §7.6, §12.2

### CH-11-037 · §11.5 Resuming Exploration
- Type: correction
- Before: "At this point characters should move their models to the closest encounter tile to the bonfire tile."
- After: "If the *fog gate* token is on the bonfire tile, the party may instead pass through it to the boss encounter without entering any encounter tile (see §15.1)."
- Rationale: "Should" made a mandatory step optional, and the sentence ignored the fog gate shortcut. The characters enter the adjacent tile, where Encounter Start (§7.5) applies, or use the shortcut.
- Source: C-019, A-054, CMP-0019

### CH-11-038 · §11.5 Resuming Exploration
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "*Example.* The Knight, the Herald, and the Assassin have rested after clearing the first and second encounters, and they set out again. The first and second encounter cards carry black cubes, and the party skips both encounters."
- Rationale: A worked example of a reset, two skipped encounters, and dropped souls left on their tile, continuing the example of §11.2.
- Source: CMP-0019; Gate 1 OQ-10

### CH-11-039 · §11 Returning to the Bonfire
- Type: style
- Before: "The Bonfire" mid-sentence; "Andre's Shop"; "tier two" for a stat tier; "face-up" for tokens; "5 greater"; curly quotes; "*hollow.*", "*failed),*"; "characters cannot equip" at a sentence start
- After: "the *bonfire*"; "the shop"; "stat tier one"; "ready side"; numerals by the house rule; straight quotes; italics on the keyword only, at first use in each H2 section
- Rationale: Style sweep: terminology, italics policy, numerals, quotation marks, and capitalisation, with no change of meaning. "Firekeeper" is unchanged.
- Source: C-030, C-027, C-029, C-042, C-043, C-044, C-045, C-051, C-060, C-111, C-114, C-116

### CH-11-040 · §11 Returning to the Bonfire
- Type: clarification
- Before: findings not applied in this chapter
- After: not applied
- Rationale: D-011 (OPTIONAL; pay one soul to ready luck): skipped, because the rest readies the luck token. C-092 (OPTIONAL; campaign cost sentence): belongs to §18.6. A-077 (OPTIONAL): applied to boss treasure only; legendary treasure is a tier two card with a deck to return to. A-104 (Demon Hunting, Section One): owned by §19.6. B-016 (stat scaling threshold): owned by §10.5. The alternative stat tier four costs of A-067 (16) and D-005 (12) and D-005's flat value of 40 are superseded by Gate 1 OQ-09.
- Source: D-011, C-092, A-077, A-104, B-016, A-067, D-005

### CH-11-041 · §11.2
- Type: ruling
- Before: "7. If the *shop* has not been restocked since a boss was last defeated, restock it (see §6.3, Restocking After a Boss).
8. The characters may visit the *Firekeeper* (see §11.3) and Blacksmith Andre (see §11.4), in either order and any number of times.
9. *Reset* every encounter (see §11.5)."
- After: "7. The characters may visit the *Firekeeper* (see §11.3) and Blacksmith Andre (see §11.4), in either order and any number of times.
8. *Reset* every encounter (see §11.5)."
- Rationale: The conditional restock never takes effect: the setup after a boss (§6.6, §6.7) and the setup of a campaign section (§18.6) restock before the rest begins.
- Source: final rulings, core-E09 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §6.6 step 16, §6.7 step 7, §18.6; chapter 11 issues list, item 7.

### CH-11-042 · §11.2
- Type: ruling
- Before: "Resting does not restock the shop except as step 7 directs. Cards bought from the shop are not replaced, and unsold cards stay in the shop, until the shop is next restocked (see §6.3, Restocking After a Boss). In campaign play, step 7 applies when a section has been completed rather than when a boss has been defeated (see §18.6, Blacksmith Andre's Shop)."
- After: "Resting does not restock the *shop*. Cards bought from the shop are not replaced, and unsold cards stay in the shop, until the shop is next restocked during the setup that follows a boss's defeat (see §6.3, Restocking After a Boss). In campaign play the shop is restocked during the setup that follows the completion of a section (see §18.6, Blacksmith Andre's Shop)."
- Rationale: Follows the deletion of step 7.
- Source: final rulings, core-E10 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As core-E09.

### CH-11-043 · §11.4, Sales
- Type: ruling
- Before: "When a *weapon* with *Titanite* attached is sold, the Titanite goes with the weapon to the bottom of the treasure deck of the weapon's tier, and every other *upgrade card* attached to a sold card returns to the character's inventory (see §4.2, Titanite). The sell value of the weapon is paid once; attached Titanite adds nothing to it."
- After: "When a *weapon* with *Titanite* attached is sold, the Titanite is sold with it, and every other *upgrade card* attached to a sold card returns to the character's inventory (see §4.2, Titanite). The sell value of the weapon is paid once; attached Titanite adds nothing to it. The weapon and the Titanite are separated, and each goes to the place stated for a sold card of its kind."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] As core-E31.
- Source: final rulings, core-E32 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As core-E31.

### CH-11-044 · §11.1
- Type: ruling
- Before: "- After an encounter is *cleared*, the party may return to the bonfire tile instead of advancing to the next tile (see §7.5, Encounter Recovery)."
- After: "- After an encounter is *cleared*, the party may return to the bonfire tile and rest instead of advancing to the next tile (see §7.5, Encounter Recovery)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Returning and resting are one act, as §7.5 already states.
- Source: final rulings, core-E35 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §7.5, Encounter Recovery; author's source 11_returning_to_the_bonfire.md l.8; official 2022 'Resting at the Bonfire' (DS-TG-Rulebook l.1927-1930, l.2075-2079).

### CH-11-045 · §11.1
- Type: ruling
- Before: "Returning to the bonfire tile costs nothing. The party spends a *spark* only when it rests (see §11.2). Until the party rests, no character is restored, the Firekeeper and Andre cannot be visited, and no encounter is *reset*."
- After: "Returning to the bonfire tile and resting there are one act. The party returns only to rest, and the rest begins when the models are placed on the tile (see §11.2). The party cannot choose to return while the *spark* dial shows zero (see §7.4)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Removes the state of standing at the bonfire without resting, which had no use except to fight cleared encounters again without spending a spark.
- Source: final rulings, core-E36 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As core-E35.

### CH-11-046 · §11.4, Sales
- Type: ruling
- Before: "- A sold boss treasure card is returned to the game box."
- After: "- A sold boss treasure card is returned to the game box, and so is any other sold card that came from no treasure deck: an *invader*'s treasure card or a Titanite Scale card."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] A card that came from outside the decks no longer enters a treasure deck when it is sold, from which it could have been dealt as an ordinary reward.
- Source: final rulings, lead-E09 (`06_final_rulings.md`); core memo, cross-cluster effects

### CH-11-047 · §11.5
- Type: correction
- Before: "After the reset the characters leave the bonfire tile and enter the encounter tile adjacent to it, where that tile's encounter begins (see §7.5, Encounter Start)."
- After: "After the reset the characters leave the bonfire tile and enter the first encounter tile of the route, where that tile's encounter begins (see §7.5, Encounter Start)."
- Rationale: As VA-014: with a route of any shape two encounter tiles may lie beside the bonfire tile.
- Source: final rulings, VA-027 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-11-048 · §11.4, Sales and Prices
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Andre pays one soul for a tier one or tier two treasure card and six souls for a boss treasure card, and the character takes the souls from the supply."
- After: "Andre pays one soul for a tier one or tier two treasure card, one soul for an *invader*'s treasure card or a Titanite Scale card, and six souls for a boss treasure card, and the character takes the souls from the supply."
- Rationale: An invader's treasure card and a Titanite Scale card could be sold but had no stated price; each sells for one soul, as the ruling on sales intended.
- Source: final rulings, VA-026a (`06_final_rulings.md`); lead editor's decision on a judgement call of the second verification (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-11-049 · §11.4, Sales and Prices
- Type: clarification
- Before: "- A sold tier one or tier two treasure card is placed face down at the bottom of the treasure deck of its tier (see §6.2)."
- After: "- A sold tier one or tier two treasure card that came from a treasure deck or from the shop is placed face down at the bottom of the treasure deck of its tier (see §6.2)."
- Rationale: Two bullets both claimed a sold invader's treasure card; the first now covers only cards that came from a deck or the shop.
- Source: final rulings, VA-026b (`06_final_rulings.md`); lead editor's decision on a judgement call of the second verification (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-11-050 · §11.4, Prices
- Type: clarification
- Before: "| Tier two card | 4 | 1 |
"
- After: "| Tier two card | 4 | 1 |
| *Invader*'s treasure card or Titanite Scale card | - | 1 |
"
- Rationale: The price table gains the row for the two cards that come from no treasure deck.
- Source: final rulings, VA-026c (`06_final_rulings.md`); lead editor's decision on a judgement call of the second verification (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

## Chapter 10 — Character Turn

### CH-10-001 · §10 Character Turn
- Type: style
- Before: "# CHARACTER TURN" / "## Character Turn Order"
- After: "# 10. Character Turn" / "## 10.1 Turn Order"; a chapter intro of 59 words with no rule in it
- Rationale: Chapter and section titles follow the section map; the chapter opens with an in-voice intro.
- Source: C-104; brief §2, §4

### CH-10-002 · §10 Character Turn
- Type: cut
- Before: "## " and "#" (empty headings used as page breaks, manuscript lines 1842 and 1882)
- After: deleted
- Rationale: Page breaks belong to layout, not structure.
- Source: C-098; clean-up list

### CH-10-003 · §10.1 Turn Order
- Type: restructure
- Before: "### Recover one stamina." / "### Minor Actions:" / "> *Walk*: Move character model to an adjacent node. > **AND** > *Run*: Move one additional node per stamina spent."
- After: "1. Recover one stamina: remove one *stamina cube* from the character's *endurance bar* (see §3.4). ... 2. Take *minor actions* and up to one *major action*, in any order."
- Rationale: Steps set as headings and a blockquote joined by AND/OR connectors are replaced by one numbered procedure, with the eight actions listed as full sentences beneath their definitions.
- Source: C-100, C-117, C-029, F-084; cut/simplify list (C-119, A-073)

### CH-10-004 · §10.1 Turn Order
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Minor Actions: Walk AND Run AND Equip AND Heroic Action / Major Action: Attack OR Rest OR Drink Estus OR Ember"
- After: "**Minor action.** An action that a character may take before the major action, after it, or both." / "A character cannot take Walk, Equip, or Heroic Action a second time in a turn, and cannot take a second major action."
- Rationale: The manuscript gave no sequencing or frequency rule. Minor actions and the major action are taken in any order; Walk, Equip, and Heroic Action once each per turn; Run any number of times.
- Source: A-073, D-017; Gate 1 — OQ-26

### CH-10-005 · §10.1 Turn Order
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "In each *round* of an *encounter* the enemies take their *turns* first, and the characters then take theirs one at a time in descending order of *threat* (see §7.5, Encounter Turn Order)."
- Rationale: The section named Turn Order never placed the character turn in the round. One paragraph cites the owning sections for the round, invaders, bosses, summons, and PvP.
- Source: tracker row "Character Turn Order — TOC mismatch"; Gate 1 — OQ-32

### CH-10-006 · §10.1 Turn Order
- Type: clarification
- Before: "### Recover one stamina."
- After: "If the character holds a *frostbite* token, skip this step (see §8.5, Frostbite)." / "A character who holds a frostbite token cannot Rest (see §8.5, Frostbite)."
- Rationale: Both the start-of-turn recovery and the Rest action are stamina recovery, which frostbite forbids; the turn procedure now says so at each point.
- Source: A-010

### CH-10-007 · §10.1 Turn Order
- Type: clarification
- Before: "*Equip*: Swap equipment cards between the back-up slot, weapon slots, and armour slots on the character dashboard. Inventory is unavailable."
- After: "Equip: once per turn, the character exchanges any number of cards among the *back-up slot*, the left and right *weapon slots*, and the *armour slot* (see §3.2)."
- Rationale: The frequency and the number of cards exchanged were unstated. The slots are named as §3.2 names them, and the inventory rule cites its owner.
- Source: A-072, A-068, D-030; Gate 1 — OQ-26

### CH-10-008 · §10.1 Turn Order
- Type: correction
- Before: "*Heroic Action*: Perform ability per dashboard; flip *heroic action* face-down."
- After: "Heroic Action: once per turn, the character flips the heroic action token to its used side and resolves the heroic action printed on the dashboard (see §3.5, Heroic Action)."
- Rationale: Tokens have a ready side and a used side, not a face; the action is available only while the token is ready.
- Source: CMP-0001; brief §3; Gate 1 — OQ-26

### CH-10-009 · §10.1 Turn Order
- Type: clarification
- Before: "*Attack*: Use an *attack option* from the left and/or right weapon slot."
- After: "Attack: the character makes up to one attack with each *weapon* or *spell* *equipped* in a weapon slot (see §10.3)."
- Rationale: The action is named once and its resolution is owned by §10.3; "and/or" is replaced by the limit the manuscript states under Character Attacks.
- Source: C-055, A-068

### CH-10-010 · §10.1 Turn Order
- Type: clarification
- Before: "*Rest*: Restore all of the character's stamina, if no *run* action was taken this turn."
- After: "A character cannot Rest in a turn in which they have run, and a character who has rested cannot run for the remainder of that turn." / "Rest removes stamina cubes only."
- Rationale: The condition forbade running only before the Rest, so a character could rest and then run freely. The bar now applies in both orders. Rest is stated to leave damage cubes, hollow cubes, and condition tokens in place, as §7.2 relies on.
- Source: A-011; dependency from §7.2

### CH-10-011 · §10.1 Turn Order
- Type: correction
- Before: "*Drink Estus*: Restore all of the character's health; flip *estus flask* face-down."
- After: "Drink Estus: the character flips the *estus flask* token to its used side and removes every *damage cube* from their endurance bar (see §3.5, Estus Flask)."
- Rationale: Characters have damage cubes, not health, and the token has a used side. The effect is owned by §3.5 and cited.
- Source: A-012, C-034, CMP-0001

### CH-10-012 · §10.1 Turn Order
- Type: correction
- Before: "*Ember*: Place *ember* token on dashboard; discard equipped *ember* card."
- After: "Ember: the character discards an *ember card* from their back-up slot and becomes *embered* (see §4.4, §7.3)."
- Rationale: An ember card is carried in the back-up slot and is never equipped. The effects of embering are stated once, in §7.3.
- Source: A-006; Gate 1 — OQ-30

### CH-10-013 · §10.1 Turn Order
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A character cannot choose to spend stamina that the bar has no empty space to hold" / "the cube is not placed and the character is *staggered* instead (see §8.5, Stagger)"
- Rationale: The preserved overflow rule (stagger, never chosen) had no home in the turn; §3.4 cites §10.1 for it.
- Source: preserve list; dependency from §3.4

### CH-10-014 · §10.1 Turn Order
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "Each action is resolved in full before another is begun: a character may walk, attack, and then run, but cannot take a minor action in the middle of an attack."
- Rationale: Free ordering of actions left open whether a minor action may fall between the two attacks of one Attack action. The major action is treated as one unit; the shift icon is the rule that allows movement inside an attack.
- Source: A-073, D-017; Gate 1 — OQ-26 (see issues)

### CH-10-015 · §10.1 Turn Order
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: three worked examples (the Knight walks, attacks, and runs; the Herald rests; the Assassin's full endurance bar limits the attack option chosen)
- Rationale: The turn sequence, the Rest action, and the stamina limit each have one example with concrete numbers.
- Source: brief §2 (worked examples); A-073

### CH-10-016 · §10.2 Character Movement
- Type: restructure
- Before: "Characters have four methods of moving from node to node during an encounter. Characters may first *walk* as a minor action which allows them to move one space to an adjacent node."
- After: "**Walk.** A minor action. The character moves to one *adjacent node* without spending stamina. A character may walk once per *turn*." / "**Run.** A minor action. The character spends one stamina and moves to one adjacent node."
- Rationale: The narrative paragraph is replaced by one sentence listing the ways a character changes node and two definitions. Dodge, shift, and push are cited to their owning sections; Walk, Run, and shift are named as moves.
- Source: F-118, C-086, C-027, C-084

### CH-10-017 · §10.2 Character Movement
- Type: cut
- Before: "A valid node is any node that does not contain terrain that would block movement such as a gravestone or chest. Additionally, characters are not permitted to *walk, run,* or *shift* to a node containing a barrel."
- After: "Every move is made into a *valid node* (see §5.2). ... a character cannot move into a node that holds a *gravestone*, a *chest*, an intact *barrel*, or a *pillar token* (see §5.5)."
- Rationale: Terrain rules were stated twice with different verb lists. §5.2 and §5.5 own them; this section keeps one sentence and the cross-references.
- Source: C-086, A-082; cut/simplify list (terrain movement rules in two places)

### CH-10-018 · §10.2 Character Movement
- Type: restructure
- Before: "Characters may move into a node already at the node model limit but must push a friendly model out of the node immediately. Characters may not leave the encounter tile(s) noted on the current encounter card during their movement."
- After: "A character who moves into a node at its *node model limit* forces a model there to be pushed (see §5.3)." / "During an encounter a character cannot leave the tile on which the encounter is fought and cannot pass through a *doorway* (see §5.2)."
- Rationale: The overcrowding push and the tile boundary are owned by §5.3 and §5.2; each is reduced to one sentence with its cross-reference.
- Source: A-086; dependency from §5.2

### CH-10-019 · §10.2 Character Movement
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Characters may first *walk* ... The character may then elect to *run*"
- After: "A character may walk and run before the *major action*, after it, or both (see §10.1). A character may run without having walked."
- Rationale: "First" and "then" implied a fixed order that the free ordering of actions removes.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-26

### CH-10-020 · §10.3 Character Attacks
- Type: restructure
- Before: "A character makes an attack by selecting an *attack option* on one of their equipped weapons, paying the stamina cost, and then nominating a valid enemy model as their target within the weapon's range value."
- After: a 12-step numbered procedure: "1. Choose one attack option of a weapon or spell equipped in a weapon slot. 2. Pay the stamina cost ... 12. If the attack option shows a shift icon after its dice, resolve the shift"
- Rationale: Attack resolution was a single paragraph mixing steps and options. It is now a procedure with one action per step, and the optional rerolls are their own steps.
- Source: F-118; brief §2 (procedures)

### CH-10-021 · §10.3 Character Attacks
- Type: clarification
- Before: "During the character's turn they may make up to one attack with each weapon or spell equipped to the weapon slots on their character dashboard."
- After: "the character may make up to one attack with each *weapon* or *spell* *equipped* in a *weapon slot*: one with the card in the left weapon slot and one with the card in the right weapon slot, in either order"
- Rationale: The limit is tied to the Attack major action and to the two named weapon slots. The spell limits and the upgrade-spell attachment are cited to §4.3 and §4.2.
- Source: A-068, D-030, A-070, C-055

### CH-10-022 · §10.3 Character Attacks
- Type: clarification
- Before: "count the number of sword icons, and apply modifiers to determine the attack's attack value"
- After: "Work out the *attack value*: add together the values rolled, apply any fixed modifier printed on the attack option, add one if the weapon is *scaled* (see §10.5), and subtract one if the character is *staggered* (see §8.5, Stagger)."
- Rationale: Every contribution to the attack value is named in one step. A fixed modifier is applied once to the total after rerolls, the attack-side counterpart of the defence modifier rule in §9.4.
- Source: dependency from §4.1 (D-033, A-120); §2.7

### CH-10-023 · §10.3 Character Attacks
- Type: clarification
- Before: "At this point the player may elect to issue any re-rolls available to them by their *luck* token or otherwise."
- After: "6. The character may reroll one die if the weapon is wielded two-handed (see §10.4). 7. The character may flip the *luck* token to reroll any or all of the dice (see §3.5, Luck)." / "in either order, and may take both on the same roll"
- Rationale: "Or otherwise" is replaced by the two rerolls that exist, each cited to its owner. "Any re-rolls available" is read as allowing both on one roll.
- Source: C-060 (see issues)

### CH-10-024 · §10.3 Character Attacks
- Type: correction
- Before: "Players then place a number of damage tokens onto the target enemy model's base equal to the final damage dealt. ... that enemy dies and is removed from play."
- After: "Place *wound tokens* with a total value equal to the damage on the target (see §8.2)." / "An enemy whose wound tokens equal or exceed its scaled health is killed and removed from the tile (see §8.2, §8.3)."
- Rationale: Enemies take wound tokens and are killed; "damage token", "wounds", and "dies" are not used for enemies. Wound tokens carry values of 1, 3, and 5 (§2.8), so the step speaks of total value, as §8.2 does.
- Source: C-036, A-113, C-061

### CH-10-025 · §10.3 Character Attacks
- Type: style
- Before: "Certain weapons and most spells will instead do magical damage if the attack option selected is labelled with the magical damage icon."
- After: "An attack deals physical damage unless its attack option bears the magical damage icon, in which case it deals magical damage."
- Rationale: Present tense; the rule is stated without the generalisation about which cards carry the icon.
- Source: F-088, F-118

### CH-10-026 · §10.3 Character Attacks
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "An enemy that is the target of an attack within range is *hit*, whether or not it suffers damage (see §8.1)." / "An attack that a rule grants outside the Attack major action, a *riposte* for example (see §9.6), is resolved by the same steps."
- Rationale: The section said nothing of hits, bosses, health dials, the weak arc, PvP defence, or attacks made outside the turn. Each is one sentence that cites its owning section.
- Source: A-119, A-037, A-032 (cross-reference only)

### CH-10-027 · §10.3 Character Attacks
- Type: style
- Before: "Though many weapons simply deal damage, some have special abilities explained in text on their cards or with icons. This includes the magical damage icon explained above, the conditions explained previously, and the following weapon icons."
- After: "Besides the magical damage icon, an attack option may show a push icon (see §8.4), a condition icon (see §8.5), and the five icons described below."
- Rationale: The hedge word and the positional cross-references are removed.
- Source: F-073, F-090, F-119, C-084

### CH-10-028 · §10.3 Character Attacks, Range
- Type: clarification
- Before: "An *attack option* specific range replaces the weapon's range for that attack action."
- After: "An attack option that prints a range of its own uses that range in place of the card's base range. The attack option's range applies to that attack option alone"
- Rationale: "Attack action" is not a term. The section adds that range is a maximum, when it is checked, and that an attack needs a permitted target, citing §5.4.
- Source: C-055, F-119

### CH-10-029 · §10.3 Character Attacks, Shift
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Movement from *Shift* icons costs 0 Stamina and does not replace the character's options to *Walk* and *Run* during their activation."
- After: "A shift costs no stamina. A shift is a move (see §5.2) ... A shift is separate from the Walk and Run minor actions and does not use them up."
- Rationale: The copied passage is rewritten; "activation" becomes "turn" and "0" becomes "no". A shift is named as a move so that the terrain rules apply to it, and the timing of a shift printed after the dice follows the manuscript's Character Movement wording.
- Source: F-120, C-039, C-116, C-086

### CH-10-030 · §10.3 Character Attacks, Area-of-Effect
- Type: clarification
- Before: "### *Area-of-Effect (AoE)*" / "Attacks with the *AoE* icon can target all enemies on a single node within range instead of targeting just one enemy."
- After: "### Area-of-Effect" / "An attack option that bears the *area-of-effect* icon targets one node within range instead of one enemy. Every enemy on that node is a target of the attack and is hit by it."
- Rationale: The heading follows the section map. The rule states that every enemy on the node is hit, so pushes and conditions apply to each, and that the attacker's own node may be targeted.
- Source: F-121, F-090; tracker row "Area-of-Effect (AoE) — TOC mismatch"

### CH-10-031 · §10.3 Character Attacks, Shaft
- Type: cut
- Before: "Bows, polearms, and other unwieldy weapons are difficult to use effectively at short range. Weapons with the *shaft* icon cannot target enemy models on the character's node."
- After: "An attack option that bears the shaft icon cannot target a model on the attacker's own node."
- Rationale: Flavour is cut from the rule. The icon belongs to the attack option, and the area-of-effect case is stated.
- Source: F-016, F-122, C-121

### CH-10-032 · §10.3 Character Attacks, Repeat
- Type: clarification
- Before: "The *repeat* icon allows the character to use that entire weapon option the number of times indicated in the circle. This represents a rapid flurry of attacks such as from an agile sword or a repeating crossbow."
- After: "The character pays the stamina cost once, before the first resolution, and then resolves steps 3 to 12 of the attack once for each time shown." / "Each resolution is a separate attack roll with its own target"
- Rationale: Flavour is cut. "Entire weapon option" is given a definite meaning: stamina once, then every later step again for each repetition.
- Source: F-017, F-122, C-055, C-121, A-037

### CH-10-033 · §10.4 Two-Handed Attacks
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Characters equipped with a two-handed weapon are decidedly on the offensive. Attack actions with two-handed weapons may reroll any single die after rolling to attack."
- After: "When a character attacks with a weapon wielded two-handed, the character may reroll one die of the attack roll after rolling (see §10.3)." / "Any card in the other weapon slot is equipment in the other hand."
- Rationale: The editorial opener is cut. The two cases of two-handed wielding are listed, a shield or any other card in the other weapon slot is stated to prevent it, and the reroll is tied to each attack roll.
- Source: F-018, A-069, C-055, C-121

### CH-10-034 · §10.4 Two-Handed Attacks
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A spell is never two-handed (see §4.3)." / "A character who wields a weapon two-handed has no shield equipped and therefore cannot *parry* (see §9.6)."
- Rationale: The spell exclusion is cited from §4.3, which refers here, and the consequence for parrying is stated where the two-handed rule lives.
- Source: preserve list; dependencies from §4.1 and §4.3; A-068

### CH-10-035 · §10.5 Stat Scaling
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "If the character's current stat value exceeds an equipment's highest stat requirement by ten or more points"
- After: "A weapon is *scaled* while it is *equipped* by a character whose value in the relevant stat exceeds that highest stat requirement by five or more (see §3.3)."
- Rationale: A ten-point margin excluded almost every tier two weapon; five lets a character at stat tier three or four scale the weapons the tier two deck supplies.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-19; B-016

### CH-10-036 · §10.5 Stat Scaling
- Type: clarification
- Before: "the character should mark the equipped item with a red damage cube to indicate that it is scaled. If two requirements are tied, use either."
- After: "**Relevant stat.** The stat whose *stat requirement* is the highest of the four printed on a *weapon* card ..." / "the character must mark the weapon card with a red cube"
- Rationale: The stat that is compared is defined, with the tie left to the character as the manuscript has it. "Should" becomes "must", "item" becomes "weapon card", and the removal of the cube is stated.
- Source: C-019, F-089, C-054

### CH-10-037 · §10.5 Stat Scaling
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Weapons are not provided additional stat scaling beyond the +1 benefits for excess stats beyond ten."
- After: "The bonus is +1 and no more, however far the stat exceeds the requirement. Only the relevant stat is compared"
- Rationale: The cap is restated without the superseded threshold.
- Source: C-015; Gate 1 — OQ-19

### CH-10-038 · §10.2 to §10.5
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: eleven worked examples, including a full attack with a scaled two-handed weapon (§10.3), one for each attack icon, the lone one-handed weapon (§10.4), and three scaling cases (§10.5)
- Rationale: The manuscript chapter had no examples. Each rule a player applies with numbers now has one, and the §10.3 example shows scaling and the two-handed reroll in a single attack.
- Source: brief §2 (worked examples); chapter instructions

### CH-10-039 · §10 Character Turn
- Type: style
- Before: "who's stats" / "its' attack option*s*" / "re-rolls" / "*walk, run,*" / "main action" / "costs 0 Stamina" / curly quotes / italic sub-headings
- After: style sweep: spelling and split italics corrected; "reroll"; each keyword italicised alone at its first use in each H2; "major action"; words for zero to nine; straight quotes; plain sub-headings
- Rationale: Spelling, italics, compound, numeral, and quotation policies applied throughout the chapter.
- Source: C-015, C-027, C-028, C-030, C-038, C-060, C-111, C-116

### CH-10-040 · §10 Character Turn (OPTIONAL findings skipped)
- Type: clarification
- Before: n/a
- After: not applied
- Rationale: A-066 (a weapon whose highest stat requirement is zero cannot be scaled) alters the preserved scaling mechanic outside the changes signed off at Gate 1; it is left for the author (see issues). The pack findings A-032 (§20.4), A-024 (§8.3, moot under OQ-07), A-064 (§18.7), and A-087 (§5.2, §9.5) call for no text in this chapter.
- Source: A-066; A-032, A-024, A-064, A-087

### CH-10-041 · §10.5 Equipment Scaling
- Type: correction
- Before: "The Assassin's rapier prints requirements of 10 STR and 20 DEX, so dexterity is its relevant stat. The Assassin's dexterity is 24, which exceeds 20 by four, and the rapier is not scaled. At the *bonfire* the Assassin raises dexterity to 29. The rapier is now scaled, and the Assassin marks it with a red cube."
- After: "The Assassin's Rapier requires 14 STR and 21 DEX (see Appendix A), so dexterity is its relevant stat. The Assassin's dexterity is 24, which exceeds 21 by three, and the Rapier is not scaled. At the *bonfire* the Assassin raises dexterity to 29. The Rapier is now scaled, and the Assassin marks it with a red cube."
- Rationale: The example used the superseded printed requirements; Appendix A sets the Rapier at 14 STR and 21 DEX and governs scaling.
- Source: QA-001 (example and consistency verification)

### CH-10-042 · §10.3, Range
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Range is checked when the target is chosen, after any shift resolved before the dice. A character cannot make an attack that has no permitted target within its range."
- After: "Range is checked when the target is chosen, after any shift resolved before the dice. A character cannot begin an attack unless a permitted target is within its range or a shift printed before its dice is able to bring one within range. If no permitted target is within range when the target is to be chosen, the attack ends without a roll, and the stamina paid is not returned."
- Rationale: Step 4 had no outcome when a shift, or a trap entered during it, left no target in range.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E26 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official 2022 pays and shifts before the target is chosen (DS-TG 1421-1423, 1546-1549); no source gives the outcome.

### CH-10-043 · §10.3, Repeat
- Type: ruling
- Before: "An attack option that bears the *repeat* icon {{icon:ast-0061-repeat-behaviour-or-attack}} is resolved in full the number of times shown in the icon. The character pays the stamina cost once, before the first resolution, and then resolves steps 3 to 12 of the attack once for each time shown."
- After: "An attack option that bears the *repeat* icon {{icon:ast-0061-repeat-behaviour-or-attack}} may be resolved in full up to the number of times shown in the icon. The character pays the stamina cost once, before the first resolution, resolves steps 3 to 12 of the attack for each resolution, and may stop after any resolution."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] A repeated attack is a permission, as every other attack of the Attack major action is ("up to one attack with each weapon").
- Source: final rulings, combat-E27 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official 2022: "perform the attack up to the number of times shown" (DS-TG 1566-1572).

### CH-10-044 · §10.4
- Type: clarification
- Before: "A spell card equipped while the other weapon slot is empty gains no reroll, and the attack options of a spell stored in a catalyst gain none."
- After: "A spell card equipped while the other weapon slot is empty gains no reroll, and the attack options of a spell stored in a catalyst gain none. A catalyst is a weapon: an attack option printed on the catalyst card itself gains the reroll while the catalyst is wielded two-handed."
- Rationale: Applies the general rule to the one card type the section left open; no special case is created.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E28 (`06_final_rulings.md`); This book, §4.1 (a catalyst is a weapon) and §10.4.

### CH-10-045 · §10.5
- Type: ruling
- Before: "*Spells* are never scaled (see §4.3)."
- After: "A weapon whose four stat requirements are all zero has no relevant stat and is never scaled. *Spells* are never scaled (see §4.3)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Without it every character scales such a weapon at once, a +1 that rewards nothing and a red cube that records no choice.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E29 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Audit finding A-066; no official or community ruleset has stat scaling.

### CH-10-046 · §10.4
- Type: clarification
- Before: "A character who wields a weapon two-handed has no shield equipped and therefore cannot *parry* (see §9.6)."
- After: "A character who wields a weapon other than a shield two-handed has no shield equipped and therefore cannot *parry* (see §9.6)."
- Rationale: Companion of the same clarification in §9.6.
- Source: final rulings, VA-025b (`06_final_rulings.md`); lead editor's decision on a judgement call of the second verification (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

## Chapter 20 — Player versus Player

### CH-20-001 · §20 Player versus Player
- Type: restructure
- Before: "# PLAYER VS PLAYER" followed by 27 bullets, a six-line escaped list, and an empty H1 used as a page break
- After: "# 20. Player versus Player" with a 45-word introduction and nine numbered sections (20.1 Setup to 20.9 After the PvP Encounter)
- Rationale: The bullet fragments are rewritten as standing rules, two numbered procedures, one list of defences, and one table in lawbook voice; the heading follows the book's "versus" form and the empty heading is deleted.
- Source: C-119, F-085, C-104, C-098, CMP-0009

### CH-20-002 · §20.1 Setup
- Type: style
- Before: "Decide during setup if PvP rules will be used or not."
- After: "Player versus player (PvP) encounters are an optional rule. They require no expansion. The party decides during setup whether PvP encounters are in play (see §6.5, Optional Rules)."
- Rationale: The fixed optional-rule formula replaces one of eight phrasings; §6.5 lists the option and cites this section.
- Source: C-124, F-088

### CH-20-003 · §20.1 Setup
- Type: correction
- Before: "After each boss (mini, main, and mega) a PvP encounter will occur."
- After: "When PvP encounters are in play, one *PvP encounter* is fought after each *mini-boss*, *main-boss*, and *mega-boss* is defeated."
- Rationale: PvP follows every boss, in the present tense; the encounter is placed on the boss's tile, after the boss's rewards and before the free rest, so that §6.6, §6.7, and §7.1 agree with it.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-15; A-031, F-088, CMP-0009

### CH-20-004 · §20.1 Setup
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "**PvP encounter.** A contest fought between the characters alone, without enemies, on the tile on which a boss has been defeated."
- Rationale: The term is defined once and the stakes are stated at the head of the chapter: the victory bonus, the ultimate victor, no souls or treasure, and no cost for death, each with a reference to its owning section.
- Source: CMP-0009, C-033

### CH-20-005 · §20.1 Setup
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Dead characters are excluded from PvP." / "Phantoms (Summons and Invaders) are excluded from PvP."
- After: "A character who died in the boss *encounter* takes no part in the PvP encounter that follows it. Every other character takes part. *Phantoms*, both *summons* and *invaders*, take no part."
- Rationale: "Dead characters" is given a definite meaning (a character who died in the boss encounter), and phantom is used as the umbrella term for summons and invaders.
- Source: CMP-0009, C-064

### CH-20-006 · §20.1 Setup
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "If fewer than two characters take part, no PvP encounter is fought and no character wins."
- Rationale: With one eligible character the manuscript awarded a victory without a fight; the case is closed.
- Source: A-034

### CH-20-007 · §20.1 Setup
- Type: completion
- Before: "Remove all enemy models and traps from the boss encounter board."
- After: "1. Remove every enemy model, *wound token*, and *trap token* from the tile." and "*Gravestones*, *barrels*, *chests*, and pillars stay on the tile as they stand and keep their effects (see §5.5)."
- Rationale: The setup becomes a numbered procedure; wound tokens are cleared with the enemy models and the fate of other terrain is stated.
- Source: CMP-0009, C-119

### CH-20-008 · §20.1 Setup
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Restore each character as if they rested at the *bonfire*."
- After: "2. Remove every *damage cube*, *stamina cube*, *hollow cube*, and *condition* token from each character who takes part. 3. Turn the *luck*, *estus flask*, and *heroic action* tokens of each character who takes part to their ready sides."
- Rationale: The restoration is enumerated and marked as not being a rest: no spark, no reset, no Firekeeper or shop; the ember token is kept. "Hollow cube" is the one component name.
- Source: CMP-0009, C-050, A-005

### CH-20-009 · §20.1 Setup
- Type: completion
- Before: "Place each character in a corner of the boss encounter board."
- After: "5. Place each character's model, in turn order, on a corner node chosen by that character's player and occupied by no other model."
- Rationale: The corner node is defined (the node nearest a corner that holds no terrain), each character has its own corner, and the order of choice follows the turn order.
- Source: CMP-0009

### CH-20-010 · §20.1 Setup
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "7. Shuffle the trap tokens in the supply face down and place them beside the tile."
- Rationale: The trap tokens placed during the PvP encounter need a shuffled, face-down supply.
- Source: CMP-0009, A-033

### CH-20-011 · §20.2 Turn Order
- Type: cut
- Before: "*First* *turn* token is given to the character with the highest threat value."
- After: deleted
- Rationale: No such token is a component and threat order already fixes the first turn.
- Source: CMP-0011, A-017, B-030, D-018, C-033, C-029

### CH-20-012 · §20.2 Turn Order
- Type: completion
- Before: "Characters take turns in descending threat order."
- After: "If two or more characters share a threat value, the players decide the order of the tied characters before the first turn. If the players cannot agree, the order of the tied characters is decided at random."
- Rationale: Ties in threat are settled, the order is kept for the whole PvP encounter, and a round is defined for an encounter with no enemy turns.
