- Source: CMP-0011, CMP-0009, A-014

### CH-20-013 · §20.2 Turn Order
- Type: style
- Before: "*Aggro* token is not used."
- After: "The *aggro token* is not used in a PvP encounter."
- Rationale: A fragment becomes a full sentence with its subject and scope.
- Source: F-085

### CH-20-014 · §20.3 Character Turns
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Character turn structure is unchanged from basic rules."
- After: "A character's *turn* in a *PvP encounter* follows the rules of the standard game (see §10.1)."
- Rationale: "The standard game" and section references replace "basic rules"; the spell limit and the tile boundary are cited rather than restated, and the action order follows §10.1.
- Source: F-053, CMP-0009; Gate 1 — OQ-26

### CH-20-015 · §20.4 Attacking a Character
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The attacker resolves the attack as an attack against an enemy (see §10.3), and the defender defends as against an enemy's attack (see §9.3), except as this section states."
- Rationale: The manuscript gave no procedure for a character attacking a character. The attacker rolls a final attack value, then the defender blocks or resists with rolled dice (two highest), dodges, parries, or declines.
- Source: A-032, CMP-0009

### CH-20-016 · §20.4 Attacking a Character
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "An attack option that bears the *AoE* icon targets every other character on the chosen node: the attacker rolls once, and each of those characters chooses and resolves a defence of their own against the same attack value."
- Rationale: The shaft, area-of-effect, and repeat icons are applied to a character target; each resolution of a repeated attack option is a separate attack.
- Source: A-032, CMP-0009

### CH-20-017 · §20.4 Attacking a Character
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The riposte is an attack under this section with the roles reversed [...] The target of a riposte may block, resist, or dodge it but cannot parry it."
- Rationale: Parry and riposte between characters are defined by reference to §9.6; the bar on parrying a riposte ends a chain of parries between two shield-bearers.
- Source: A-032, CMP-0009

### CH-20-018 · §20.4 Attacking a Character
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "\[0\] -\> 4 … \[1\] -\> 3 … \[2\] -\> 2 … \[3\] -\> 1 … \[4\] -\> 1 … \[5+\] -\> 0"
- After: a two-column table, stamina cost 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 / 5 or more against dodge difficulty 3 / 2 / 2 / 1 / 1 / 1
- Rationale: Difficulty 4 cannot be rolled on fewer than four dice and difficulty 0 is an automatic dodge; the revised values keep cheap attacks harder to dodge within the range the dice produce. The escaped list becomes a table.
- Source: B-024 (approved at Gate 1), C-079, C-109

### CH-20-019 · §20.4 Attacking a Character
- Type: correction
- Before: "Dodge difficulty is based on an the attacker attack option*'s* stamina cost:"
- After: "The dodge difficulty of a character's attack is set by the stamina cost printed on the attack option."
- Rationale: The garbled sentence is completed; the keyword is lower-case and wholly italicised at first use.
- Source: C-015, C-028, C-047

### CH-20-020 · §20.4 Attacking a Character
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "*Example.* The Assassin attacks the Knight, who stands on an adjacent node, with an attack option that costs one stamina."
- Rationale: Two worked examples show an attack met by a failed dodge and an attack met by a successful dodge; the source held no example.
- Source: chapter instruction; A-032

### CH-20-021 · §20.5 Pushes and Conditions
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "An *attack option* that bears the *push* icon pushes each character it *hits*. The push follows the rules of the standard game (see §8.4), and *terrain* affects the pushed character as in any other encounter (see §5.5)."
- Rationale: Pushes and conditions between characters are defined by reference to §8.1, §8.4, and §8.5, so the poison and stagger rules settled at Gate 1 apply without restatement.
- Source: A-032, CMP-0009; Gate 1 — OQ-13, OQ-16 (by reference)

### CH-20-022 · §20.5 Pushes and Conditions
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "In a *PvP encounter*, a choice that a rule of the standard game leaves to the players is made by the player whose character makes the attack or the movement that calls for the choice."
- Rationale: The standard game leaves several choices (equal push directions, the model displaced from a full node) to the players jointly, which has no meaning between opponents. No finding covers the point; it is listed in the issues file for confirmation.
- Source: none (drafting completion; see issues)

### CH-20-023 · §20.6 Traps
- Type: completion
- Before: "Players place a trap token on an outermost available node after each turn."
- After: "At the end of each character's *turn*, that character's player draws one *trap token* from the supply without looking at its face and places it face down on an outermost available *node* of that player's choice."
- Rationale: Who places the token, which token, its facing, and the meaning of "available" and "outermost" (edge nodes first, then each ring inward) are stated, with the cases of no node and no token.
- Source: A-033, CMP-0009

### CH-20-024 · §20.6 Traps
- Type: completion
- Before: "Characters that move onto a trap token may attempt to *dodge*." / "Characters that fail to *dodge* a trap immediately die."
- After: "If the token is not blank, each character on that node must attempt to *dodge* it as an attack with the *dodge difficulty* shown on the token (see §9.5). The damage value shown on the token is not used."
- Rationale: Triggering follows §5.5; the PvP differences are stated: the dodge is compulsory, a failed dodge or an unpayable dodge kills, a blank token does nothing, and a face-up token has no further effect. A worked example is added.
- Source: A-033, A-083, CMP-0009

### CH-20-025 · §20.7 Death
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Dying in PvP does not cause any penalty. Souls are not dropped. Ember is not removed. Hollow cubes are not placed."
- After: "Death in a PvP encounter costs nothing. The steps that follow a death in any other encounter are not carried out (see §7.2). The character drops no *souls*, keeps the *ember token*, receives no *hollow cubes*, and is not *lost*."
- Rationale: The two ways of dying are listed and the absence of cost is stated against §7.2, which cites this section.
- Source: CMP-0009, C-050, F-085

### CH-20-026 · §20.7 Death
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A PvP encounter is neither *cleared* nor *failed* (see §7.5, Encounter Resolution). However it ends, the party spends no *spark* for it and is not required to rest."
- Rationale: Without this sentence the death of every character would read as a failed encounter under §7.5 and cost a spark.
- Source: CMP-0009 (design rationale), A-034

### CH-20-027 · §20.8 Victory
- Type: completion
- Before: "The last character alive is awarded victory."
- After: "The last character on the tile wins the PvP encounter. If every remaining character dies at the same time, the PvP encounter ends and no character wins."
- Rationale: The end condition and the case of simultaneous death are stated, so that the victory bonus is never awarded twice.
- Source: CMP-0009

### CH-20-028 · §20.8 Victory
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "If no character suffers damage and no character dies during three consecutive *rounds*, each character still in play suffers one damage at the end of every round that follows, until the PvP encounter ends."
- Rationale: Two ranged characters could retreat without end once the trap supply is exhausted; the rule guarantees that the PvP encounter terminates. It adds a mechanic and is listed in the issues file.
- Source: A-035

### CH-20-029 · §20.8 Victory
- Type: completion
- Before: "Victory on non-final bosses provides +1 attack damage in future PvP only."
- After: "One *wound token* of value 1 is placed beside the winner's dashboard to mark it. In every subsequent PvP encounter, the character adds one to the *attack value* of each of their attacks for each victory bonus held (see §20.4)."
- Rationale: The carry-over is named (victory bonus), given a physical marker, and stated to be cumulative, confined to PvP encounters, and kept until the game ends.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-15; CMP-0009

### CH-20-030 · §20.8 Victory
- Type: correction
- Before: "Victory on the final boss determines the ultimate champion."
- After: "The winner of the PvP encounter that follows the defeat of the final boss is the *ultimate victor*."
- Rationale: One term, "ultimate victor", replaces "ultimate champion" here and "intimate victor" in the objective; the shared victory over the final boss is unaffected, and the last PvP encounter awards no bonus.
- Source: CMP-0009, A-031

### CH-20-031 · §20.9 After the PvP Encounter
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "It is the one rest that follows the boss's defeat (see §16.5); a PvP encounter grants no second rest."
- Rationale: The manuscript did not say what follows a PvP encounter. Trap tokens return to the supply, the setup after the boss follows with its free rest, and the game ends after the last PvP encounter.
- Source: CMP-0009, A-005

### CH-20-032 · §20 Player versus Player (style sweep)
- Type: style
- Before: "*First* *turn*", "attack option*'s*", "Dodge difficulty", "will occur", "-\>"
- After: whole-word italics for glossary keywords at first use in each section, lower-case keywords, present tense, no ASCII arrows or escaped brackets
- Rationale: Typography, italics policy, and tense are normalised throughout the chapter.
- Source: C-015, C-028, C-029, C-030, C-047, C-109, F-088

### CH-20-033 · §20 Player versus Player (optional findings)
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: new text
- Rationale: A-014 applied (tie in threat settled by the players). A-035 applied (CH-20-028). A-034 applied in part: its fewer-than-two rule is adopted (CH-20-006); its soul-token marker is not used, because Gate 1 — OQ-15 names the wound token of CMP-0009; its reading that every character takes part is not used, because CMP-0009 keeps the manuscript's exclusion of characters who died in the boss encounter. B-030 and D-018 (list a first player token) are resolved by deleting the token under CMP-0011.
- Source: A-014, A-034, A-035, B-030, D-018

### CH-20-034 · §20.1 PvP Encounters
- Type: clarification
- Before: "and precedes the rest at the *bonfire* that follows every boss (see §20.9)."
- After: "and precedes the rest at the *bonfire* that follows every boss but the *final boss* (see §20.9)."
- Rationale: §16.5 and §20.9 grant no rest after the final boss.
- Source: QA observation (example and consistency verification)

### CH-20-035 · §20.4
- Type: correction
- Before: "- The defender may *parry* as against an enemy's attack, with the attacker in the place of the enemy, if the conditions for a parry are met (see §9.6). The dodge difficulty is given by the same table.
- The defender may decline to defend. The defender suffers damage equal to the attack value and is hit."
- After: "- The defender may *parry* as against an enemy's attack, with the attacker in the place of the enemy, if the conditions for a parry are met (see §9.6). The dodge difficulty is given by the same table."
- Rationale: A block or resist costs nothing and is never worse than no defence, so the fourth option was dead text and disagreed with the three defences of §9.3.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E33 (`06_final_rulings.md`); This book, §9.3, Defence and §9.4.

### CH-20-036 · §20.4
- Type: ruling
- Before: "The icons and text of an attack option apply to a defender as they apply to an enemy (see §10.3)."
- After: "The icons and text of an attack option apply to a defender as they apply to an enemy (see §10.3). In a PvP encounter, the word enemy in a *heroic action* or on a treasure card means another character."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] The setup readies every heroic action token, so the chapter must say whether a heroic action that names an enemy works; one sentence extends the rule already stated for attack options.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E26 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §20.1 step 3 and §3.5, Heroic Action of this book; no official or community PvP rules exist to consult.

### CH-20-037 · §20.4, step 4
- Type: ruling
- Before: "4. Add one to the result for each *victory bonus* the attacker holds (see §20.8)."
- After: "4. Add one to the result if the attacker holds the *victory bonus* (see §20.8)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] The victory bonus is no longer cumulative.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E27 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Source manuscript, chapter PLAYER VS PLAYER: 'Victory on non-final bosses provides +1 attack damage in future PvP only' (a flat +1; cumulation was added by CMP-0009).

### CH-20-038 · §20.4 (table)
- Type: clarification
- Before: "| 0 | 3 |
| 1 | 2 |
| 2 | 2 |
| 3 | 1 |
| 4 | 1 |
| 5 or more | 1 |"
- After: "| 0 | 3 |
| 1 or 2 | 2 |
| 3 or more | 1 |"
- Rationale: The six rows hold three values; three rows are easier to remember and change no result.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E29 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Audit B_report.md §10.1 (probabilities for the green die) and finding B-024.

### CH-20-039 · §20.8
- Type: ruling
- Before: "When a character wins a PvP encounter that follows the defeat of a boss other than the final boss, the character gains one *victory bonus*. One *wound token* of value 1 is placed beside the winner's dashboard to mark it. In every subsequent PvP encounter, the character adds one to the *attack value* of each of their attacks for each victory bonus held (see §20.4). Victory bonuses are cumulative, have no effect outside PvP encounters, and are kept until the game ends."
- After: "When a character wins a PvP encounter that follows the defeat of a boss other than the final boss, the character gains the *victory bonus*. A spare token of any kind is placed beside the winner's dashboard to mark it. In every subsequent PvP encounter, the character adds one to the *attack value* of each of their attacks (see §20.4). A character holds no more than one victory bonus, however many PvP encounters the character wins. The victory bonus has no effect outside PvP encounters and is kept until the game ends."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] A flat +1 gives the earlier contests a stake without deciding the last one in advance; a cumulative bonus reaches +4 or +5 in a campaign of five or six bosses. A spare token replaces the value 1 wound token, of which the core set has six and enemies need all.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E30 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Source manuscript PvP bullets (flat +1); §2.8 of this book (6x wound tokens of value 1); §19 (campaigns list up to six bosses).

### CH-20-040 · §20.8 (example)
- Type: clarification
- Before: "*Example.* The Assassin wins the PvP encounter that follows the mini-boss, and one wound token is placed beside the Assassin's dashboard."
- After: "*Example.* The Assassin wins the PvP encounter that follows the mini-boss, and a spare token is placed beside the Assassin's dashboard."
- Rationale: Keeps the example in step with the marker named in the rule.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E31 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Follows phantom-E30.

### CH-20-041 · §20.9
- Type: clarification
- Before: "It is the one rest that follows the boss's defeat (see §16.5); a PvP encounter grants no second rest."
- After: "It is the one rest that follows the boss's defeat (see §16.5); a PvP encounter grants no second rest. In campaign play, what follows a PvP encounter is stated with the campaign rules (see §18.1, Player versus Player Encounters)."
- Rationale: §18.1 already settles PvP encounters in a campaign; §20.9 lacked the pointer.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E32 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §18.1, Player versus Player Encounters of this book.

### CH-20-042 · §20.1
- Type: correction
- Before: "When PvP encounters are in play, one *PvP encounter* is fought after each *mini-boss*, *main-boss*, and *mega-boss* is defeated."
- After: "When PvP encounters are in play, one *PvP encounter* is fought after each *mini-boss*, *main-boss*, and *mega-boss* is defeated. In campaign play it is fought only after a boss whose defeat completes a *section* (see §18.1, Player versus Player Encounters)."
- Rationale: §20.1 owns the rule and lacked the campaign exception made today in §18.1.
- Source: final rulings, VB-013 (`06_final_rulings.md`); independent verification, work/phase3/verify_b.md

## Chapter 08 — Combat Basics

### CH-08-001 · §8 Combat Basics
- Type: style
- Before: "# COMBAT BASICS" / "## Target versus Hit" (unnumbered headings, no chapter intro)
- After: "# 8. Combat Basics" / "## 8.1 Target versus Hit" … "## 8.5 Conditions"; a chapter intro of 58 words with no rule in it
- Rationale: Chapter and section titles follow the section map; every reference in the book targets these numbers.
- Source: C-104; brief §4

### CH-08-002 · §8.1 Target versus Hit
- Type: restructure
- Before: "When a character or an enemy attacks, the model defending the attack is considered the attack's target. Attacks have a single target by default but some attacks have special modifiers …"
- After: "**Target.** The model at which an attack is directed." … "Some attacks target every opposing model on one *node* (see §9.3; §10.3, Area-of-Effect), and some *boss* attacks target every character on several nodes (see §16.3)."
- Rationale: Target is stated as a definition in the glossary's form and the copied explanatory prose is rewritten in the book's voice; the multi-target attacks are cited in their owning sections instead of being left as "special modifiers".
- Source: F-108, C-119, A-023, D-008

### CH-08-003 · §8.1 Target versus Hit
- Type: style
- Before: "Characters may only target other characters if playing a PvP encounter per the optional rules detailed later."
- After: "A character targets another character only in a player versus player (PvP) *encounter* (see §20)."
- Rationale: The positional cross-reference becomes a numbered one, and PvP is spelt out at its first use in the chapter. The resolution of a character's attack on a character is owned by §20.4.
- Source: F-068, C-084, A-032

### CH-08-004 · §8.1 Target versus Hit
- Type: clarification
- Before: "A model is considered hit by an attack even if the attack deals zero damage so long as the attack was in range and the target model did not successfully dodge the attack."
- After: "**Hit.** An attack hits its target when the target is within the attack's *range* (see §5.4) and does not avoid the attack by a successful *dodge* (see §9.5) or a successful *parry* (see §9.6)."
- Rationale: Hit is stated once as a definition with its two tests; a successful parry avoids the hit exactly as a successful dodge does, as the glossary already stated. An out-of-range target is stated to be not hit.
- Source: D-008, A-023, C-047; glossary Hit

### CH-08-005 · §8.1 Target versus Hit
- Type: style
- Before: "Therefore, targets who successfully *dodge* an attack are not considered hit and therefore are not pushed or afflicted with conditions associated with being hit by the attack."
- After: "An attack has no effect on a target it does not hit: it deals no damage, does not *push* the target, and places no *condition* token on it."
- Rationale: The connectives are removed and each outcome is stated as a rule; the companion rule (pushes and conditions apply to every model hit, with or without damage) is its own sentence.
- Source: F-091, F-108

### CH-08-006 · §8.1 Target versus Hit
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "*Example.* An enemy attack with an *attack value* of 4 bears the push icon and targets the Knight and the Herald, who share a node within its range. …"
- Rationale: A worked example shows a hit that deals no damage and a dodge that avoids the push.
- Source: brief §2 (worked examples)

### CH-08-007 · §8.2 Enemy Data Cards
- Type: restructure
- Before: "Each enemy type in the game has a data card breaking down how it works in the game. … Each enemy data card includes the following information: 1. Name 2. Threat level 3. Attack range …"
- After: "An enemy data card shows the following. - The name identifies the kind of enemy. - The *threat* value fixes the order in which enemies take their turns (see §7.5, Encounter Turn Order). …"
- Rationale: The copied anatomy list and its four explanatory paragraphs are merged into one bulleted list of full sentences, each carrying its icon and the section that resolves it.
- Source: F-109, F-110, C-119, A-125

### CH-08-008 · §8.2 Enemy Data Cards
- Type: completion
- Before: "The behaviour icons are the core element of any enemy which determine how the enemy behaves during its turn."
- After: "Smaller icons printed around a behaviour icon modify it, as follows. - The skull icon directs the movement or attack at the character who holds the *aggro token* (see §9.2; §9.3). …"
- Rationale: The satellite icons (skull, nearest character, node, push, push with damage, repeat, condition) and the physical or magical attack icon are named here with the icon slugs; each is resolved in the section cited.
- Source: A-023, D-008; brief chapter instructions

### CH-08-009 · §8.2 Enemy Data Cards
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A *boss* data card shows a *behaviour deck* size in place of behaviour icons and adds a *heat-up* point (see §15.4). The data cards of *phantoms* and *mimics* are described with those models (see §12; §13)."
- Rationale: The reader is told where the other data cards differ; their anatomy is owned by §15.4, §12, and §13.
- Source: brief chapter instructions

### CH-08-010 · §8.2 Enemy Data Cards
- Type: correction
- Before: "When playing with more than one character, calculate a scaled health value by adding one health for each character (including dead characters) in play."
- After: "**Scaled health.** An enemy's base health plus the increase that enemy scaling gives for the number of characters (see §8.3)."
- Rationale: The prose gave Base + 2 for two characters where the table gave Base + 1. Scaled health is now defined by reference to the one table in §8.3.
- Source: C-066, A-026, B-006

### CH-08-011 · §8.2 Enemy Data Cards
- Type: correction
- Before: "place one wound token on the base of that enemy model for each point of damage not prevented by the enemies *block* or *resist*. Once an enemy model has wounds equal to (or exceeding) its scaled health …"
- After: "When the total value of the wound tokens on an enemy model equals or exceeds its *scaled health*, the enemy is killed. Remove the model and its wound tokens from the tile at once."
- Rationale: Wound tokens have values of 1, 3, and 5, so the rule counts their total value; "wounds" and "the enemies block" are corrected; enemies are killed; the calculation of damage is left to §10.3. Bosses, phantoms, and mimics are pointed to the health dial.
- Source: C-015, C-036, A-113, C-061

### CH-08-012 · §8.2 Enemy Data Cards
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "*Example.* The Knight, the Herald, and the Assassin fight a Hollow Soldier whose data card shows a base health of 1. With three characters its scaled health is 3. …"
- Rationale: A worked example shows scaled health, wound tokens, and the kill.
- Source: brief §2 (worked examples)

### CH-08-013 · §8.3 Enemy Scaling
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "If playing with three or more characters, increase all non-boss attack values by one." … "If playing with four characters, add *repeat to* each non-boss enemy's attack behaviour." Table row 4: Base + 3, movement 1 to 2, +1, *Repeat* 2
- After: Table rows "3 | Base health + 2 | 1 becomes 2 | No change" and "4 | Base health + 4 | 1 becomes 2 | +1"; "With four characters, every attack value on an enemy's data card is increased by one."
- Rationale: Repeat 2 at four characters is deleted with its table column; the +1 attack moves from three characters to four; four characters give Base + 4 health. The undefined Repeat for enemies and its interaction with push-moves fall away with it.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-07; B-003, B-004, B-002, C-067, A-024, B-005

### CH-08-014 · §8.3 Enemy Scaling
- Type: restructure
- Before: bulleted fragments and an HTML table with the columns Character Number, Enemy Health, Enemy Movement, Enemy Damage, and Enemy Attack Repeats, and the cell "1 -&gt; 2"
- After: one four-column table ("Characters | Enemy health | Enemy movement | Enemy *attack value*") followed by one short paragraph for each column
- Rationale: The rule is stated once, in a table with the prose explaining each column; "Enemy Damage" becomes attack value, the term used everywhere else; ASCII arrows are replaced by "1 becomes 2".
- Source: C-067, C-109, C-119

### CH-08-015 · §8.3 Enemy Scaling
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Non-boss enemies gain benefits depending on the number of total characters in a game to ensure balance." … "Increase each non-boss enemy's base health by the number of characters (including dead characters) beyond one."
- After: "The number of characters is fixed at setup (see §6.1) and does not change when a character dies: a dead character and a *lost* character still count."
- Rationale: States when the number is fixed, that dead and lost characters count, and when scaling is applied; the design motive ("to ensure balance") is removed.
- Source: A-026, B-006; brief chapter instructions

### CH-08-016 · §8.3 Enemy Scaling
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If playing with two or more characters, increase any non-boss enemy behaviour of one movement to two movements."
- After: "With two or more characters, every movement icon on an enemy's *data card* that shows a value of 1 is resolved with a value of 2. A *push-move* is scaled in the same way (see §8.4)."
- Rationale: Names the icons affected and those left alone. The attack increase is likewise stated to cover each attack icon and the damage value of a push-move.
- Source: A-025

### CH-08-017 · §8.3 Enemy Scaling
- Type: completion
- Before: "Boss enemies (mini, main, and mega) do NOT modify their base health or receive scaling benefits otherwise."
- After: "Every enemy is scaled, with four exceptions. A *boss*, a *mimic*, a *phantom*, and a *crystal lizard* are never scaled (see §15.4; §13; §12; §14)."
- Rationale: The exemptions for invaders and crystal lizards covered health only and were worded as multiplication; the full list of unscaled models is stated in the owning section and the capitals are removed.
- Source: A-028, A-029, D-036, B-036

### CH-08-018 · §8.3 Enemy Scaling
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "In campaign play, the *bonfire ascetic* further increases the health of every enemy that is subject to enemy scaling (see §18.7). The number of characters in a campaign changes only when a player joins or leaves it (see §18.4)."
- Rationale: The ascetic's increase (health, not attack) and the effect of adding or dropping a player are owned by §18.7 and §18.4 and are cited, not restated.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-07; B-031, A-064, A-027

### CH-08-019 · §8.3 Enemy Scaling
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "*Example.* A Hollow Soldier's data card shows a base health of 1, a movement value of 1, and an attack value of 4. …"
- Rationale: A worked example walks one enemy through all four character counts and shows that a dead character still counts.
- Source: brief §2 (worked examples)

### CH-08-020 · §8.4 Pushing
- Type: completion
- Before: "Certain movements and attacks are labelled as push-movements and push-attacks (respectively). These special push effects may push a model from its current node to an adjacent node. Push movement does not cost stamina."
- After: "A *push* forces a model from its node to a *valid node* (see §5.2). A push is not a *move*, and it costs no stamina. … A model is pushed for one of four reasons."
- Rationale: Push, push-attack, and push-move are defined in the glossary's form, and all four causes of a push are listed in the owning section, including the node model limit (§5.3) and two boss models on one node (§17.1).
- Source: F-111; chapter 05 issues list; brief chapter instructions

### CH-08-021 · §8.4 Pushing
- Type: completion
- Before: "A model being pushed directly into a corner remains on the corner node."
- After: "A node containing a *gravestone*, a *chest*, or a *pillar token* is not a valid node for a push … If no valid node exists for a push, the push does not happen and the model stays on its node."
- Rationale: The push rule gave no outcome for blocked nodes other than a corner. Terrain, traps, and a full node are cited in their owning sections, and a push with no valid node is stated not to happen.
- Source: A-085

### CH-08-022 · §8.4 Pushing
- Type: clarification
- Before: "The pushed model(s) must move to the adjacent node most directly opposite of the attacking model. If there are multiple adjacent nodes equally opposite to the attacking model the players choose the node."
- After: "The model is pushed into the valid node most directly opposite the attacker's node, chosen from the adjacent nodes that are farther from the attacker than the model's own node."
- Rationale: The direction rule is kept and given its fallback: when the opposite node is blocked, the model goes to another valid node farther from the attacker, and when none is farther (the corner case) it is not pushed. "Farther" is defined by range.
- Source: A-085, F-111

### CH-08-023 · §8.4 Pushing
- Type: restructure
- Before: "If the push icon appears on an enemy's movement, first immediately move each character on that enemy's node onto an adjacent node chosen by the players. Then, the enemy moves one node …"
- After: "For each node of the movement, the following steps are resolved in order. 1. Push each character on the enemy's node to a valid node chosen by the players. 2. Move the enemy one node …"
- Rationale: The push-move paragraph becomes a numbered procedure resolved once for each node moved; "In other words" and the duplicate statement of the direction are removed.
- Source: C-120, F-091

### CH-08-024 · §8.4 Pushing
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "… resolve a physical attack with that *attack value* against each character on the node the enemy has entered, one character at a time." … "The characters pushed in step 1 are not attacked and suffer no damage."
- Rationale: The manuscript never said that the number on a push icon is an attack, nor whether characters on the enemy's starting node are damaged. The damage is an attack against the characters on each node entered; characters moved from the starting node are not attacked.
- Source: A-023, D-016, D-008

### CH-08-025 · §8.4 Pushing
- Type: style
- Before: "Players should take note that only enemy behaviours that deal damage can be dodged. … Of course, characters who block or resist a push behaviour are pushed regardless of damage dealt."
- After: "A push-move without a damage value is not an attack: a character cannot *dodge*, *block*, or *resist* it. … A character who blocks that attack is hit and is pushed whether or not damage is dealt."
- Rationale: The advisory frame and connectives are removed and the rules are stated one to a sentence.
- Source: F-015, C-021, C-123, F-091

### CH-08-026 · §8.4 Pushing
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Boss models cannot be pushed by a character's movement or attack. The only way a boss model can be pushed is if another boss model moves onto its node."
- After: "A boss model cannot be pushed by a character's attack. The only push that affects a boss model is the push caused by another boss model moving onto its node (see §17.1)."
- Rationale: Rewritten in voice with the owning section cited; the arc limit on a push off a boss's node and boss push-moves are cited in §15.6 and §16.2, and pushes between characters in §20.5.
- Source: F-111, A-032

### CH-08-027 · §8.4 Pushing
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: three *Example.* paragraphs (a push-attack with a gravestone behind the Knight; the Herald on a corner node; a push-move of two nodes with a damage value of 5)
- Rationale: Worked examples show the fallback node, the push that does not happen, and a push-move resolved node by node.
- Source: A-085; brief §2 (worked examples)

### CH-08-028 · §8.5 Conditions
- Type: restructure
- Before: "*Bleed* tokens are limited to one per model." … "*Bleed* tokens are not removed at the end of activations." … "*Bleed* tokens are only removed at the end of the encounter."
- After: "A model holds at most one bleed token, and the token alone has no effect. … A bleed token is not removed at the end of any turn."
- Rationale: The bullet fragments of all six conditions are rewritten as short paragraphs with a subject, a trigger, and an outcome; "activation" becomes turn; the sub-headings lose their italics and expansion tags.
- Source: C-119, F-085, C-039, C-029, C-030

### CH-08-029 · §8.5 Conditions
- Type: correction
- Before: "When a model ends its turn, remove one poison token and any *frostbite*/*stagger* tokens. Each condition has its own rules and effects listed below."
- After: "Where a token is removed at the end of a model's next complete *turn*, a token placed during that model's own turn is not removed at the end of that turn …"
- Rationale: The general removal sentence contradicted the poison rule and removed a parry's stagger before it had any effect. Each condition now states its own timing once, and "next complete turn" is explained once.
- Source: A-078, F-069, F-112; Gate 1 — OQ-16

### CH-08-030 · §8.5 Conditions
- Type: completion
- Before: "Some attacks inflict conditions on their target. When an attack has a condition icon, place the corresponding condition token on the model(s) hit by that attack."
- After: "The token is placed after the attack's damage and any *push* have been resolved, and it is placed whether or not the attack dealt damage." … "Any model may hold condition tokens …"
- Rationale: States when the token is placed, that bosses and phantoms hold conditions, how condition damage is recorded, that a reduced value stops at zero, and that all tokens are removed when the encounter ends.
- Source: F-112, B-038, C-063

### CH-08-031 · §8.5 Conditions
- Type: clarification
- Before: "*Stagger* tokens are limited to one per model."
- After: "When a token of a condition is to be placed on a model that already holds a token of that condition, the token is not placed and has no effect."
- Rationale: The one-token limit is stated once for every condition with its outcome, which also settles a second stagger from stamina overflow.
- Source: A-009

### CH-08-032 · §8.5 Conditions, Bleed
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If a second *bleed* token would be placed on a model, deal three damage and remove all *bleed* tokens from that model." … "*Bleed* damage is not affected by *block* or *resist*."
- After: "When a bleed token is to be placed on a model that already holds one, the token is not placed. … Bleed damage is not an attack: it is not reduced by *block* or *resist* …"
- Rationale: The mechanic is unchanged; the second token is stated not to be placed and the damage is stated not to be an attack. A worked example is added.
- Source: C-119, B-038

### CH-08-033 · §8.5 Conditions, Poison
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "A model suffers one damage for each *poison* token it has at the end of its activation, then removes only one *poison* token."
- After: "At the end of each of its turns, a model that holds poison tokens suffers one damage for each poison token it holds and then removes all of them."
- Rationale: A model with n poison tokens suffers n damage once and removes them all, in place of the triangular total of the manuscript. A worked example is added.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-13; B-025, C-039

### CH-08-034 · §8.5 Conditions, Frostbite
- Type: clarification
- Before: "*Frostbite* affected characters cannot recover stamina." … "*Frostbite* affected enemies reduce their movement values by one."
- After: "The character does not recover one stamina at the start of their turn and cannot take the *Rest* *major action* (see §10.1). … after *enemy scaling* has been applied (see §8.3; §9.2)."
- Rationale: Names the two recoveries that are blocked and fixes the order of the movement reduction and enemy scaling. Condition tokens are already removed before stamina cubes in §7.5, Encounter Recovery.
- Source: A-010

### CH-08-035 · §8.5 Conditions, Frostbite
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "*Frostbite* tokens are removed at the end of the model's turn."
- After: "A frostbite token is removed at the end of the affected model's next complete turn."
- Rationale: The approved fix for the removal timing covers stagger and frostbite alike; a frostbite token placed during a model's own turn (for example by a riposte) now lasts through that model's following turn. The outcome is unchanged for a token placed outside the model's turn.
- Source: A-078; Gate 1 — OQ-16

### CH-08-036 · §8.5 Conditions, Stagger
- Type: clarification
- Before: "*Staggered* models subtract one from *attack*, *block*, *resist*, and *dodge*."
- After: "An enemy, boss, or phantom with a stagger token reduces each of its *attack values*, its block and resist values, and its *dodge difficulty* by one."
- Rationale: Characters roll and enemies have fixed values, so the single sentence is stated once for each.
- Source: A-079

### CH-08-037 · §8.5 Conditions, Stagger
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "*Stagger* tokens are removed at the end of the model's turn."
- After: "A stagger token is removed at the end of the affected model's next complete turn."
- Rationale: A stagger placed by a parry during the enemy's own turn was removed at the end of that turn, before it had any effect. The causes of stagger outside attacks (stamina overflow, parry) are cited, and a worked example shows the parry case.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-16; A-078

### CH-08-038 · §8.5 Conditions, Calamity
- Type: clarification
- Before: "### *Calamity (*Kalameet Expansion*)*" … "*Calamity* tokens are only removed at the end of the encounter."
- After: "### Calamity" … "A calamity token is not removed at the end of any turn or when the model suffers damage. It is removed only when the encounter ends."
- Rationale: The encounter-long calamity is kept and is stated not to be removed by damage, so that it is not read as the corrosion rule. The expansion is named in the first sentence, not in the heading.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-24; A-080, C-029, A-115

### CH-08-039 · §8.5 Conditions, Corrosion
- Type: correction
- Before: "*Corrosion* tokens are removed if the affected character takes damage."
- After: "It is removed when the affected model next suffers damage, after that damage has been recorded."
- Rationale: "Character" broke the "model" wording used by every other condition; the token is stated to apply to the roll against the damaging attack before it is removed.
- Source: A-080, C-029

### CH-08-040 · §8 Combat Basics (style sweep)
- Type: style
- Before: curly apostrophes; "node.If"; "the enemies *block*"; "*Dodge* difficulty"; "condition marker"; "-&gt;"; "do NOT"; italicised fragments ("*repeat to*")
- After: straight quotes; corrected spacing and possessives; lower-case keywords italicised at first use in each section; "condition token"; no capitals for emphasis
- Rationale: Spelling, typography, and keyword italics follow the style sheet.
- Source: C-111, C-015, C-047, C-063, C-109, C-029, C-030, C-061

### CH-08-041 · §8 Combat Basics (findings not applied in this chapter)
- Type: clarification
- Before: n/a
- After: n/a
- Rationale: OPTIONAL findings skipped: A-081 (no supply limit on poison tokens is stated; the manuscript's "not limited" stands and substitutes serve when the seven tokens run out), B-036 (the optional boss health increase is not adopted; bosses never scale). Superseded by Gate 1: A-024 and B-005 (Repeat 2 is deleted by OQ-07, so its definition is moot), B-026 (OQ-24 keeps the encounter-long calamity). B-002's alternative (three highest dice with a shield) is not adopted because it alters the preserved two-highest-dice rule, which §9.4 owns. Owned by other chapters and only cited here: A-032 (§20.4, §20.5), A-115 (§17.8), B-031 and A-064 (§18.7), A-027 (§18.4), A-125 (§3.1 and the glossary).
- Source: A-081, B-036, A-024, B-005, B-026, B-002, A-032, A-115, B-031, A-064, A-027, A-125

### CH-08-042 · §8.2 Reading an Enemy Data Card
- Type: correction
- Before: "shows the number of times the behaviour is resolved (see §9.3)."
- After: "shows the number of times the attack beside it is resolved (see §9.3)."
- Rationale: §9.3 applies an enemy's repeat icon to the attack icon beside it only.
- Source: QA observation (example and consistency verification)

### CH-08-043 · §8.1
- Type: clarification
- Before: "A character targets enemies, bosses, and *invaders*."
- After: "A character targets hostile models: enemies, bosses, *invaders*, *mimics*, and *crystal lizards* (see §10.3)."
- Rationale: §8.1 and §10.3 gave two different lists of what a character may target.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E11 (`06_final_rulings.md`); This book, §10.3; §13; §14.

### CH-08-044 · §8.2
- Type: ruling
- Before: "- The *repeat* icon {{icon:ast-0061-repeat-behaviour-or-attack}} shows the number of times the attack beside it is resolved (see §9.3)."
- After: "- The *repeat* icon {{icon:ast-0061-repeat-behaviour-or-attack}} modifies no single icon: it shows the number of times the enemy's whole row of behaviour icons is resolved (see §9.1)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] One meaning for the repeat icon on every data card and behaviour card: the whole row is repeated.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E12 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official 2017: the enemy performs its entire behaviour (DS-Core-Rules-lores.txt 1441-1444); official 2022: entire row of behaviour symbols (DS-TG 2256-2264); this book, §16.1 step 4 and glossary Repeat.

### CH-08-045 · §8.4
- Type: correction
- Before: "A model is pushed for one of four reasons."
- After: "A model is pushed for one of the following reasons."
- Rationale: The list was not complete: a leap and other boss rules also push.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E13 (`06_final_rulings.md`); This book, §16.2, Leap; §17.8; §17.9.

### CH-08-046 · §8.4
- Type: correction
- Before: "- A *boss* model moves onto the node of another boss model (see §17.1)."
- After: "- A *boss* model moves onto the node of another boss model (see §17.1).
- A rule of a boss pushes the model, as a *leap* does (see §16.2, Leap; §17)."
- Rationale: Adds the pushes that the boss chapters already make.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E14 (`06_final_rulings.md`); This book, §16.2, Leap; §17.8; §17.9.

### CH-08-047 · §8.4
- Type: clarification
- Before: "The attack of a push-move with a damage value is defended as any other enemy attack (see §9.4; §9.5)."
- After: "The attack of a push-move with a damage value is defended by a block or a dodge, as any other enemy attack is (see §9.4; §9.5). No *parry* of it is permitted (see §9.6)."
- Rationale: §8.4 read as if a parry were allowed; §9.2 and §9.6 forbid it.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E15 (`06_final_rulings.md`); This book, §9.2 and §9.6.

### CH-08-048 · §8.5
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Each condition lasts until the rule under its heading removes it. Where a token is removed at the end of a model's next complete *turn*, a token placed"
- After: "Each condition lasts until the rule under its heading removes it. The end of the affected model's own *turn* is the only moment at which a turn acts on a condition: poison is then resolved, and frostbite and stagger tokens are then removed. Where a token is removed at the end of a model's next complete turn, a token placed"
- Rationale: States the one clock that poison, frostbite and stagger share, which step 3 of the character turn relies on.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E16 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official 2017 and 2022 resolve all three at the end of the model's own activation (DS-Core-Rules-lores.txt 968-971, 986-988; DS-TG 1483-1512).

### CH-08-049 · §8.5
- Type: clarification
- Before: "A value reduced by a condition cannot fall below zero."
- After: "A value reduced by a condition cannot fall below zero. When the *aggro token* is assigned, bleed damage counts as damage inflicted by the character whose attack triggered it, and poison damage counts for no character (see §7.5, Encounter Turn Order; §16.4)."
- Rationale: Settles which condition damage counts towards the aggro token without any new book-keeping.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E17 (`06_final_rulings.md`); No source addresses it; follows from when the damage is recorded.

### CH-08-050 · §8.3
- Type: clarification
- Before: "The number of characters is fixed at setup (see §6.1) and does not change when a character dies: a dead character and a *lost* character still count."
- After: "The number of characters is the number taking part in the game (see §6.1). It does not change when a character dies: a dead character and a *lost* character still count. It changes only when a player joins or leaves the game (see §18.4)."
- Rationale: A player may join or leave a standard game as well as a campaign (§18.4), so the count is not fixed at setup.
- Source: final rulings, lead-E05 (`06_final_rulings.md`); campaign memo, cross-cluster effects

### CH-08-051 · §8.3
- Type: clarification
- Before: " The number of characters in a campaign changes only when a player joins or leaves it (see §18.4)."
- After: ""
- Rationale: Now stated once, for both kinds of game (lead-E05).
- Source: final rulings, lead-E06 (`06_final_rulings.md`)

### CH-08-052 · §8.5, Stagger
- Type: correction
- Before: "A character with a stagger token subtracts one from the total of each attack roll and of each block, resist, and dodge roll that the character makes. An enemy, boss, or phantom with a stagger token reduces each of its *attack values*, its block and resist values, and its *dodge difficulty* by one."
- After: "A character or a *summon* with a stagger token subtracts one from the total of each attack roll and of each block, resist, and dodge roll that it makes. An enemy, boss, or *invader* with a stagger token reduces each of its *attack values*, its block and resist values, and its *dodge difficulty* by one."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] A summon rolls dice to attack and to defend, as a character does (§12.1), and has no fixed block, resist, or dodge value for the token to reduce.
- Source: final rulings, lead-E20 (`06_final_rulings.md`); review finding on the pull request, verified against §12.1

### CH-08-053 · §8.4
- Type: correction
- Before: "The only push that affects a boss model is the push caused by another boss model moving onto its node (see §17.1)."
- After: "The only push that affects a boss model is the push caused by another boss model that moves onto its node or is placed on it (see §17.1; §17.10, The Four Kings)."
- Rationale: Outside half B; consequential on VB-005, so that §8.4 does not forbid the push that the royal summons requires.
- Source: final rulings, VB-006 (`06_final_rulings.md`); independent verification, work/phase3/verify_b.md

### CH-08-054 · §8.1
- Type: correction
- Before: "bears the push icon and targets the Knight and the Herald, who share a node within its range."
- After: "bears the node icon and the push icon and targets the Knight and the Herald, who share a node within its range."
- Rationale: By §8.1 and §9.3 an enemy attack has one target unless it bears the node icon; the push icon adds none.
- Source: final rulings, VA-022 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-08-055 · §8.4
- Type: correction
- Before: "The enemy then moves its second node, which holds no character, and no model is pushed."
- After: "The enemy's movement targets the Herald, and its path turns at that node, so the second node it enters is not the node to which the Knight was pushed. That node holds no character, and no model is attacked or pushed."
- Rationale: By §9.2, Push-Move the enemy moves on towards its target, and a character pushed directly away along the enemy's path is entered and attacked again, as the example of §9.2 shows; the stated outcome needs a target and a path that the example did not give.
- Source: final rulings, VA-023 (`06_final_rulings.md`); second verification, chapters 1 to 11 (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

### CH-08-056 · §8.5, Frostbite
- Type: ruling
- Before: "An enemy, boss, or phantom with a frostbite token reduces the value of each of its movement icons by one, after *enemy scaling* has been applied (see §8.3; §9.2)."
- After: "An enemy, boss, or *invader* with a frostbite token reduces the value of each of its movement icons by one, after *enemy scaling* has been applied (see §8.3; §9.2). A *summon* with a frostbite token reduces by one the number of nodes of each *shift* icon on its behaviour cards (see §12.1)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] A summon has no stamina and no movement icons, so neither half of the frostbite rule reached it; the token now slows a summon as it slows every other model that is not a character.
- Source: final rulings, VA-024a (`06_final_rulings.md`); lead editor's decision on a judgement call of the second verification (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

## Chapter 09 — Enemy Turn

### CH-09-001 · §9 Enemy Turn
- Type: style
- Before: "# ENEMY TURN" / "## Overview" / "### *Block* & *Resist*" / "### *Parry* & *Riposte*"
- After: "# 9. Enemy Turn" with H2s 9.1 Overview, 9.2 Enemy Movement, 9.3 Enemy Attacks, 9.4 Block and Resist, 9.5 Dodge, 9.6 Parry and Riposte; a chapter intro of 48 words with no rule in it
- Rationale: Titles follow the section map. The three defences become numbered sections of their own, with no ampersand and no italics in headings, and every H3 is in Title Case.
- Source: C-101, C-104, C-112, C-047; brief §4

### CH-09-002 · §9.1 Overview
- Type: cut
- Before: "For enemies with equal threat levels, the enemy model closer to a character model goes first, if multiple enemy models are equidistant to a character model then the enemy model with more wound token's goes first."
- After: "The round as a whole, and the order between enemy models that share a threat value, are stated with the encounter turn order (see §7.5, Encounter Turn Order)."
- Rationale: The round structure and the turn-order tie-breaks are owned by §7.5. The chapter cites them once and does not restate them.
- Source: cut/simplify list; A-124, C-015, C-020, C-036, A-113; chapter 07 issues list

### CH-09-003 · §9.1 Overview
- Type: correction
- Before: "If there is no valid target or the enemy behaviour otherwise cannot be reasonably executed, that enemy behaviour is skipped and the next icon to the right on the enemy behaviour card is then resolved."
- After: "An icon that has no valid *target*, or that cannot be resolved, is skipped, and the next icon to the right is resolved."
- Rationale: The hedge is removed so that the condition is mechanical. "Behaviour card" is corrected: a non-boss enemy's icons are printed on its data card.
- Source: C-024, A-116, F-113

### CH-09-004 · §9.1 Overview
- Type: clarification
- Before: "An enemy model ends its turn once all of the icons on its behaviour card have been resolved."
- After: "The enemy's turn ends when its last icon has been resolved or when the enemy is killed."
- Rationale: A riposte may kill an enemy during its own turn (§9.6); the turn then ends.
- Source: A-119

### CH-09-005 · §9.1 Overview
- Type: restructure
- Before: "Non-boss enemies follow the behaviour icons on their data cards. Bosses follow the icons on their behaviour cards instead."
- After: "Invaders do not take their turns with the enemies: an *invader* takes a turn after each character's turn (see §12.2)."
- Rationale: Bosses, invaders, summons, and mimics are sent to their owning sections (§16, §12.2, §12.1, §13) in one sentence each. The invader's cadence is a preserved rule.
- Source: preserve list; chapter instructions

### CH-09-006 · §9.1 Overview
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "Enemies target only characters; an enemy never targets another enemy (see §8.1). An enemy's health, movement, and attack values are scaled for the number of characters (see §8.3)."
- Rationale: Targets, scaling, and the conditions that alter an enemy's icons are cited to their owning sections instead of being left implied.
- Source: A-079; brief §5

### CH-09-007 · §9.2 Enemy Movement
- Type: style
- Before: "The enemy movement icon shows how an enemy model will move. Various smaller icons can appear around the movement icon, modifying how the enemy moves."
- After: "The number printed in the arrow is the number of *nodes* the enemy moves. An arrow that points upwards moves the enemy towards the target; an arrow that points downwards moves it away from the target."
- Rationale: Copied explanatory prose and the future tense are replaced by the rule in the book's voice.
- Source: F-114, F-088

### CH-09-008 · §9.2 Enemy Movement
- Type: completion
- Before: "If two or more characters are tied for being nearest, the enemy moves towards the character with the aggro token." / "the enemy moves towards the character with the higher threat level."
- After: "**Nearest character.** The living character at the shortest *range* from the enemy (see §5.4). If two or more characters are tied for nearest, the nearest character is the one of them who holds the aggro token."
- Rationale: "Nearest" is defined once, by range and for living characters only, and serves movement and attacks alike. The manuscript's tie-break is kept; the players choose only between tied characters of equal threat, a case the manuscript left open.
- Source: D-008, A-023, F-116; chapter instructions

### CH-09-009 · §9.2 Enemy Movement (Paths)
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "When the icon moves the enemy towards its target, the enemy follows the shortest path of valid nodes from its own node to the target's node."
- Rationale: The manuscript did not say what an enemy does when the node its icon points to is blocked. The enemy now moves around blocking terrain by the shortest valid path and does not move when no path exists, as §5.5 requires of this section.
- Source: A-082; chapter 05 issues list; chapter instructions

### CH-09-010 · §9.2 Enemy Movement (Paths)
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If an enemy movement icon instructs an enemy to move away from a character that is already at maximum distance, then it simply does not move."
- After: "When the icon moves the enemy away from its target, each node the enemy enters must be a valid node at a greater range from the target than the node it leaves."
- Rationale: "Maximum distance" becomes a test on each node entered, measured in range, and the filler word is removed.
- Source: F-090, C-123

### CH-09-011 · §9.2 Enemy Movement (Paths)
- Type: style
- Before: "When two different nodes would take an enemy closer to where it is trying to go, the players can choose which node the enemy enters."
- After: "If two or more such paths are equally short, the players choose which path the enemy follows."
- Rationale: A verbatim official sentence is rewritten, and "can" is not used for a permission.
- Source: F-115

### CH-09-012 · §9.2 Enemy Movement (Paths)
- Type: restructure
- Before: new text
- After: "An enemy never leaves the tile and never passes through a *doorway* (see §5.2)."
- Rationale: The tile boundary, the node model limit, traps, scaling, and frostbite are owned by other sections; each is cited in one sentence.
- Source: chapter 05 issues list; brief §5

### CH-09-013 · §9.2 Enemy Movement (Towards Aggro, Away from Aggro, Towards Nearest, Away from Nearest)
- Type: style
- Before: "The enemy moves the stated number of nodes away from to the nearest character."
- After: "The enemy moves the number of nodes shown away from the nearest character. An enemy with no valid node farther from that character does not move."
- Rationale: The four sub-sections take Title Case and one wording. The typographical error and the verbatim official sentences are removed, and the tie-break moves to the definition of the nearest character.
- Source: C-101, F-116, C-015

### CH-09-014 · §9.2 Enemy Movement (Push-Move)
- Type: completion
- Before: "If the Movement icon includes the push icon, the enemy's movement pushes characters out of its way."
- After: "A push-move that shows no damage value is not an attack. The characters it pushes are not *hit*, and they cannot *block*, *dodge*, or *parry* it (see §8.4)."
- Rationale: The icon is defined here: the target is fixed before any character is pushed, and the enemy follows the target's current node. The push steps are owned by §8.4 and are cited, not restated. The rule that only a damaging behaviour may be dodged is stated with the icon it concerns.
- Source: F-117, A-023; manuscript Pushing (lines 1594–1596)

### CH-09-015 · §9.2 Enemy Movement (Push-Move with a Damage Value)
- Type: completion
- Before: "If the push icon includes a number, characters pushed out of the way suffer that amount of damage during the push."
- After: "Each time the enemy enters a node, it makes a physical attack against every character on that node before those characters are pushed (see §8.4)."
- Rationale: The damage number is defined as a physical attack that is defended as any attack is. Characters on the starting node are pushed but not attacked, and the value is scaled as an attack value is. The manuscript's sub-heading "Push-attack" is retitled, because §8.4 and the glossary keep that term for an attack icon that bears the push icon.
- Source: A-023, D-016, A-025

### CH-09-016 · §9.3 Enemy Attacks (Targets)
- Type: completion
- Before: "Additional symbols beside the attack type icon modify the attack with special rules detailed later."
- After: "If the target is not within the attack's range, the attack misses: the icon is skipped and has no effect. No other character is attacked in the target's place."
- Rationale: Enemy attack targeting was never defined. The skull icon, the nearest-character icon, the node icon, and the out-of-range miss are now stated, and the dangling pointer is replaced by section references.
- Source: A-023, D-008, F-070, C-083, C-084

### CH-09-017 · §9.3 Enemy Attacks
- Type: clarification
- Before: "The number in the centre of the attack icon is the enemy's attack value."
- After: "An enemy's attack value is fixed and is not rolled. It is scaled for the number of characters (see §8.3) and is reduced while the enemy holds a *stagger* token (see §8.5, Stagger)."
- Rationale: The two things that change a printed attack value are cited where the value is defined, and the attack's range is cited to the data card, as §5.4 expects.
- Source: A-079; chapter 05 §5.4

### CH-09-018 · §9.3 Enemy Attacks
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "A repeat icon applies only to the attack icon beside it, not to a movement icon."
- Rationale: A printed repeat icon cannot multiply a push-move. Repeat 2 is no longer added by scaling.
- Source: B-005; Gate 1 — OQ-07

### CH-09-019 · §9.3 Enemy Attacks (Defence)
- Type: restructure
- Before: "Characters may *block*, *resist*, *dodge*, *parry*, or do nothing. Since the enemy attack values are fixed, the character's defensive dice rolls determine the ultimate result of enemy attacks. More on each defensive option listed below."
- After: "When an attack targets a character, the character's player must choose one defence before any dice are rolled. The choice cannot be changed once it is made."
- Rationale: The choice is given a moment and made binding. "Do nothing" is dropped because a block with no dice has the same result. The commentary and the prose pointer are cut.
- Source: F-071, C-084

### CH-09-020 · §9.3 Enemy Attacks (Defence)
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "A character is hit by an attack unless the dodge or the parry succeeds, even if the attack deals no damage (see §8.1)."
- Rationale: The hit, damage cubes, the embered reduction, the attacks of bosses, invaders, and mimics, and PvP are tied to their owning sections.
- Source: A-032; brief §5

### CH-09-021 · §9.4 Block and Resist
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Determine the defence total by adding the number of sword pips on the highest two dice results. Additional dice beyond the two with the highest shown face value are ignored."
- After: "Add together the two highest results. If one die was rolled, its result is the sum. If no dice were rolled, the sum is zero."
- Rationale: The preserved two-highest-dice rule is kept and its two edge cases are stated.
- Source: preserve list; chapter instructions

### CH-09-022 · §9.4 Block and Resist
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "A fixed modifier is applied after the two highest results are added, whether or not a die of its card is among the two highest."
- Rationale: The manuscript defined dice modifiers for attacks only. The point at which a defence modifier applies is now fixed, as §4.1 expects of this section.
- Source: D-033, A-120; chapter 04 issues list

### CH-09-023 · §9.4 Block and Resist
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Gather all dice of the appropriate type noted on the character's currently equipped armour, weapon(s), and shield(s)."
- After: "Cards in the *back-up slot* and in the *inventory* provide no dice (see §3.2). A *spell* stored in a *catalyst* provides no dice (see §4.3)."
- Rationale: The cards that provide dice are named: every equipped card with its attached upgrade cards, and nothing else.
- Source: A-070; chapter 03 §3.2

### CH-09-024 · §9.4 Block and Resist
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If the enemy's attack value is less than or equal to the character's defence value, the character takes no damage."
- After: "A character who blocks or resists is *hit* by the attack, whatever the defence total (see §8.1)."
- Rationale: "Defence value" becomes "defence total". A full block still counts as a hit, and a total of less than zero is treated as zero.
- Source: C-036; brief §3

### CH-09-025 · §9.5 Dodge
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Characters may spend two additional stamina (total of three stamina cost) before rolling dice to add a single additional *dodge* die to their roll, up to a maximum of four *dodge* dice."
- After: "The character may pay two further stamina to add one green die. This may be done once for each dodge, and no dodge is rolled with more than four dice."
- Rationale: The preserved cost is kept. The option is its own step and is taken before the roll.
- Source: preserve list; C-095

### CH-09-026 · §9.5 Dodge
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If the enemy dodge difficulty is zero, the character does not need to roll dice and the dodge automatically succeeds, even if they have no dice to roll."
- After: "A character whose equipped cards show no dodge dice may still attempt a dodge. If no dice are rolled, the count is zero, and the dodge fails unless the dodge difficulty is zero."
- Rationale: Both zero cases are stated, and a dodge is open only to a character with an empty space for the stamina cube (§3.4).
- Source: chapter instructions; chapter 03 §3.4

### CH-09-027 · §9.5 Dodge (Successful Dodge)
- Type: clarification
- Before: "The character must move to an adjacent node."
- After: "The character must then move to an *adjacent node* that is a *valid node*, chosen by the character's player (see §5.2). If no valid node exists, the character stays on their node."
- Rationale: The mandatory dodge movement gains its fallback. Its effect on barrels, traps, and the node model limit is cited, and it is stated not to be a move.
- Source: A-087; chapter 05 §5.2, §5.5

### CH-09-028 · §9.5 Dodge (Failed Dodge)
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Determine damage to the defending character as above."
- After: "The roll is resolved from step 2 of the block and resist procedure (see §9.4)"
- Rationale: The prose pointer becomes a section reference. The armour-only roll is stated to include the upgrade cards attached to the armour card.
- Source: F-072, C-084

### CH-09-029 · §9.5 Dodge (Traps)
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A trap is dodged under the rules of this section, against the dodge difficulty shown on its trap token (see §5.5, Traps)."
- Rationale: §5.5 sends the trap dodge to this section; the section now receives it.
- Source: chapter 05 §5.5; chapter instructions

### CH-09-030 · §9.5 Dodge
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "Only an attack that targets the character may be dodged; a *push-move* that shows no damage value is not an attack (see §9.2, Push-Move)."
- Rationale: The section states what may be dodged and defines the dodge difficulty in one place for enemies, bosses, and traps.
- Source: A-023; manuscript Pushing (line 1596)

### CH-09-031 · §9.6 Parry and Riposte
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Characters must be equipped with a shield as their left weapon."
- After: "A shield in the right weapon slot does not permit a parry. A character who wields a weapon *two-handed* has no shield equipped and cannot parry (see §10.4)."
- Rationale: The preserved requirement is tied to the named left weapon slot of §3.2, and the two cases the manuscript left open are closed.
- Source: A-068 (in part), D-030, B-022; chapter 03 issues list

### CH-09-032 · §9.6 Parry and Riposte
- Type: clarification
- Before: "*Push, area-of-effect,* and *boss-arc* attacks cannot be *parried*."
- After: "The attack made by a *push-move* that shows a damage value (see §9.2, Push-Move with a Damage Value)."
- Rationale: The excluded attacks are named in the book's terms: a push-attack, the attack of a push-move with a damage value, a node attack, and a boss's attack except where §15.6 allows.
- Source: C-027; chapter instructions

### CH-09-033 · §9.6 Parry and Riposte
- Type: restructure
- Before: "1. Pay one stamina to attempt to *parry* an attack. 2. Determine if current enemy attack can be *parried*."
- After: "A parry is permitted only if both of the following are true."
- Rationale: The conditions are tested before the stamina is paid. The procedure states that the extra die of a dodge is not available to a parry.
- Source: brief §2 (procedures)

### CH-09-034 · §9.6 Parry and Riposte (Riposte)
- Type: clarification
- Before: "The character may make one *riposte* attack out of turn against the attacker using any *attack option* on the weapon equipped to the right weapon slot after paying the associated stamina cost."
- After: "It is not the character's *Attack* major action, and it does not count against the attacks the character makes in their own turn (see §10.3)."
- Rationale: A riposte is an attack in every respect but two: it is made in the attacker's turn and it lies outside the character's own allowance. The stagger token is placed first, an enemy cannot dodge or parry a riposte, a riposte against a character is left to §20.4, and a killed attacker's turn ends.
- Source: A-119, A-078; Gate 1 — OQ-16

### CH-09-035 · §9.6 Parry and Riposte (Failed Parry)
- Type: style
- Before: "The character takes full damage without the option to block*/*resist."
- After: "When a parry fails, the character is hit and suffers damage equal to the full *attack value*. No block or resist dice are rolled."
- Rationale: Split italics are repaired. The failed parry is stated as a hit, so the attack's conditions apply.
- Source: C-028

### CH-09-036 · §9.2 to §9.6 (worked examples)
- Type: completion
- Before: new text (the manuscript chapter has no worked example)
- After: 11 examples: two for movement, one for a push-move with a damage value, two for targets, two for block and resist, two for the dodge, and two for the parry and riposte
- Rationale: Each procedure is shown with concrete numbers, succeeding once and failing once where it has two outcomes. A dodge example and a parry example were required. A named enemy is used only where its values match the example in §8.3; elsewhere the examples say "an enemy", so that no example reads as card data.
- Source: chapter instructions; brief §2

### CH-09-037 · §9 Enemy Turn (style sweep)
- Type: style
- Before: "wound token's"; "*Block* & *Resist*"; "flip their *luck* token face-down"; "it simply does not move"; "how an enemy model will move"; curly apostrophes
- After: "wound tokens"; "Block and Resist"; "flip the *luck* token"; "does not move"; present tense; straight quotes
- Rationale: Spelling, typography, tense, hedge words, and the italics policy (a headword italicised at its first use in each H2, the whole word, no punctuation inside) are applied throughout.
- Source: C-015, C-027, C-028, C-030, C-047, C-060, C-111, C-123, F-088, F-089, F-090

### CH-09-038 · §9 Enemy Turn (findings not applied here)
- Type: clarification
- Before: n/a
- After: not applied here
- Rationale: OPTIONAL findings skipped: D-039 (restoring "taunt" for characters conflicts with the single term "threat"); A-018 (riposte and boss aggro, owned by §16.4); A-042 (heat-up during the boss's own turn, owned by §15.5). Approved PROPOSED findings not applied: B-002 and B-033, which alter preserve-list rules. Owned elsewhere and cited only: A-013 (§7.5), A-032 (§20.4), A-078 and A-079 (§8.5), B-022 (§4.1). No change required: A-124, C-095.
- Source: brief §5; preserve list; chapter 09 issues list

### CH-09-039 · §9.1
- Type: ruling
- Before: "An icon that has no valid *target*, or that cannot be resolved, is skipped, and the next icon to the right is resolved. The enemy's turn ends when its last icon has been resolved or when the enemy is killed."
- After: "An icon that has no valid *target*, or that cannot be resolved, is skipped, and the next icon to the right is resolved. If the data card bears the *repeat* icon {{icon:ast-0061-repeat-behaviour-or-attack}}, the enemy resolves its whole row of icons the number of times shown, and the target of each icon is determined again each time. The enemy's turn ends when its last icon has been resolved for the last time or when the enemy is killed."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] States the repeat rule for an enemy data card where the row of icons is described, in the words §16.1 uses for a boss.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E18 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As combat-E12.

### CH-09-040 · §9.3
- Type: ruling
- Before: "- A *condition* icon places the matching condition token on each character hit by the attack (see §8.5).
- A *repeat* icon {{icon:ast-0061-repeat-behaviour-or-attack}} causes the attack to be resolved the number of times shown, and the target is determined afresh each time. A repeat icon applies only to the attack icon beside it, not to a movement icon."
- After: "- A *condition* icon places the matching condition token on each character hit by the attack (see §8.5).

A *repeat* icon {{icon:ast-0061-repeat-behaviour-or-attack}} on the data card repeats the enemy's whole row of icons, not one attack (see §9.1)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Deletes the attack-only special case, which was written for the scaling rule that Gate 1 removed.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E19 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As combat-E12; Gate 1 OQ-07.

### CH-09-041 · §9.3
- Type: clarification
- Before: "### Defence

When an attack targets a character, the character's player must choose one defence before any dice are rolled."
- After: "### Attack Sequence

An enemy attack is resolved as follows.

1. Determine the target (see §9.3, Targets).
2. Check the range. If the target is not within range, skip the icon.
3. Choose the character's defence (see §9.3, Defence).
4. Resolve the defence (see §9.4; §9.5; §9.6). If a dodge or a parry succeeds, the character is not hit, and the attack on that character ends.
5. Place the damage that the defence has not prevented (see §3.4).
6. If the attack is a push-attack, push the character (see §8.4).
7. Place any condition token the attack carries (see §8.5).

Steps 6 and 7 are resolved even if the character suffered no damage. A character who dies in step 5 is not pushed and receives no token. A node attack resolves steps 3 to 7 for one character at a time, in an order chosen by the players. The attack of a push-move with a damage value follows the same steps from […]"
- Rationale: States the order of an enemy attack once, as §10.3 does for a character attack, so that §8.4, §8.5, §9.4, §9.5, §9.6 and §16.3 have one sequence to match.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E20 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official 2022 numbered attack steps (DS-TG 1667-1763); this book, §8.4 and §8.5 (damage, then push, then condition).

### CH-09-042 · §9.4
- Type: correction
- Before: "7. Place any *condition* token the attack carries (see §8.5) and resolve any *push* (see §8.4), even if the character suffered no damage."
- After: "7. Resolve any *push* (see §8.4), then place any *condition* token the attack carries (see §8.5), even if the character suffered no damage."
- Rationale: Step 7 gave the condition before the push; §8.5 and §10.3 give the push first, and the order decides whether a stagger token affects the dodge of a trap entered by the push.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E21 (`06_final_rulings.md`); This book, §8.5 second paragraph and §10.3 step 11.

### CH-09-043 · §9.6
- Type: ruling
- Before: "- A *shield* is equipped in the character's left *weapon slot* (see §3.2; §4.1)."
- After: "- A *shield* is equipped in one of the character's *weapon slots* (see §3.2; §4.1)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] The parry was the only rule in the book that depended on which weapon slot held a card; the choice of slot carried no decision.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E22 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Audit finding A-068 (approved, REQUIRED) proposed the same; no official or community ruleset has a left-hand rule.

### CH-09-044 · §9.6
- Type: ruling
- Before: "A shield in the right weapon slot does not permit a parry. A character who wields"
- After: "The shield may be in either weapon slot. A character who wields"
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] As combat-E22.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E23 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As combat-E22.

### CH-09-045 · §9.6, Riposte
- Type: ruling
- Before: "A riposte is one attack against the attacker, made with one *attack option* of the *weapon* equipped in the character's right weapon slot."
- After: "A riposte is one attack against the attacker, made with one *attack option* of the *weapon* equipped in the character's other weapon slot, the slot that does not hold the shield used for the parry. A *spell* cannot be used for a riposte, whether it is equipped in that slot or stored in a *catalyst* there (see §4.3)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Follows the either-slot parry and states the spell exclusion that the word "weapon" only implied.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E24 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As combat-E22; this book, §4.1 and §4.3.

### CH-09-046 · §9.6, Riposte
- Type: ruling
- Before: "No riposte is made if the right weapon slot holds no weapon with an attack option,"
- After: "No riposte is made if the other weapon slot holds no weapon with an attack option,"
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] As combat-E22.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E25 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As combat-E22.

### CH-09-047 · §9.6
- Type: clarification
- Before: "A character who wields a weapon *two-handed* has no shield equipped and cannot parry (see §10.4)."
- After: "A character who wields a weapon other than a shield *two-handed* has no shield equipped and cannot parry (see §10.4)."
- Rationale: A shield equipped alone is itself wielded two-handed, and the rule that a shield in either weapon slot parries governs; no riposte follows, because the other slot holds no weapon.
- Source: final rulings, VA-025a (`06_final_rulings.md`); lead editor's decision on a judgement call of the second verification (work/phase3/verify_a.md)

## Chapter 12 — Phantoms

### CH-12-001 · §12 Phantoms
- Type: restructure
- Before: "# SUMMONS" / "## Phantoms" / "## Invaders"
- After: "# 12. Phantoms" / "## 12.1 Summons" / "## 12.2 Invaders"; "**Phantom.** A *summon* or an *invader*."
- Rationale: The manuscript used "phantom" both for the superset and for its friendly subset, and filed invaders under a chapter called "Summons". Phantom is now the umbrella term, a summon is friendly, and an invader hostile; the chapter and its sections are renamed to match.
- Source: C-064, C-103, C-104; Gate 1 — OQ-31

### CH-12-002 · §12 Phantoms
- Type: style
- Before: new text
- After: "*Summons* are an optional rule. They require the Phantoms expansion. The party decides during setup whether summons are in play."
- Rationale: The chapter gains an intro that holds no rule, and each of its two sections opens with the fixed optional-rule formula, because §6.5 lets the party adopt summons and invaders separately.
- Source: C-124; brief §2

### CH-12-003 · §12.1 Summons, Summoning
- Type: clarification
- Before: "*Embered* characters may elect to pay two *souls* to draw a phantom card. ... Draw a random phantom card of appropriate level. ... Players must agree on only one of the drawn phantoms to summon."
- After: "Each embered character may pay two *souls*. A character pays no more than once. ... Choose one of the drawn data cards. The summon on that card joins the encounter. ... Return every other drawn data card to the supply."
- Rationale: The number of cards drawn and the fate of the unused cards were unstated. One card is drawn per paying character, one summon is chosen, the rest return, and the souls stay paid. The payment is named as an exception to the rule that souls are spent only at the bonfire.
- Source: A-100, CMP-0018

### CH-12-004 · §12.1 Summons, Summoning
- Type: completion
- Before: "*Embered* characters may summon a random friendly phantom at the fog gate prior to encountering a mini-boss or main-boss."
- After: "Each time the characters are about to pass through the *fog gate* into a mini-boss, main-boss, or mega-boss encounter (see §15.1), the embered characters may call a summon as follows."
- Rationale: The opening sentence omitted the mega-boss that the list beneath it allowed. Summoning is tied to the fog gate and is available at every attempt on a boss.
- Source: CMP-0018

### CH-12-005 · §12.1 Summons, Summoning
- Type: completion
- Before: "Shuffle all of the phantom's behaviour cards to form the behaviour deck."
- After: "Take the chosen summon's model, its four *behaviour cards*, and the summon *health dial*. ... Shuffle the four behaviour cards and place them face down as the summon's *behaviour deck*."
- Rationale: The deck is all four of the summon's cards, and the step that sets the health dial was missing.
- Source: CMP-0018

### CH-12-006 · §12.1 Summons, Summoning
- Type: correction
- Before: "Place the chosen phantom's model on the boss tile's entrance node after the characters have placed their character models on their starting node."
- After: "When the character models have been placed on the *entry nodes* of the *boss tile* (see §15.2), place the summon model on an entry node that holds fewer than three models."
- Rationale: "Entrance node" and "starting node" are the defined entry node, and the node model limit decides which entry node may take the summon. The step also answers where a summon arrives, which §5.2 leaves to this chapter.
- Source: C-062, C-033, CMP-0018; chapter 05 issues list
