### CH-12-007 · §12.1 Summons, Summon Turns
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "Phantoms take their turn after all character turns are resolved and before the first enemy turn begins."
- After: "The summon takes a turn after each character's turn and before the boss's next turn. The summon takes no turn after a boss's turn."
- Rationale: Summons appear only in boss encounters, where boss and character turns alternate and no phase "after all character turns" exists. Gate 1 fixed the cadence at one summon turn after each character's turn.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-32; A-020, D-002

### CH-12-008 · §12.1 Summons, Summon Turns
- Type: correction
- Before: "Each phantom turn, draw and resolve the top card of the phantom behaviour deck.i ... After each icon on the encounter card is resolved in left to right order, the phantom's turn ends."
- After: "If the summon's behaviour deck is empty, turn its discard pile face down, without shuffling, to form a new behaviour deck. ... Resolve each icon on the behaviour card in order from left to right."
- Rationale: The stray "i" is removed and the wrong card name corrected: the icons are on the behaviour card. The renewal of an empty deck, which the manuscript never stated for a summon, is added.
- Source: C-107, C-065, A-099, A-016, CMP-0007, CMP-0018

### CH-12-009 · §12.1 Summons, Summon Turns
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A summon that attacks a boss from a node in the boss's *weak arc* ... adds one black die ... to the attack roll (see §15.6)." / "**Distract.** An icon found only on summon behaviour cards."
- Rationale: The manuscript adopted the summon components without the rules that make their behaviour cards usable: how an attack is resolved, the weak-arc die, and the Shift and Distract icons.
- Source: CMP-0018, A-016, D-010

### CH-12-010 · §12.1 Summons, Summons in Combat
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The boss's behaviours treat a summon as a character. A summon is counted when a behaviour *targets* the nearest character or compares threat (see §16.3)."
- Rationale: A summon with a health dial must be attackable. Targeting, pushes, conditions, defence with the two highest dice, the free dodge to an adjacent valid node, and the rule that a summon never holds the aggro token are stated.
- Source: A-016, D-010, CMP-0018

### CH-12-011 · §12.1 Summons, Summons in Combat
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "An ability that is used once per encounter may be used at any moment the players choose. When it is used, a *wound token* is placed on the data card to mark the ability as used."
- Rationale: The data card's special ability was adopted with the component but never given a rule.
- Source: CMP-0018, D-010

### CH-12-012 · §12.1 Summons, Summons in Combat
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Summons neither trigger traps nor suffer them (see §5.5, Traps)."
- Rationale: §5.5 limits traps to characters. The summon's exclusion is repeated as a pointer in the section where a reader looks for it.
- Source: chapter 05 issues list; chapter instructions

### CH-12-013 · §12.1 Summons, Removal of a Summon
- Type: correction
- Before: "Phantoms are removed from play if their health dial is reduced to or the encounter ends."
- After: "A summon is removed from play when its health dial is reduced to zero or when the encounter ends."
- Rationale: The truncated sentence is completed with the missing word "zero".
- Source: CMP-0007, A-099

### CH-12-014 · §12.1 Summons, Removal of a Summon
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The encounter is not *failed* by the death of a summon, and no character becomes *hollow* because of it."
- Rationale: The consequence of a summon's death was unstated; a summon is not a character, so the death and hollowing rules of §7.2 do not apply to it.
- Source: CMP-0018, D-010

### CH-12-015 · §12.1 Summons
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "*Example.* The Assassin and the Knight fight the Gargoyle with Eygon of Carim as their summon. The Gargoyle takes its turn, then the Assassin, then Eygon."
- Rationale: Two worked examples are added, one for the cost of summoning and one for the turn cadence. Neither states a rule.
- Source: A-100, A-020, D-002

### CH-12-016 · §12.2 Invaders, Invasions
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "*Embered* characters provoke an invader if no invasion has occurred yet."
- After: "No more than one invasion is provoked before the mini-boss is defeated, and no more than one after it."
- Rationale: "If no invasion has occurred yet" did not say whether the limit was one per game or one per leg. Gate 1 fixed it at one before the mini-boss and one after.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-28; A-045

### CH-12-017 · §12.2 Invaders, Invasions
- Type: completion
- Before: "*Embered* characters provoke an invader if no invasion has occurred yet."
- After: "At setup: when the *encounter cards* are dealt to the tiles (see §6.5; §6.6), if one or more characters are embered." / "The embering of every character at a boss's defeat (see §16.5) provokes no invasion at that moment."
- Rationale: A boss's defeat embers every character at a moment when the old encounter cards are about to be removed and the new ones are not yet dealt. The two moments of provocation give §6.5 and §6.6 the rule they cite.
- Source: A-045; chapter 06 issues list

### CH-12-018 · §12.2 Invaders, Invader Tokens
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Shuffle enough blank invader tokens with the selected invader token so there are equal tokens to face-down encounter cards."
- After: "Add blank invader tokens, face down, until there is one token for each face-down encounter card on the encounter tiles."
- Rationale: The placing of tokens is a numbered procedure with one action per step. What becomes of a blank token, a revealed invader token, and an unrevealed token at a reset, a skip, or the mini-boss's defeat is stated.
- Source: C-120, A-045

### CH-12-019 · §12.2 Invaders, Invader Tokens
- Type: clarification
- Before: "When the characters move their models onto an encounter tile with a face-down invader token, flip the invader token face-up before revealing the encounter card."
- After: "The token on an encounter card is revealed during Encounter Start, after the enemy models and *terrain* have been placed (see §7.5, Encounter Start)."
- Rationale: The manuscript revealed the token before the encounter card and then placed the invader "after the encounter card setup is complete". The reveal now follows the step that §7.5 gives it, so the two passages agree.
- Source: CMP-0007; §7.5, Encounter Start

### CH-12-020 · §12.2 Invaders, Invader Tokens
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "No invader appears in a *boss* encounter, and none takes part in a mimic encounter (see §13)."
- Rationale: The exclusion followed from the placing of tokens on encounter cards but was never stated. A pointer to the two campaigns that place invaders by name is added.
- Source: A-021

### CH-12-021 · §12.2 Invaders, Invader Tokens
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "*Example.* The Herald discards an ember card while two face-down encounter cards lie on the tiles ahead, before the mini-boss is defeated."
- Rationale: A worked example of an invasion provoked during the exploration. It states no rule.
- Source: A-045

### CH-12-022 · §12.2 Invaders, Invader Setup
- Type: correction
- Before: "deal a number equal to their starting decks deck size to create the invader behaviour deck" / "Set the invader heal dial to the invader's starting health value."
- After: "Deal a number of behaviour cards equal to the behaviour deck size ... on the data card, face down, to form the invader's *behaviour deck*."
- Rationale: Two typographical defects in a procedure: the deck size is the value printed on the data card, and the component is the health dial.
- Source: A-098, C-015, C-037, CMP-0007

### CH-12-023 · §12.2 Invaders, Invader Setup
- Type: correction
- Before: "If the central node is blocked by train, then play the invader model in a node adjacent to the centre node of the players' choosing."
- After: "If terrain blocks the central node (see §5.5), place the model instead on a node adjacent to the central node that no terrain blocks, chosen by the players."
- Rationale: "Train" is terrain and "play" is place. The sentence that an invader is placed directly on its node answers the arrival question that §5.2 leaves to this chapter.
- Source: A-098, C-015, CMP-0007; chapter 05 issues list

### CH-12-024 · §12.2 Invaders, Invader Turns
- Type: cut
- Before: "Thus, the invader will take many more turns than any other model in the encounter."
- After: deleted
- Rationale: Commentary on a consequence; the rule is complete without it. Steps 1 and 2 of the manuscript's Invader Combat stated the same cadence twice and are merged.
- Source: F-022, F-091, C-122

### CH-12-025 · §12.2 Invaders, Invader Turns
- Type: correction
- Before: "After each icon on the encounter card is resolved in left to right order, the invader's turn ends."
- After: "Resolve each icon on the behaviour card in order from left to right, as the icons of a boss's behaviour card are resolved (see §16.2; §16.3)."
- Rationale: The wrong card was named. The renewal of an empty deck is added, as for the summon.
- Source: C-065, A-099, CMP-0007

### CH-12-026 · §12.2 Invaders, Invader Turns
- Type: clarification
- Before: "When the invader is reduced to its Heat Up value, add an unused invader behaviour card to the behaviour deck and shuffle."
- After: "When an invader's health is reduced to or below the *heat-up* point ... on its data card, one of its unused behaviour cards, taken at random, is shuffled into its behaviour deck in place of a heat-up card (see §15.5)."
- Rationale: The heat-up is worded as the mimic's is and names the heat-up point printed on the data card. The absence of arcs, and so of the weak-arc die, is stated.
- Source: C-097, C-057, C-033, A-044, CMP-0007

### CH-12-027 · §12.2 Invaders, Combat with an Invader
- Type: correction
- Before: "Invaders do not multiply their health by the number of characters like other enemy models."
- After: "An invader is not scaled. Neither enemy scaling (see §8.3) nor the *bonfire ascetic* (see §18.7) changes its health or its attacks."
- Rationale: Enemy scaling adds; it does not multiply. The manuscript's exemption covered health only, although the bonfire ascetic already spared invaders, which shows the intent.
- Source: A-028, A-029, D-036

### CH-12-028 · §12.2 Invaders, Combat with an Invader
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If any character dies, the invader immediately vanishes without award."
- After: "If any character dies while an invader is on the tile, the invader vanishes at once. ... The encounter continues against the remaining enemy models, and the character's death is resolved as any other (see §7.2)."
- Rationale: The effect on the encounter of the invader's departure was unstated. The mechanic is unchanged.
- Source: preserve list; C-120

### CH-12-029 · §12.2 Invaders, Invader Rewards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "The invader dies if their health dial is reduced to zero. Immediately award *souls* and all invader treasures when the invader dies."
- After: "When an invader is killed, every character gains *souls* equal to the invader reward at once, in addition to the reward of the encounter (see §7.6)."
- Rationale: Who receives the souls and the treasure card was unstated. Every character gains the invader reward of §7.6; the treasure card goes to the party, with the aggro holder as tie-break. Enemies are killed; characters die.
- Source: C-061; Gate 1 — OQ-29; chapter 07 issues list

### CH-12-030 · §12.2 Invaders, End of an Invasion
- Type: completion
- Before: "Invaders do not reset or return once encountered regardless of outcome."
- After: "An invader does not count towards clearing an encounter. The encounter is *cleared* when every other enemy model has been killed, whether or not the invader is still on the tile (see §7.5, Encounter Resolution)."
- Rationale: The manuscript did not say whether an invader must be killed for its encounter to be cleared, and §7.5 leaves the point to this section. The rule that an invader appears once is kept.
- Source: chapter 07 issues list; chapter instructions

### CH-12-031 · §12 Phantoms
- Type: style
- Before: "Heat Up"; "face-down" as a predicate; curly and straight quotation marks mixed; procedures written as prose; "will take"; keywords italicised irregularly
- After: "heat-up"; "face down"; straight quotation marks; numbered procedures with one action per step; present tense; each glossary headword italicised at its first use in each section
- Rationale: Style sweep under the book's style sheet. No rule is changed.
- Source: C-030, C-057, C-111, C-115, C-120, C-122

### CH-12-032 · §12 Phantoms
- Type: style
- Before: findings mapped to the chapter and not applied in it
- After: no change
- Rationale: A-015 (OPTIONAL) is not applied here because §7.5, Encounter Turn Order owns the aggro measure and already counts damage on an invader's health dial; §12.2 cites it. C-077 is owned by the component list of §2.9. A-124 and C-115 record policies and ask for no change. The other OPTIONAL findings (A-021, A-044, F-091) are applied in CH-12-020, CH-12-026, and CH-12-024.
- Source: A-015, C-077, A-124, C-115

### CH-12-033 · §12.2 Invaders (End of an Invasion)
- Type: clarification
- Before: (draft) "An invader does not count towards clearing an encounter … An invader on the tile when the encounter is cleared is removed and awards nothing."
- After: "An invader is an enemy model and counts towards clearing an encounter. While the invader is on the tile, the encounter is not cleared"
- Rationale: The manuscript is silent. The general rule (an encounter is cleared when every enemy model has been killed, §7.5) is applied to the invader, which §12.2 treats as an enemy model, rather than adding a new removal rule. Lead editor's decision at integration; the alternative is recorded in the issues list for the author.
- Source: A-021, manuscript lines 2023–2037

### CH-12-034 · §12.2 Invaders (Invader Tokens)
- Type: completion
- Before: "2. Add blank invader tokens, face down, until there is one token for each face-down encounter card on the encounter tiles."
- After: "2. Add blank invader tokens, face down, until there is one token for each face-down encounter card on the encounter tiles. If the blank tokens run out, make up the number with invader tokens of the kind not drawn in step 1; such a token is treated as blank when it is revealed."
- Rationale: Three blank tokens are supplied, and a campaign section of five encounters needs four. [MECHANICAL CHANGE] A substitution rule is added.
- Source: QA-010 (example and consistency verification)

### CH-12-035 · §12.1, Summoning
- Type: ruling
- Before: "The players choose the summon together in step 3. Souls paid in step 1 are not returned, whichever card is chosen."
- After: "The players choose the summon together in step 3. If they cannot agree, the drawn data cards are shuffled face down and one is drawn. Souls paid in step 1 are not returned, whichever card is chosen."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Gives the one joint choice in the chapter that had no tie-break a tie-break that needs no token and no new term.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E01 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official Phantoms booklet picks the summon at random from the cards set aside (DS-Phantoms-Rules-lores.txt lines 238-240); Hollowed Edition: 'Summons are chosen at random' (line 389).

### CH-12-036 · §12.1, Summons in Combat
- Type: clarification
- Before: "A summon has no *endurance bar* and pays no stamina. It has no *boss arcs* and no *heat-up* point, and its health is not scaled."
- After: "A summon has no *endurance bar* and pays no stamina. It has no *boss arcs* and no *heat-up* point, and its health is not scaled. A summon does only what its behaviour cards and its data card direct. It takes no *minor action* and no *major action*, and it cannot *parry*."
- Rationale: States in one place what a summon cannot do, so that no player asks whether a model with dodge dice may parry, walk, or attack outside its card.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E02 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official Phantoms booklet: summons are 'partially controlled by their behaviour cards and partially controlled by the players' and 'have fewer options each activation' (lines 236-239, 259-261); a data card shows block, resist and dodge only (lines 152-158).

### CH-12-037 · §12.2 (opening paragraph)
- Type: ruling
- Before: "Standard invaders appear before the *mini-boss* is defeated, and advanced invaders after it (see §2.4)."
- After: "Standard invaders appear before the *mini-boss* is defeated, and advanced invaders after it (see §2.4). In campaign play, standard invaders appear while the current tier is tier one, and advanced invaders once it is tier two (see §18.5)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] A campaign has several mini-bosses or none, so 'the mini-boss' names no moment there; the current tier is the one switch every campaign has, and it keeps the stronger invaders in step with the stronger treasure.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E03 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §18.5 and §18.7 of this book (tier two opens only when the bonfire ascetic is burnt); official Phantoms booklet ties the split to 'the mini boss' of a one-mini-boss game only (lines 363-377).

### CH-12-038 · §12.2, Invasions
- Type: ruling
- Before: "- During the exploration: when a character becomes embered by discarding an *ember card* (see §7.3), if one or more face-down encounter cards lie on the encounter tiles."
- After: "- During the exploration: when a character becomes embered by discarding an *ember card* (see §7.3), if one or more face-down encounter cards that carry no black cube lie on the encounter tiles (see §7.5, Encounter Resolution)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] A reset turns every encounter card face down, cleared ones included; without the qualifier an invader token can land on an encounter the party skips and the invasion is wasted.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E04 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official Phantoms booklet: tokens go to 'each unexplored encounter tile' (lines 363-369); Hollowed Edition: 'place invader tokens on all undefeated encounters' (lines 342-343); §11.5 step 1 of this book.

### CH-12-039 · §12.2, Invasions
- Type: clarification
- Before: "No more than one invasion is provoked before the mini-boss is defeated, and no more than one after it. Once an invasion has been provoked, no further invasion is provoked in that part of the game, however many characters are or become embered. If no character is embered when the encounter cards are dealt, no invader token is placed at that step.

The embering of every character at a boss's defeat (see §16.5) provokes no invasion at that moment. The invasion that follows the mini-boss's defeat is provoked when the encounter cards of the next setup are dealt (see §6.6)."
- After: "No more than one invasion is provoked before the mini-boss is defeated, and no more than one after it, however many characters are or become embered. The embering of every character at a boss's defeat provokes no invasion at that moment (see §16.5). The invasion that follows the mini-boss's defeat is provoked when the encounter cards of the next setup are dealt (see §6.6). In campaign play, no more than one invasion is provoked in each *section* (see §18.1)."
- Rationale: Two paragraphs become one without losing a rule: the dropped sentences restated the two trigger moments; the campaign limit of §18.1 is cited where the limit is stated.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E05 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Gate 1 OQ-28 (one invasion per leg); §18.1, Section Setup of this book.

### CH-12-040 · §12.2, Invader Tokens, step 2
- Type: ruling
- Before: "2. Add blank invader tokens, face down, until there is one token for each face-down encounter card on the encounter tiles. If the blank tokens run out, make up the number with invader tokens of the kind not drawn in step 1; such a token is treated as blank when it is revealed."
- After: "2. Add blank invader tokens, face down, until there is one token for each face-down encounter card that carries no black cube. If the blank tokens run out, make up the number with invader tokens of the kind not drawn in step 1; such a token is treated as blank when it is revealed."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Same qualifier as the trigger: a cleared encounter receives no token. The substitution rule for a shortage of blank tokens is kept unchanged.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E06 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official Phantoms booklet lines 363-369 (unexplored tiles only) and components list, three blank tokens (line 103).

### CH-12-041 · §12.2, Invader Tokens, step 4
- Type: ruling
- Before: "4. Place one token face down on each face-down encounter card."
- After: "4. Place one token face down on each of those encounter cards."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Keeps step 4 in line with step 2.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E07 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Follows phantom-E06.

### CH-12-042 · §12.2, Invader Tokens
- Type: clarification
- Before: "An invader token stays face down on its encounter card until the characters fight that encounter. It stays in place when the encounters are *reset* (see §11.5) and when the party skips its encounter (see §7.5, Encounter Start). Invader tokens still on the tiles when the mini-boss is defeated are returned to the supply unrevealed (see §6.6)."
- After: "An invader token stays face down on its encounter card until the characters fight that encounter, and it stays in place when the encounters are *reset* (see §11.5). Invader tokens still on the tiles when the mini-boss is defeated are returned to the supply unrevealed (see §6.6)."
- Rationale: Once tokens go only to encounter cards without a black cube, no encounter that holds a token can be skipped, so the skip clause is dead text and is cut.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E08 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §7.5, Encounter Start of this book: only an encounter whose card carries a black cube may be skipped.

### CH-12-043 · §12.2, Invader Tokens (example)
- Type: clarification
- Before: "*Example.* The Herald discards an ember card while two face-down encounter cards lie on the tiles ahead, before the mini-boss is defeated."
- After: "*Example.* The Herald discards an ember card while two face-down encounter cards, neither of which carries a black cube, lie on the tiles ahead, before the mini-boss is defeated."
- Rationale: The example states the condition the rule now names; its numbers are unchanged.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E09 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Follows phantom-E04 and phantom-E06.

### CH-12-044 · §12.2, Invader Rewards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "The invader's treasure card is awarded to the party at once (see §7.7). The players decide which character receives it. If they cannot agree, the card goes to the character who holds the aggro token at that moment."
- After: "The invader's treasure card is awarded to the party at once, and the players decide which character receives it as for any card awarded to the party (see §7.7)."
- Rationale: One tie-break, stated once in §7.7 and worded to cover a card awarded during an encounter, replaces a local restatement that differed from it by one phrase.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E10 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Gate 1 OQ-29 (tie-break to the aggro holder everywhere); official Phantoms booklet: treasure card gained 'immediately' (lines 528-533).

### CH-12-045 · §12.2, End of an Invasion
- Type: clarification
- Before: "An invader is an enemy model and counts towards clearing an encounter. While the invader is on the tile, the encounter is not *cleared*: it is cleared only when every enemy model, the invader included, has been killed, or when the invader has vanished and every other enemy model has been killed (see §7.5, Encounter Resolution)."
- After: "An invader is an enemy model, and its encounter is not *cleared* while it is on the tile (see §7.5, Encounter Resolution). An invader leaves the tile only when it is killed or when it vanishes."
- Rationale: The ruling is confirmed; the paragraph says the same thing in half the words and lets the general rule of §7.5 do the work.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E11 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official Phantoms booklet: the invader 'will fight alongside the other enemies present' and leaves only if killed or if the party is defeated (lines 492-495, 527-533).

### CH-12-046 · §12.2, End of an Invasion
- Type: clarification
- Before: "An invader appears once. Whether it was killed or vanished,"
- After: "An invader appears once, unless a campaign states otherwise (see §19.4; §19.15). Whether it was killed or vanished,"
- Rationale: Two campaigns bring a listed invader back until it is killed; the general rule now points to them.
- Source: final rulings, lead-E04 (`06_final_rulings.md`); campaign memo, cross-cluster effects

### CH-12-047 · §12.2
- Type: clarification
- Before: "one of its unused behaviour cards, taken at random, is shuffled into its behaviour deck in place of a heat-up card (see §15.5)."
- After: "one of its unused behaviour cards, taken at random, serves as its heat-up card, and the invader heats up as a boss does (see §15.5, Heat-Up)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] The invader follows the one heat-up procedure and timing of boss-E01: deck and discard pile reshuffled with the added card at the start of its next turn.
- Source: final rulings, lead-E17 (`06_final_rulings.md`); boss memo, cross-cluster effects

### CH-12-048 · §12.2
- Type: correction
- Before: "1. Draw one invader token at random and keep it face down: a standard invader token if the mini-boss has not been defeated, an advanced invader token if it has."
- After: "1. Draw one invader token at random and keep it face down: a standard invader token if the mini-boss has not been defeated, an advanced invader token if it has. In campaign play, draw a standard invader token while the current tier is tier one and an advanced invader token once it is tier two (see §18.1, Section Setup)."
- Rationale: The numbered step gave only the standard-game test; §12.2's opening paragraph and §18.1 key the token to the current tier in campaign play.
- Source: final rulings, VB-001 (`06_final_rulings.md`); independent verification, work/phase3/verify_b.md

### CH-12-049 · §12.2
- Type: correction
- Before: "Some campaigns place named invaders in listed encounters and use no invader tokens (see §19.3; §19.4)."
- After: "Some campaigns place named invaders in listed encounters and use no invader tokens (see §19.3; §19.4; §19.15)."
- Rationale: §19.15, added today, is a third campaign that lists named invaders and places no tokens.
- Source: final rulings, VB-002 (`06_final_rulings.md`); independent verification, work/phase3/verify_b.md

## Chapter 13 — Mimics

### CH-13-001 · §13 Mimics
- Type: restructure
- Before: "# MIMICS" followed by four prose paragraphs and an empty heading used as a page break
- After: "# 13. Mimics" / "## 13.1 Chests and Ambush Cards" / "## 13.2 The Mimic Encounter" / "## 13.3 Mimic Rewards" / "## 13.4 Failed Mimic Encounters"
- Rationale: The chapter owns the whole chest-and-mimic procedure, which the manuscript split between three chapters. It is divided into the decision, the encounter, its rewards, and its failure; the empty heading is deleted and an intro without rules is added.
- Source: CMP-0014, C-089, C-098, C-104, C-120

### CH-13-002 · §13 Mimics
- Type: cut
- Before: "Players with the Encounters expansion set available may choose whether they wish to include mimics in their game during setup. Groups with new players should default to leaving mimics out as they can be quite punishing."
- After: "*Mimics* are an optional rule. They require the Explorers expansion. The party decides during setup whether mimics are in play."
- Rationale: The advice to groups and the scope disclaimer are replaced by the fixed optional-rule formula. The expansion is the Explorers expansion; "Encounters" named a product that does not exist.
- Source: F-023, F-051, C-018, C-040, A-101, C-124, C-121

### CH-13-003 · §13.1 Chests and Ambush Cards
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "When *mimics* are in play, one *ambush card* is dealt face down beside each encounter tile at setup (see §6.5; §6.6). An ambush card shows Treasure or Mimic on its face (see §2.6)."
- Rationale: The chapter used the ambush card without saying where it came from or what it shows. The dealing itself is owned by the setup sections and is cited.
- Source: CMP-0014, A-092

### CH-13-004 · §13.1 Chests and Ambush Cards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "After defeating an encounter that contains an unopened chest, characters do not recover normally and remain in their current node."
- After: "The characters remain on their nodes. No character recovers, no dead character is revived, and no *souls* or treasure are collected."
- Rationale: The deferral left open whether dead characters were revived before the chest was opened and whether rewards were collected first. Resolution and recovery are both deferred until the chest is settled.
- Source: A-060, CMP-0014

### CH-13-005 · §13.1 Chests and Ambush Cards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Characters must collectively decide whether they wish to open the chest or leave it be out of fear of encountering a mimic. If the characters cannot agree, the character with the *aggro* token makes the final decision."
- After: "The party then decides whether to open the chest or to forgo it. If the players cannot agree, the player whose character holds the *aggro token* decides."
- Rationale: Three passages gave three decision rules. The party is the collective actor, the players disagree, and the aggro holder settles it, as everywhere else in the book. The flavour clause is cut.
- Source: A-059, F-024, F-086, C-087; Gate 1 — OQ-29

### CH-13-006 · §13.1 Chests and Ambush Cards
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "If the characters elect to leave the chest alone, they proceed to recover all stamina and two damage normally."
- After: "If the party forgoes the chest, the encounter is resolved and the characters recover in the ordinary way, except that the chest is not opened (see §7.5, Encounter Resolution)."
- Rationale: The manuscript healed two damage here and one in Encounter Recovery. Gate 1 fixed the amount at one damage cube; the chapter cites §7.5 and states the amount once, in §13.3.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-12; A-061, D-035

### CH-13-007 · §13.1 Chests and Ambush Cards
- Type: cut
- Before: "The then are faced with the typical option of continuing onto the next encounter or returning to rest at the bonfire for the cost of one *spark.*"
- After: deleted
- Rationale: The choice between advancing and resting belongs to every cleared encounter and is owned by §7.5, Encounter Recovery, which now states it. The sentence also carried a typographical error and punctuation inside the italics.
- Source: A-063, C-015, C-027

### CH-13-008 · §13.1 Chests and Ambush Cards
- Type: correction
- Before: "However, if the characters wish to open the chest, they must reveal one an *ambush* card to determine if the chest is a lurking mimic."
- After: "If the party opens the chest, the ambush card beside the tile is turned face up."
- Rationale: The defective "one an ambush card" is the single ambush card dealt beside that tile.
- Source: CMP-0007, C-015, F-024

### CH-13-009 · §13.1 Chests and Ambush Cards
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "If the *ambush* card shows treasure, the characters receive a total of one tier appropriate treasure which they must assign per normal treasure chest rules."
- After: "Treasure: the chest is opened, and the *treasure card* beneath it is awarded as a chest's card is (see §7.7)."
- Rationale: A chest carries a card dealt beneath it when it is first placed; drawing a fresh card left that card unresolved. Gate 1 made the pre-dealt card the reward.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-11; A-058, C-084

### CH-13-010 · §13.1 Chests and Ambush Cards
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Each later time that encounter is cleared, the party again decides whether to open the chest." / "In the campaign A Hollow Pursuit, one chest is a mimic without an ambush card (see §19.8)."
- Rationale: The fate of a forgone chest was unstated. The campaign exception is owned by §19.8 and is cited so that a reader of this chapter finds it.
- Source: CMP-0014, A-102

### CH-13-011 · §13.2 The Mimic Encounter
- Type: correction
- Before: "If the *ambush* card shows a mimic, an additional min-boss encounter ensues before the party can claim their treasure. ... then the Voracious Mimic's data and encounter cards are used."
- After: "A mimic encounter is fought as a *mini-boss* encounter, with the differences stated in this section (see §15; §16; §17.6)."
- Rationale: "Min-boss" is mini-boss, and a mimic has behaviour cards, not encounter cards. The mimic encounter is defined as a term.
- Source: C-015, CMP-0014

### CH-13-012 · §13.2 The Mimic Encounter
- Type: completion
- Before: "Characters remain on the nodes they occupied at the end of the encounter containing the chest."
- After: "Remove the chest token from its node and place the mimic model on that node." / "A character who died in the cleared encounter stays dead and takes no part in the mimic encounter."
- Rationale: The manuscript never said where the mimic stands, how its deck is built, or what state the characters are in. The setup is a numbered procedure; the mimic takes the chest's node, and the characters keep their cubes and tokens.
- Source: CMP-0014, A-060, C-120

### CH-13-013 · §13.2 The Mimic Encounter
- Type: style
- Before: "The mimic will take its turn first. Refer to "**Boss Encounter**" rules for more details on how to manage the mimic's behaviour cards."
- After: "The mimic takes the first *turn*. The mimic and the characters then alternate turns as a boss and the characters do (see §16.1), and the aggro token moves as it does in a boss encounter (see §16.4)."
- Rationale: Present tense replaces the future. The quoted heading named no section; the reference is to the numbered boss sections, without an imperative addressed to the reader.
- Source: C-082, C-084, C-122, F-056, F-074, F-088

### CH-13-014 · §13.2 The Mimic Encounter
- Type: clarification
- Before: "The only difference is that the mimic does not have specific *heat-up* behaviour cards and instead just shuffles in one unused normal behaviour card when it heats-up."
- After: "When a mimic's health is reduced to or below the *heat-up* point ... on its data card, one of its unused behaviour cards, taken at random, is shuffled into its behaviour deck in place of a heat-up card (see §15.5)."
- Rationale: The heat-up is worded as the invader's is. It was not the only difference: a mimic has no arcs, so no attack against it gains the weak-arc die.
- Source: C-097, C-057, A-044

### CH-13-015 · §13.2 The Mimic Encounter
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The killing of a mimic is not the defeat of a boss. It grants no boss soul reward, no *boss treasure*, no *spark*, no embering, and no rest (see §16.5)."
- Rationale: "Fought as a mini-boss encounter" would otherwise import the consequences of a boss's defeat. The chapter also states that a mimic is not scaled and that no summon or invader takes part.
- Source: CMP-0014, A-021; §8.3

### CH-13-016 · §13.3 Mimic Rewards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If the characters kill the mimic, they gain two tier appropriate treasures (instead of one), and may mark the encounter *cleared.*."
- After: "Award the party two *treasure cards*: the card that lay beneath the *chest*, and one card drawn from the treasure deck of the current tier (see §6.2)."
- Rationale: The two cards are the chest's own card and one more, in place of the chest's one. The deferred resolution and recovery of the encounter follow. The double full stop and the punctuation inside the italics are removed.
- Source: CMP-0014, CMP-0007, C-027, C-106; Gate 1 — OQ-11

### CH-13-017 · §13.3 Mimic Rewards
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "they proceed to recover all stamina and two damage normally"
- After: "Each surviving character removes all condition tokens, all stamina cubes, and one damage cube, and each character who died in either encounter is revived *hollow* (see §7.5, Encounter Recovery)."
- Rationale: The recovery deferred by the mimic is the ordinary Encounter Recovery, with one damage cube removed as Gate 1 decided.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-12; A-060, A-061, D-035

### CH-13-018 · §13.3 Mimic Rewards
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A mimic has no soul reward of its own. The *souls* and the treasure that the characters collect in step 3 are the deferred rewards of the encounter that contained the chest (see §7.6; §7.7)."
- Rationale: No source gives a mimic a soul value. The sentence prevents the boss reward of §7.6 from being read into a mimic encounter.
- Source: CMP-0014

### CH-13-019 · §13.3 Mimic Rewards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If the encounter is *cleared* then the characters may return to repeat the encounter again but do not encounter the mimic a second time."
- After: "A killed mimic does not return. Its chest token is not placed again when the encounter is *reset*, and the chest's node is used as a basic node (see §5.1)."
- Rationale: The chest was the mimic, so the token leaves the tile with it. A worked example of a mimic encounter is added after this rule; it states no rule.
- Source: CMP-0014

### CH-13-020 · §13.4 Failed Mimic Encounters
- Type: completion
- Before: "If the mimic kills all of the characters, the encounter is *failed*"
- After: "A *mimic encounter* is *failed* when every character has died, or when a *hollow* character dies and is *lost* (see §7.2). The *encounter* that contained the *chest* is then failed, although its enemies had been killed."
- Rationale: The manuscript did not say whether the original encounter's rewards survived a lost mimic encounter. They do not: the encounter is resolved as any failed encounter, under §7.5.
- Source: A-060, CMP-0014

### CH-13-021 · §13.4 Failed Mimic Encounters
- Type: clarification
- Before: "the characters will encounter the mimic next time they complete the normal encounter in which the mimic lives. However, once the mimic has been fought once, it must be fought again at the end of the encounter until it is killed."
- After: "Each later time that encounter is *cleared*, the party again decides whether to open the chest (see §13.1). Opening the chest begins a new mimic encounter, set up afresh (see §13.2)."
- Rationale: The two sentences were incompatible. The party keeps its choice, since it may always decline to open the chest, and the chest yields nothing until the mimic is killed.
- Source: CMP-0014

### CH-13-022 · §13.2 The Mimic Encounter
- Type: clarification
- Before: finding B-035 (approved PROPOSED): the characters "remove all stamina cubes, but no damage cubes, before the mimic takes its first turn"
- After: not applied: "Each character keeps their damage cubes, stamina cubes, and condition tokens"
- Rationale: B-035 alters deferred recovery, which is on the preserve list, and contradicts CMP-0014, which governs this chapter. It is not one of the mechanical changes signed off at Gate 1. The point is raised in the issues list.
- Source: B-035; preserve list; CMP-0014

### CH-13-023 · §13 Mimics
- Type: style
- Before: "heats-up"; "*spark.*"; curly quotation marks; "will take"; "characters" as the deciding actor; keywords italicised irregularly
- After: "heat-up"; punctuation outside italics; straight quotation marks; present tense; the party and the players decide; each glossary headword italicised at its first use in each section
- Rationale: Style sweep under the book's style sheet. No rule is changed.
- Source: C-027, C-030, C-057, C-106, C-111, C-122, F-086, F-088

### CH-13-024 · §13 Mimics
- Type: style
- Before: findings mapped to the chapter and settled elsewhere
- After: no change
- Rationale: A-092 (ambush cards in the component list) is owned by §2.9. C-087 (one tie-break for contested treasure) is owned by §7.7, which this chapter cites. The OPTIONAL findings A-044 and C-121 are applied in CH-13-014 and CH-13-002; none is skipped.
- Source: A-092, C-087, A-044, C-121

### CH-13-025 · §13.1
- Type: clarification
- Before: "- Mimic: the chest is a mimic. A *mimic encounter* begins at once, before any recovery or reward (see §13.2)."
- After: "- Mimic: the chest is a mimic. A *mimic encounter* begins at once, before the recovery and the rewards of the cleared encounter (see §13.2)."
- Rationale: 'Before any recovery' no longer holds once the characters shed their stamina cubes before the mimic's first turn.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E18 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Follows phantom-E19.

### CH-13-026 · §13.2
- Type: ruling
- Before: "The characters begin the mimic encounter as the cleared encounter left them. Each character keeps their damage cubes, stamina cubes, and condition tokens, and the *aggro token* stays with its holder."
- After: "Before the mimic's first turn, each character removes every stamina cube from their *endurance bar*. Nothing else is recovered. Each character keeps their damage cubes and condition tokens, no token is turned to its ready side, and the *aggro token* stays with its holder."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] The ambush stays dangerous because damage carries over, but the fight no longer opens with characters who cannot act, and the party has no reason to stall on the last enemy to shed stamina before opening a chest.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E19 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Audit finding B-035 (approved at Gate 1, not applied: CH-13-022); official Explorers booklet removes all cubes before the mimic (DS-Explorers-Rules-Web.txt lines 464-467); Hollowed Edition: 'Players heal as normal' before opening (lines 378-380).

### CH-13-027 · §13.2
- Type: ruling
- Before: "The Hungry Mimic is fought if the mini-boss has not been defeated, and the Voracious Mimic if it has."
- After: "The Hungry Mimic is fought if the mini-boss has not been defeated, and the Voracious Mimic if it has. In campaign play, the Hungry Mimic is fought while the current tier is tier one, and the Voracious Mimic once it is tier two (see §18.5)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Same anchor as for invaders: a campaign has no single mini-boss, and the mimic's extra card is drawn from the treasure deck of the current tier, so the stronger mimic guards the stronger treasure.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E20 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §13.3 step 2, §18.5 and §18.7 of this book; official Explorers booklet: 'a mini boss' (lines 476-482).

### CH-13-028 · §13.3, step 4
- Type: ruling
- Before: "4. Return the chest token and the *ambush card* to the supply."
- After: "4. Return the chest token to its node, open side up, and the *ambush card* to the supply."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] An opened chest already stays on its node for the rest of the game; treating the looted mimic's chest the same way removes an exception from §5.5, §7.5 and §11.5.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E21 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §5.5, Chests of this book ('an opened chest is never closed again'); official Explorers booklet removes only the mimic (lines 549-556).

### CH-13-029 · §13.3
- Type: ruling
- Before: "A killed mimic does not return. Its chest token is not placed again when the encounter is *reset*, and the chest's node is used as a basic node (see §5.1)."
- After: "A killed mimic does not return. Its chest stays on its node as an opened chest (see §5.5, Chests)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Follows the change to step 4: no special terrain state is left behind by a killed mimic.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E22 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §5.5, Chests of this book.

### CH-13-030 · §13.3 (example)
- Type: ruling
- Before: "The Hungry Mimic replaces the chest token, takes the first turn, and attacks the Knight, who still carries all seven cubes. The characters kill the mimic. The party gains the chest's treasure card and one card drawn from the tier one treasure deck. Each character then gains four souls and one of the treasure cards dealt to the tile, and the Knight removes all four stamina cubes and one damage cube."
- After: "The Hungry Mimic replaces the chest token, and the Knight removes the four stamina cubes. The mimic takes the first turn and attacks the Knight, who still carries the three damage cubes. The characters kill the mimic. The party gains the chest's treasure card and one card drawn from the tier one treasure deck. Each character then gains four souls and one of the treasure cards dealt to the tile, and the Knight removes the stamina cubes placed during the mimic encounter and one damage cube."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] The example now computes from §13.2 as amended: stamina cubes are removed before the mimic's first turn, damage cubes are kept.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E23 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Follows phantom-E19; Level 2 full reward of four souls from the table in §7.6.

### CH-13-031 · §13.2
- Type: clarification
- Before: "one of its unused behaviour cards, taken at random, is shuffled into its behaviour deck in place of a heat-up card (see §15.5)."
- After: "one of its unused behaviour cards, taken at random, serves as its heat-up card, and the mimic heats up as a boss does (see §15.5, Heat-Up)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] As lead-E17, for the mimic.
- Source: final rulings, lead-E18 (`06_final_rulings.md`); boss memo, cross-cluster effects

## Chapter 14 — Crystal Lizards

### CH-14-001 · §14 Crystal Lizards
- Type: style
- Before: "# CRYSTAL LIZARDS" / "Crystal lizards are strange and glimmering reptiles that offer character's powerful rewards if the lizard is slain before it can escape." / an empty heading used as a page break
- After: "# 14. Crystal Lizards" / "Crystal lizards are glimmering reptiles whose hides are studded with the rarest titanite."
- Rationale: The first sentence is kept as a chapter intro that holds no rule, with its apostrophe error and "slain" removed. The empty heading is deleted. The chapter is short enough to need no numbered sections.
- Source: F-025, C-015, C-061, C-098, C-104, C-121

### CH-14-002 · §14 Crystal Lizards
- Type: cut
- Before: "Players should include crystal lizards as an optional game component if they own the Iron Keep expansion set. The following rules are assuming the players are playing with the Iron Keep expansion set and the included crystal lizards."
- After: "*Crystal lizards* are an optional rule. They require the Iron Keep expansion. The party decides during setup whether crystal lizards are in play."
- Rationale: The advice and the scope disclaimer are replaced by the fixed optional-rule formula, in the same form as the mimics' opener.
- Source: F-025, C-018, C-124

### CH-14-003 · §14 Crystal Lizards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Whenever the character reveals an encounter card with the crystal lizard symbol, a crystal lizard appears. During encounter set-up, place a crystal lizard on the terrain node indicated by the encounter card. Some encounters contain multiple crystal lizards."
- After: "When an *encounter card* shows the crystal lizard symbol, one crystal lizard model is placed on each *terrain node* ... that the card marks with that symbol. The models are placed during Encounter Start (see §7.5, Encounter Start)."
- Rationale: Three sentences said one thing. The placing is tied to the step that §7.5 gives it, "set-up" becomes the noun setup elsewhere, and the crystal lizard is stated to be an enemy model under the enemy rules except as the chapter says.
- Source: C-059, C-120

### CH-14-004 · §14 Crystal Lizards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Crystal lizards take their turns like any other enemy model in descending *threat* order. However, their behaviours dictate that they simply run from the characters which makes killing them challenging."
- After: "On its turn it resolves the *behaviour icons* on its data card, which move it away from the characters. A crystal lizard makes no attack."
- Rationale: The comment on difficulty is cut and the behaviour is stated as a rule: the crystal lizard acts in threat order with the enemies, moves away, and does not attack.
- Source: C-121, C-120

### CH-14-005 · §14 Crystal Lizards
- Type: correction
- Before: "Crystal lizards do not scale their health depending on the number of characters."
- After: "A crystal lizard is not scaled. Neither enemy scaling (see §8.3) nor the *bonfire ascetic* (see §18.7) changes its health or its movement."
- Rationale: The exemption covered health only, so a crystal lizard would still have gained from the other effects of scaling and from the bonfire ascetic. §8.3 lists the crystal lizard among the models that are not scaled and cites this chapter.
- Source: A-028

### CH-14-006 · §14 Crystal Lizards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "When the last enemy of an encounter (beside crystal lizards) dies, any remaining crystal lizards are immediately removed from play. ... Note that an encounter is still considered *cleared* regardless of any surviving crystal lizards."
- After: "Crystal lizards do not count towards clearing an *encounter*. The encounter is *cleared* when every other enemy model has been killed (see §7.5, Encounter Resolution)."
- Rationale: The rule is stated once, without the address to the reader, and in the verbs of the style sheet: enemies are killed, encounters are cleared. The escape without award is kept.
- Source: F-026, C-061, C-122

### CH-14-007 · §14 Crystal Lizards
- Type: correction
- Before: "Whenever a character kills a crystal lizard, they immediately place one Titanite Scale card into their inventory."
- After: "When a character kills a crystal lizard, that character takes one Titanite Scale card from the supply at once and places it in their *inventory* (see §4.2)."
- Rationale: The Treasure Rewards section of the manuscript awarded a Titanite Chunk for the same kill. Titanite Scale is the card the Iron Keep expansion supplies for the purpose and the name this chapter already used; §7.7 now cites this chapter.
- Source: A-096, C-056, D-028, B-032, CMP-0015

### CH-14-008 · §14 Crystal Lizards
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The Iron Keep expansion supplies three Titanite Scale cards. When none remains, a killed crystal lizard awards nothing, and no crystal lizard is placed in any later encounter."
- Rationale: With a card awarded for each kill, the manuscript let crystal lizards keep appearing after the three cards were gone, which left the reward undefined.
- Source: CMP-0015, A-097, D-028

### CH-14-009 · §14 Crystal Lizards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If any number of crystal lizards die during an encounter, no further crystal lizards will spawn on repeated attempts of that encounter. Mark encounter tiles with an upside-down crystal lizard model if any crystal lizard dies during an encounter."
- After: "Once a crystal lizard has been killed in an encounter, no crystal lizard is placed in that encounter again, however many times the encounter is *reset* and fought (see §11.5)."
- Rationale: Present tense replaces the future and the mechanic is unchanged: crystal lizards return until one is killed and never afterwards. The upside-down model is kept as an example of a marker, because only two models are supplied.
- Source: C-122, F-088; preserve list

### CH-14-010 · §14 Crystal Lizards
- Type: style
- Before: "set-up"; "slain" and "dies" for enemies; "will respawn"; two long prose paragraphs; keywords italicised irregularly
- After: "setup" and "set up"; "killed"; present tense; short standing rules with one rule to a sentence; each glossary headword italicised at its first use
- Rationale: Style sweep under the book's style sheet. No rule is changed.
- Source: C-030, C-059, C-061, C-120, C-122, F-088

### CH-14-011 · §14 Crystal Lizards
- Type: style
- Before: findings mapped to the chapter and not applied in it
- After: no change
- Rationale: A-062 (OPTIONAL) would limit the repeating of a cleared encounter; that rule is owned by §7.5, which did not adopt it, and nothing in this chapter depends on it. C-077 (Titanite Scale cards in the component list) is owned by §2.9. The OPTIONAL findings A-097 and C-121 are applied in CH-14-008 and CH-14-004.
- Source: A-062, C-077, A-097, C-121

### CH-14-012 · §14
- Type: ruling
- Before: "When a character kills a crystal lizard, that character takes one Titanite Scale card from the supply at once and places it in their *inventory* (see §4.2)."
- After: "When a crystal lizard is killed, the party is awarded one Titanite Scale card from the supply at once (see §4.2). The players decide which character receives it, as for any card awarded to the party (see §7.7)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] An award to the party needs no rule for a crystal lizard killed by a condition or by an attack that hits several models, and it treats this card as every other card won from a kill is treated.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E24 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official Iron Keep booklet: the card is added 'to the inventory', which is shared (lines 125-127); §7.7 of this book.

### CH-14-013 · §14
- Type: ruling
- Before: "Once a crystal lizard has been killed in an encounter, no crystal lizard is placed in that encounter again, however many times the encounter is *reset* and fought (see §11.5). The party marks the tile to record the kill, for example by laying the killed model upside down beside the tile's encounter card. Until a crystal lizard has been killed there, the crystal lizards are placed again each time the encounter is set up."
- After: "Once a crystal lizard has been killed in an encounter, no crystal lizard is placed in that encounter again, however many times the encounter is *reset* and fought (see §11.5). To record the kill, one *damage cube* is placed on the encounter card of that encounter, where it stays until the card is returned to the game box. Until a crystal lizard has been killed there, the crystal lizards are placed again each time the encounter is set up."
- Rationale: A definite marker in the book's own idiom (a cube on the encounter card, as for a cleared encounter) replaces an example that fails on the second marked tile, because only two crystal lizard models are supplied.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E25 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §2.2 of this book (2x Crystal Lizard); official Iron Keep booklet suggests 'an opened chest token beside the tile' (lines 128-131); §7.5 (black cube marks a cleared encounter).

## Chapter 17 — Boss Special Rules

### CH-17-001 · §17 Boss Special Rules
- Type: restructure
- Before: "# BOSS SPECIAL RULES" / "## Asylum Demon & Stray Demon" / "### Pillar Smasher" / "### Wind Up"
- After: "# 17. Boss Special Rules" with the ten numbered H2s of the section map (17.1 to 17.10) and a 48-word intro that contains no rule
- Rationale: The chapter covered two bosses and is now the full CMP-0005 chapter; headings follow the section map and carry no ampersand.
- Source: CMP-0005; C-104, C-112; brief §4

### CH-17-002 · §17 Boss Special Rules
- Type: cut
- Before: "#" (empty heading used as a page break, manuscript line 2215)
- After: deleted
- Rationale: Page breaks belong to layout, not structure.
- Source: C-098; clean-up list

### CH-17-003 · §17.1 Rules Common to Several Bosses
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Every *boss* is played from its *data card* and the general boss rules (see §15 and §16). ... A boss with no entry in this chapter has no rules beyond its data card."
- Rationale: The chapter needed a statement of scope and of precedence between this book and a data card; the CMP-0005 preamble held rules and so sits in §17.1, not in the intro.
- Source: CMP-0005; C-081

### CH-17-004 · §17.1 Rules Common to Several Bosses
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A character targeted by an attack on such a card may pay one stamina to *dodge* it without rolling, and the dodge succeeds even if the character has no dodge dice."
- Rationale: Sif, Artorias, and the Pursuer have behaviour cards with a dodge difficulty of 0; the rule is stated once and cited from each entry.
- Source: CMP-0005

### CH-17-005 · §17.1 Rules Common to Several Bosses
- Type: completion
- Before: "If a boss model moves onto a node that already contains a boss, the players must push the boss that was on the node first." (manuscript, Node Model Limits)
- After: "When a boss model moves onto a node that holds another boss model, the model already on the node is *pushed* to a *valid node* chosen by the players and keeps its facing (see §8.4)."
- Rationale: §5.3 cites §17.1 for the two-boss displacement; placement, separate arcs, the facing of the pushed model, and the arcs of characters on either node are now stated.
- Source: CMP-0005; A-039; dependency of §5.3

### CH-17-006 · §17.1 Rules Common to Several Bosses
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "When two models are one boss, as the models of Ornstein & Smough are (see §17.2), they share one data card, one *health dial*, and one *behaviour deck*, and the boss has one heat-up."
- Rationale: The Gate 1 answer makes Ornstein & Smough one boss with a shared dial and deck; §2.3 cites §17.1 for it. A-039's proposed fix (two dials, two decks) is superseded by the Gate 1 answer.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-25; A-039; CMP-0005; dependency of §2.3

### CH-17-007 · §17.1 Rules Common to Several Bosses
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Each behaviour card names the model that acts. Only the named model resolves the card: its movement, its facing, the *range* of its attack, its attack arcs, and the direction of any push are all taken from that model."
- Rationale: States which model acts on a boss turn and from which model every measurement is taken.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-25

### CH-17-008 · §17.1 Rules Common to Several Bosses
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A character gains the weak-arc bonus (see §15.6) when attacking either model from an arc of that model that the card shows as weak."
- Rationale: The weak-arc bonus is judged against the arcs of the model attacked, as Gate 1 directs.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-25

### CH-17-009 · §17.1 Rules Common to Several Bosses
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A *condition* token gained by either model is placed beside the health dial and affects the boss, whichever model acts. Both models stay on the tile until the boss is defeated (see §16.5)."
- Rationale: Consequences of the one-boss reading that play requires; logged in the issues file for the author's confirmation.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-25 (consequence)

### CH-17-010 · §17.1 Rules Common to Several Bosses
- Type: completion
- Before: "Gargoyle (Mini-Boss) / Gargoyle (Mini-Boss)" (campaign lists; no rule)
- After: "The Twin Gargoyles are one *mini-boss* encounter in two parts, fought on the mini-boss tile against one Gargoyle and then, at once, against a second."
- Rationale: Three campaigns list the Gargoyle twice with no rule. Gate 1 fixes the reading: back to back, one encounter.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-25; CMP-0005; A-040; D-012

### CH-17-011 · §17.1 Rules Common to Several Bosses
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Remove the Gargoyle model from the tile and place it on the mini-boss *spawn node* ... Set the health dial to the Gargoyle's starting health. ... build a new behaviour deck (see §15.5)."
- Rationale: The change-over is a five-step procedure: model, characters on the spawn node, dial, deck, and the next turn. What carries over between the parts is listed after it.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-25; CMP-0005; A-040

### CH-17-012 · §17.1 Rules Common to Several Bosses
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The soul reward, one Gargoyle *boss treasure* card, and one spark are awarded, every character becomes *embered*, and the characters rest at the bonfire, at that time and not before."
- Rationale: Boss Death is resolved once, after the second Gargoyle; no rest or reward falls between the two. The PvP encounter of §20.1 likewise follows the second Gargoyle only.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-25; D-012; dependency of §20.1

### CH-17-013 · §17.1 Rules Common to Several Bosses
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "If the encounter is *failed* while either Gargoyle is in play, the whole encounter is failed ... When the characters next enter the encounter, both Gargoyles are fought again"
- Rationale: States the result of a failure in the first part and in the second; the official footnote covered only the second.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-25; D-012; A-040

### CH-17-014 · §17.1 Rules Common to Several Bosses
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "*Example.* The Knight and the Herald fight the Twin Gargoyles. The Knight defeats the first Gargoyle while holding four damage cubes. ..."
- Rationale: A worked example of the change-over and of a failure in the second part; it introduces no rule.
- Source: brief §2 (worked examples)

### CH-17-015 · §17.2 Core Set
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "These five bosses have no rules beyond their *data cards*. A campaign *section* that lists the Gargoyle twice in succession sets the Twin Gargoyles *encounter* (see §17.1, Twin Gargoyles)."
- Rationale: Every core boss is accounted for, so that a missing entry is never read as a missing rule.
- Source: CMP-0005

### CH-17-016 · §17.2 Core Set
- Type: completion
- Before: "Dragonslayer Ornstein (Core)" / "Executioner Smough (Core)" (two component entries; no rule)
- After: "One model is placed on each, as the data card directs. If the data card names no *node* for a model, the players choose which model takes which spawn node."
- Rationale: The entry gives components, setup, special ability, and heat-up for the pair. Placement uses the two boss spawn nodes of the joined boss tiles and defers to the data card for which model takes which.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-25; CMP-0005; A-039

### CH-17-017 · §17.2 Core Set
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Ornstein & Smough heats up once, when the shared health dial is reduced to or below the *heat-up* point ... on the data card (see §15.5)."
- Rationale: One boss, one heat-up, as the shared-dial reading requires.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-25

### CH-17-018 · §17.3 Asylum Demon and Stray Demon
- Type: restructure
- Before: "### Pillar Smasher" / "### Wind Up"
- After: "### Components" / "### Setup" / "### Special Ability" / "### Unique Icons", with Pillar Smasher under Special Ability and Wind Up under Unique Icons
- Rationale: Every boss entry in the chapter uses the same five-part structure, omitting empty parts.
- Source: chapter instructions; CMP-0005

### CH-17-019 · §17.3 Asylum Demon and Stray Demon
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "**Pillar node.** One of the four *terrain nodes* printed on the mega-boss side of the Asylum Demon expansion's *mega-boss game board*, on each of which a *pillar token* is placed."
- Rationale: Pillar tokens and pillar nodes were used but never listed or located.
- Source: A-114; C-077

### CH-17-020 · §17.3 Asylum Demon and Stray Demon
- Type: clarification
- Before: "During the boss encounter setup, place one pillar token on each of the four pillar nodes found on the mega-boss game board. Pillars block movement."
- After: "At the start of every boss *encounter* fought on the mega-boss side of that game board, one pillar token is placed on each of its four pillar nodes, before the *boss* takes its first *turn*."
- Rationale: Fixes when the tokens are placed and in which encounters; the blocking rule is owned by §5.5 and is cited.
- Source: A-114; dependency of §5.5

### CH-17-021 · §17.3 Asylum Demon and Stray Demon
- Type: clarification
- Before: "check to see if there are any pillar tokens within the affected area. characters within one node range of the pillar token immediately suffer one damage and the stagger condition. Then remove that pillar token from the encounter tile."
- After: "For each such pillar token, deal one damage to each character within range 1 of the pillar's node (see §5.4), and give each of those characters a *stagger* ... token."
- Rationale: The sequence is a four-step procedure; "the affected area" is defined by the attack's range and attack arcs; the lower-case sentence start is corrected.
- Source: C-114; CMP-0005

### CH-17-022 · §17.3 Asylum Demon and Stray Demon
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Depending on positioning, it is possible for a demon to smash through multiple pillars at once or even for a model to be hit by multiple shattering pillars at once."
- After: "One attack may destroy several pillars. A character within range 1 of several destroyed pillars suffers one damage for each of them and holds one stagger token (see §8.5, Stagger)."
- Rationale: Filler removed and the rule stated, with the one-token limit on stagger made explicit.
- Source: C-123

### CH-17-023 · §17.3 Asylum Demon and Stray Demon
- Type: clarification
- Before: "immediately suffer one damage"
- After: "The damage of a destroyed pillar is not an attack, and it cannot be *blocked*, *resisted*, or dodged. ... A magical attack, or an attack that does not show the area icon, destroys no pillar."
- Rationale: Settles whether a defence applies and which attacks destroy pillars; logged in the issues file for confirmation.
- Source: CMP-0005 (clarified)

### CH-17-024 · §17.3 Asylum Demon and Stray Demon
- Type: style
- Before: "Once a pillar is destroyed, pillar nodes are treated as basic nodes for the remainder of the encounter. When the party rests at the bonfire, return any destroyed pillars to their pillar nodes on the mega-boss game board."
- After: "Once a pillar is destroyed, its node is used as a *basic node* for the rest of the encounter (see §5.5, Pillars). When the characters rest at the *bonfire*, every destroyed pillar token is returned to its pillar node."
- Rationale: Preserved mechanic in the book's voice; §5.5 relies on this statement for restoration.
- Source: dependency of §5.5; preserve list

### CH-17-025 · §17.3 Asylum Demon and Stray Demon
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "*Example.* The Stray Demon resolves a behaviour card with a physical area attack whose attack arcs are its front arc and its left arc. Two pillar tokens stand within range in the front arc. ..."
- Rationale: A worked example of two pillars destroyed by one attack and of the order of damage, stagger, and attack; it introduces no rule.
- Source: brief §2 (worked examples)

### CH-17-026 · §17.3 Asylum Demon and Stray Demon
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The *Wind Up* icon appears on behaviour cards of the Asylum Demon and of the Stray Demon. A Wind Up icon printed on any other behaviour card is resolved in the same way."
- Rationale: Fixes the scope of the icon: the rule stays with the two bosses known to use it and covers any other card that shows it.
- Source: CMP-0017

### CH-17-027 · §17.3 Asylum Demon and Stray Demon
- Type: clarification
- Before: "When the Wind Up behaviour icon is resolved, the characters may each spend one stamina to immediately move their model to an adjacent node. This movement is considered a *Run* for purposes of rules interaction."
- After: "The movement counts as a *Run* (see §10.2), and every rule that applies to a Run applies to it. The characters complete the movement before the next icon on the behaviour card is resolved (see §16.1)."
- Rationale: Preserved mechanic; the timing against the rest of the card and each player's choice are made explicit.
- Source: CMP-0017; CMP-0005

### CH-17-028 · §17.4 Darkroot
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Sif's behaviour cards include the cool-down card Limping Strike, which shows Sif weakened and close to death. The cool-down card is used only as Sif's *data card* directs."
- Rationale: The data card is the only source for the cool-down; the entry states the boundary and invents no trigger.
- Source: CMP-0005

### CH-17-029 · §17.4 Darkroot
- Type: completion
- Before: new text

### CH-17-053 · §17.1, Two Boss Models in One Encounter
- Type: ruling
- Before: "### Two Boss Models in One Encounter

Some bosses are fought with more than one model. The rules under this heading apply whenever two or more boss models stand on separate *nodes* in one encounter. The two models of the Executioner's Chariot share one node and act as a single model (see §17.9).

Each boss model is placed at the start of the encounter on the node that the boss's entry names. Each boss model has its own four *boss arcs* (see §15.6). A character who shares a node with a boss model is placed in the arcs of that model.

A node holds at most one boss model (see §5.3). When a boss model moves onto a node that holds another boss model, the model already on the node is *pushed* to a *valid node* chosen by the players and keeps its facing (see §8.4). Characters on either node are placed in the arcs of the boss model that now shares their node, as when a boss m […]"
- After: "### Several Boss Models in One Encounter

Some bosses are fought with more than one boss model. The rules under this heading apply to a boss whose models stand on separate *nodes*: Ornstein & Smough (see §17.2) and the Four Kings (see §17.10, The Four Kings). The two models of the Executioner's Chariot share one node and act as a single model (see §17.9).

The models are one boss, fought in one encounter with one *behaviour deck*. Each boss model has its own *health dial*, its own four *boss arcs* (see §15.6), and its own *condition* tokens. A character who shares a node with a boss model is placed in the arcs of that model.

A node holds at most one boss model (see §5.3). When a boss model moves onto a node that holds another boss model, the model already on the node is *pushed* to a *valid node* chosen by the players and keeps its facing (see §8.4). Characters on ei […]"
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] One framework for every boss with several models: each model has its own health dial and all models act on each card, as the official cards for Ornstein & Smough and the booklet for the Four Kings both direct. Revises Gate 1 OQ-25 for Ornstein & Smough.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E08 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Ornstein data card 'United We Stand' and Smough data card 'Divided You Fall' (community transcription, DSBG-Shuffle); Four Kings booklet lines 445-456; 2017 core rules lines 430-433 and 968-971.

### CH-17-054 · §17.2
- Type: ruling
- Before: "### Dragon Slayer Ornstein and Executioner Smough

Ornstein & Smough is one main-boss fought with two models, Dragon Slayer Ornstein and Executioner Smough (see §2.3).

**Components.** The two models share one data card, one *health dial*, one set of *behaviour cards*, and one set of *boss treasure* cards.

**Setup.** The joined *boss tiles* of a main-boss encounter show two boss *spawn nodes*: the main-boss spawn node and the mini-boss spawn node (see §15.3). One model is placed on each, as the data card directs. If the data card names no *node* for a model, the players choose which model takes which spawn node. One *behaviour deck* is built for the *boss* (see §15.5), and the health dial is set to the starting health on the data card.

**Special ability.** The rules for two boss models in one encounter apply in full (see §17.1, Two Boss Models in One Encounter). Eac […]"
- After: "### Dragon Slayer Ornstein and Executioner Smough

Ornstein & Smough is one main-boss fought with two boss models, Dragon Slayer Ornstein and Executioner Smough (see §2.3). Both models are in play from the start of the encounter, and the boss is defeated only when both have been defeated. The rules for several boss models in one encounter apply (see §17.1, Several Boss Models in One Encounter).

**Components.** Each model has its own data card and its own *health dial*. The boss has 15 *behaviour cards*: five paired standard cards, each of which shows one behaviour for Ornstein and one for Smough; five *heat-up cards* for Ornstein; and five heat-up cards for Smough. The two models share one set of *boss treasure* cards.

**Setup.** The joined *boss tiles* of a main-boss encounter show two boss *spawn nodes*: the main-boss spawn node and the mini-boss spawn node (see § […]"
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Restores the official structure: two models with their own health, both acting each turn, and a heat-up when the first falls. It matches the printed cards and keeps the fight's one real decision, which of the two to defeat first. Revises Gate 1 OQ-25.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E10 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Data cards 'United We Stand' and 'Divided You Fall' and card list (community transcription, DSBG-Shuffle lib/dsbg_shuffle_behaviors.py lines 32 and 1703-1835; lib/dsbg_shuffle_behavior_decks.py lines 718-737: +10 and +15 health, capped); publisher forum thread 37344 summary; 2024 core rulebook component list (2 data cards, 15 behaviour cards).

### CH-17-055 · §17.3, Setup
- Type: completion
- Before: "At the start of every boss *encounter* fought on the mega-boss side of that game board, one pillar token is placed on each of its four pillar nodes, before the *boss* takes its first *turn*. A *node* that holds a pillar token is closed to every model (see §5.5, Pillars). An encounter fought on a tile without pillar nodes uses no pillar tokens."
- After: "The Asylum Demon is fought as any mini-boss, except that its *encounter* takes place on the mega-boss side of the expansion's mega-boss game board in place of the mini-boss tile (see §15.3). During setup that game board is placed where the mini-boss tile is placed, with its mega-boss side face up and the *fog gate* token on its doorway (see §6.5), and the Asylum Demon's model is placed on the mega-boss *spawn node* when the encounter is set up. The Stray Demon's encounter is set up as any mega-boss encounter (see §6.7).

At the start of every *boss* encounter fought on the mega-boss side of that game board, one pillar token is placed on each of its four pillar nodes, before the boss takes its first *turn*. A *node* that holds a pillar token is closed to every model (see §5.5, Pillars)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Settles the open fact: the Asylum Demon mini-boss is fought on the mega-boss game board, so both demons meet the pillars. The sentence about tiles without pillar nodes is no longer needed.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E11 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Asylum Demon booklet, DS-AD-Rules-web-EN.txt lines 183-205.

### CH-17-057 · §17.4, Great Grey Wolf Sif
- Type: completion
- Before: "**Special ability.** Sif's behaviour cards include the cool-down card Limping Strike, which shows Sif weakened and close to death. The cool-down card is used only as Sif's *data card* directs. Sif has no other rules beyond the data card."
- After: "**Special ability.** Sif's special ability is Mournful Howl. The cool-down card Limping Strike, which shows Sif weakened and close to death, is neither a standard behaviour card nor a *heat-up card*. It is set aside when the *behaviour deck* is built. When Sif's health is reduced to three or less, the behaviour deck and its discard pile are returned to the game box at once, and Limping Strike alone forms the behaviour deck: Sif draws and resolves it on each of its *turns* until the *encounter* ends (see §15.5, Deck Renewal). Sif has no other rules beyond its *data card*."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Replaces 'as the data card directs' with the data card's rule, so the entry is complete.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E13 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Sif data card 'Mournful Howl' (community transcription, DSBG-Shuffle card image and lib/dsbg_shuffle_behavior_decks.py line 445); Darkroot booklet lines 174-183.

### CH-17-060 · §17.10, The Four Kings
- Type: completion
- Before: "**The Four Kings (mega-boss, The Four Kings expansion).** The Four Kings is played from its *data card* and the general boss rules (see §15 and §16). Apply the special rules supplied with that expansion. The Four Kings is fought with several boss models (see §17.1, Two Boss Models in One Encounter)."

### The Four Kings

The Four Kings is a mega-boss fought with four *boss* models, the kings numbered one to four, who enter the *encounter* one at a time. The rules for several boss models in one encounter apply (see §17.1, Several Boss Models in One Encounter).

**Components.**

- 4x Four Kings models
- 4x *Health dials*, numbered one to four
- 4x Identifier tokens, numbered one to four
- 1x *Data card*
- 20x *Behaviour cards*: 8 standard cards, 4 second king cards, 4 third king cards, and 4 fourth king cards
- 2x Mega-boss treasure cards
- 4x Level 4 encounter cards
- 1x Mega-boss game boa […]"
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Replaces the placeholder with the official rules of the Four Kings, restated in this book's terms.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E20 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Four Kings booklet, work/text/DS-Four-Kings-Rules-web.txt lines 56-80 (contents), 368-395 (encounter start), 439-480 (One through Four, Royal Summons, Take a Breather).

### CH-17-061 · §17.10, Gaping Dragon
- Type: completion
- Before: "**Gaping Dragon (mega-boss, Gaping Dragon expansion).** The Gaping Dragon is played from its data card and the general boss rules (see §15 and §16). Apply the special rules supplied with that expansion. The Gaping Dragon's attacks inflict the *corrosion* *condition* (see §8.5, Corrosion)."
- After: "### Gaping Dragon

The Gaping Dragon is a mega-boss.

**Components.**

- 1x Gaping Dragon model
- 1x Data card
- 1x Health dial
- 14x Behaviour cards: 7 standard cards, 2 signature cards (both named Stomach Slam), 4 heat-up cards, and 1 Crawling Charge card
- 4x Corrosion tokens
- 2x Mega-boss treasure cards
- 4x Level 4 encounter cards
- 1x Mega-boss game board

**Setup.** The mega-boss encounter is set up as any mega-boss encounter (see §6.7), with the following differences.

1. Place the Crawling Charge card face up beside the data card. It is never part of the behaviour deck.
2. Shuffle the seven standard behaviour cards and draw three.
3. Shuffle the four *heat-up cards* and draw one.
4. Add the two *signature cards* to the four drawn cards, and shuffle the six cards to form the behaviour deck.
5. Return the four undrawn standard cards to the game box, and place […]"
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Replaces the placeholder with the official rules of the Gaping Dragon, restated in this book's terms.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E21 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Gaping Dragon booklet, work/text/DS-GaD-Rules-web.txt lines 57-80 (contents), 367-400 (encounter start), 437-470 (Corrosion, Crawling Charge).

### CH-17-062 · §17.10, Guardian Dragon
- Type: completion
- Before: "**Guardian Dragon (mega-boss, Guardian Dragon expansion).** The Guardian Dragon is played from its data card and the general boss rules (see §15 and §16). Apply the special rules supplied with that expansion."

The Guardian Dragon is a mega-boss.

- 1x Guardian Dragon model
