**Cage node.** One of the four nodes at the corners of the mega-boss side of the Guardian Dragon's game board. No model may *move*, *dodge*, or be pushed into a cage node. The Guardian Dragon's model is placed on a cage node only when it resolves an Inferno icon.

**Fiery Breath card.** A card that shows the cage node on which the Guardian Dragon's model is placed, the nodes that an Inferno attack targets, and the *attack value* and dodge difficulty of that attack.

**Setup.** The mega-boss encou […]"

- Source: final rulings, boss-E22 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Guardian Dragon booklet, work/text/DS-GD-Rules-web.txt lines 54-75 (contents), 359-395 (encounter start), 432-470 (Cage Nodes, Cage Grasp Inferno, Bonus Behaviour); Fiery Breath card diagram on booklet p. 13 (attack value and dodge difficulty printed on the card).

5. Set aside the Falling Slam card, and shuffle the five heat-up cards face down beside the game board.

**Special ability.** The Last Giant's special ability is Armed and Dangerous. When the Last Giant h […]"

### CH-17-064 · §17.10, Manus, Father of the Abyss
- Type: completion
- Before: "**Manus, Father of the Abyss (mega-boss, Manus, Father of the Abyss expansion).** Manus, Father of the Abyss is played from its data card and the general boss rules (see §15 and §16). Apply the special rules supplied with that expansion."
- After: "### Manus, Father of the Abyss

Manus, Father of the Abyss is a mega-boss. Its behaviour deck is built by the general procedure (see §15.5, Deck Construction).

**Components.**

- 1x Manus, Father of the Abyss model
- 1x Data card
- 1x Health dial
- 14x Behaviour cards: 9 standard cards and 5 heat-up cards
- 2x Mega-boss treasure cards
- 4x Level 4 encounter cards
- 1x Mega-boss game board

**Special ability.** Manus's special ability is Overextended. At the end of each of Manus's turns, each character may spend one stamina to move to an adjacent node. Each player decides for their own character. The movement counts as a *Run* (see §10.2), and every rule that applies to a Run applies to it. The characters complete the movement before the next character's turn begins (see §16.1).

**Heat-up.** Manus does not heat up as other bosses do (see §15.5, Heat-Up). When Manus's […]"
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Replaces the placeholder with the official rules of Manus, restated in this book's terms.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E24 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Manus booklet, work/text/DS-MFA-Rules.txt lines 58-75 (contents), 370-400 (encounter start and heat-up, 'Do not shuffle the behaviour deck!'), 440-480 (Shaft, Overextended).

### CH-17-065 · §17.10, Old Iron King
- Type: completion
- Before: "**Old Iron King (mega-boss, Old Iron King expansion).** The Old Iron King is played from its data card and the general boss rules (see §15 and §16). Apply the special rules supplied with that expansion."
- After: "### Old Iron King

The Old Iron King is a mega-boss that stands in lava and is placed only on three nodes of its game board.

**Components.**

- 1x Old Iron King model
- 1x Data card
- 1x Health dial
- 12x Behaviour cards: 6 standard cards, 3 signature cards (each named Fire Beam), and 3 heat-up cards
- 6x Beam cards
- 2x Mega-boss treasure cards
- 4x Level 4 encounter cards
- 1x Mega-boss game board, whose mega-boss side shows three Iron King nodes

**Iron King node.** One of the three nodes, printed on the mega-boss side of the Old Iron King's game board, on which the Old Iron King's model is placed. The Old Iron King never stands on any other node.

**Beam card.** A card that shows the Iron King node on which the Old Iron King's model is placed and the nodes that a Beam attack targets.

The area behind the Iron King nodes is lava. It holds no nodes and is treated a […]"
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Replaces the placeholder with the official rules of the Old Iron King, restated in this book's terms.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E25 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Old Iron King booklet, work/text/DS-OIK-Rules-web.txt lines 54-75 (contents), 349-385 (encounter start and heat-up), 418-460 (Molten Iron Dweller, Beam Attacks); Beam card diagram on booklet p. 13.

### CH-17-066 · §17.10, Vordt of the Boreal Valley
- Type: completion
- Before: "**Vordt of the Boreal Valley (mega-boss, Vordt of the Boreal Valley expansion).** Vordt of the Boreal Valley is played from its data card and the general boss rules (see §15 and §16). Apply the special rules supplied with that expansion. Vordt's attacks inflict the *frostbite* condition (see §8.5, Frostbite)."
- After: "### Vordt of the Boreal Valley

Vordt of the Boreal Valley is a mega-boss that draws from two behaviour decks and heats up twice.

**Components.**

- 1x Vordt of the Boreal Valley model
- 1x Data card
- 1x Health dial
- 17x Behaviour cards: 6 standard movement cards, 2 heat-up movement cards, 6 standard attack cards, 2 heat-up attack cards, and 1 Frostbreath card
- 2x Mega-boss treasure cards
- 4x Level 4 encounter cards
- 1x Mega-boss game board

**Setup.** The mega-boss encounter is set up as any mega-boss encounter (see §6.7), with the following differences. Two decks are built in place of the general behaviour deck.

1. Shuffle the six standard movement cards and draw four as the movement deck.
2. Shuffle the six standard attack cards and draw three as the attack deck.
3. Place the attack deck face down to the right of the movement deck. The two decks and their di […]"
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Replaces the placeholder with the official rules of Vordt, restated in this book's terms.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E26 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Vordt booklet, work/text/DS-VotBV-Rules-web.txt lines 57-85 (contents), 378-420 (encounter start, two heat-ups), 457-490 (two decks, Frostbreath); data card 'Icy Blows'.

### CH-17-067 · §17.10, Crossbreed Priscilla
- Type: clarification
- Before: "**Crossbreed Priscilla (main-boss, Painted World of Ariamis expansion).** Crossbreed Priscilla is played from its data card and the general boss rules (see §15 and §16). Apply the special rules supplied with that expansion. Its data card and *behaviour cards* are the replacement cards listed in the card amendments (see Appendix B)."
- After: "### Crossbreed Priscilla

Crossbreed Priscilla is a *main-boss* of the Painted World of Ariamis expansion. It is played from its data card and the general boss rules (see §15 and §16). Apply the special rules supplied with that expansion. Its data card and behaviour cards are the replacement cards listed in the card amendments (see Appendix B)."
- Rationale: Placeholder kept: the rule that makes Priscilla invisible and visible again was not found. Only the form changes, to match the other entries of §17.10.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E27 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Printed data card 'Snowblind' found (community transcription, DSBG-Shuffle); no source found for the invisible state; see boss-009.

### CH-17-068 · §17.10, Heavy Knight
- Type: completion
- Before: "**Heavy Knight (mini-boss, Painted World of Ariamis expansion).** The Heavy Knight is played from its data card and the general boss rules (see §15 and §16). Apply the special rules supplied with that expansion. Its data card and behaviour cards are the replacement cards listed in the card amendments (see Appendix B)."

The Heavy Knight is a *mini-boss* of the Painted World of Ariamis expansion. It is played with the replacement data card and behaviour cards listed in the card amendments (see Appendix B), and it has no rules beyond its data card."

- Source: final rulings, boss-E28 (`06_final_rulings.md`); 2022 rulebook lines 2099-2290 (boss rules shared by both 2022 sets; no special rule per boss); printed data card 'Brutal Impact' (community transcription, DSBG-Shuffle), a self-contained ability.

### CH-17-069 · §17.6
- Type: correction
- Before: "When the Pursuer's health is reduced to or below its *heat-up* point {{icon:ast-0030-heat-up-point}}, one random heat-up card is shuffled into its behaviour deck, as for any *boss* (see §15.5). From that moment until the *encounter* ends, the *attack value* of every attack on the Pursuer's behaviour cards is increased by one."
- After: "When the Pursuer's health is reduced to or below its *heat-up* point {{icon:ast-0030-heat-up-point}}, it heats up as the general rule directs (see §15.5, Heat-Up). From the moment the heat-up is carried out until the *encounter* ends, the *attack value* of every attack on the Pursuer's behaviour cards is increased by one."

## Chapter 19 — Campaigns

### CH-19-001 · §19 Campaigns
- Type: restructure
- Before: "# CAMPAIGNS" followed by thirteen H3 campaign headings, nested bullet lists, and two empty headings used as page breaks
- After: "# 19. Campaigns" with twelve numbered H2 campaigns (19.1 The First Journey to 19.12 Big Game Hunting); each section is an H3 followed by a numbered list of tiles
- Rationale: The chapter jumped from H1 to H3, used bullets for ordered tile sequences, and carried empty headings. Every campaign now has the same shape: requirement sentence, special rules, sections.
- Source: C-104, C-098, C-101, C-105, CMP-0008

### CH-19-002 · §19 Campaigns
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Each campaign states the sets it requires and lists the tiles of each *section* in order outward from the *bonfire* tile; a section is laid out and completed as the campaign setup rules state (see §18.1)."
- Rationale: The chapter had no introduction. A 39-word introduction and one pointer sentence are added; the count is twelve after the duplicate campaign is removed, and layout and completion are delegated to their owning section.
- Source: CMP-0008, C-074, A-103, Gate 1 (twelve campaigns)

### CH-19-003 · §19.1 to §19.12 (campaign headings)
- Type: cut
- Before: "### The Coiled Sword - Dark Souls 3 (Core)"
- After: "## 19.2 The Coiled Sword"
- Rationale: The video-game title is an outside reference, and the component tag duplicates the requirement sentence that now opens every campaign. Trailing spaces in the headings are removed.
- Source: F-052, C-101, C-104

### CH-19-004 · §19.1 to §19.12 (requirement sentences)
- Type: style
- Before: "This campaign requires the Core set and Iron Keep expansion. Players should add the Explorers and Characters expansions if available."
- After: "This campaign requires the core set and the Iron Keep expansion. The party may add the Explorers and Characters expansions."
- Rationale: One fixed sentence form for every campaign. "Should add" left it unclear whether the expansions were required; it is now a permission given to the party.
- Source: C-018, F-089

### CH-19-005 · §19.1 to §19.12 (list items)
- Type: style
- Before: "Bonfire Tile / Level 3 Encounter - Hydra Lake / Great Grey Wolf Sif (Main Boss)."
- After: "Bonfire tile / Level 3 encounter: Hydra Lake / Great Grey Wolf Sif (*main-boss*)"
- Rationale: Five fixed forms are used in every list: bonfire tile, Level N encounter, named encounter after a colon, encounter with a named invader, and boss with a lower-case hyphenated rank. No list item carries terminal punctuation.
- Source: C-041, C-042, C-105, CMP-0008

### CH-19-006 · §19.2 and §19.12 (boss names)
- Type: correction
- Before: "The Dancer of the Boreal Valley (main-boss)"; "Manus (Mega Boss)"
- After: "Dancer of the Boreal Valley (*main-boss*)"; "Manus, Father of the Abyss (mega-boss)"
- Rationale: Boss names match the component lists of §2.3 so that the model and data card can be found. "Ornstein & Smough" keeps its ampersand as a printed name.
- Source: C-013, C-112

### CH-19-007 · §19.1, §19.3, and §19.6 (Twin Gargoyles)
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Gargoyle (Mini-Boss) / Gargoyle (Mini-Boss)"
- After: "Gargoyle (*mini-boss*), fought twice (see §17.1)"
- Rationale: The doubled line had no rule attached. The Gargoyle is listed once and the back-to-back fight is cited to its owning section.
- Source: A-040, D-012, C-105, CMP-0008, Gate 1 — OQ-25

### CH-19-008 · §19.1 The First Journey and §19.2 The Coiled Sword
- Type: style
- Before: "Use only encounters from the core set when setting up each section."
- After: "The encounter decks of every *section* are built only from the *encounter cards* of the core set (see §6.5, Expansion Encounters)."
- Rationale: The instruction becomes a standing rule that names the component and cites the section that owns deck building. In §19.1 it is limited to Sections One to Three because Section Four has its own source.
- Source: F-089, CMP-0021

### CH-19-009 · §19.1 The First Journey, Section Four: Bathed in Flame
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Section Four is an optional rule. It requires the Black Dragon Kalameet expansion. The party decides during setup whether Section Four is in play."
- Rationale: Black Dragon Kalameet was in the component list but in no campaign. The optional fourth section is added with its list (bonfire tile, two Level 3 encounters, a Level 4 encounter, Kalameet) and the fixed optional-rule formula.
- Source: CMP-0021

### CH-19-010 · §19.1 The First Journey, Section Four: Bathed in Flame
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The *mega-boss game board* is placed with its Level 4 encounter side face up and with one of its *doorways* aligned with a doorway of the second Level 3 encounter tile, not with a doorway of the bonfire tile (see §18.1)."
- Rationale: States the one layout fact particular to this section. The flip of the board and the no-reset rule are cited to §6.7, which owns them, and the Darkroot encounter cards are given as a conditional sentence.
- Source: CMP-0021

### CH-19-011 · §19.3 Ambush Lies Ahead and §19.4 The Gauntlet
- Type: correction
- Before: "Do no place invader tokens during the campaign. Instead, just add the invaders below. Invaders only appear the first time the characters enter an encounter. Regardless of outcome, invaders only appear once each."
- After: "The party does not place *invader tokens* during this campaign (see §12.2). […] Each listed invader appears once in the campaign, whether that encounter is *cleared* or *failed*."
- Rationale: "Do no place" read as a permission; it is now a prohibition with the party as actor. The filler "just" and the prose reference "below" are removed, and the invader is fought under its owning rules.
- Source: C-015, F-081, F-090, CMP-0008

### CH-19-012 · §19.3 Ambush Lies Ahead and §19.4 The Gauntlet
- Type: style
- Before: "Use encounters from the core set and the phantoms expansion plus any other expansions available."
- After: "This campaign requires the core set and the Phantoms expansion. The party may add the *encounter cards* of any other expansion."
- Rationale: The sentence is split into the fixed requirement form and a permission.
- Source: C-018, F-089

### CH-19-013 · §19.4 The Gauntlet, Section Three
- Type: correction
- Before: "Section Three - Invader Ruins"
- After: "### Section Three: Invader Spire"
- Rationale: The name duplicated Section One's. Checked against the supplied Phantoms text, which names the section Invader Spire.
- Source: A-109, CMP-0008

### CH-19-014 · §19.4 The Gauntlet (all three sections)
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Section One is completed when the Level 2 encounter with Kirk, Knight of Thorns is cleared (see §18.1)."
- Rationale: The Gauntlet has no boss, and every completion rule was keyed to a boss. Each section now states the encounter whose clearing completes it.
- Source: A-048, CMP-0008

### CH-19-015 · §19.4 The Gauntlet, Section Three
- Type: correction
- Before: "Level 2 Encounter with Armourer Dennis." / "Level 3 Encounter with Marvellous Chester."
- After: "Level 2 encounter with Armorer Dennis" / "Level 3 encounter with Marvelous Chester"
- Rationale: Proper nouns printed on cards keep their printed spelling, and the names now match §2.4.
- Source: D-027, Gate 1 — OQ-17

### CH-19-016 · §19.5 Facing the Abyss
- Type: cut
- Before: "### Facing the Abyss - Dark Souls (Darkroot Basin)" and "### Facing the Abyss - Dark Souls (Darkroot)" (two entries; the second gives Section Two as Level 1, Level 2, Level 2, Level 3)
- After: one entry, "## 19.5 Facing the Abyss", with Section Two as bonfire tile, Level 1, Level 2, Level 3, Level 3, Great Grey Wolf Sif (main-boss)
- Rationale: The campaign appeared twice. The first copy is kept because its lists agree with the supplied Darkroot text; the second copy's Section Two was a transcription error.
- Source: C-102, A-106, CMP-0008

### CH-19-017 · §19.5 Facing the Abyss
- Type: restructure
- Before: "Use encounters from only the Darkroot basin expansion during this campaign." / "This campaign requires the Core set and Darkroot expansion but players should add the Explorers and Characters expansions if available."
- After: "This campaign requires the core set and the Darkroot expansion. The party may add the Explorers and Characters expansions." followed by the deck rule citing §6.5
- Rationale: The two preambles are merged so that neither the requirement nor the encounter-card source is lost.
- Source: C-102, A-106, C-018, CMP-0008

### CH-19-018 · §19.5 Facing the Abyss and §19.11 Playing with Fire, Section One
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Section One is completed when Hydra Lake is *cleared* (see §18.1)."
- Rationale: Both sections end in a named encounter and no boss. One sentence under each states when the section is completed and cites the owning rule.
- Source: A-048, CMP-0008

### CH-19-019 · §19.6 Demon Hunting, Section One
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Bonfire Tile (characters revive here if they die in Section One)"
- After: "Section One has no *bonfire* tile. When the characters rest during Section One, the party spends one *spark* and the characters are restored as for any rest (see §11.2)."
- Rationale: The parenthetical said where the characters revive but not what happens to the section. The rule now gives the spark, the revival on Section Two's bonfire tile, the restart of Section One, and the absence of the Firekeeper and Blacksmith Andre.
- Source: A-104, CMP-0008

### CH-19-020 · §19.8 A Hollow Pursuit
- Type: correction
- Before: "This campaign requires the core set and Asylum Demon expansion but players should add the Explorers and characters expansions if available."
- After: "This campaign requires the core set and the Explorers expansion. The party may add the Characters expansion."
- Rationale: The line was copied from Demon Hunting. The Old Dragonslayer, the Voracious Mimic, and the Pursuer are all Explorers components.
- Source: A-107, C-076, CMP-0008

### CH-19-021 · §19.8 A Hollow Pursuit, Section Two
- Type: correction
- Before: "Add Voracious Mimic during encounter set-up.."
- After: "No *ambush card* is placed beside the tile of Gallery of the Hidden Warrior. The *chest* on that tile is a Voracious Mimic."
- Rationale: Placing the mimic at encounter setup contradicted the mimic rules. The chest is the mimic, the mimic encounter begins when the chest is opened, and the section is completed when the mimic is killed.
- Source: A-102, C-059, C-106, CMP-0008

### CH-19-022 · §19.8 A Hollow Pursuit, Section One
- Type: correction
- Before: "Section One - Crestfallen's Retreat"
- After: "### Section One: The Crestfallen's Retreat"
- Rationale: Transcription error found when the campaign was checked against the supplied Explorers text, which prints the article.
- Source: A-111 (verification), CMP-0008

### CH-19-023 · §19.9 The Undead Colosseum
- Type: correction
- Before: "This campaign requires the Core set, Darkroot expansion, Vordt of the Boreal Valley expansion, and Executioner Chariot expansion. Players should add the Explorers and Characters expansions if available."
- After: "This campaign requires the core set, the Darkroot expansion, the Explorers expansion, the Vordt of the Boreal Valley expansion, and the Executioner's Chariot expansion. The party may add the Characters expansion."
- Rationale: The Old Dragonslayer and the Pursuer are Explorers bosses, so Explorers is required. This follows C-076 where the CMP-0022 draft left Explorers optional.
- Source: C-076, C-018, CMP-0022

### CH-19-024 · §19.9 The Undead Colosseum
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The Level 1, Level 2, and Level 3 encounter decks are built from the *encounter cards* of the core set, the Darkroot expansion, and the Explorers expansion together."
- Rationale: Adds the campaign's deck-building rules: shared Level 1 to 3 decks, a shared Level 4 deck from the two mega-boss expansions, and the six named encounters from the Executioner's Chariot expansion.
- Source: CMP-0022

### CH-19-025 · §19.9 The Undead Colosseum
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A cleared encounter is not fought again, and the party passes its tile without paying the cost of skipping it (see §7.5, Encounter Start)."
- Rationale: Restores the campaign's three special rules: no reset of cleared encounters, no repeat fights, and a soul reward of the campaign reward plus two. Flavour and rationale in the draft are dropped.
- Source: CMP-0022, D-013, A-110

### CH-19-026 · §19.9 The Undead Colosseum, Sections Two and Six
- Type: correction
- Before: "Bonfire Tile / Level 3 Encounter - Desolate Cemetery / Vordt of the Boreal Valley (Mega Boss)"; "Bonfire Tiles"
- After: "Bonfire tile / Level 3 encounter: Desolate Cemetery / Level 4 encounter / Vordt of the Boreal Valley (mega-boss)"; "Bonfire tile"
- Rationale: Section Six lacked its Level 4 encounter, confirmed against the supplied Executioner's Chariot text. Section Two's plural was a typing error.
- Source: D-013, A-110, C-105, CMP-0008

### CH-19-027 · §19.10 Into the Depths
- Type: correction
- Before: "This campaign requires the Core set and Gaping Dragon expansion. Players should add the Darkroot, Explorers, and Characters expansions if available."
- After: "This campaign requires the core set, the Darkroot expansion, and the Gaping Dragon expansion. The party may add the Explorers and Characters expansions."

### CH-19-028 · §19.12 Big Game Hunting
- Type: correction
- Before: "This campaign requires the Core set, Manus expansion, and Asylum Demon expansion. Players should add the Explorers and Characters expansions if available."
- After: "This campaign requires the core set, the Asylum Demon expansion, The Last Giant expansion, and the Manus, Father of the Abyss expansion. The party may add the Explorers and Characters expansions."
- Rationale: Section Two A ends in The Last Giant, whose expansion was missing from the requirement. The Last Giant stays in the campaign as a mega-boss.
