### CH-19-029 · §19.12 Big Game Hunting
- Type: style
- Before: "For this campaign, all boss encounters take place on a mega boss encounter tile, even the mini bosses. Section 2A, 2B, 2C are available for exploration in any order the players choose."
- After: "Every *boss* encounter of this campaign, including the *mini-boss* encounter, is fought on a *mega-boss game board*. After Section One is completed, the party plays Section Two A, Section Two B, and Section Two C in the order it chooses."
- Rationale: Canonical component and rank terms, one rule per sentence, and the party as the actor. Section labels are written as the headings give them.
- Source: C-041, C-122, F-089

### CH-19-030 · §19 Campaigns (style sweep)
- Type: style
- Before: "Andor Londo"; "Lower Earthern Peak"; "Old Dragon Slayer"; "Section Four- Haunting Warrior"; "Section Five Dark Champion"; "Gaping Dragon(Mega Boss)"; "Executioner Chariot"; "Core set"; full stops after list items; curly apostrophes
- After: "Anor Londo"; "Lower Earthen Peak"; "Old Dragonslayer"; "Section Four: Haunting Warrior"; "Section Five: Dark Champion"; "Gaping Dragon (*mega-boss*)"; "Executioner's Chariot"; "core set"; no terminal punctuation; straight apostrophes
- Rationale: Spelling, spacing, and typography only. "Xanthous King Jeremiah" and "Great Grey Wolf Sif" are confirmed in every list.
- Source: C-003, C-004, C-005, C-009, C-015, C-105, CMP-0008

### CH-19-031 · §19 Campaigns (keyword italics)
- Type: style
- Before: keywords set in plain type throughout the campaign lists
- After: each glossary headword is italic at its first use in each campaign, in prose or in a list item, and plain thereafter
- Rationale: One italics policy for the book. Only existing headwords are italicised; headings carry no italics.
- Source: C-030

### CH-19-032 · §19 Campaigns (verification record)
- Type: correction
- Before: section lists as transcribed in the manuscript
- After: lists of §19.1 to §19.5, §19.8, §19.9, and §19.11 checked line by line against the supplied texts
- Rationale: The First Journey, The Coiled Sword, Ambush Lies Ahead, The Gauntlet, Facing the Abyss (first copy), A Hollow Pursuit, The Undead Colosseum, Playing with Fire, and the Kalameet section agree with their sources except for the errors logged in CH-19-013, CH-19-015, CH-19-016, CH-19-022, and CH-19-026. The four campaigns from unsupplied sources are kept as the manuscript gives them.
- Source: A-111, CMP-0008

### CH-19-033 · §19 Campaigns (findings owned by other chapters; optional findings)
- Type: clarification
- Before: findings A-039, A-049, A-094, C-074, and A-103 mapped to this chapter's pack
- After: no text in this chapter
- Rationale: A-039 is settled in §2.3 and §17.2 (one boss, listed here as "Ornstein & Smough"). A-049 is moot (Gate 1 — OQ-14). A-094 belongs to §6.7 and §18.1 and is cited from §19.1 only. C-074 and A-103 correct §18.1; the introduction here states twelve. No OPTIONAL finding was skipped: A-110, F-052, and D-027 are applied.
- Source: A-039, A-049, A-094, C-074, A-103, A-110, F-052, D-027, Gate 1 — OQ-14, OQ-25

### CH-19-034 · §19.4
- Type: correction
- Before: "## 19.4 The Gauntlet

This campaign requires the core set and the Phantoms expansion. The party may add the *encounter cards* of any other expansion.

The party does not place *invader tokens* during this campaign (see §12.2). An *encounter* that lists an *invader* includes that invader the first time the characters enter that encounter, and the invader is fought under the invader rules (see §12.2). Each listed invader appears once in the campaign, whether that encounter is *cleared* or *failed*."
- After: "## 19.4 The Gauntlet

This campaign requires the core set and the Phantoms expansion. The party may add the *encounter cards* of any other expansion.

The party does not place *invader tokens* during this campaign (see §12.2). An *encounter* that lists an *invader* includes that invader every time the characters enter that encounter, until the invader is killed. A listed invader that has been killed does not appear again. The invader is fought under the invader rules (see §12.2), except that an invader that vanishes, or whose encounter is *failed*, returns the next time the characters enter that encounter."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] The Gauntlet's own rule differs from Ambush Lies Ahead: each invader must be beaten, which is the point of the campaign.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E35 (`06_final_rulings.md`); DS-Phantoms-Rules-lores.txt 636-640: 'Unlike the standard invader rules or other campaign encounters, when an encounter lists an invader, that invader is part of the encounter every time the party enters that encounter until the invader is defeated.'

### CH-19-035 · §19.6
- Type: correction
- Before: "Section One has no *bonfire* tile. When the characters rest during Section One, the party spends one *spark* and the characters are restored as for any rest (see §11.2). The characters revive on the bonfire tile listed in Section Two and enter Section One again at its first *encounter* tile. The *Firekeeper* and *Blacksmith Andre* cannot be visited until Section Two.

### Section One: Undead Asylum

1. Level 1 encounter
2. Level 1 encounter
3. Asylum Demon (*mini-boss*)"
- After: "In Section One the Asylum Demon is fought on the mega-boss side of the Asylum Demon expansion's *mega-boss game board*, with its pillar tokens (see §17.3).

### Section One: Undead Asylum

1. *Bonfire* tile
2. Level 1 *encounter*
3. Level 1 encounter
4. Asylum Demon (*mini-boss*)"
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] The official list gives Section One a bonfire tile, so the special rest rule answers a problem that does not exist and is deleted.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E36 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official Asylum Demon booklet, Demon Hunting: 'Section 1 Undead Asylum: Bonfire Tile, Level 1 Encounter, Level 1 Encounter, Asylum Demon (Mini Boss)'; same booklet: the Asylum Demon's encounter 'takes place on the Mega Boss game board instead of the Mini Boss tile'.

### CH-19-036 · §19.6
- Type: correction
- Before: "### Section Four: Sen's Fortress"
- After: "### Section Four: Northern Undead Asylum"
- Rationale: The duplicated section name was a copying error.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E37 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official Asylum Demon booklet: 'Section 4 Northern Undead Asylum: Bonfire Tile, Level 2 Encounter, Level 3 Encounter, Level 4 Encounter, Stray Demon (Mega Boss)'.

### CH-19-037 · §19.7
- Type: correction
- Before: "### Section Four: The Abyss

1. Bonfire tile
2. *Level 4 encounter*
3. Level 4 encounter
4. The Four Kings (*mega-boss*)"
- After: "### Section Four: Penultimate Trial

1. Bonfire tile
2. Level 3 encounter
3. Level 3 encounter
4. *Level 4 encounter*
5. Ornstein & Smough (main-boss)

### Section Five: The Abyss

1. Bonfire tile
2. Level 4 encounter
3. Level 4 encounter
4. The Four Kings (*mega-boss*)"
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] The official campaign has five sections; the manuscript omitted the fourth.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E38 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official Four Kings booklet, Call of the Abyss: 'Section 4 Penultimate Trial: Bonfire Tile, Level 3 Encounter, Level 3 Encounter, Level 4 Encounter, Ornstein & Smough (Main Boss)'; 'Section 5 The Abyss: Bonfire Tile, Level 4 Encounter, Level 4 Encounter, The Four Kings (Mega Boss)'; publisher's production update: 'over five game sessions'.

### CH-19-038 · §19.10
- Type: correction
- Before: "2. Level 3 encounter
3. Level 3 encounter: Outskirts of Blighttown
4. *Level 4 encounter*
5. Ornstein & Smough (main-boss)"
- After: "2. Level 3 encounter
3. Level 3 encounter
4. *Level 4 encounter*: Outskirts of Blighttown
5. Ornstein & Smough (main-boss)"
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Outskirts of Blighttown is the Level 4 encounter of the section, not a Level 3 encounter.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E39 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official Gaping Dragon booklet, Into the Depths: 'Section 4 Warriors' Revenge: Bonfire Tile, Level 3 Encounter, Level 3 Encounter, Outskirts of Blighttown Level 4 Encounter, Ornstein & Smough (Main Boss)'.

### CH-19-039 · §19.10
- Type: completion
- Before: "This campaign requires the core set, the Darkroot expansion, and the Gaping Dragon expansion. The party may add the Explorers and Characters expansions."
- After: "This campaign requires the core set, the Darkroot expansion, and the Gaping Dragon expansion. The party may add the Explorers and Characters expansions.

The party may play this campaign without the Darkroot expansion. The Dancer of the Boreal Valley then takes the place of Artorias in Section Three, and the encounter decks of Levels 1 to 3 are built only from the *encounter cards* of the core set."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] The official campaign gives this substitution, which opens the campaign to a party without Darkroot.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E40 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official Gaping Dragon booklet: 'If you would like to play this campaign but do not have access to the Darkroot expansion, you can leave the Darkroot encounter cards out of the encounter deck and substitute the Dancer for Artorias in the Crypt of the Fallen.'

### CH-19-040 · §19.12
- Type: completion
- Before: "Every *boss* encounter of this campaign, including the *mini-boss* encounter, is fought on a *mega-boss game board*. After Section One is completed, the party plays Section Two A, Section Two B, and Section Two C in the order it chooses. The players win the campaign when all three are completed."
- After: "Every *boss* encounter of this campaign, including the *mini-boss* encounter, is fought on a *mega-boss game board*. The Asylum Demon is fought on the mega-boss side of the Asylum Demon expansion's game board (see §17.3). When Section One is completed, every character gains 16 *souls* in addition to the boss reward (see §7.6). The party then plays Section Two A, Section Two B, and Section Two C in the order it chooses, and sets up each of them when the section before it is completed (see §18.1, Section Setup). The players win the campaign when all three are completed.

The party may replace the mega-boss of any of these three sections with another mega-boss it owns, and may add further sections of the same form. Each such section uses the game board and the Level 4 encounter cards of its own mega-boss."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Restores the campaign's own soul grant, names the board for the mini-boss, and says when each of the three free-order sections is set up.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E41 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official Manus booklet, Big Game Hunting: 'When the party successfully defeats Section 1, each character gains 16 souls in addition to the souls they would gain normally'; 'each boss fight takes place on a mega boss tile, even the mini boss fight'; 'Players are also welcome to play Big Game Hunting with additional mega bosses or with alternative mega bosses'.

### CH-19-041 · §19.1
- Type: correction
- Before: "### Section Four: Bathed in Flame"
- After: "### Section Four: Royal Wood"
- Rationale: Every other section heading is the section's printed name; 'Bathed in Flame' is the title of the scenario, not of the section.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E42 (`06_final_rulings.md`); DS-BDK-Rules-web.txt 500-530: scenario 'Bathed in Flame', 'Section 4 Royal Wood'.

### CH-19-042 · §19.9
- Type: clarification
- Before: "every character gains the campaign soul reward for its level and two further *souls* (see §18.5)."
- After: "every character gains the soul reward for its level and two further *souls* (see §7.6; §18.5)."
- Rationale: Campaign play has no reward table of its own, so 'the campaign soul reward' points at nothing.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E43 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §18.5 (standard rewards apply); official Undead Colosseum grants a raised flat reward because cleared encounters are never fought again (DS-EC-Rules-Web.txt 615-628).

### CH-19-043 · §19.8
- Type: clarification
- Before: "When the party opens the chest, a *mimic* encounter begins as if an ambush card showing Mimic had been revealed (see §13.1)."
- After: "Each time the party opens the chest, a *mimic* encounter begins as if an ambush card showing Mimic had been revealed (see §13.1). A failed mimic encounter returns the mimic to the chest (see §13.4)."
- Rationale: With no ambush card to serve as the record, the text must say that the chest is a mimic on every attempt.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E44 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §13.4; Explorers booklet: 'Do not place an Ambush card beside this tile; the chest is automatically a mimic. The party proceeds to Section 3 when the mimic is defeated.'

### CH-19-044 · §19 introduction
- Type: completion
- Before: "Twelve roads lead out from the first bonfire. One climbs through the Undead Burg to Anor Londo, another descends into the Abyss, and a third never leaves the arena. This chapter sets out all twelve campaigns, section by section."
- After: "Sixteen roads lead out from the first bonfire. One climbs through the Undead Burg to Anor Londo, another descends into the Abyss, and a third never leaves the arena. This chapter sets out all 16 campaigns, section by section."
- Rationale: The chapter now holds 16 campaigns.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E46 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As campaign-E45.

### CH-19-045 · §19.12 (insert §19.13 to §19.16 after it)
- Type: completion
- Before: "3. Manus, Father of the Abyss (mega-boss)"
- After: "3. Manus, Father of the Abyss (mega-boss)

## 19.13 Go Beyond Death

This campaign requires the core set, the Explorers expansion, the Iron Keep expansion, and the Old Iron King expansion. The party may add the Characters expansion.

The Level 1, Level 2, and Level 3 encounter decks are built from the *encounter cards* of the core set, the Explorers expansion, and the Iron Keep expansion together (see §6.5, Expansion Encounters). Both *Level 4 encounters* are fought on the Old Iron King expansion's *mega-boss game board* (see §18.1).

### Section One: Tower of Flame

1. *Bonfire* tile
2. Level 1 *encounter*
3. Level 1 encounter
4. Level 1 encounter
5. Level 2 encounter
6. Old Dragonslayer (*mini-boss*)

### Section Two: Threshold Bridge

1. Bonfire tile
2. Level 1 encounter
3. Level 2 encounter
4. Level 2 encounter
5. Level 3 encounter
6. Smelter Demon (*main-boss*) […]"
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Adds the four published campaigns the book lacked (items campaign-041 to campaign-044), restated in this book's terms.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E48 (`06_final_rulings.md`); work/text/DS-OIK-Rules-web.txt (Go Beyond Death), DS-TLG-Rules.txt (Footsteps of Giants), DS-VotBV-Rules-web.txt (Treasures of the Ice Cave), DS-GD-Rules-web.txt (Demons & Dragons), campaign pages.

### CH-19-046 · §19.16
- Type: correction
- Before: "The defeat of the Stray Demon does not complete Section Five."
- After: "The encounters of Section Five are fought in the order listed: the Stray Demon is fought when the first Level 4 encounter is cleared, and the second Level 4 encounter is set up when the Stray Demon is defeated. The defeat of the Stray Demon does not complete Section Five."
- Rationale: §18.1 sets a mega-boss up after the section's last Level 4 encounter; this section's listed order (and its booklet) puts the Stray Demon between the two.
- Source: final rulings, VB-011 (`06_final_rulings.md`); independent verification, work/phase3/verify_b.md

### CH-19-047 · §19.12
- Type: correction
- Before: "The party may replace the mega-boss of any of these three sections with another mega-boss it owns, and may add further sections of the same form."
- After: "The party may replace the mega-boss of any of these three sections with another mega-boss it owns, and may add further sections of the same form; the players then win the campaign when every such section is completed."
- Rationale: 'The players win the campaign when all three are completed' does not hold once further sections are added.
- Source: final rulings, VB-012 (`06_final_rulings.md`); independent verification, work/phase3/verify_b.md

### CH-19-048 · §19.15
- Type: ruling
- Before: "Both invaders are part of that encounter every time the characters enter it, until both have been killed during one visit. If either invader vanishes, or the encounter is *failed*, both return the next time the characters enter it."
- After: "Both invaders are part of that encounter every time the characters enter it, until both have been killed during one visit. If either invader vanishes, or the encounter is *failed*, both return the next time the characters enter it. An encounter that is *cleared* before both of its invaders have been killed is fought again when the characters next enter it, the Level 4 encounter of Section Three included, and it is not skipped."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Without this the campaign could dead-end: the invaders vanish at a character's death, the survivors clear the encounter, and a cleared Level 4 encounter is otherwise never fought again.
- Source: final rulings, VB-010 (`06_final_rulings.md`); independent verification, work/phase3/verify_b.md (judgement call decided by the lead editor)

## Chapter 15 — Boss Encounters

### CH-15-001 · §15 Boss Encounters
- Type: style
- Before: "Boss encounters are the heart of the character's adventure and the bosses are the targets of their bloodlust. Bosses are single models that are not only large in stature, but formidable opponents with increased health, attack power, and special abilities."
- After: "Boss encounters are the heart of the adventure. A boss is a single model of great health and power, with a behaviour deck in place of fixed behaviours. There are three types: mini-boss, main-boss, and mega-boss."
- Rationale: The chapter intro is trimmed to the approved 36-word draft; "bloodlust", "can expect to die often", and the strategy sentence are removed.
- Source: F-027, C-021, C-121, C-114, F-125

### CH-15-002 · §15 Boss Encounters
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Boss encounters follow many of the same rules as other enemies as described previously, but there are key differences outlined as follows."
- After: "Bosses follow the enemy rules of §8 and §9 except as stated in this chapter and §16."
- Rationale: The prose cross-reference becomes a numbered one and the relation to the enemy rules is stated as a rule after the intro.
- Source: F-027, F-075, C-084

### CH-15-003 · §15 Boss Encounters
- Type: cut
- Before: "The core game does not come with mega-bosses as they are optional objectives which players can attempt after defeating a main-boss should they want a more epic final battle."
- After: deleted
- Rationale: The sentence describes the product line; the mega-boss option is a rule of §6.5 and §6.7, which §15.2 cites.
- Source: F-027, F-050, C-021

### CH-15-004 · §15.1 The Fog Gate
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Once a character has reached a boss encounter they must pass through the fog gate."
- After: "The characters enter a boss encounter only by passing through the fog gate, and the party passes through it together. The boss encounter begins when the characters pass through (see §15.2)."
- Rationale: The rule names the component (a token on a doorway), cites the setup steps that place it, and states what passing through does and when the party may do so.
- Source: C-058, C-062, A-089, C-120

### CH-15-005 · §15.1 The Fog Gate
- Type: correction
- Before: "The first time a character passes through the fog gate the fog gate tile should be removed from the boss-tile entryway and placed on the bonfire tile to serve as a shortcut."
- After: "The first time the characters pass through the fog gate of a boss, the fog gate token is moved from the doorway to the *bonfire* tile."
- Rationale: "should" made a mandatory step optional, and the fog gate is a token, not a tile. The chapter adds that the token stays on the bonfire tile until the boss is defeated and is then moved by the next setup.
- Source: C-019, F-089, C-058, C-062

### CH-15-006 · §15.1 The Fog Gate
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If the characters fail their first attempt at a boss encounter, they may use the fog gate to travel directly to the boss encounter from the bonfire tile to try again without having to fight any encounters."
- After: "The encounters on the route are then neither fought nor skipped: no stamina cube is placed for them (see §7.5, Encounter Start). An encounter on the route that is not marked *cleared* does not bar the shortcut."
- Rationale: States when the shortcut may be used (whenever the token is on the bonfire tile and the characters set out), and that bypassed encounters are not skipped encounters, so the skip cost does not apply.
- Source: A-054; Gate 1 — OQ-10 (skip cost, not charged for the shortcut)

### CH-15-007 · §15.1 The Fog Gate
- Type: style
- Before: "If the characters wish to forgo the fog gate to gain more souls and treasure they may proceed back to the boss encounter through their previous route encounter tiles."
- After: "The party may instead leave the shortcut unused and travel the route from the first encounter tile."
- Rationale: The motive is removed and the option stated as a rule. The campaign exception is cited to §18.2, and a worked example of the shortcut is added.
- Source: C-121, C-103, F-083

### CH-15-008 · §15.2 Overview
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Place the boss model on the boss *spawn node*, with the centre of its front arc facing the *adjacent node* closest to the entry nodes (see §15.6)."
- Rationale: The manuscript never stated how a boss encounter is set up. The chapter gives a nine-step procedure: fog gate token, entry nodes, stamina owed for skipped encounters, summon, health dial, boss placement and facing, special rules, aggro token, first boss turn.
- Source: A-122, C-120, C-062; Gate 1 — OQ-32 (summon cited to §12.1)

### CH-15-009 · §15.2 Overview
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "The boss takes the first turn of the encounter. The boss and the characters then alternate turns: one character takes a turn after each boss turn (see §16.1)."
- Rationale: The overview states in one place what a boss encounter is, that bosses are not scaled, that no invader joins, that the boss heats up, and how the encounter ends, each with a citation to its owning section.
- Source: A-122, C-041, C-097, preserve list (boss and character alternation)

### CH-15-010 · §15.2 Overview
- Type: correction
- Before: new text (the manuscript's mega-boss sudden-death clause stood in the setup chapter)
- After: "This rule applies to every boss, a mega-boss included: a character who dies in a mega-boss encounter is revived as after any other encounter (see §7.2)."
- Rationale: A failed boss encounter is resolved as any failed encounter, and mega-boss permadeath is not kept.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-14

### CH-15-011 · §15.2 Overview
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The boss model is removed from the boss tile and kept with its data card, and its behaviour deck is built again (see §15.5, Deck Reset)."
- Rationale: §11.5 delegates the reset of a failed boss encounter to this section; the manuscript was silent on the boss model, health dial, behaviour deck, and dropped souls between attempts.
- Source: A-043 (consequence of building the deck at setup); cross-chapter dependency from §11.5

### CH-15-012 · §15.3 Boss Tiles
- Type: clarification
- Before: "main-boss encounters require two boss encounter tiles to be placed adjacent to one another and are treated as one large tile for all purposes."
- After: "The joined tiles are one tile for every rule. No wall runs along the join, and each *node* beside the join is adjacent to the nodes that face it across the join (see §5.1)."
- Rationale: "For all purposes" is spelled out for adjacency, movement, pushes, and range, as §5.1 requires; "typically" and "special encounter tile rules" are removed.
- Source: F-090, C-041, C-114; cross-chapter dependency from §5.1

### CH-15-013 · §15.3 Boss Tiles
- Type: correction
- Before: "Mega-boss encounters usually have a unique mega-boss encounter tile included with the mega-boss expansion."
- After: "A *mega-boss* encounter is fought on the mega-boss side of the *mega-boss game board* supplied with that mega-boss (see §6.7)."
- Rationale: The hedge is removed and the component is named as §2.9 and §6.7 name it.
- Source: F-090, A-095

### CH-15-014 · §15.3 Boss Tiles
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The *entry nodes* of a boss encounter are the basic nodes adjacent to the *doorway* on which the *fog gate* token is placed during setup (see §5.1)."
- Rationale: Defines the boss spawn node and the entry nodes of a boss encounter (which do not change when the token moves to the bonfire tile) and states that a boss tile receives no encounter card, traps, or terrain.
- Source: A-122, C-062

### CH-15-015 · §15.4 Boss Data Cards
- Type: style
- Before: "Boss data cards include similar information to enemy data cards but are noticeably lacking behaviours to dictate how they act on their turns. Instead, bosses have variable behaviours determined by a special deck of behaviour cards."
- After: "A *boss*'s *data card* lists no behaviours. A boss acts according to its *behaviour deck* (see §15.5)."
- Rationale: Editorial adverb and the signposting sentence ("will follow") are removed.
- Source: F-028, C-084

### CH-15-016 · §15.4 Boss Data Cards
- Type: restructure
- Before: "1. Name 2. Threat level 3. Behaviour deck size 4. Heat-up point 5. Block and resist values 6. Special ability 7. Encounter levels 8. Starting health 9. Boss type icon (mini, main, or mega) 10. Set symbol"
- After: "**Heat-up point.** The value beside the heat-up point icon {{icon:ast-0030-heat-up-point}} is the health at or below which the boss heats up (see §15.5)."
- Rationale: The bare callout list becomes an anatomy list in the form used in §4.1, each part with its meaning, its icon, and its owning section; the three explanatory paragraphs that followed are folded into it.
- Source: C-057, F-126, F-076, F-077, C-027

### CH-15-017 · §15.4 Boss Data Cards
- Type: style
- Before: "Boss starting health is treated the same as well but instead of using dozens of wound tokens, players track the boss's remaining health on a health tracker dial specific to each boss."
- After: "Boss health is tracked on that boss's *health dial*, not with *wound tokens* (see §8.2)."
- Rationale: One component name ("health dial"); the aside is removed. The chapter adds when the dial is set, how damage lowers it, and that zero defeats the boss, and one worked example.
- Source: F-029, C-037, C-036, F-076

### CH-15-018 · §15.4 Boss Data Cards
- Type: style
- Before: "Boss special ability provides an overarching theme to the boss which often gives the boss unique powers."
- After: "**Special ability.** A rule unique to that boss. It applies for the whole encounter (see §17)."
- Rationale: "often" is a hedge and "theme" is not a rules concept.
- Source: F-030, F-090

### CH-15-019 · §15.5 Boss Behaviour Cards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Each behaviour card will dictate the boss's attack range and dodge difficulty until the next boss behaviour card is drawn."
- After: "The card a boss drew most recently is its current behaviour card until the next card is drawn: the card being resolved during the boss's turn, and at every other time the card it resolved most recently."
- Rationale: Names the card whose range, dodge difficulty, and weak arcs apply between draws, so that an out-of-turn riposte has a defined card. The anatomy list gains icons; "generally more powerful" is cut.
- Source: F-088, F-090, C-047, C-057

### CH-15-020 · §15.5 Boss Behaviour Cards
- Type: correction
- Before: "To construct the initial boss behaviour deck, first set aside all of the Heat Up behaviours. Then, shuffle the normal boss behaviour cards and deal a number equal to the boss's behaviour deck size face-down into a pile"
- After: "The *behaviour deck* of each boss is built during setup, at the step that places the boss's components on its tile (see §6.5, §6.6, §6.7). It is not built at the start of the boss encounter."
- Rationale: The gravestone scry of the next boss's top card needs a deck that exists before the boss encounter. The prose paragraph becomes a five-step procedure.
- Source: A-043, C-120, C-057, F-127

### CH-15-021 · §15.5 Boss Behaviour Cards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Return any unused normal boss behaviour cards to the box, face-down."
- After: "Set aside the standard behaviour cards that were not dealt, face down, without looking at them. They take no part in the encounter."
- Rationale: The undealt cards are needed again when the deck is rebuilt after a failed attempt and when §17.1 builds a new deck for the second Gargoyle, so they are set aside and not boxed.
- Source: A-043; cross-chapter dependency from §17.1

### CH-15-022 · §15.5 Boss Behaviour Cards
- Type: restructure
- Before: "If there are no cards remaining in the behaviour deck at the start of a boss's turn, flip the discard pile face-down without shuffling the cards to renew the boss behaviour deck."
- After: "When the behaviour deck is empty at the start of a boss turn, the discard pile is turned face down, without shuffling, and becomes the behaviour deck."
- Rationale: The renewal rule moves here from the boss turn overview so that the deck is owned by one section; the mechanic is unchanged. The advice about memorising the order is cut.
- Source: A-041, F-132, F-033, C-121; preserve list (deck renewed unshuffled)

### CH-15-023 · §15.5 Boss Behaviour Cards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "When the boss's health is reduced to (or below) its Heat Up point, take a random Heat Up behaviour card and shuffle it into the boss behaviour deck."
- After: "Shuffle that card, face down, into the cards that remain in the behaviour deck. The discard pile is not shuffled and its order is not changed."
- Rationale: States which cards are shuffled, what happens when the deck is empty at that moment, and that a boss heats up once per attempt; the heat-up rule is stated once, in a numbered procedure.
- Source: A-042, C-097, C-057, C-120

### CH-15-024 · §15.5 Boss Behaviour Cards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Some bosses have special Heat Up rules which may allow them to Heat Up multiple times or otherwise override these rules."
- After: "If a boss heats up during its own turn, as a *riposte* may cause, the steps are carried out after the current behaviour card has been resolved and discarded."
- Rationale: Fixes the timing of a heat-up caused during a character's turn and during the boss's own turn. Special heat-up rules are delegated to §17. A worked heat-up example is added.
- Source: A-042, F-090

### CH-15-025 · §15.5 Boss Behaviour Cards
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Each attempt at a boss is fought against a newly built behaviour deck."
- Rationale: With the deck built at setup, the rules needed a statement of what happens to the deck, the discard pile, and a drawn heat-up card when a failed boss encounter is reset.
- Source: A-043; cross-chapter dependency from §11.5 and §17.1

### CH-15-026 · §15.6 Arcs and Defence
- Type: cut
- Before: "These arcs are a key part of what makes boss encounters unique, and they add an extra layer of tactics to fighting against bosses."
- After: deleted
- Rationale: Commentary; no rule.
- Source: F-031, F-128

### CH-15-027 · §15.6 Arcs and Defence
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Bosses always directly face an adjacent node. For a model to directly face a node, the centre of the model's front arc must line up with the centre of that node."
- After: "**Facing.** The direction in which the centre of a boss's front arc points. A boss faces directly towards an *adjacent node*, so that the centre of its front arc lines up with the centre of that node."
- Rationale: Facing becomes a defined term. The chapter adds where the lines between arcs run for an orthogonal and for a diagonal facing, so that players can tell which nodes lie in two arcs.
- Source: F-129, A-122

### CH-15-028 · §15.6 Arcs and Defence
- Type: restructure
- Before: "The *turn* symbol indicates the boss turns 90 degrees to the left or right as indicated by the direction of the quarter-turn arrow. The *rotate* symbol indicates the boss turns 180 degrees to face their rear."
- After: "The turn around icon {{icon:ast-0036-turn-around}} turns the boss 180°, so that its front arc points where its back arc pointed."
- Rationale: The turn icons move here from the boss movement section and take the names of the icon set. The chapter states that a boss turns only by movement or a turn icon, never when it attacks, and that no other model moves when it turns.
- Source: F-133, C-116, C-019, F-089

### CH-15-029 · §15.6 Arcs and Defence
- Type: completion
- Before: "When resolving a boss behaviour attack note the four boss arcs coded as either normal (black), attack (green), or weak (red)."
- After: "If that card bears more than one arc diagram, the diagram beneath its last attack icon shows the boss's weak arcs."
- Rationale: The three arc states are defined with their icons, and the card whose diagram decides the weak arcs is fixed, including for a card with several attack icons.
- Source: F-131, C-030

### CH-15-030 · §15.6 Arcs and Defence
- Type: style
- Before: "Remember that a character can be in more than one arc if it is on a node along the arc line. This makes it easier to gain the weak arc bonus, but it also means the character will be targeted by attacks against either arc."
- After: "A character on a node that lies in two arcs attacks from both arcs, and the attack gains the die if either arc is weak. The same character is targeted by an area attack made into either arc (see §16.3, Area Attacks)."
- Rationale: Reader address ("Remember that", "Note that") and tactical commentary are rewritten as rules. The weak-arc die itself is unchanged.
- Source: F-032, F-131; preserve list (weak-arc black die)

### CH-15-031 · §15.6 Arcs and Defence
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If the character is attacking a weak arc, the attack gains an additional black die."
- After: "The die is added to every attack roll made from a weak arc. This includes each roll of an *attack option* that bears the *repeat* icon (see §10.3, Repeat) and the roll of a *riposte* (see §9.6)."
- Rationale: States from which arc a character attacks, that the die applies at any range, and that it applies to each roll of a repeated attack and to a riposte. A worked arc example is added.
- Source: A-037, A-119

### CH-15-032 · §15.6 Arcs and Defence
- Type: clarification
- Before: "This may be repeated if the character can move multiple nodes (i.e.: *Run*) which can allow a character initially on the front-arc of the base (for example) to move to a side-arc of the base and then to the rear-arc of the base."
- After: "Each node of movement granted by the *Walk* or *Run* *minor action* (see §10.2) or by a *shift* icon (see §10.3, Shift) may be used in this way."
- Rationale: The misused "i.e." and the embedded example are replaced by the rule (which arcs are adjacent, which movement may be spent) and a separate worked example. The same-arc rule for moving onto and off the boss's node is kept.
- Source: C-096, F-083, F-130, F-091; preserve list (same-arc movement)

### CH-15-033 · §15.6 Arcs and Defence
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If the character is pushed off of the boss's node, they must move to the centre-most node in the boss's arc they originate from, if possible. Otherwise, the character is pushed to any valid node in that arc."
- After: "A character who is *pushed* off a boss's node must be pushed to a valid adjacent node that lies in the arc in which the character is placed."
- Rationale: "Centre-most node" is undefined; the players choose among the valid adjacent nodes of the arc. The wall fallback is kept, and the rule is stated here because §8.4 cites this section for it.
- Source: A-036; preserve list (same-arc pushes)

### CH-15-034 · §15.6 Arcs and Defence
- Type: clarification
- Before: "However, they are not required to remain in the boss's arc when moving due to dodge. A character may move to any adjacent node or to any arc on the base of the boss's model."
- After: "A character who dodges while on a boss's node must move either to any valid adjacent node, whatever arc it lies in, or to any other arc of the boss's base."
- Rationale: The dodge movement stays mandatory and free of arcs; the chapter also places a character who dodges onto the boss's node.
- Source: C-028, C-047; preserve list (dodge ignores arcs)

### CH-15-035 · §15.6 Arcs and Defence
- Type: clarification
- Before: "*Push, area-of-effect,* and *boss-arc* attacks cannot be *parried*." (Enemy Turn chapter)
- After: "A character may parry a boss's attack only when the attack targets one character and the conditions for a parry are met (see §9.6). An area attack, a *node attack*, and a push-attack cannot be parried."
- Rationale: §9.6 forbids the parry of a boss's attack "except where §15.6 allows"; this section states the allowance in the manuscript's terms. The rule that characters cannot push a boss is restated in one sentence with its citation.
- Source: C-027; cross-chapter dependency from §9.6 and §8.4

### CH-15-036 · §15.6 Arcs and Defence
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "*Mimics*, *invaders*, and *summons* have no arcs. The rules of this section for arcs do not apply to attacks against them or to movement on their nodes (see §12; §13)."
- Rationale: The mimic and phantom chapters defer to the boss rules; their models have no arc bases.
- Source: A-044

### CH-15-037 · §15 Boss Encounters (style sweep)
- Type: style
- Before: "Heat Up", "Behaviour Deck", "fog-gate", "health tracker dial", "dodge*s*", "characters can expect", "should"
- After: "heat-up", "behaviour deck", "fog gate", "health dial", "*dodge*", "Characters", "must"
- Rationale: Terminology, italics, capitalisation, numerals, tense, headings (numbered H2s from the section map, "and" for "&"), and prose cross-references converted to "(see §N.M)".
- Source: C-019, C-021, C-027, C-028, C-030, C-036, C-037, C-041, C-047, C-057, C-058, C-062, C-084, C-103, C-104, C-114, F-088, F-089, F-090, F-091, F-125, F-126, F-127, F-128, F-129, F-130

### CH-15-038 · §15 Boss Encounters (OPTIONAL findings skipped)
- Type: clarification
- Before: n/a
- After: n/a
- Rationale: D-004 (attack pushes off a boss's node free of arcs): alters the preserved same-arc push mechanic; A-036's wording is applied in its place.
- Source: D-004

### CH-15-039 · §15 Boss Encounters (findings owned by other chapters)
- Type: clarification
- Before: n/a
- After: n/a
- Rationale: Not restated here because another section owns the rule and this chapter cites it: A-089 (fog gate placement, §6.5), A-095 (fog gate before the mega-boss, §6.7), A-087 (dodge with no valid node, §5.2 and §9.5), A-119 (riposte as an attack, §9.6; its weak-arc consequence is applied in CH-15-031).
- Source: A-089, A-095, A-087, A-119

### CH-15-040 · §15.5, Heat-Up
- Type: ruling
- Before: "A boss heats up when its health is reduced to or below its *heat-up point* {{icon:ast-0030-heat-up-point}}. A boss heats up once in each attempt at its encounter. When a boss heats up, a heat-up card is added to its behaviour deck as follows.

1. Take one card at random from the boss's heat-up cards, without looking at it.
2. Shuffle that card, face down, into the cards that remain in the behaviour deck. The discard pile is not shuffled and its order is not changed.
3. If no cards remain in the behaviour deck, place the heat-up card face down as the behaviour deck. The discard pile is not renewed until that card has been drawn.

If a boss heats up during a character's turn, these steps are carried out as soon as the attack that caused the heat-up has been resolved. If a boss heats up during its own turn, as a *riposte* may cause, the steps are carried out after the cu […]"
- After: "A boss heats up when its health is reduced to or below its *heat-up point* {{icon:ast-0030-heat-up-point}}. A boss heats up once in each attempt at its encounter. The heat-up is carried out at the start of the boss's next turn, before a card is drawn (see §16.1), as follows.

1. Gather the behaviour deck and the discard pile.
2. Take one card at random from the boss's heat-up cards, without looking at it, and add it to the gathered cards.
3. Shuffle the gathered cards face down to form the new behaviour deck.

The boss then draws the top card of the new deck. Until that card is drawn, the boss's current behaviour card and its weak arcs do not change (see §15.5, Card Anatomy). The boss's other heat-up cards stay face down beside the boss tile and take no part in the encounter.

Some bosses heat up in other ways. The special rules of such a boss replace the steps of thi […]"
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] The whole deck is reshuffled with the heat-up card, so the learned order is lost as both official rulebooks intend, and one timing (the start of the boss's next turn) replaces two.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E01 (`06_final_rulings.md`); 2017 core rules lines 1392-1396 and 2022 rules lines 2146-2153 ('players will now need to relearn the boss's attack pattern'); community consensus (BoardGameGeek thread 1819893 summary; DSBG-Shuffle heat-up code); Vordt booklet lines 475-490 times its heat-up at the start of the next activation.

### CH-15-041 · §15.5, Heat-Up
- Type: ruling
- Before: "*Example.* A *mini-boss* has a heat-up point of 12, and its *health dial* shows 14. Two cards remain in its behaviour deck and three lie in its discard pile. The Warrior's attack inflicts three damage, and the dial is turned to 11. The boss heats up: the players take one of its heat-up cards at random and shuffle it, unseen, into the two cards of the behaviour deck. The three cards of the discard pile are left as they lie. The boss draws from a deck of three cards on its next turn."
- After: "*Example.* A *mini-boss* has a heat-up point of 12, and its *health dial* shows 14. Two cards remain in its behaviour deck and three lie in its discard pile. The Warrior's attack inflicts three damage, and the dial is turned to 11. At the start of the boss's next turn the players gather the two cards of the deck and the three cards of the discard pile, add one of the boss's heat-up cards, taken at random and unseen, and shuffle the six cards to form the new behaviour deck. The boss then draws the top card."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] The example must compute from the heat-up rule as changed by boss-E01.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E02 (`06_final_rulings.md`); follows boss-E01

### CH-15-042 · §15.3
- Type: completion
- Before: "A mini-boss encounter is fought on the mini-boss tile alone."
- After: "A mini-boss encounter is fought on the mini-boss tile alone, unless the mini-boss's special rules name another tile (see §17.3)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] The Asylum Demon mini-boss is fought on its expansion's mega-boss game board; §15.3 must allow the exception that §17.3 now states.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E03 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Asylum Demon booklet, DS-AD-Rules-web-EN.txt lines 183-190 ('the boss encounter takes place on the Mega Boss game board instead of the Mini Boss tile').

### CH-15-043 · §15 (introduction)
- Type: correction
- Before: "A boss is a single model of great health and power,"
- After: "A boss is a foe of great health and power,"
- Rationale: Two bosses are fought with more than one model.
- Source: final rulings, lead-E19 (`06_final_rulings.md`); boss memo, cross-cluster effects

### CH-15-044 · §15.2
- Type: correction
- Before: "If a boss's special rules state where the boss is placed or how it faces, those rules replace step 6 (see §17)."
- After: "If a boss's special rules state where the boss is placed or how it faces, those rules replace that part of step 6, and the rest of the step applies (see §17)."
- Rationale: Entries that name only a node (Four Kings, Old Iron King, Asylum Demon, Executioner's Chariot) otherwise lose the starting facing, which the Old Iron King's booklet and back-arc rule require.
- Source: final rulings, VB-003 (`06_final_rulings.md`); independent verification, work/phase3/verify_b.md

### CH-15-045 · §15.3
- Type: correction
- Before: "The boss model is placed on it at the start of every boss encounter (see §15.2)."
- After: "The boss model is placed on it at the start of every boss encounter, unless the boss's special rules name another node (see §15.2; §17)."
- Rationale: Smough, the Asylum Demon and the Old Iron King are placed on other nodes (§17.2, §17.3, §17.10).
- Source: final rulings, VB-004 (`06_final_rulings.md`); independent verification, work/phase3/verify_b.md

### CH-15-046 · §15.6
- Type: correction
- Before: "A boss model is pushed only when another boss model moves onto its node (see §17.1)."
- After: "A boss model is pushed only when another boss model moves onto its node or is placed on it (see §17.1; §17.10, The Four Kings)."
- Rationale: The royal summons places a king on the spawn node and pushes every other model there, a king included (§17.10, step 2).
- Source: final rulings, VB-005 (`06_final_rulings.md`); independent verification, work/phase3/verify_b.md

## Chapter 16 — Boss Turns

### CH-16-001 · §16 Boss Turns
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A boss does not take its place in the line of lesser foes. It answers every blow, and each card it turns shows a new way to die."
- Rationale: The chapter had no intro; a 50-word intro in voice is added, with no rule inside it. The H2 titles follow the section map ("Overview", "Boss Movement and Symbols").
- Source: C-104, C-112

### CH-16-002 · §16.1 Overview
- Type: style
- Before: "During a boss encounter, the boss always takes their turn first. … After each boss turn, one character may take their turn, then the boss takes another turn, a second character, and so-on."
- After: "The *boss* takes the first *turn* of a boss *encounter* (see §15.2). After each boss turn one character takes a turn, and the boss then takes its next turn."
- Rationale: Filler ("and so-on") and "always" are removed; the alternation of boss and character turns is unchanged.
- Source: C-123; preserve list (bosses alternate boss and character)

### CH-16-003 · §16.1 Overview
- Type: correction
- Before: "characters must take an equal number of turns and cannot take a turn until every other character has taken an equal number of turns."
- After: "No living character takes a second turn in a round until every other living character has taken one. A dead character takes no turn and is not counted."
- Rationale: A dead character can take no turns, so the literal equal-turns rule blocked every living character after a death. The chapter also states that the players choose which living character takes each turn, which "one character may take their turn" implied.
- Source: A-019, A-022, C-114

### CH-16-004 · §16.1 Overview
- Type: correction
- Before: "Phantoms take their turn after all character turns are resolved and before the first enemy turn begins." (Summons chapter)
- After: "If a *summon* is in play, it takes a turn after each character's turn and before the boss's next turn (see §12.1)."
- Rationale: The summon's cadence is fitted to the alternating turns of a boss encounter; §12.1 owns the rule and this section cites it.
- Source: A-020, D-002; Gate 1 — OQ-32

### CH-16-005 · §16.1 Overview
- Type: restructure
- Before: "Draw the top card from the boss behaviour deck at the beginning of every boss turn. The boss's actions are then determined by resolving each symbol on the behaviour card from left to right. When the right-most symbol is resolved, the boss's turn ends."
- After: "Draw the top card of the behaviour deck and place it face up beside the deck. This card is the boss's current *behaviour card* (see §15.5, Card Anatomy)."
- Rationale: The boss turn becomes a five-step procedure: renew the deck if empty, draw, resolve left to right, repeat, discard. The renewal rule itself is owned by §15.5 and cited.
- Source: C-120, F-132, A-041

### CH-16-006 · §16.1 Overview
- Type: clarification
- Before: "The *repeat* symbol is similar to the character's equipment symbol and allows a boss to repeat its entire behaviour card multiple times before ending its turn."
- After: "If the card bears the *repeat* icon {{icon:ast-0061-repeat-behaviour-or-attack}}, resolve the whole row of icons again from left to right, until the row has been resolved the number of times the icon shows."
- Rationale: The repeat rule moves from Boss Attacks to the turn sequence, states the count, and is distinguished from an enemy's repeat icon, which repeats one attack (§9.3). Targets are determined again at each resolution.
- Source: A-024, C-027

### CH-16-007 · §16.1 Overview
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "If the boss is defeated during its own turn, as a *riposte* may cause, the turn ends at once and no further icon is resolved (see §16.5)."
- Rationale: States what happens to an icon that cannot be resolved (cited to §9.1), that conditions affect a boss, and the timing of a heat-up or a defeat that falls within the boss's own turn. A worked example of the turn order and of deck renewal is added.
- Source: A-042, A-018

### CH-16-008 · §16.1 Overview
- Type: cut
- Before: "Players can gain an advantage over a boss by memorising the order in which the boss behaviour cards will appear each time the deck is renewed."
- After: deleted
- Rationale: Tactical advice is not a rule; the unshuffled renewal it relies on is stated in §15.5.
- Source: F-033, C-121

### CH-16-009 · §16.2 Boss Movement and Symbols
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Bosses abide by the same enemy movement and attack rules previously described with the following exceptions."
- After: "A *boss* *moves* as an enemy moves (see §9.2), with the differences stated in this section."
- Rationale: The prose cross-reference becomes a numbered one. The section adds that boss movement is not scaled, that every node entered must be a valid node, and how a movement icon names its target.
- Source: F-078, C-084, A-082

### CH-16-010 · §16.2 Boss Movement and Symbols
- Type: restructure
- Before: "When a boss moves towards a target, it first rotates to align the centre of its front arc with the adjacent node closest to the target character. Once facing is resolved, the boss moves forward into the adjacent node it has just centred in its front arc."
- After: "1. Turn the boss so that the centre of its front arc faces the next node of its path. 2. Move the boss forwards onto that node."
- Rationale: Movement towards and away from a target become two-step procedures tied to the path rules of §9.2, so that a boss goes round blocking terrain and stops when no node is open to it, as §5.5 requires this section to state.
- Source: C-120, A-082; cross-chapter dependency from §5.5

### CH-16-011 · §16.2 Boss Movement and Symbols
- Type: correction
- Before: "the boss remains in the same node but rotates to bring the target model into the front-arc of the boss's base. The target character model should remain static while the boss model rotates underneath to change facing towards the target."
- After: "The boss turns in place until the arc in which the target is placed is its front arc: 90° towards a target in a side arc, or 180° for a target in the back arc."
- Rationale: "should" is replaced by a rule, and the turn is stated in the quarter turns that keep the boss facing an adjacent node.
- Source: C-019, F-089

### CH-16-012 · §16.2 Boss Movement and Symbols
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If a boss move behaviour does not indicate a target it moves the number of nodes indicated in the direction shown without rotating to change facing."
- After: "Each node it enters is the *adjacent node* at the centre of its front arc, its back arc, its left arc, or its right arc. If that node is not a valid node, the boss stops, and the rest of the movement is lost."
- Rationale: Names the node a boss enters when it moves in a direction and what happens when that node is blocked.
- Source: A-082

### CH-16-013 · §16.2 Boss Movement and Symbols
- Type: clarification
- Before: "the character is placed on the boss's base in the arc the character was in at the beginning of the boss's movement. If the character was on a node split between two arcs the player may choose either."
- After: "Each character is placed against the boss's base in the arc in which the node lay immediately before the boss entered it."
- Rationale: A boss turns before each node of a movement, so the arc is read at the moment of entry; the choice for a node in two arcs is kept.
- Source: F-130; preserve list (same-arc movement)

### CH-16-014 · §16.2 Boss Movement and Symbols
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A character *pushed* off the boss's own node before the boss moves must be pushed within the arc in which the character is placed (see §15.6, Characters and Arcs)."
- Rationale: §8.4 resolves a boss's push-move as an enemy's "with the differences stated for boss movement"; this section states the three differences (turning, the arc limit, and the wall fallback for a character who cannot be pushed directly away) and the dodge difficulty used against a push-move with a damage value.
- Source: A-036; cross-chapter dependency from §8.4; preserve list (same-arc pushes)

### CH-16-015 · §16.2 Boss Movement and Symbols
- Type: completion
- Before: "If a boss *leaps* into a node occupied by a character, the boss maintains its original facing and the character(s) are pushed into any adjacent node of their choosing unrestricted by the boss arc."
- After: "**Leap.** A boss movement marked with the leap icon. The boss is placed directly on the node of its target, at any distance."
- Rationale: The leap icon was used but never defined. The chapter defines it, gives its three steps, and settles a leap onto the boss's own node and a character with no valid adjacent node.
- Source: A-038, C-033, A-082

### CH-16-016 · §16.2 Boss Movement and Symbols
- Type: restructure
- Before: "The *turn* symbol indicates the boss turns 90 degrees to the left or right as indicated by the direction of the quarter-turn arrow."
- After: "The turn 90° right icon {{icon:ast-0034-turn-90-right}}, the turn 90° left icon {{icon:ast-0035-turn-90-left}}, and the turn around icon {{icon:ast-0036-turn-around}} turn the boss in place on its node."
- Rationale: The turn icons are defined once, in §15.6, and cited here with their icon names. Two worked examples (a push-move to the wall and a turn towards a target on the boss's node) are added.
- Source: F-133, C-116

### CH-16-017 · §16.3 Boss Attacks
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Boss attacks follow the same rules as enemy attacks but can contain additional symbols with more powerful effects."
- After: "A *boss* attacks as an enemy attacks (see §9.3), with the differences stated in this section."
- Rationale: States where the attack value, range, and dodge difficulty of a boss attack are found, that they are not scaled, and that a boss does not turn to attack.
- Source: C-084, B-036 (no change to boss scaling)

### CH-16-018 · §16.3 Boss Attacks
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The target is attacked in whatever *boss arc* they stand. If the target is not within the card's range, the icon is skipped, and no other character is attacked in the target's place (see §9.3, Targets)."
- Rationale: The manuscript described only area attacks; attacks on the aggro holder, on the nearest character, and on a node are stated by reference to §9.3, with a summon counted as a character as §12.1 requires.
- Source: cross-chapter dependencies from §8.1, §9.3, and §12.1

### CH-16-019 · §16.3 Boss Attacks
- Type: clarification
- Before: "The *area* symbol indicates that the boss is attacking entire arcs instead of an individual character. Bosses do not change facing when they attack and instead deal damage to characters within range that occupy nodes in their attack arcs."
- After: "An *area attack* targets every character who is within the card's range of the boss and who is in an *attack arc* {{icon:ast-0040-attack-boss-arc}} of the arc diagram beneath the icon (see §15.6, Arc Diagrams)."
- Rationale: The area attack becomes a defined term with its icon; a node in two arcs and a character on the boss's own node are both covered.
- Source: C-027, C-114

### CH-16-020 · §16.3 Boss Attacks
- Type: clarification
- Before: "characters targeted by an *area* attack either block*, resist,* or dodge as they normally would."
- After: "Each character targeted by a boss's attack chooses one defence before any dice are rolled, as against any enemy attack (see §9.3, Defence)."
- Rationale: Damage type, push-attacks, conditions, and the defences are stated by reference to their owning sections, with the dodge difficulty taken from the behaviour card. A worked area-attack example is added.
- Source: C-027, C-114, C-047

### CH-16-021 · §16.4 Boss Aggro
- Type: style
- Before: "Because the boss takes a turn after each individual character takes their turn, one can assume any damage dealt by a character will be the most damage prior to the boss's activation. Therefore, simply move the *aggro* token to a character's dashboard if they deal any damage to the boss during their turn."
- After: "At the end of each character's *turn*, if that character inflicted damage on the boss during the turn, the aggro token moves to that character."
- Rationale: The reasoning is replaced by the rule it derived; "one", "simply", "Therefore", and "activation" are removed. The mechanic is unchanged.
- Source: F-034, F-087, F-091, F-090, C-039, C-123; preserve list (boss aggro moves on any damage)

### CH-16-022 · §16.4 Boss Aggro
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Similar to basic encounters, the *aggro* token moves to the character who dealt the most damage before the boss's next turn."
- After: "In a boss encounter the aggro token does not move at the end of a *round* as it does in other encounters (see §7.5, Encounter Turn Order)."
- Rationale: Separates the boss rule from the round-based rule of §7.5 and cites the placement of the token at setup to §15.2.
- Source: F-034

### CH-16-023 · §16.4 Boss Aggro
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If no damage is dealt, the *aggro* token remains stationary for that turn."
- After: "If a riposte inflicts damage on the boss, the aggro token moves to the character who made it at the end of that boss turn, after the behaviour card has been resolved."
- Rationale: Covers damage dealt out of turn by a riposte, an attack reduced to no damage, the death of the holder, and a summon's damage (cited to §12.1). A worked example is added.
- Source: A-018

### CH-16-024 · §16.5 Boss Death
- Type: style
- Before: "When the boss's health dial is reduced to zero, the boss is slain and the encounter ends."
- After: "A *boss* is defeated when its *health dial* is reduced to zero (see §15.4). The boss *encounter* ends at once."
- Rationale: Bosses are "defeated", not "slain". The chapter adds that the model is removed and no further icon is resolved.
- Source: C-061, C-037

### CH-16-025 · §16.5 Boss Death
- Type: restructure
- Before: "If not, determine boss treasures and collect soul rewards as previously described. Then, increase the spark dial by one and give each character an *ember* token to mark them as *embered.*"
- After: "Place an *ember token* in the ember slot of every character's dashboard, including each character who died in the encounter. Every character is *embered*, and no *ember card* is discarded (see §7.3)."
- Rationale: The consequences of a boss's defeat become a seven-step procedure with numbered cross-references: soul reward, one random boss treasure card, embering, the PvP encounter, and the setup that follows.
- Source: F-079, C-084, C-120, C-027, A-007; Gate 1 — OQ-30

### CH-16-026 · §16.5 Boss Death
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Finally, the characters return to The Bonfire to rest at no spark cost. They may visit the Firekeeper and Andre if they wish."
- After: "The spark is gained once and the rest is taken once for each defeated boss, whether or not a PvP encounter is fought (see §20.9)."
- Rationale: §6.6 and §6.7 add the spark and take the free rest as setup steps, and §20 places the PvP encounter before them; this section states the order and that neither is gained twice.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-15; C-042, C-043, C-052, A-005; cross-chapter dependencies from §6.6, §7.4, §11.2, §20.1, and §20.9

### CH-16-027 · §16.5 Boss Death
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A character who died in the boss encounter stays out of play until the rest, which restores every character (see §11.2)."
- Rationale: The manuscript did not say what becomes of a character who died in a boss encounter that was won, or of the boss treasure cards not awarded. The chapter follows §20.1 (such a character takes no part in the PvP encounter) and returns the unawarded cards to the game box.
- Source: cross-chapter dependencies from §7.5, §11.2, and §20.1

### CH-16-028 · §16.5 Boss Death
- Type: cut
- Before: "This is often a reasonable time to end a game session if time is limited. Either today or another day the players may continue on with post-boss setup rules as described earlier in these rules."
- After: deleted
- Rationale: Advice about table logistics, with a hedge; the setup that follows a boss is cited in step 7.
- Source: F-035, C-021, F-080, C-121

### CH-16-029 · §16.5 Boss Death
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If this was the final boss (main or mega), the characters are victorious and the players win."
- After: "A boss fought with two models, and the *Twin Gargoyles*, are defeated as their own rules state (see §17.1)."
- Rationale: The final-boss rule is kept and cited to §7.1. Bosses with two models, the invasion that embering provokes, and campaign play are each delegated in one sentence to the section that owns them. A worked example is added.
- Source: A-039, A-045; Gate 1 — OQ-25, OQ-28

### CH-16-030 · §16 Boss Turns (style sweep)
- Type: style
- Before: "Boss Movement & Symbols", "activation", "slain", "The Bonfire", "block*, resist,*", "*embered.*", "characters must take"
- After: "Boss Movement and Symbols", "turn", "defeated", "the *bonfire*", "*blocks*", "*embered*", "Characters"
- Rationale: Terminology, italics, capitalisation, numerals, degree signs, and the conversion of every prose cross-reference to "(see §N.M)".
- Source: C-019, C-021, C-027, C-030, C-033, C-037, C-039, C-042, C-043, C-044, C-052, C-061, C-084, C-104, C-112, C-114, C-116, C-123, F-089, F-090, F-091, F-132, F-133

### CH-16-031 · §16 Boss Turns (OPTIONAL findings skipped)
- Type: clarification
- Before: n/a
- After: n/a
- Rationale: A-047 (a spark maximum in the standard game): would add a cap the manuscript does not have, and sparks are owned by §7.4. B-036 (optional health for bosses at four characters): bosses are not scaled, which is preserved.
- Source: A-047, B-036

### CH-16-032 · §16 Boss Turns (findings owned by other chapters)
- Type: clarification
- Before: n/a
- After: n/a
- Rationale: Not restated here because another section owns the rule and this chapter cites it: A-024 (enemy repeat, §8.3 and §9.3), A-039 (two boss models, §17.1), A-045 (invasion timing, §12.2), A-082 (terrain and movement, §5.5), A-122 (boss encounter setup, §15.2), A-005 (removal of hollow, §7.2), A-007 (ember card held at embering, §7.3), A-042 (heat-up timing, §15.5).
- Source: A-024, A-039, A-045, A-082, A-122, A-005, A-007, A-042

### CH-16-033 · §16.4
- Type: clarification
- Before: "A *summon* does not hold the aggro token, and damage inflicted by a summon does not move it (see §12.1)."
- After: "A *summon* does not hold the aggro token, and damage inflicted by a summon does not move it. While the Distract icon of a summon's behaviour card is in effect, the boss treats the summon as the holder (see §12.1)."
- Rationale: §16.4 owns boss aggro and did not mention the one effect that redirects it; the sentence points to §12.1 without restating the rule.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E17 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official Phantoms booklet, Distract (lines 293-298).

### CH-16-034 · §16.1
- Type: ruling
- Before: "A boss's turn is resolved as follows."
- After: "A boss's turn is resolved as follows. If the boss is due to heat up, its heat-up is carried out before step 1 (see §15.5, Heat-Up)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] States in the boss turn where the heat-up of boss-E01 is carried out, without renumbering the steps.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E04 (`06_final_rulings.md`); follows boss-E01

### CH-16-035 · §16.1
- Type: ruling
- Before: "If the boss heats up during its own turn, the *heat-up card* joins the behaviour deck after step 5 (see §15.5, Heat-Up)."
- After: "If the boss's health is reduced to or below its heat-up point during its own turn, the *heat-up card* joins the behaviour deck at the start of the boss's next turn (see §15.5, Heat-Up)."
- Rationale: Keeps §16.1 in step with the single heat-up timing of boss-E01; the result for a heat-up caused during the boss's own turn is unchanged.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E05 (`06_final_rulings.md`); follows boss-E01

### CH-16-036 · §16.4
- Type: ruling
- Before: "At the end of each character's *turn*, if that character inflicted damage on the boss during the turn, the aggro token moves to that character. If the character inflicted no damage on the boss, the token stays with its holder."
- After: "At the end of each character's *turn*, if that character inflicted damage on the boss during the turn, the aggro token moves to that character. Damage inflicted on another enemy model that the boss's special rules place in the encounter moves the token in the same way (see §17). If the character inflicted no such damage, the token stays with its holder."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Without this the token cannot move during the first part of the Executioner's Chariot encounter, when the boss cannot be damaged; one general clause closes the gap for every boss that brings other enemies.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E06 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Issue list ch. 17, Executioner's Chariot (c); Chariot booklet DS-EC-Rules-Web.txt lines 446-462 (other enemies act in the encounter).

### CH-16-037 · §16.5
- Type: ruling
- Before: "A boss fought with two models, and the *Twin Gargoyles*, are defeated as their own rules state (see §17.1)."
- After: "A boss fought with several boss models is defeated only when its own rules say so: a boss model whose health dial is reduced to zero is removed from the tile, and the encounter continues against the models that remain (see §17.1, Several Boss Models in One Encounter). The *Twin Gargoyles* are defeated as their own rule states (see §17.1, Twin Gargoyles)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Boss Death must not be resolved when one of several boss models falls (Ornstein & Smough, the Four Kings).
- Source: final rulings, boss-E07 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Ornstein and Smough data cards (community transcription, DSBG-Shuffle); Four Kings booklet lines 445-447.

### CH-16-038 · §16.4
- Type: clarification
- Before: "A character inflicts damage on a boss when an attack lowers the boss's *health dial* by one or more."
- After: "A character inflicts damage on a boss when the character's attack, or a *bleed* that the attack triggers, lowers the boss's *health dial* by one or more."
- Rationale: Bleed triggered by a character's attack counts for the aggro token (combat-E17), and bleed is not itself an attack.
- Source: final rulings, lead-E13 (`06_final_rulings.md`); combat memo, cross-cluster effects

### CH-16-039 · §16.5
- Type: correction
- Before: "In campaign play the spark, the rest, and the setup that follow a boss differ (see §18.1; §18.3)."
- After: "In campaign play the PvP encounter, the spark, the rest, and the setup that follow a boss differ (see §18.1; §18.3)."
- Rationale: Step 6 orders a PvP encounter after every boss; in campaign play it follows only a boss that completes a section (§18.1).
- Source: final rulings, VB-007 (`06_final_rulings.md`); independent verification, work/phase3/verify_b.md

## Chapter 23 — Appendices

### CH-23-001 · Appendix A Weapon Stat Revisions and Appendix B Card Amendments
- Type: restructure
- Before: "# COMMUNITY UPDATE PACK" with the bold labels "Treasure - Official", "Treasure - Errata/Updated", "Weapons - Updated Stat Requirements", "Enemy Data", "Boss Data & Behaviours"
- After: "# Appendix A. Weapon Stat Revisions" and "# Appendix B. Card Amendments", with the sections Revised Requirements, Treasure Type, Additional Treasure, Revised Treasure, Enemy Data Cards, and Boss Data and Behaviour Cards
- Rationale: The chapter becomes the two appendices fixed at Gate 1. The pack is never named; the text speaks of the revised cards and the cards listed in the appendix.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-31; CMP-0004; F-047

### CH-23-002 · Appendix A (lead)
- Type: cut
- Before: "The first edition of DStBG has many nonsensical weapon stat requirements which this edition aims to remedy. Specifically, many high strength requirement weapons from the video game series where given intelligence and/or Faith requirements."
- After: "The *stat requirements* in this appendix replace those printed on the *weapon* cards listed. A weapon not listed keeps its printed requirements."
- Rationale: Design rationale and comparison with another edition are removed; the rule the paragraph carried (the listed values replace the printed ones) is kept, with the tier split at 25 and the scaling rule cited to their owning sections. The CMP-0004 rationale sentence is not adopted for the same reason.
- Source: F-048; CMP-0004

### CH-23-003 · Appendix A, Revised Requirements
- Type: restructure
- Before: "**Weapon Name STR DEX INT FTH**" followed by 60 plain-text lines such as "Avelyn 23 30 0 0"
- After: "| Weapon | STR | DEX | INT | FTH | Tier |" with one row per weapon, sorted alphabetically, digits throughout
- Rationale: Plain-text columns set as a table with a computed Tier column. All 60 rows were checked by script against the source and against CMP-0004: the two agree on every value, and the only differences in the chapter are the ten corrected rows logged in CH-23-004 to CH-23-007. CMP-0004 has no grouping, so the table has none.
- Source: C-072; CMP-0004

### CH-23-004 · Appendix A, Revised Requirements (Falchion)
- Type: correction
- Before: "Falchion 37 30 0 0"
- After: "| Falchion | 17 | 30 | 0 | 0 | 2 |"
- Rationale: Digit transposition corrected. Source and CMP-0004 both print 37; the Gate 1-approved finding governs. The card stays tier two on DEX.
- Source: B-018; Gate 1 (approve all)

### CH-23-005 · Appendix A, Revised Requirements (Carthus Curved Greatsword, Carthus Curved Sword)
- Type: correction
- Before: "Carthus Curved Greatsword 19 30 0 0 / Carthus Curved Sword 25 35 0 0"
- After: "| Carthus Curved Greatsword | 25 | 35 | 0 | 0 | 2 |" and "| Carthus Curved Sword | 19 | 30 | 0 | 0 | 2 |"
- Rationale: The two lines are swapped so that the heavier weapon has the higher requirements. Source and CMP-0004 both print the unswapped values; the approved finding governs. Both stay tier two.
- Source: B-019; Gate 1 (approve all)

### CH-23-006 · Appendix A, Revised Requirements (boundary rows)
- Type: correction
- Before: "Butcher Knife 25 0 0 0 / Dark Silver Tracer 0 25 0 0 / Warden Twinblades 17 25 0 0"
- After: "| Butcher Knife | 24 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 |", "| Dark Silver Tracer | 12 | 26 | 0 | 0 | 2 |", "| Warden Twinblades | 17 | 26 | 0 | 0 | 2 |"
- Rationale: The three rows that sat exactly on the split at 25 are moved off it. The finding offers Butcher Knife 24 (tier one) or 28 (tier two); 24 is adopted as the first-listed value and the one that answers the audit's concern (see issues file). Butcher Knife moves from tier two to tier one; the other two stay tier two.
- Source: B-020 (OPTIONAL, applied); Gate 1 (approve all)

### CH-23-007 · Appendix A, Revised Requirements (claws, dagger, Caestus, zero requirements)
- Type: correction
- Before: "Claws 0 20 0 0 / Manikin Claws 24 24 0 0 / Parrying Dagger 14 21 0 0 / Caestus 0 15 0 0"
- After: "Claws 12 20", "Manikin Claws 14 24", "Parrying Dagger 10 18", "Caestus 12 10", and "A weapon shown with 0 STR or 0 DEX has no requirement in that stat, and every character meets it."
- Rationale: Approved value corrections; all four rows remain tier one. The finding's note sentence ("A requirement of 0 is intentional where shown") is restated as a rule, together with the fact that every listed weapon has 0 INT and 0 FTH (C-072).
- Source: B-021; C-072; Gate 1 (approve all)

### CH-23-008 · Appendix A, Revised Requirements (Tier column) and Treasure Type
- Type: correction
- Before: CMP-0004: "A weapon is tier one if every requirement is below 25 and tier two otherwise." with the column values "One" / "Two"
- After: "The Tier column shows 1 for *tier one treasure* and 2 for *tier two treasure*." and "A listed card that is *transposed treasure* or *legendary treasure* is *tier two treasure* whatever its requirements (see §6.2)."
- Rationale: Tier recomputed from the final values: 23 rows are tier one and 37 tier two (CMP-0004's note of nineteen and forty-one did not match its own table). The only tier that differs from CMP-0004 is Butcher Knife (Two to 1). The treasure-type sentences keep the column consistent with §6.2 for transposed, legendary, and boss treasure.
- Source: CMP-0004; B-020; §6.2

### CH-23-009 · Appendix A, Revised Requirements (example)
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: "*Example.* The Brigand Axe's revised requirements are 19 STR and 16 DEX, so strength is its *relevant stat*. The Knight has 24 STR and 20 DEX, meets both requirements, and equips the Brigand Axe."
- Rationale: One worked example shows a revised requirement used for equipping and for scaling at five over (OQ-19). It introduces no rule.
- Source: CMP-0004; Gate 1 — OQ-19

### CH-23-010 · Appendix B (lead)
- Type: completion
- Before: new text (CMP-0004: "The Community Update Pack is a set of print-and-play cards that accompanies this edition. … Where a card in the pack and a published card disagree, the pack card governs.")
- After: "The *revised cards* replace the printed cards of the same name. Where a card listed here differs from its printed text, the text given here governs. Where only part of a card is amended, every other part of the card is as printed."
- Rationale: The general rule is stated once. The description of the pack as a print-and-play set and its purpose is cut; the precedence rule and the "unchanged parts as printed" rule are kept.
- Source: CMP-0004; F-047; Gate 1 — OQ-31

### CH-23-011 · Appendix B, Additional Treasure
- Type: completion
- Before: "**Treasure - Official**" over 34 bare card names in two lists split by a stray comment marker
- After: "Each card listed in this section is added to the *treasure cards* in play. Each card is sorted during setup by the treasure type marked on it and by its *stat requirements* (see §6.2)." with a table Card | Amendment of 34 rows
- Rationale: The bare list becomes a rule and an alphabetical table. All 34 source names are present (CMP-0004 counts 33 but lists 34). The eight weapons that also appear in Appendix A point to it, and Blue Titanite points to its amendment. CMP-0004's three type groups are not kept: a card's type is read from the card, and the supplied sources do not confirm the grouping (see issues file).
- Source: CMP-0004; F-047; C-117

### CH-23-012 · Appendix B, Revised Treasure (Titanite Chunk and Titanite Slab)
- Type: completion
- Before: "Titanite Chunk (+2 attack, must replace Titanite Shard) \[25/25/0/0 Common\]" and "Titanite Slab (+3 attack, must replace Titanite Chunk) \[25/25/0/0 legendary\]"
- After: "Every *attack option* of the *weapon* adds 2 to its *attack value*. This card may be attached only in place of a Titanite Shard." and "A replaced Titanite Shard is returned to the bottom of the tier one treasure deck, and a replaced Titanite Chunk is returned to the bottom of the tier two treasure deck."
- Rationale: The notes become card text, stat requirements, and treasure type, and the Shard to Chunk to Slab chain is stated in the sub-section Titanite Replacement with its exception to the permanence of Titanite (§4.2). Escaped brackets are replaced by "25 STR, 25 DEX, 0 INT, and 0 FTH".
- Source: CMP-0004; F-047; C-109; A-074

### CH-23-013 · Appendix B, Revised Treasure (Blue Titanite and Demon Titanite)
- Type: completion
- Before: "Demon Titanite" (no note) and "Blue Titanite (legendary Item instead of Boss Drop, increase INT req to 25)"
- After: "Replace the printed treasure type with *legendary treasure*. Replace the printed INT requirement with 25. The rules text is unchanged." and "Replace the printed card with the *revised card*. The rules text, stat requirements, and treasure type are unchanged."
- Rationale: Blue Titanite's two changes are stated as replacements. Demon Titanite carries no note in the manuscript, so the card is listed with no change of rule, as CMP-0004 proposes. Bare "Item" is removed.

### CH-23-014 · Appendix B, Revised Treasure (Tiny Being's Ring and Chloranthy Ring)
- Type: completion
- Before: "Tiny Being’s Ring (remove one damage cube each turn)", "Chloranthy Ring (+1 passive stamina gain each turn to characters on node)", and "Chloranthy Ring (will need custom card to state +1 stamina gain per turn)"
- After: "At the start of each of the wearer's turns, the wearer removes 1 *damage cube* from their *endurance bar*." and "At the start of each of the wearer's turns, the wearer and every other character on the wearer's *node* each recover 1 stamina."
- Rationale: Each note becomes card text with a trigger, an actor, and an outcome. The two Chloranthy Ring entries are merged into one and the working note is cut. The wearer of a ring is defined once in the section lead.
- Source: CMP-0004; F-047

### CH-23-015 · Appendix B, Revised Treasure (dodge cards)
- Type: completion
- Before: "Black Leather Armour (will keep old version with free dodge)", "Wolf Ring (change to allow re-rolling dodge rolls for one stamina)", "Eastern Armour (will keep old version of +1 stamina cost to dodge)", "Carthus Milkring (will use new version of increasing dodge by one)"
- After: "The character who has this card equipped pays no stamina to attempt a *dodge*." / "pays 1 additional stamina to attempt a dodge." / "The wearer adds 1 to the count of dodge icons rolled for a dodge." / "the wearer may pay 1 stamina to reroll any or all of those dice. The wearer may do this once for each dodge."
- Rationale: The "will keep" and "will use" notes are cut and each card's governing text is stated and tied to the numbered steps of a dodge (§9.5). The choice between printings is stated without history: the printing that carries the text is used.
