### CH-23-016 · Appendix B, Revised Treasure (Sellsword Twinblades)
- Type: completion
- Before: "Sellsword Twinblade (will keep old one-handed version)"
- After: "The one-handed card of this name is used. A two-handed card of this name is returned to the game box."
- Rationale: Working note rewritten as a rule. The card name follows CMP-0004 ("Twinblades"), which differs from the source ("Twinblade").
- Source: CMP-0004; F-047; Gate 1 — OQ-17

### CH-23-017 · Appendix B, Revised Treasure (Covetous Silver Serpent Ring)
- Type: completion
- Before: "Covetous Silver Serpent Ring (+1 soul to an equipped character per encounter)"
- After: "When the wearer gains the soul reward for a *cleared* *encounter*, the wearer gains 1 additional soul." The additional soul is gained with the full reward and with the *reduced reward* (see §7.6).
- Rationale: The bonus is tied to the encounter soul reward of §7.6, full or reduced, as CMP-0004 proposes.
- Source: CMP-0004; F-047; Gate 1 — OQ-05

### CH-23-018 · Appendix B, Revised Treasure (Mon Ring)
- Type: completion
- Before: "Mon Ring (one use per floor)"
- After: "This effect may be used only while this card is face up. When the effect is used, turn this card face down." The card is turned face up each time the characters rest at the *bonfire* (see §11.2).
- Rationale: "Floor" is not a term of the book. It is read, as CMP-0004 proposes, as the play between two rests, and the limit is tracked by turning the card.

### CH-23-019 · Appendix B, Enemy Data Cards

- After: "Each enemy listed in this section is played from the revised *data card* of the same name, which shows the values of an enemy data card (see §8.2)." with a table Card | Amendment of 12 rows naming each enemy's expansion
- Rationale: The bare list becomes a rule and a table. "Skeleton Beast" is the spelling used, matching §2.2. The values shown on a data card are cited to §8.2 and not repeated.

- Rationale: The bare list becomes a rule and a table that names each boss's type and expansion, consistent with §2.3, §17.7, and §17.10. The printed boss treasure, model, and health dial stay in use. The ampersand is replaced by "and".

- Type: clarification
- Before: (CMP-0003 draft) "In the basic game a new character joins on the same terms, while the characters are resting at the bonfire."
- After: "Yes, while the characters are on the bonfire tile and at the cost of one spark … (see §18.4)."

### CH-22-007 · §22 F.A.Q. (invaders)
- Type: correction
- Before: "**A.** Yes. While the invader is on the tile, the encounter is not cleared (see §12.2, End of an Invasion)."
- After: "**A.** Not always. While the invader is on the tile, the encounter is not cleared. It is cleared when every enemy model, the invader included, has been killed, or when the invader has vanished and every other enemy model has been killed (see §12.2, End of an Invasion)."
- Rationale: §12.2 also clears the encounter when the invader has vanished and every other enemy model is dead.
- Source: QA-004 (example and consistency verification)

### CH-22-008 · §22.2
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**A.** No. A parry requires a shield in the left weapon slot. A weapon that bears"
- After: "**A.** No. A parry requires a shield in a weapon slot. A weapon that bears"
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Follows §9.6 as changed by combat-E22.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E34 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As combat-E22.

### CH-22-009 · §22.2
- Type: ruling
- Before: "Each of its attack options then adds one to its attack value. Spells are never scaled (see §10.5)."
- After: "Each of its attack options then adds one to its attack value. A weapon whose stat requirements are all zero is never scaled, and spells are never scaled (see §10.5)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Follows §10.5 as changed by combat-E29.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E35 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As combat-E29.

### CH-22-010 · §22.1
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**A.** Only to burn the *bonfire ascetic*, which the party pays for together (see §18.7). Every other cost is paid from the acting character's own souls: the Firekeeper's stat tiers, Blacksmith Andre's cards, a *summon*, and *transposition* (see §7.6)."
- After: "**A.** Only in campaign play, to burn the *bonfire ascetic* or to buy back a *spark*, which the party pays for together (see §18.3; §18.7). Every other cost is paid from the acting character's own souls: the Firekeeper's stat tiers, Blacksmith Andre's cards, a *summon*, and *transposition* (see §7.6)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] F.A.Q. follows §18.3 and §18.7.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E23 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As campaign-E18.

### CH-22-011 · §22.6
- Type: clarification
- Before: "**A.** Not always. While the invader is on the tile, the encounter is not cleared. It is cleared when every enemy model, the invader included, has been killed, or when the invader has vanished and every other enemy model has been killed (see §12.2, End of an Invasion)."
- After: "**A.** Yes, unless it vanishes. The encounter is not cleared while the invader is on the tile, and the invader leaves the tile only when it is killed or when the death of a character makes it vanish (see §12.2, End of an Invasion)."
- Rationale: Same rule, answered the way the question is asked and in the words of §12.2 as amended.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E40 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Follows phantom-E11.

### CH-22-012 · §22.6
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**A.** The mimic encounter is failed, and with it the encounter that held the chest. The mimic returns to its chest, and the characters must rest at the bonfire. Each later time that encounter is cleared, the party again decides whether to open the chest (see §13.4)."
- After: "**A.** The mimic encounter is failed, and with it the encounter that held the chest. The mimic returns to its chest, and the characters must rest at the bonfire. Each later time that encounter is cleared, the party again decides whether to open the chest (see §13.4).

**Q.** Do the characters recover before a mimic is fought?

**A.** They remove their stamina cubes and nothing else. Each character keeps their damage cubes and condition tokens, and a character who died in the cleared encounter stays dead. The recovery and the rewards of the cleared encounter follow the mimic's death (see §13.2; §13.3)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Players who know the published rule expect a full recovery before the mimic; the answer states this book's rule as amended.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E41 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Follows phantom-E19.

### CH-22-013 · §22.1
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**Q.** Which weapon slot is the left weapon slot, and which is the right?

**A.** The left weapon slot is the weapon slot on the left of the dashboard as its player reads it, and the right weapon slot is the one on the right. A character moves cards between them only by the Equip minor action during an encounter (see §3.2; §10.1).

"
- After: ""
- Rationale: After the parry ruling (combat-E22) no rule depends on which weapon slot is which, so the question is withdrawn.
- Source: final rulings, lead-E11 (`06_final_rulings.md`); combat memo, cross-cluster effects

### CH-22-014 · §22.4
- Type: clarification
- Before: "**Q.** Who holds the aggro token when its holder dies?"
- After: "**Q.** May the party return to the bonfire tile without resting?

**A.** No. The party returns to the bonfire tile only to rest (see §11.1).

**Q.** Who holds the aggro token when its holder dies?"
- Rationale: States the consequence of core-E36 where players will look for it.
- Source: final rulings, lead-E12 (`06_final_rulings.md`); core memo, cross-cluster effects

### CH-22-015 · §22.5
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**A.** One boss with two models. The models share one data card, one health dial, and one behaviour deck, and the boss heats up once. Each behaviour card names the model that acts. A character who attacks either model attacks the boss, and the weak-arc die is judged against the arcs of the model attacked (see §17.2)."
- After: "**A.** One boss with two boss models. Each model has its own data card and health dial, the two share one behaviour deck, and both act on each boss turn. When the first model is defeated the boss heats up: the survivor's heat-up cards become the behaviour deck, and the survivor regains health. The boss is defeated when both models have been defeated (see §17.2)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Keeps the F.A.Q. in step with boss-E10.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E46 (`06_final_rulings.md`); follows boss-E10

### CH-22-016 · §22.5
- Type: clarification
- Before: "**Q.** A campaign section lists the Gargoyle twice. Are the two fights separate encounters?"
- After: "**Q.** When a boss heats up, is its discard pile shuffled as well?

**A.** Yes. At the start of the boss's next turn the behaviour deck and the discard pile are gathered, one heat-up card taken at random is added, and all the cards are shuffled to form a new behaviour deck. The order learned before the heat-up is lost (see §15.5, Heat-Up).

**Q.** A campaign section lists the Gargoyle twice. Are the two fights separate encounters?"
- Rationale: The most asked question about boss fights now has its answer in the F.A.Q.; the rule itself is boss-E01.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E48 (`06_final_rulings.md`); BoardGameGeek thread 1819893 (search summary); follows boss-E01

### CH-22-017 · §22.1
- Type: correction
- Before: "**A.** Yes, while the characters are on the bonfire tile and at the cost of one *spark*. The new character begins with starting equipment, base stats, and no souls (see §18.4)."
- After: "**A.** Yes, while the characters are on the bonfire tile, no encounter is in progress, and the spark dial shows one or more. The party spends one *spark* for each player who joins. The new character begins with starting equipment, base stats, and no souls (see §18.4)."
- Rationale: The answer omitted two conditions of §18.4: no encounter in progress, and a spark dial that shows one or more.
- Source: final rulings, VB-014 (`06_final_rulings.md`); independent verification, work/phase3/verify_b.md

### CH-22-018 · §22.6
- Type: correction
- Before: "**A.** Yes. The invader vanishes at once and awards nothing, and the encounter continues against the remaining enemy models. The invader does not return (see §12.2, Combat with an Invader)."
- After: "**A.** Yes, unless that death fails the encounter. The invader vanishes at once and awards nothing, and the encounter continues against the remaining enemy models. The invader does not return, unless a campaign states otherwise (see §12.2, Combat with an Invader; §12.2, End of an Invasion)."
- Rationale: The death of a hollow or last character fails the encounter; the rule that the invader does not return, and its campaign exception, stand under End of an Invasion.
- Source: final rulings, VB-015 (`06_final_rulings.md`); independent verification, work/phase3/verify_b.md

### CH-22-019 · §22.5
- Type: correction
- Before: "**A.** Yes. The character is pushed to a valid adjacent node in the arc in which the character is placed. If a wall leaves no such node, the character is pushed to a valid adjacent node that touches the wall (see §15.6, Characters and Arcs)."
- After: "**A.** Yes, except after a *leap*. The character is pushed to a valid adjacent node in the arc in which the character is placed. If a wall leaves no such node, the character is pushed to a valid adjacent node that touches the wall. The push that follows a leap is not limited by arcs (see §15.6, Characters and Arcs; §16.2, Leap)."
- Rationale: The cited section excepts the push that follows a leap.
- Source: final rulings, VB-016 (`06_final_rulings.md`); independent verification, work/phase3/verify_b.md

## Chapter 18 — Campaign Play

### CH-18-001 · §18 Campaign Play
- Type: restructure
- Before: "#  CAMPAIGN PLAY" / "## Adding & Dropping Players" / "## Encounter Soul Rewards" / "### Campaign Soul Reward Table" / "## Encounter Treasure Rewards" / "## The Bonfire Tile" / "### Bonfire Ascetic" / "### Transposition" and three empty headings
- After: "# 18. Campaign Play" with H2s 18.1 Setup, 18.2 The Fog Gate, 18.3 Sparks, 18.4 Adding and Dropping Players, 18.5 Encounter Rewards, 18.6 The Bonfire, 18.7 Bonfire Ascetic, 18.8 Transposition
- Rationale: Headings follow the section map. The two reward sections and their table merge into §18.5, the ascetic and transposition become sections of their own, "&" becomes "and", and the empty headings used as page breaks are deleted.
- Source: C-098, C-101, C-104, C-112; tracker rows; brief §4

### CH-18-002 · §18 Campaign Play
- Type: style
- Before: new text
- After: "A campaign is a long road between many fires. The party carries its *souls*, its equipment, and its scars from one *section* to the next"
- Rationale: A chapter intro of under 60 words with no rule in it replaces the advisory opening.
- Source: F-036; brief §2

### CH-18-003 · §18.1 Setup
- Type: clarification
- Before: "The setup for a campaign is largely the same as the basic game. However, each section of a scenario has varying numbers of exploration tiles, may contain multiple bosses, and may have encounters with special rules."
- After: "Campaign play follows the standard game except as this chapter states. Where a *campaign* states a rule of its own, that rule takes precedence over this chapter (see §19)."
- Rationale: One governing sentence replaces the hedge "largely" and the stray terms "scenario", "exploration tiles", and "the basic game". The chapter is written as deltas from the standard game.
- Source: F-053, F-090, F-134, C-053, C-084

### CH-18-004 · §18.1 Setup
- Type: cut
- Before: "Campaigns provide a more challenging and longer play experience suitable for players who have played the basic game at least a few times.. Players might expect to meet over multiple play sessions to complete a campaign."
- After: deleted
- Rationale: Audience and session advice is not a rule. The closing advice sentence of the CMP-0008 draft is not adopted for the same reason.
- Source: F-036, F-037, C-023, C-106

### CH-18-005 · §18.1 Setup
- Type: correction
- Before: "the included campaigns are all three sections long. … This rule-set includes three campaigns which require only the components available in the core set. Many of the game's expansions also add additional campaigns for selection."
- After: "The party chooses one of the 12 campaigns (see §19). Two of them, The First Journey and The Coiled Sword, require only the core set (see §19.1, §19.2). … A campaign has three to six sections"
- Rationale: The introductory claims contradicted the campaign chapter. The count is twelve after the duplicate campaign is removed.
- Source: C-074, A-103, F-038, C-060, CMP-0008; brief §3

### CH-18-006 · §18.1 Setup
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "**Section.** One list of tiles within a campaign: a *bonfire* tile, a number of encounter tiles with their *encounter levels*, and the bosses to be faced."
- Rationale: The manuscript used "section" without defining it. Campaign and Section are defined, and the carry-over of stat tiers, cards, souls, and tokens between sections is stated.
- Source: CMP-0008

### CH-18-007 · §18.1 Setup, Campaign Setup
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The party does not select bosses, and random boss selection is not used (see §6.5, Boss Selection). The bosses are those the campaign lists."
- Rationale: The differences from the standard setup are listed once: no boss selection, legendary treasure set aside, the spark maximum, the first section's layout, and no optional mega-boss.
- Source: CMP-0008; manuscript "Never add legendary or boss treasure to either tier of treasure deck"

### CH-18-008 · §18.1 Setup, Section Setup
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Each encounter tile is dealt one encounter card of the level the section lists for its position, in place of the levels printed on a boss's data card."
- Rationale: The manuscript never said how a section is laid out. The section's list replaces the standard route within the setup of §6.5 and §6.6; the Twin Gargoyles, the fog gate, the shop, the spark, and the invasion limit are each cited to their owning sections.
- Source: CMP-0008, A-048, OQ-28

### CH-18-009 · §18.1 Setup, Section Completion
- Type: completion
- Before: "characters no longer rest at the *bonfire* for free after defeating a boss except for the final boss of each section."
- After: "A section is completed when the last *boss* it lists is defeated (see §16.5). A section whose list ends in an encounter instead of a boss is completed when that encounter is *cleared* for the first time"
- Rationale: Sections that end in an encounter had no way to end. Completion grants a restock and a free rest; a spark is gained only for a boss. Boss Death (§16.5) is cited, with the spark gained at once and the next section's setup in place of the standard one. A-048's spark for a section ended by an encounter is not adopted because CMP-0008 governs.
- Source: CMP-0008, A-048

### CH-18-010 · §18.1 Setup, Level 4 Encounters and Mega-Bosses
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "A *Level 4 encounter* listed in a section is fought on the Level 4 encounter side of a *mega-boss game board*. The game board takes the place of an encounter tile at that position in the section's route."
- Rationale: §6.7 relies on this section for where a campaign's Level 4 encounter is fought and for the mega-boss that is not the final boss, which is fought as any boss and does not end the campaign. Death and hollowing follow the standard rules in every encounter.
- Source: A-049, OQ-14; chapter 06 issues list (A-094)

### CH-18-011 · §18.1 Setup, The Final Boss
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "The campaign's final boss is the last boss listed in its last section. The players win the campaign when the final boss is defeated (see §7.1)."
- Rationale: No source named the campaign's final boss or its victory condition, on which §7.1 and §20.8 depend.
- Source: A-049; chapter 20 issues list

### CH-18-012 · §18.1 Setup, Player versus Player Encounters
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Player versus player (PvP) encounters remain an optional rule in campaign play (see §20.1). When they are in play, a *PvP encounter* follows the defeat of each boss, as in the standard game."
- Rationale: The manuscript did not say whether PvP encounters apply in campaign play. The optional rule applies as in §20.1, and the case of a boss that does not complete its section is given a definite outcome.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-15; chapter 20 issues list

### CH-18-013 · §18.2 The Fog Gate
- Type: clarification
- Before: "The fog gate does not provide a short-cut to the boss encounter in campaign play as it does in the basic game. The fog gate token is purely aesthetic in campaign play."
- After: "In campaign play the *fog gate* token marks the *doorway* through which the characters enter a boss encounter and has no other effect."
- Rationale: The token still marks where the boss encounter begins and where a summon is called; only the move to the bonfire tile and the shortcut are removed. The route after a rest is stated.
- Source: C-058, F-053, C-084

### CH-18-014 · §18.3 Sparks
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "The starting number of sparks also serves as the maximum number of sparks."
- After: "The dial's maximum is that starting value plus one. The dial cannot be turned past its maximum, and a spark gained while the dial shows its maximum is not added."
- Rationale: Under the hard cap a boss defeated at a full dial gave nothing. A table of starting and maximum values for one to four characters is added.
- Source: B-013; Gate 1 — OQ-22

### CH-18-015 · §18.3 Sparks
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "Sparks cannot be purchased or gained otherwise."
- After: "While the party rests, it may buy back one spark. Every character pays four *souls* of their own to the supply, and the spark dial is turned up by one."
- Rationale: The approved buy-back is stated with its limits: one spark per rest, not above the maximum, and no pooling of souls.
- Source: B-013; Gate 1 — OQ-22

### CH-18-016 · §18.3 Sparks
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Each time a mini-boss, main-boss, or mega-boss is defeated, the characters gain one *spark* as in the rules for the basic game. However, characters no longer rest at the *bonfire* for free after defeating a boss except for the final boss of each section."
- After: "A rest is free only when it follows the completion of a section (see §18.1, Section Completion). The defeat of a boss that does not complete its section grants a spark but no free rest."
- Rationale: The free rest is tied to section completion, which covers sections that end in an encounter. The Twin Gargoyles grant one spark, and the spark is added once.
- Source: A-048, CMP-0008, F-086, F-053

### CH-18-017 · §18.3 Sparks
- Type: clarification
- Before: "The players lose the campaign if all characters are lost to the hollow without a *spark* to revive them."
- After: "If the characters are required to rest while the spark dial shows zero, the players lose the campaign (see §7.1)."
- Rationale: "Lost to the hollow" used an undefined sense of lost. The loss condition is that of §7.1, and the buy-back cannot prevent it.
- Source: A-001, C-033, CMP-0006

### CH-18-018 · §18.3 Sparks
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "*Example.* The Knight, the Herald, and the Assassin begin a campaign with the spark dial at three, and its maximum is four."
- Rationale: A worked example of the spark buy-back with three characters.
- Source: chapter instructions; Gate 1 — OQ-22

### CH-18-019 · §18.4 Adding and Dropping Players
- Type: style
- Before: "During campaign play, sometimes a new player would like to join the characters part way through or an existing player is required to leave. New players can join a campaign that is in progress at the cost of one spark each."
- After: "A player may join or leave a *campaign* in progress. Either change is made only while the characters are on the *bonfire* tile and no *encounter* is in progress."
- Rationale: The conversational lead-in is replaced by two numbered procedures. The change is made on the bonfire tile rather than only during a rest, so that joining costs the manuscript's one spark and not a second for the rest.
- Source: F-039, F-134, C-048, F-086

### CH-18-020 · §18.4 Adding and Dropping Players
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "New players can join a campaign that is in progress at the cost of one spark each."
- After: "The party spends one *spark* for each player who joins. A player may join only while the spark dial shows two or more."
- Rationale: The threshold prevents a join from leaving the party with no spark. A-105 proposed a threshold of one; the approved B-013 governs.
- Source: B-013; Gate 1 — OQ-22; A-105

### CH-18-021 · §18.4 Adding and Dropping Players
- Type: clarification
- Before: "If a player character leaves the characters, the characters gain one spark, the character's equipment is added to the inventory, and the maximum number of sparks is changed to reflect the new number of characters."
- After: "Give every treasure card the departing character holds to the remaining characters, as the players choose. Each card is placed in the *inventory* of the character who receives it."
- Rationale: Inventories and souls are personal, so "the inventory" no longer resolved. The departing character's souls return to the supply, and the maximum is set before the spark is gained.
- Source: A-105, C-048

### CH-18-022 · §18.4 Adding and Dropping Players
- Type: completion
- Before: "The new maximum number of sparks is based on the new number of characters."
- After: "From the moment a player joins or leaves, the new number of characters is used for enemy scaling (see §8.3), for the spark dial's maximum, and for every cost and reward counted per character."
- Rationale: §8.3 relies on this section for the number of characters in a campaign. Treasure cards on uncleared tiles are adjusted so that the reward stays one card per character; no ember card is added to the shop. A worked example is added.
- Source: A-027, A-057

### CH-18-023 · §18.5 Encounter Rewards
- Type: completion
- Before: "Encounter *soul* rewards are reduced by one compared to the basic game." with a one-column table 2 / 3 / 4 / 5
- After: "Each full reward is one soul lower than in the standard game. Each *reduced reward* is half the full reward of this table, rounded down." with columns 2 / 3 / 4 / 5 and 1 / 1 / 2 / 2
- Rationale: The reduced reward had no value. The HTML table becomes a Markdown table with both columns; "&" in the row labels becomes "and".
- Source: A-053, C-068, C-069, CMP-0010; Gate 1 — OQ-05

### CH-18-024 · §18.5 Encounter Rewards
- Type: style
- Before: "Encounters that have been *cleared* may be either skipped for stamina cost or repeated for *souls* rewards but not treasure rewards, just as in the basic game."
- After: "An encounter already marked *cleared* is skipped or fought again as in the standard game (see §7.5, Encounter Start). Fought again, it awards the reduced reward and no treasure."
- Rationale: Bullet fragments become sentences with a numbered cross-reference; the hedge "just" and "the basic game" are removed.
- Source: C-119, F-090, F-053, C-084

### CH-18-025 · §18.5 Encounter Rewards
- Type: clarification
- Before: "All encounters and chests reward tier one treasures initially, even after a mini-boss is defeated. All encounters and chests reward tier two treasures after tier two treasure deck is unlocked by paying 16 *souls* to burn bonfire ascetic."
- After: "In campaign play the current tier is tier one from the start of the *campaign* until the *bonfire ascetic* is burnt, whatever bosses have been defeated, and tier two from then on (see §18.7)."
- Rationale: The pre-dealt card is the reward, so the tier rule is stated for the deal. Cards already dealt are not replaced when the ascetic is burnt. Boss, Level 4, and legendary treasure are each cited to their owning sections.
- Source: A-057, C-045, C-119; Gate 1 — OQ-11

### CH-18-026 · §18.6 The Bonfire
- Type: cut
- Before: "The rules for returning to the *bonfire* are essentially unchanged from the basic rules. … The cost to level up stats at the Firekeeper is increased in Campaign play to slow the characters' progression to scale better across a longer journey."
- After: "The *bonfire* rules of the standard game apply in campaign play (see §11), with the changes of this section."
- Rationale: Design rationale and the hedge "essentially" are removed. The three campaign services of a rest (spark buy-back, ascetic, transposition) are cited.
- Source: F-040, F-090, F-053, C-084, C-042

### CH-18-027 · §18.6 The Bonfire, Stat Tier Costs
- Type: clarification
- Before: "| Tier One | **4** |" … "| Tier Four | **20** |"
- After: "| Stat tier one | 4 |" … "| Stat tier four | 20 |" and "The value of a stat at stat tier four is the same as in the standard game (see §3.3)."
- Rationale: The stat sense of "tier" is always qualified. The campaign costs 4 / 8 / 16 / 20 are unchanged and are double the standard 2 / 4 / 8 / 10.
- Source: C-045, C-071, B-007, A-067; Gate 1 — OQ-09

### CH-18-028 · §18.6 The Bonfire, Blacksmith Andre's Shop
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Andre's Shop functions similarly to the basic rules with some changes to how the shop is stocked to better suit the progression of the campaign. … Tier one and two shops are only restocked after each section is completed."
- After: "The shop is restocked only when a *section* is completed, during the setup of the next section and before the rest that follows (see §18.1, Section Completion)."
- Rationale: Rationale is removed and the bullets become short paragraphs. The sweep of unsold cards, the closed tier two shop, the omitted legendary step, the ban on new ember cards, and step 7 of the rest are stated to match §6.3 and §11.2.
- Source: F-041, C-043, C-119, A-046; Gate 1 — OQ-27; chapter 06 and chapter 11 issues lists

### CH-18-029 · §18.6 The Bonfire, Shop Price Table
- Type: clarification
- Before: "| Boss Treasure | **-** | **4** |" / "| Legendary Shop | **4 + Boss Treasure** | **4** |"
- After: "A boss treasure card sells for four souls in campaign play, in place of the six of the standard game, and is returned to the game box when sold (see §11.4, Sales)."
- Rationale: The table's values had no prose. The legendary row is renamed for transposition, the sale of legendary treasure is stated, and where each sold card goes is given.
- Source: C-070, A-077

### CH-18-030 · §18.7 Bonfire Ascetic
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "After the bonfire ascetic has been burnt, all non-boss enemies increase their attack damage by one for the remainder of the campaign. This effect is cumulative with stat scaling for character count."
- After: "every enemy that is subject to enemy scaling has its base health increased by two for the rest of the campaign (see §8.3). The increase is cumulative with the health increase for the number of characters."
- Rationale: A second +1 attack on top of the scaling for four characters made ordinary enemies lethal in one turn. The health increase lengthens fights instead. "Stat scaling" is corrected to enemy scaling.
- Source: B-031, A-064; Gate 1 — OQ-07

### CH-18-031 · §18.7 Bonfire Ascetic
- Type: clarification
- Before: "A total of sixteen souls may be paid from any combination of character contributions while resting at the bonfire. … This does not affect invaders, mini-bosses, main-bosses, or mega-bosses."
- After: "This payment is the one exception to the rule that souls cannot be pooled (see §7.6)." / "A boss, a *mimic*, a *phantom*, and a *crystal lizard* are not affected by the bonfire ascetic (see §8.3)."
- Rationale: The pooled payment is named as the exception §7.6 and §11.3 require. The exemptions match the enemies that §8.3 does not scale. The stocking of the tier two shop cites §6.3, and "sixteen" becomes 16.
- Source: A-028, C-116, C-120; chapter 11 issues list

### CH-18-032 · §18.7 Bonfire Ascetic
- Type: cut
- Before: "They must decide at what point their need for better equipment outweighs the cost of increased difficulty. … However, burning the bonfire ascetic invites stronger enemies."
- After: deleted
- Rationale: Decision advice and a flavour lead-in inside a rule.
- Source: F-042, F-043

### CH-18-033 · §18.7 Bonfire Ascetic
- Type: completion
- Before: new text
- After: "Its health is 5: the base health of 1, plus two for the bonfire ascetic, plus two for three characters. Its attack value stays at 4."
- Rationale: A worked example of the health increase with three characters.
- Source: chapter instructions; B-031; Gate 1 — OQ-07

### CH-18-034 · §18.8 Transposition
- Type: correction
- Before: "Transposition is only available after the characters spend a *spark* to rest at the *bonfire*."
- After: "From then on a character may transpose during any rest at the *bonfire*, including a rest that costs no *spark* (see §11.2)."
- Rationale: The rest that follows a section's last boss is free, so the literal text denied transposition at the moment it was unlocked. The manuscript's "final boss of the first section" is worded "last boss", because final boss is a defined term for the campaign's last boss.
- Source: A-051

### CH-18-035 · §18.8 Transposition
- Type: restructure
- Before: "First, players gather all available legendary treasure cards and shuffle them to create the legendary treasure deck. Second, five legendary treasure cards are dealt face-up near the *bonfire* tile. Third, …"
- After: "1. Return one boss treasure card that the character holds to the game box. The card takes no further part in the campaign. 2. Pay four *souls* to the supply."
- Rationale: The prose procedure becomes two numbered procedures: the preparation of the five cards and the exchange. The empty-deck case and the limit of one boss treasure card and four souls per exchange are stated.
- Source: C-120, C-119

### CH-18-036 · §18 Campaign Play
- Type: style
- Before: "the *bonfire*" / "The Bonfire" / "Andre's Shop" / "Tier one and two shops" / "16 *souls collectively*" / "face-up near" (mixed forms)
- After: "the *bonfire*" / "Blacksmith Andre's *shop*" / "the tier two shop" / "16 *souls* in all"
- Rationale: Style sweep. Glossary headwords are italicised at first use in each section; "tier" is lower-case in prose; players, the party, and characters are used by the actor policy; straight quotes; numerals by the house rule.
- Source: C-030, C-042, C-043, C-044, C-045, C-058, C-111, C-116, F-086

### CH-18-037 · §18 Campaign Play
- Type: clarification
- Before: new text
- After: (not applied)
- Rationale: Findings not applied. B-011 (PROPOSED): the campaign table keeps the manuscript's 2 / 3 / 4 / 5; see the issues list for the conflict between OQ-21 and OQ-05. B-034 (PROPOSED): the ascetic stays a communal fee of 16 souls, per the chapter instructions and the glossary. A-025 (OPTIONAL): moot, since the ascetic no longer raises attack values and §8.3 owns push-move scaling. A-047 (OPTIONAL): a maximum for the standard game belongs to §7.4. C-103 (CLEAN-UP): the section map fixes the heading titles.
- Source: B-011, B-034, A-025, A-047, C-103

### CH-18-038 · §18.5 Encounter Rewards
- Type: mechanical change
- Before: "Encounter soul rewards are reduced by one compared to the basic game." (campaign table 2 / 3 / 4 / 5)
- After: "The souls awarded are those of the standard reward table: the full reward and the reduced reward for each encounter level, and the rewards for invaders and bosses, are not changed in campaign play (see §7.6)."
- Rationale: Gate 1 answered the campaign-pacing question with the single-lever option: the standard rewards (3 / 4 / 5 / 6) are kept in campaign play and the doubled stat tier costs are retained. With the reduction removed, the campaign needs no reward table of its own; the reduced reward is half the standard reward, rounded down (1 / 2 / 2 / 3). The draft's table (2 / 3 / 4 / 5) followed the manuscript and the illustrative figures beside OQ-05; the lead editor replaced it at integration. Flagged for the author's confirmation because the report did not label OQ-21 as a mechanical change.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-21; B-011

### CH-18-039 · §18.4 Adding and Dropping Players
- Type: clarification
- Before: "A player may join or leave a campaign in progress."
- After: "… A player joins or leaves a standard game in progress under the same rules."
- Rationale: The approved F.A.Q. ruling lets a player join a standard game on the campaign's terms; the rule is stated here so that the F.A.Q. carries no rule of its own.
- Source: CMP-0003

### CH-18-040 · §18.1 Sections
- Type: clarification
- Before: "- A section that lists the Gargoyle twice in succession sets the Twin Gargoyles encounter"
- After: "- A section that lists the Gargoyle as fought twice sets the Twin Gargoyles encounter"
- Rationale: Matches the wording of the campaign lists in §19.
- Source: QA observation (example and consistency verification)

### CH-18-041 · §18.1 Sections (boss before the end of a section)
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Each character who died in the boss encounter is revived as after any cleared encounter (see §7.2), and the exploration continues (see §7.5, Encounter Recovery)."
- After: "Each character who died in the boss encounter is revived as after any cleared encounter, except that the ember token of the boss's defeat keeps that character from becoming *hollow*, so no hollow cube is placed (see §7.5, Encounter Recovery; §16.5). The exploration then continues."
- Rationale: §16.5 step 4 embers every character, the dead included, and an embered character is not hollow; the revival wording implied hollow cubes. No published campaign lists two bosses in one section, so the case arises only in a campaign of the players' own design.
- Source: QA observation (example and consistency verification)

### CH-18-042 · §18.6, Blacksmith Andre's Shop
- Type: ruling
- Before: "Before the shop is restocked, its unsold cards, other than *ember cards*, are placed on the bottom of their treasure decks (see §6.3, Restocking After a Boss). Step 7 of a rest restocks the shop only if a section has been completed and the shop has not been restocked since (see §11.2)."
- After: "Before the shop is restocked, its unsold cards, other than *ember cards*, are placed on the bottom of their treasure decks (see §6.3, Restocking After a Boss)."
- Rationale: Removes the reference to the deleted step.
- Source: final rulings, core-E11 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As core-E09.

### CH-18-043 · §18.1, Level 4 Encounters and Mega-Bosses
- Type: ruling
- Before: "The Level 4 encounter is set up, fought, and rewarded as in the standard game (see §6.7, Level 4 Encounters), with the soul reward of the standard game (see §18.5)."
- After: "The Level 4 encounter is set up and fought as in the standard game (see §6.7, Level 4 Encounters). It is rewarded as any other encounter of its section: every character gains the soul reward of a Level 4 encounter (see §18.5), and one treasure card per character of the current tier is dealt to the game board with its encounter card. No mega-boss treasure card is dealt to a Level 4 encounter in campaign play; a mega-boss's treasure cards are gained only by defeating that mega-boss (see §16.5)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] A mega-boss has two treasure cards, and Call of the Abyss and Into the Depths each list three Level 4 encounters before one mega-boss, so the standard reward cannot be paid; the campaign's own rule is that boss treasure comes only from bosses.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E16 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §6.7 step 5 (one mega-boss treasure card per Level 4 encounter); official booklets: two treasure cards per mega-boss; official campaign lists (Four Kings and Gaping Dragon booklets); source manuscript 'Boss treasure is only obtained by defeating bosses'.

### CH-18-044 · §18.5
- Type: ruling
- Before: "The treasure of a *Level 4 encounter* is that of the standard game (see §6.7, Level 4 Encounters)."
- After: "A *Level 4 encounter* awards treasure cards of the current tier, as any other encounter does, and no mega-boss treasure card (see §18.1, Level 4 Encounters and Mega-Bosses)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Keeps §18.5 in step with §18.1 and removes the contradiction with the sentence before it.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E17 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As campaign-E16.

### CH-18-045 · §18.3
- Type: ruling
- Before: "While the party rests, it may buy back one spark. Every character pays four *souls* of their own to the supply, and the spark dial is turned up by one. If any character holds fewer than four souls, the party cannot buy the spark, because souls cannot be transferred or pooled (see §7.6)."
- After: "While the party rests, it may buy back one spark. The cost is four *souls* for each character in the party, paid to the supply by any combination of the characters, and the spark dial is turned up by one. The spark is not bought unless the full cost is paid. This cost and the cost of the *bonfire ascetic* are the only costs that the characters pay together (see §18.7); souls are never passed from one character to another (see §7.6)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] A party purchase is paid by the party, as the bonfire ascetic already is, so that one character without souls cannot block it.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E18 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official 2017 campaign rules: sparks bought 'at a cost of 2 souls per character in the group' from the shared soul cache (DS-Core-Rules-lores.txt 1601-1606); source manuscript and §18.7: the ascetic is paid 'from any combination of character contributions'.

### CH-18-046 · §18.3
- Type: ruling
- Before: "The Knight holds six souls, the Herald four, and the Assassin five. Each of them pays four souls, and the dial is turned up to two. The Knight is left with two souls, the Herald with none, and the Assassin with one."
- After: "The Knight holds six souls, the Herald four, and the Assassin five. A spark costs 12 souls for three characters. The Knight pays six souls, the Herald two, and the Assassin four, and the dial is turned up to two. The Knight is left with no souls, the Herald with two, and the Assassin with one."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] The example must compute from the pooled payment.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E19 (`06_final_rulings.md`); 4 x 3 = 12; 6 + 2 + 4 = 12.

### CH-18-047 · §18.7
- Type: ruling
- Before: "This payment is the one exception to the rule that souls cannot be pooled (see §7.6)."
- After: "This payment and the cost of a bought *spark* are the only exceptions to the rule that souls cannot be pooled (see §7.6; §18.3)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Two party purchases now share one payment rule.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E20 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As campaign-E18.

### CH-18-048 · §18.6, Shop Price Table
- Type: clarification
- Before: "| *Bonfire ascetic* | 16, paid by the party | - |"
- After: "| *Spark* | 4 for each character, paid by the party | - |
| *Bonfire ascetic* | 16, paid by the party | - |"
- Rationale: Puts every campaign price in the one table.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E21 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §18.3.

### CH-18-049 · §18.4
- Type: ruling
- Before: "A player may join only while the spark dial shows two or more."
- After: "A player cannot join while the spark dial shows zero."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] The cost of joining is one spark, so one spark is enough; a second condition is not needed.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E28 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official 2017 rule: 'New players can join a campaign that is in progress at the cost of 1 spark each' (DS-Core-Rules-lores.txt 1615-1618); source manuscript the same; A-105.

### CH-18-050 · §18.4
- Type: clarification
- Before: "A player joins or leaves a standard game in progress under the same rules."
- After: "A player joins or leaves a standard game in progress under the same rules; the steps that set the spark dial's maximum apply only in campaign play."
- Rationale: The standard game has no campaign maximum, so two steps of each procedure do not apply to it.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E29 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §7.4 and §18.3.

### CH-18-051 · §18.1, Section Setup
- Type: completion
- Before: "- The encounter decks are built from the sets the campaign names, in place of the set that supplies the next boss (see §6.5, Expansion Encounters)."
- After: "- The encounter decks are built from the encounter cards of every set the campaign requires, shuffled together by level, in place of the set that supplies the next boss (see §6.5, Expansion Encounters). Where a campaign names the sets for its encounter decks, only those sets are used."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Six of the twelve campaigns name no sets for their decks; one default closes the gap for all of them.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E30 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official booklets: Call of the Abyss and Into the Depths 'shared deck of encounters from the core game and the Darkroot expansion'; Go Beyond Death 'shared deck ... core game, the Explorers expansion, and the Iron Keep expansion'.

### CH-18-052 · §18.8
- Type: clarification
- Before: "Transposition is available after the last boss of the first *section* is defeated. In a *campaign* whose first section ends in an encounter, it is available after that section is completed (see §18.1, Section Completion)."
- After: "Transposition is available once the first *section* of the *campaign* is completed (see §18.1, Section Completion)."
- Rationale: Section completion already covers a section that ends in a boss and one that ends in an encounter; one sentence replaces two.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E31 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §18.1, Section Completion; F.A.Q. §22.7 already words it this way.

### CH-18-053 · §18.1, Section Setup
- Type: ruling
- Before: "- If *invaders* are in play, no more than one invasion is provoked in each section (see §12.2)."
- After: "- If *invaders* are in play, no more than one invasion is provoked in each section (see §12.2). The invader token drawn for an invasion is a standard invader token until the *bonfire ascetic* is burnt and an advanced invader token from then on (see §18.7)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] §12.2 chooses the token by whether the mini-boss has been defeated, which has no meaning in a campaign; the bonfire ascetic already takes the mini-boss's place for treasure and the shop.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E33 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §12.2, Invader Tokens, step 1; §18.5 to §18.7 (the ascetic opens tier two in place of the mini-boss's defeat); official Phantoms rules key the two invader sets to the mini-boss's defeat.

### CH-18-054 · §18.1
- Type: completion
- Before: "The party chooses one of the 12 campaigns (see §19)."
- After: "The party chooses one of the 16 campaigns (see §19)."
- Rationale: Four campaigns are added as §19.13 to §19.16.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E45 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Old Iron King, The Last Giant, Vordt of the Boreal Valley and Guardian Dragon booklets, campaign pages.

### CH-18-055 · §18.1, Player versus Player Encounters
- Type: ruling
- Before: "When they are in play, a *PvP encounter* follows the defeat of each boss, as in the standard game."
- After: "When they are in play, a *PvP encounter* follows the defeat of each boss whose defeat completes a section. A boss defeated before its section is completed is followed by no PvP encounter."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] A contest in the middle of a section handed a co-operating party a full restoration; tying the contest to the end of a section removes that and the rule that restored characters to an earlier state.
- Source: final rulings, lead-E02 (`06_final_rulings.md`); phantom memo, cross-cluster effects

### CH-18-056 · §18.1, Player versus Player Encounters
- Type: ruling
- Before: "When the boss's defeat completes a section, the setup and the rest that follow the PvP encounter are those of the completed section (see §18.1, Section Completion; §20.9). When the boss's defeat does not complete its section, no rest follows the PvP encounter. Every character who took part is instead restored as at the start of the PvP encounter (see §20.1), each character who died in it returns to the tile, and the exploration continues."
- After: "The setup and the rest that follow the PvP encounter are those of the completed section (see §18.1, Section Completion; §20.9)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Follows lead-E02.
- Source: final rulings, lead-E03 (`06_final_rulings.md`)

### CH-18-057 · §18.1, Section Setup
- Type: completion
- Before: "- A mini-boss is fought on the mini-boss tile, and a *main-boss* on the two *boss tiles* joined as one (see §15.3)."
- After: "- A mini-boss is fought on the mini-boss tile, or on its expansion's game board when it is the Asylum Demon (see §17.3), and a *main-boss* on the two *boss tiles* joined as one (see §15.3)."
- Rationale: As lead-E15, for campaign play.
- Source: final rulings, lead-E16 (`06_final_rulings.md`); boss memo, cross-cluster effects

### CH-18-058 · §18.1, Section Completion
- Type: ruling
- Before: "Each character who died in the boss encounter is revived as after any cleared encounter, except that the ember token of the boss's defeat keeps that character from becoming *hollow*, so no hollow cube is placed (see §7.5, Encounter Recovery; §16.5). The exploration then continues."
- After: "Each surviving character recovers, and each character who died in the boss encounter is revived, as after any cleared encounter, except that the ember token of the boss's defeat keeps a revived character from becoming *hollow*, so no hollow cube is placed (see §7.5, Encounter Recovery; §16.5). The exploration then continues."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] After a boss that does not complete its section no rest follows, so the survivors recover as after any cleared encounter; otherwise a dead character would return in better state than a living one.
- Source: final rulings, VB-009 (`06_final_rulings.md`); independent verification, work/phase3/verify_b.md (judgement call decided by the lead editor)

## Chapter 21 — Glossary

### CH-21-001 · §21 Glossary
- Type: completion
- Before: no glossary in the body; the Introduction promised one and the contents listed it
- After: a glossary of 172 headwords, each a bold headword with a definition in the present tense and a reference to its owning section
- Rationale: The chapter was missing. It was seeded from the approved draft, reconciled with the Gate 1 answers, and then amended chapter by chapter as each section fixed its rule, so that every italicised term in the book has an entry.
- Source: CMP-0002, C-031, C-032; C §5 (keyword audit). C-017 recorded a consistent usage and required no change.

### CH-21-002 · §21 Glossary (seed reconciliation)
- Type: correction
- Before: (approved draft) eight-space endurance bar; mega-boss permadeath; poison removes one token; scaling at ten over; summon acts once per round; ascetic adds attack; event cards in scry
- After: entries restated to the Gate 1 answers before chapter drafting began
- Rationale: The draft pre-dated the decisions; a reference that contradicts the rules is worse than none.
- Source: Gate 1 — OQ-06, OQ-07, OQ-08, OQ-13, OQ-14, OQ-16, OQ-19, OQ-22, OQ-32

### CH-21-003 · §21 Glossary (final reconciliation)
- Type: correction
- Before: entries left by the last chapter to amend them: Threat without the dashboard's printed label; Invader "does not count towards clearing"; Behaviour card and Shift naming "phantom" where "summon" is meant; Treasure card omitting ember cards
- After: eleven entries restated by the lead editor (Threat, Invader, Behaviour card, Shift, Encounter, Boss, Embered, Treasure card, Main-boss, Mini-boss, Hollow)
- Rationale: Where chapters drafted in parallel amended the same headword, the last merge won regardless of ownership. The owning section's rule is restored, and the bridge from threat to the dashboard's printed Taunt Level is kept as the Preserve list requires.
- Source: D-039, CH-12-033, chapter issues lists

### CH-21-004 · §21 Glossary (verification amendments)
- Type: correction
- Before: "Dropped souls: reclaimed only by surviving to clear that encounter. Shop: a treasure card other than starting equipment sells for one soul. Tier two treasure: every treasure card with a stat requirement of 25 or more. Twin Gargoyles: a section that lists the Gargoyle twice in succession."
- After: "Dropped souls: also returned when a boss is defeated. Shop: a tier one or tier two treasure card sells for one soul and a boss treasure card for six. Tier two treasure: common and class-specific treasure with a stat requirement of 25 or more. Twin Gargoyles: a section that lists the Gargoyle as fought twice."
- Rationale: Four entries disagreed with the sections they cite (§6.6, §11.4, §6.2, §19).
- Source: QA-003, QA-008, QA-009, QA observation (example and consistency verification)

### CH-21-005 · Glossary
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**Hollow cube.** One of eight cubes of a colour distinct from the damage, stamina, and stat cubes. Two hollow cubes block the two rightmost spaces of a *hollow* character's endurance bar (see §7.2)."
- After: "**Hollow cube.** A white cube, taken from the white cubes that are not in use as stat cubes, placed on an endurance bar. Two hollow cubes block the two rightmost spaces of a *hollow* character's endurance bar (see §2.8, §7.2)."
- Rationale: Glossary follows the component ruling.
- Source: final rulings, core-E04 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As core-E02.

### CH-21-006 · Glossary
- Type: completion
- Before: "**Ember card.** A tier one shop card. Discarding an *ember* card from the back-up slot (the *Ember* major action, or outside an encounter) makes the character *embered* (see §7.3). A character may carry only one *ember* card and cannot carry one while *embered*; a card held when the character becomes *embered* passes to another character (see §7.3)."
- After: "**Ember card.** A tier one shop card. Discarding an *ember* card from the back-up slot (the *Ember* major action, or outside an encounter) makes the character *embered*, and the card is returned to the game box (see §7.3). A character may carry only one *ember* card and cannot carry one while *embered*; a card held when the character becomes *embered* passes to another character (see §7.3)."
- Rationale: Glossary follows §7.3.
- Source: final rulings, core-E25 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As core-E23.

### CH-21-007 · Glossary
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**Titanite.** A weapon upgrade (Titanite Shard, Titanite Chunk, Titanite Slab, or Titanite Scale) that is fused permanently to the weapon it is attached to. A Titanite Chunk is attached only in place of an attached Titanite Shard, and a Titanite Slab only in place of an attached Titanite Chunk; the replaced card returns to the bottom of the treasure deck of its tier (see Appendix B). When that weapon is sold, the Titanite is returned with it to the bottom of the treasure deck of the weapon's tier (see §4.2)."
- After: "**Titanite.** A weapon upgrade card whose name includes the word Titanite (Titanite Shard, Titanite Chunk, Titanite Slab, Titanite Scale, Demon Titanite, and Blue Titanite), fused permanently to the weapon it is attached to. A Titanite Chunk is attached only in place of an attached Titanite Shard, and a Titanite Slab only in place of an attached Titanite Chunk; the replaced card returns to the bottom of the treasure deck of its tier (see Appendix B). When that weapon is sold, the Titanite is sold with it for no additional *souls*, and each card goes where a sold card of its kind goes (see §4.2; §11.4)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Carries both Titanite rulings: Titanite is recognised by name (core-063) and a sold Titanite card goes to the place of its own kind (core-048).
- Source: final rulings, core-E33 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As core-E31 and core-E34.

### CH-21-008 · Glossary
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**Bonfire.** The characters' safe haven, represented by the *bonfire* tile, which has no nodes. The Firekeeper and Blacksmith Andre wait there and are visited only while the party rests. Returning to the bonfire tile costs nothing; resting there costs one *spark* (see §11.1, §11.2)."
- After: "**Bonfire.** The characters' safe haven, represented by the *bonfire* tile, which has no nodes. The Firekeeper and Blacksmith Andre wait there and are visited only while the party rests. The party returns to the bonfire tile only to rest, and a rest costs one *spark* unless it follows the defeat of a boss (see §11.1, §11.2)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Glossary follows §11.1.
- Source: final rulings, core-E37 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As core-E35.

### CH-21-009 · Glossary, Gravestone
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**Gravestone.** A terrain token or model. No model may *move*, *dodge*, or be pushed into a node containing a gravestone. When an encounter that contains a gravestone is *cleared*, the players may *scry* one card (see §5.5, Gravestones; §7.5, Encounter Resolution)."
- After: "**Gravestone.** A terrain token or model. No model may *move*, *dodge*, or be pushed into a node containing a gravestone. When an encounter is *cleared*, the players may *scry* one card for each gravestone on its tile (see §5.5, Gravestones; §7.5, Encounter Resolution)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Glossary follows §5.5.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E04 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As combat-E01.

### CH-21-010 · Glossary, Entry node
- Type: clarification
- Before: "**Entry node.** A basic node beside the doorway through which the characters entered the tile. Characters are placed on entry nodes at the start of an encounter, no more than three to a node. In a Level 4 encounter all four nodes along the wall beside the doorway are entry nodes (see §6.7)."
- After: "**Entry node.** A basic node along the tile edge that holds the doorway through which the characters entered the tile. Characters are placed on entry nodes at the start of an encounter, no more than three to a node (see §5.1; §7.5, Encounter Start). In a Level 4 encounter the four basic nodes along that edge are the entry nodes (see §6.7)."
- Rationale: Glossary follows §5.1.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E06 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As combat-E05.

### CH-21-011 · Glossary, Basic node
- Type: clarification
- Before: "**Basic node.** The most common node type. Basic nodes adjacent to a doorway are also entry nodes."
- After: "**Basic node.** The most common node type, printed in yellow. Trap tokens are dealt to basic nodes, and the basic nodes along the edge through which the characters entered a tile are its entry nodes (see §5.1)."
- Rationale: Removes the second, looser definition of an entry node.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E07 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As combat-E05.

### CH-21-012 · Glossary, Doorway
- Type: clarification
- Before: "**Doorway.** A gap in a tile's wall through which characters pass between tiles. Basic nodes beside a doorway are entry nodes."
- After: "**Doorway.** A gap in a tile's wall through which the characters pass from one tile to the next. No model passes through a doorway during an encounter, and the basic nodes along the edge of the doorway by which the characters entered are the entry nodes (see §5.1; §5.2)."
- Rationale: Removes the third, looser definition of an entry node and states the doorway rule of §5.2.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E08 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As combat-E05.

### CH-21-013 · Glossary, Repeat
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**Repeat.** An icon that causes an entire attack option or behaviour card to be resolved the number of times shown. For a character's attack option the stamina cost is paid once and each resolution is a separate attack roll with its own target (see §10.3, Repeat)."
- After: "**Repeat.** An icon that causes an entire attack option, or the whole row of behaviour icons on an enemy data card or a behaviour card, to be resolved more than once. A character may resolve the attack option up to the number of times shown: the stamina cost is paid once, and each resolution is a separate attack roll with its own target (see §10.3, Repeat). An enemy or a boss resolves its row of icons the number of times shown (see §9.1; §16.1)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Glossary follows §9.1, §10.3 and §16.1.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E36 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As combat-E12 and combat-E27.

### CH-21-014 · Glossary, Push
- Type: correction
- Before: "**Push.** Forced movement of a model from its node to an adjacent valid node. A push is caused by a push-attack, by a push-move, by the node model limit, or by a boss model moving onto the node of another boss model. A push is not a move and costs no stamina; a gravestone, a chest, and a pillar token block it, and a push with no valid node does not happen (see §8.4)."
- After: "**Push.** Forced movement of a model from its node to an adjacent valid node. A push is caused by a push-attack, by a push-move, by the node model limit, by a boss model moving onto the node of another boss model, or by a rule of a boss such as a leap. A push is not a move and costs no stamina; a gravestone, a chest, and a pillar token block it, and a push with no valid node does not happen (see §8.4)."
- Rationale: Glossary follows §8.4.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E37 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As combat-E13.

### CH-21-015 · Glossary, Condition
- Type: clarification
- Before: "**Condition.** A lasting effect marked by a condition token on a model: *bleed*, *poison*, *frostbite*, *stagger*, *calamity*, or *corrosion*. A condition token is placed on each model hit by an attack that bears the matching icon, after the attack is resolved. A model holds at most one token of each condition, except poison (see §8.5)."
- After: "**Condition.** A lasting effect marked by a condition token on a model: *bleed*, *poison*, *frostbite*, *stagger*, *calamity*, or *corrosion*. A condition token is placed on each model hit by an attack that bears the matching icon, after the attack's damage and any push have been resolved. A model holds at most one token of each condition, except poison (see §8.5)."
- Rationale: Glossary states the same order as §8.5 and the attack sequence.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E38 (`06_final_rulings.md`); This book, §8.5.

### CH-21-016 · Glossary, Parry
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**Parry.** A defence available to a character with a shield equipped in the left weapon slot when the attacker is on the character's node; an attack that pushes, a node attack, and a boss attack outside §15.6 cannot be parried. The character pays one stamina and rolls dodge dice against the attack's dodge difficulty. Success makes the attack miss, staggers the attacker, and allows a *riposte*; failure deals the full attack value with no block or resist roll and staggers the character (see §9.6)."
- After: "**Parry.** A defence available to a character with a shield equipped in either weapon slot when the attacker is on the character's node; an attack that pushes, a node attack, and a boss attack outside §15.6 cannot be parried. The character pays one stamina and rolls dodge dice against the attack's dodge difficulty. Success makes the attack miss, staggers the attacker, and allows a *riposte*; failure deals the full attack value with no block or resist roll and staggers the character (see §9.6)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Glossary follows §9.6.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E39 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As combat-E22.

### CH-21-017 · Glossary, Riposte
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**Riposte.** The attack a character may make at once against the attacker after a successful *parry*, using one attack option of the weapon in the right weapon slot and paying its stamina cost. A riposte cannot be parried, and it does not count against the attacks of the character's own turn (see §9.6)."
- After: "**Riposte.** The attack a character may make at once against the attacker after a successful *parry*, using one attack option of the weapon in the other weapon slot, never a spell, and paying its stamina cost. A riposte cannot be parried, and it does not count against the attacks of the character's own turn (see §9.6)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Glossary follows §9.6.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E40 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As combat-E22.

### CH-21-018 · Glossary, Scaled (weapon)
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**Scaled (weapon).** A weapon equipped by a character whose *relevant stat* exceeds the weapon's highest printed stat requirement by five or more. It is marked with a red cube and each of its attack options adds one to its attack value. Spells are never scaled (see §10.5)."
- After: "**Scaled (weapon).** A weapon equipped by a character whose *relevant stat* exceeds the weapon's highest printed stat requirement by five or more. It is marked with a red cube and each of its attack options adds one to its attack value. A weapon whose stat requirements are all zero is never scaled, and spells are never scaled (see §10.5)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Glossary follows §10.5.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E41 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As combat-E29.

### CH-21-019 · Glossary, Attack value
- Type: clarification
- Before: "**Attack value.** The total of the values rolled on an attack's dice after rerolls, with any fixed modifier applied once to the total, plus one for a *scaled* weapon and minus one for a *staggered* attacker, to a minimum of zero (see §10.3). For enemies the attack value is the fixed number printed in the attack icon, scaled for the number of characters (see §9.3)."
- After: "**Attack value.** The total of the values rolled on an attack's dice after rerolls, with any fixed modifier applied once to the total, plus one for a *scaled* weapon and minus one for a *staggered* attacker, to a minimum of zero (see §10.3). For enemies the attack value is the fixed number printed in the attack icon, or the damage value of a push-move, scaled for the number of characters and reduced by one while the enemy is *staggered* (see §8.3; §8.4; §9.3)."
- Rationale: Adds the push-move damage value and the stagger reduction, which §8.3, §8.4 and §8.5 already treat as part of an enemy attack value.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E42 (`06_final_rulings.md`); This book, §8.3; §8.4; §8.5, Stagger.

### CH-21-020 · Glossary, Magical damage
- Type: clarification
- Before: "**Magical damage.** Damage from an attack marked with the white circle icon. Magical damage is reduced by resist."
- After: "**Magical damage.** Damage from an attack that bears the magical damage icon. Magical damage is reduced by resist (see §9.3; §10.3)."
- Rationale: Names the icon as §4.3, §8.2, §9.3 and §10.3 name it.
- Source: final rulings, combat-E43 (`06_final_rulings.md`); This book, §9.3 and §10.3.

### CH-21-021 · Glossary, Physical damage
- Type: clarification
- Before: "**Physical damage.** Damage from an attack marked with the crossed swords icon. Physical damage is reduced by block."
- After: "**Physical damage.** Damage from an enemy attack marked with the crossed swords icon, or from a character's attack option that does not bear the magical damage icon. Physical damage is reduced by block (see §9.3; §10.3)."
- Rationale: A character's attack option shows no crossed swords icon; it deals physical damage by default (§10.3).
- Source: final rulings, combat-E44 (`06_final_rulings.md`); This book, §10.3.

### CH-21-022 · Bonfire ascetic
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**Bonfire ascetic.** In campaign play, a one-time communal payment of 16 *souls* made while resting at the *bonfire*, the one exception to the rule that souls cannot be pooled. It opens tier two treasure and the tier two shop and increases by two the base health of every enemy subject to enemy scaling for the rest of the campaign, cumulative with the health scaling for the number of characters. Enemy attack values are not changed (see §18.7)."
- After: "**Bonfire ascetic.** In campaign play, a one-time communal payment of 16 *souls* made while resting at the *bonfire*. With the cost of a bought *spark*, it is one of the two exceptions to the rule that souls cannot be pooled. It opens tier two treasure and the tier two shop and increases by two the base health of every enemy subject to enemy scaling for the rest of the campaign, cumulative with the health scaling for the number of characters. Enemy attack values are not changed (see §18.7)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Glossary follows §18.3 and §18.7.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E22 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As campaign-E18.

### CH-21-023 · Transposition
- Type: clarification
- Before: "**Transposition.** In campaign play, exchanging one boss treasure card and four *souls* for one of the five face-up legendary treasure cards while resting at the *bonfire*. It is available after the last boss of the first section is defeated, and the boss treasure card is returned to the game box (see §18.8)."
- After: "**Transposition.** In campaign play, exchanging one boss treasure card and four *souls* for one of the five face-up legendary treasure cards while resting at the *bonfire*. It is available once the first section is completed, and the boss treasure card is returned to the game box (see §18.8)."
- Rationale: Glossary follows §18.8.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E32 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As campaign-E31.

### CH-21-024 · Fog gate
- Type: clarification
- Before: "**Fog gate.** The token that marks the doorway of a boss encounter. A boss encounter begins when the characters pass through it. The first time they pass through, the token is moved to the *bonfire* tile, where it is a shortcut to that boss until the boss is defeated (see §15.1). In campaign play it is aesthetic only (see §18.2)."
- After: "**Fog gate.** The token that marks the doorway of a boss encounter. A boss encounter begins when the characters pass through it. The first time they pass through, the token is moved to the *bonfire* tile, where it is a shortcut to that boss until the boss is defeated (see §15.1). In campaign play it only marks the doorway of the boss encounter: it is not moved and gives no shortcut (see §18.2)."
- Rationale: The glossary should say what §18.2 says: the token still marks the doorway and is where a summon is called.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E34 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §18.2.

### CH-21-025 · Campaign
- Type: completion
- Before: "**Campaign.** A game played through a fixed list of *sections*, taken in order, in which the characters keep what they have gained from one section to the next. The book supplies 12 campaigns; campaign play follows the standard game except as its own chapter states (see §18.1, §19)."
- After: "**Campaign.** A game played through a fixed list of *sections*, taken in order, in which the characters keep what they have gained from one section to the next. The book supplies 16 campaigns; campaign play follows the standard game except as its own chapter states (see §18.1, §19)."
- Rationale: Glossary follows §18.1 and §19.
- Source: final rulings, campaign-E47 (`06_final_rulings.md`); As campaign-E45.

### CH-21-026 · Invader token
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**Invader token.** A token from the Phantoms expansion that shows an invader or is blank. When an *invasion* is provoked, one token is placed face down on each face-down encounter card, one of them showing an invader; a token is revealed when the characters fight that encounter (see §12.2)."
- After: "**Invader token.** A token from the Phantoms expansion that shows an invader or is blank. When an *invasion* is provoked, one token is placed face down on each face-down encounter card that carries no black cube, one of them showing an invader; a token is revealed when the characters fight that encounter (see §12.2)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Keeps the entry in step with §12.2, Invader Tokens.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E33 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Follows phantom-E06.

### CH-21-027 · Invasion
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**Invasion.** The placing of invader tokens that brings one invader into the game. An invasion is provoked when the encounter cards are dealt while a character is *embered*, or when a character becomes embered by discarding an *ember card* while a face-down encounter card lies on a tile; no more than one is provoked before the mini-boss is defeated and no more than one after it (see §12.2)."
- After: "**Invasion.** The placing of invader tokens that brings one invader into the game. An invasion is provoked when the encounter cards are dealt while a character is *embered*, or when a character becomes embered by discarding an *ember card* while a face-down encounter card that carries no black cube lies on a tile; no more than one is provoked before the mini-boss is defeated and no more than one after it (see §12.2)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Keeps the entry in step with §12.2, Invasions.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E34 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Follows phantom-E04.

### CH-21-028 · Weak arc
- Type: correction
- Before: "**Weak arc.** A boss arc shown in red on the arc diagram of the boss's current behaviour card, the card it drew most recently. A character who attacks a boss from a weak arc adds one black die to each attack roll, however many weak arcs the character is in (see §15.6)."
- After: "**Weak arc.** A boss arc shown in red on the arc diagram of the boss's current behaviour card, the card it drew most recently. A character or a *summon* that attacks a boss from a weak arc adds one black die to each attack roll, however many weak arcs the attacker is in (see §15.6)."
- Rationale: §12.1 and §15.6 give the weak-arc die to a summon; the entry named characters only.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E35 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Official Phantoms booklet: 'Summons, like characters, gain an additional black die on attacks in the weak arc' (lines 264-266).

### CH-21-029 · Turn
- Type: correction
- Before: "**Turn.** One model's opportunity to act. In each round the enemies take their turns, then the characters take theirs in descending threat order (see §7.5, Encounter Turn Order; §10.1). In a boss encounter the boss takes a turn before each character's turn, and the players choose the order of the characters (see §16.1)."
- After: "**Turn.** One model's opportunity to act. In each round the enemies take their turns, then the characters take theirs in descending threat order (see §7.5, Encounter Turn Order; §10.1). In a boss encounter the boss takes a turn before each character's turn, and the players choose the order of the characters (see §16.1). An *invader* takes a turn after each character's turn, and so does a *summon* in a boss encounter (see §12)."
- Rationale: The entry omitted the two models whose turns follow each character's turn.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E36 (`06_final_rulings.md`); §12.1, Summon Turns and §12.2, Invader Turns of this book.

### CH-21-030 · Crystal lizard
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**Crystal lizard.** An optional enemy from the Iron Keep expansion, placed on a terrain node that the encounter card marks with the crystal lizard symbol. It flees rather than fights, is not scaled, does not count towards clearing the encounter, and escapes without award when the encounter is *cleared*. The character who kills one takes a Titanite Scale card, and once one has been killed in an encounter none is placed there again (see §14)."
- After: "**Crystal lizard.** An optional enemy from the Iron Keep expansion, placed on a terrain node that the encounter card marks with the crystal lizard symbol. It flees rather than fights, is not scaled, does not count towards clearing the encounter, and escapes without award when the encounter is *cleared*. Killing one awards the party a Titanite Scale card, and once one has been killed in an encounter none is placed there again (see §14)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Keeps the entry in step with §14: the card is awarded to the party.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E37 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Follows phantom-E24.

### CH-21-031 · Mimic encounter
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**Mimic encounter.** The encounter fought against a *mimic* on the tile of a *cleared* encounter whose chest the party has opened. It is fought as a mini-boss encounter without arcs, with the characters on the nodes they occupy and before any recovery or reward (see §13.2)."
- After: "**Mimic encounter.** The encounter fought against a *mimic* on the tile of a *cleared* encounter whose chest the party has opened. It is fought as a mini-boss encounter without arcs, with the characters on the nodes they occupy. The characters remove their stamina cubes before the mimic's first turn; the recovery and the rewards of the cleared encounter wait until the mimic is killed (see §13.2)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Keeps the entry in step with §13.2 as amended.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E38 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Follows phantom-E19.

### CH-21-032 · Victory bonus
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**Victory bonus.** The reward for winning a *PvP encounter* that follows the defeat of a boss other than the final boss, marked by one wound token of value 1 placed beside the winner's dashboard. Each victory bonus adds one to the attack value of each of the character's attacks in every subsequent PvP encounter; victory bonuses are cumulative and are kept until the game ends (see §20.8)."
- After: "**Victory bonus.** The reward for winning a *PvP encounter* that follows the defeat of a boss other than the final boss, marked by a spare token placed beside the winner's dashboard. It adds one to the attack value of each of the character's attacks in every subsequent PvP encounter. A character holds no more than one victory bonus, and it is kept until the game ends (see §20.8)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Keeps the entry in step with §20.8: one bonus, not cumulative, marked by a spare token.
- Source: final rulings, phantom-E39 (`06_final_rulings.md`); Follows phantom-E30.

### CH-21-033 · Glossary, Boss
- Type: clarification
- Before: "**Boss.** A single powerful enemy whose turns are driven by a behaviour deck and whose base is divided into four arcs. There are three ranks: *mini-boss*, *main-boss*, and *mega-boss*. A boss is not scaled for the number of characters and cannot be pushed by characters (see §15; §16)."
- After: "**Boss.** A powerful enemy whose turns are driven by a behaviour deck and whose base is divided into four arcs. Most bosses are fought with one model; Ornstein & Smough and the Four Kings are fought with several boss models, each with its own health dial (see §17.1). There are three ranks: *mini-boss*, *main-boss*, and *mega-boss*. A boss is not scaled for the number of characters and cannot be pushed by characters (see §15; §16)."
- Rationale: A boss is no longer always a single model.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E30 (`06_final_rulings.md`); follows boss-E08

### CH-21-034 · Glossary, Heat-up
- Type: ruling
- Before: "**Heat-up.** The event in which a boss, mimic, or invader becomes more dangerous. When a boss's health is reduced to or below its heat-up point, one heat-up card taken at random is shuffled into the cards that remain in its behaviour deck (see §15.5); a mimic or an invader adds one of its unused behaviour cards instead. Some bosses heat up differently (see §17)."
- After: "**Heat-up.** The event in which a boss, mimic, or invader becomes more dangerous. When a boss's health is reduced to or below its heat-up point, its behaviour deck and discard pile are gathered at the start of its next turn and shuffled together with one heat-up card taken at random to form a new behaviour deck (see §15.5); a mimic or an invader adds one of its unused behaviour cards instead. Some bosses heat up differently (see §17)."
- Rationale: [MECHANICAL CHANGE] Keeps the glossary in step with the heat-up rule of boss-E01.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E31 (`06_final_rulings.md`); follows boss-E01

### CH-21-035 · Glossary, Signature card
- Type: clarification
- Before: "**Signature card.** One of Black Dragon Kalameet's two behaviour cards, Mark of Calamity and Hellfire Blast, that are included in every behaviour deck built for Kalameet (see §17.8)."
- After: "**Signature card.** A behaviour card that is included in every behaviour deck built for its boss: Black Dragon Kalameet's Mark of Calamity and Hellfire Blast, the Gaping Dragon's two Stomach Slam cards, and the Old Iron King's three Fire Beam cards (see §17.8; §17.10)."
- Rationale: The term now serves three bosses.
- Source: final rulings, boss-E32 (`06_final_rulings.md`); follows boss-E14, boss-E21, boss-E25

### CH-21-036 · Glossary, Wind Up
- Type: clarification
- Before: "**Wind Up.** A behaviour icon printed on behaviour cards of the Asylum Demon and the Stray Demon. When it is resolved, each character may spend one stamina to move to an adjacent node before the next icon is resolved; the movement counts as a *Run* (see §17.3)."
- After: "**Wind Up.** A behaviour icon printed on behaviour cards of the Asylum Demon, the Stray Demon, and the Last Giant. When it is resolved, each character may spend one stamina to move to an adjacent node before the next icon is resolved; the movement counts as a *Run* (see §17.3)."
- Rationale: The Last Giant's cards show the same icon.
